# AILANG Changelog: Current (Bytecode VM & Runtime)

> Archived from CHANGELOG.md. See [CHANGELOG.md](../CHANGELOG.md) for latest and links to all archives.

## [Unreleased]

### Fixed — `ailang install <version>` returns an actionable error

`ailang install v0.23.0` previously failed with the opaque `invalid package name: "v0.23.0" (must be vendor/name)` — `ailang install` is for registry *packages* (`vendor/name[@version]`), not the toolchain, but nothing said so. It now detects a version-like argument and points at the real toolchain-install path (`go install github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/cmd/ailang@<tag>` or the release page), while still suggesting the package form. Surfaced by an external integrator (motoko_agent PR #33) who hit the confusing error during a v0.22→v0.23 upgrade. (`cmd/ailang/pkg_install.go` + `looksLikeToolchainVersion` unit test.)

## [v0.23.0] - 2026-05-29

### Changed — Notification daemon fans out to multiple channels (M-NOTIFY-FANOUT, v0.23.0)

Build-order Step 3 — the "join". The existing laptop notification daemon (`internal/daemon`, wired in `cmd/ailang/daemon.go`) already subscribed to the `events`/`messages` Pub/Sub topics, mapped task `pending_approval`/`completed`/`failed` and inbox messages to notifications, deduped, excluded, and **nacked on failure** — but it fired to a single channel (macOS desktop). It now fans out over a `notify.Registry` (macOS + any env-gated remote channels like Discord). **Additive/backward-compatible**: with no `AILANG_DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL` set, the registry holds only macOS and behaviour is identical to before.

- **Local best-effort, remote authoritative** (`internal/notify/registry.go::SendAll` + `LocalChannel` interface, + tests). A native desktop notifier reports success the moment it *queues* a banner — even if the user is away — so letting it gate the ack would silently drop a simultaneously-failed Discord send (the channel that actually reaches the phone off-session). `MacOSChannel` declares `IsLocal()`; the fan-out fires it but never counts it toward the ack and never retries on its failure. Remote channels (Discord) are authoritative: the ack requires every remote channel to succeed, else `SendAll` returns an error and the daemon nacks. With no remote channel registered, it falls back to today's "ack if the local one delivered".
- **Retry actually retries** (`internal/daemon/dedup.go::forget` + handler nack paths, + test). The dedup recorded an event's key *before* firing, so a nack-on-failure was eaten by the dedup window on redelivery. Handlers now `forget` the key on every nack path (delivery failure, fetch error, "message not yet visible"), so Pub/Sub redelivery genuinely re-attempts — also repairing the pre-existing "not yet visible → retry" path that had the same latent bug. Successful deliveries stay deduped (no duplicate banners from Pub/Sub at-least-once).

### Added — Outbound notification channel framework + Discord webhook (M-NOTIFY-CHANNELS, v0.23.0)

Build-order Step 2 of the autonomous-triage work: a pluggable outbound-notification framework in `internal/notify`, ported from the design behind Aitana's v6 Channels Framework (`BaseChannel` + `ChannelRegistry`). It reuses the **existing** `internal/notify.Notification` (the macOS notifier's type) rather than inventing a second one, so the long-standing macOS desktop notifier becomes just one `Channel` alongside the new Discord webhook. Self-contained library this step — the daemon fan-out + per-channel routing is Step 3. See [`m-notify-channels-framework`](../design_docs/planned/v0_24_0/m-notify-channels-framework.md).

- **M1: Framework core** (`internal/notify/channel.go` + `registry.go` + tests, +197 LOC). `Channel{ Name() string; Send(ctx, Notification) error }` and a `Registry` output router (`Register`/`Get`/`Names`; idempotent on the same instance, errors on a *different* instance for the same name). `MacOSChannel` wraps the existing `Notify` path so the desktop notifier is a registered channel.
- **M2: Discord webhook + rune-safe chunker** (`internal/notify/discord.go` + `chunk.go` + tests, +231 LOC). `DiscordChannel.Send` renders a notification (bold title + subtitle/body/URL; macOS-only fields ignored) and POSTs it to the incoming webhook, chunked at Discord's 2000-char limit via `chunkMessage` (port of Aitana `_chunk.py`, never splits a multi-byte rune). Transport errors and non-2xx responses return typed errors for dead-lettering. HTTP is injected (`httpDoer`) so tests cover happy path / chunking / 500 / transport failure with zero network.
- **M3: Fail-closed registration + smoke contract + docs** (`internal/notify/register.go` + `smoke_test.go` + `docs/docs/guides/notification-channels.md`, +~120 LOC). `RegisterChannels` registers Discord only if a webhook URL is configured (logs a skip otherwise) — a fresh host with no secrets boots with zero channels. A cross-channel contract test enrolls every registered channel (non-empty `Name` matching its registry key). The how-to guide documents adding a channel in ~1–2h.
- **M3.1: Keychain-backed secret + live smoke** (`internal/notify/secret_{darwin,other}.go` + `discord_live_test.go`). The webhook URL resolves from `AILANG_DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL` first, then the macOS login Keychain (`security find-generic-password -s ailang-discord-webhook`) — so the daemon (a user LaunchAgent with Keychain access) needs no plaintext secret in its plist. Lookup is an overridable var so registration tests stay hermetic. `TestDiscordLive` (skipped unless the env var is set) performs a real send through the render→chunk→POST path; verified live against a real webhook (reached Discord mobile).
- **Deferred:** daemon fan-out over the registry + per-channel event filters + `approval_pending` mapping (Step 3); a second channel + dead-letter wiring (Step 4); inbound reply-to-approve (future).

### Added — Auto-triage router: inbound bug/feature → design-doc-creator inbox (M-MSG-TRIAGE-ROUTER, v0.23.0)

Build-order Step 1 of [`m-msg-auto-triage-pipeline`](../design_docs/planned/v0_24_0/m-msg-auto-triage-pipeline.md): connects the existing message-triage, forward, and coordinator-agent-runner pieces so a clear inbound bug/feature report is automatically promoted to the `design-doc-creator` inbox — where the existing `ApprovalWatcher` chain (design-approved → sprint-planner → sprint-executor) already takes over. The human's first touch stays at the `design-approved` label. **Opt-in**: nothing changes until `coordinator.triage.enabled: true`.

- **M1: Reusable clustering** (`internal/messaging/triage.go` + test, +138/-76 LOC). Moved the greedy envelope-slot clustering out of the private CLI helper (`cmd/ailang/messages_triage.go`) into the exported `ClusterMessages([]InboxMessage, slot string, threshold float64) []Cluster`, reusing the existing `CosineSimilarity`. The CLI `ailang messages triage` now calls the library with identical JSON output; the router (and future cluster-batching) reuse it.
- **M2: Deterministic promotion classifier** (`internal/coordinator/triage_router.go` + table tests, +212 LOC). `classify(msg, cfg) → promote/hold/drop`: a categorized bug/feature that is not a duplicate promotes regardless of sender; uncategorized messages from noise agents (e.g. `eval-suite`) drop; everything else holds for a human. `TriageConfig` (the `coordinator.triage` block) carries `Enabled`, `IntakeInboxes`, `PromoteInbox`, `PromoteCategories`, `NoiseAgents`, `ClusterSlot`, `SimilarityThreshold`, `PollIntervalSecs`, all with normalized defaults.
- **M3: TriageRouter daemon tick** (`internal/coordinator/triage_router.go` + `agent_config.go` + `daemon.go` + test, +217 LOC). Lifecycle mirrors `ApprovalWatcher` (`Start`/`Stop` + ticker `pollLoop` with an initial run). Each `tickOnce` lists unread intake-inbox messages (duplicates collapsed), classifies, and forwards promotions to the promote inbox; idempotent (a forwarded message leaves the intake listing). Wired into the daemon's local-mode startup gated by `Triage.Enabled` (off by default → zero behaviour change). Depends on a narrow `triageStore` interface for testability.
- **M4: Integration test** (`internal/coordinator/triage_router_integration_test.go`, +80 LOC). Against a real SQLite temp store: a seeded `bug` lands in `design-doc-creator`, a `general` note stays held, a `DupOf`-set message is never forwarded.
- **Deferred (noted in the design doc):** "drop = archive" and "hold = surface in dashboard" are no-ops for now; cluster-based batching of related promotions is reserved (the library exists, the tick classifies per-message). Notification fan-out so a human is *paged* off-session is build-order Steps 2–3 (the channel framework + dispatcher).

### Added / Fixed — docparse resilience: `callJsonSimpleResult` + MCP missing-param rejection (M-DOCPARSE-RESILIENCE-FIXES, v0.23.0)

Two independent robustness fixes surfaced by the docparse external project (source messages `623811a0`, `b08617cb`). Both harden the boundary between a host-supplied request and AILANG execution so a malformed/partial input returns a typed error instead of crashing deep in stdlib. Design docs: [`m-ai-call-json-simple-result`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_23_0/m-ai-call-json-simple-result.md) (M1), [`m-mcp-unit-param-binding`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_23_0/m-mcp-unit-param-binding.md) (M2).

- **M1: `std/ai.callJsonSimpleResult`** (`internal/effects/ai_step.go` + `internal/builtins/ai_step.go` + `internal/builtins/ai.go` + `std/ai.ail` + example + golden, +90 LOC). A `Result`-returning variant of `callJsonSimple` — `callJsonSimpleResult(input: string) -> Result[string, AIError] ! {AI}`. It calls `CallJson(input, "")` (empty schema, same as `callJsonSimple`) but routes provider/transport failures through the shared `classifyOpError` mapper, so a transient 5xx surfaces as `Err(AIError{retryable:true})` for the caller to back off on, rather than aborting the host. This is the retry-vs-surface pattern that bare `callJsonSimple` (which crashes on provider error) cannot express. A nil AI handler returns `Err(AIError{code:"ProviderNotFound"})`. New builtin `_ai_call_json_simple_result` added to `builtin_types.golden`; worked example at `examples/runnable/ai_call_json_simple_result.ail` verified on both the `--ai-stub` Ok path and the no-handler Err path. (Prompt teaching-list mention deferred — the active teaching prompt `v0.16.1` is hash-locked for eval reproducibility; the new variant lands in the next deliberate prompt-version bump.)

- **M2: MCP `tools/call` rejects omitted/null params before the engine** (`internal/apiserver/mcp.go::makeToolHandler`, +22 LOC). A declared tool parameter the client omitted (absent key) or sent as JSON `null` was bound to `nil`, which the engine converted to Unit; the AILANG function then crashed deep in stdlib (e.g. `_str_len: expected String, got Unit`) with no actionable message. The handler now presence-checks each declared param and returns a structured `missing required parameter(s): <names>` error (`isError: true`, names in declaration order) **before** `CallPreserveFloats`. Type-agnostic — an omitted `int` rejects identically to a `string`. The legacy positional `{"args":[...]}` form opts out of named binding and is unaffected.
  - **`@route` audit (systemic-fix rule):** confirmed NOT the same bug. The HTTP `@route` path zero-pads omitted *typed* params via `zeroValueForType` (`""`/`0`/`false`/`[]`/`{}`), never `nil`→Unit for known types — the deliberate v0.21.0 `m-serveapi-get-query-shadow` design that lets handlers self-validate partial GET/POST input. MCP rejects (RPC contract) while `@route` substitutes a typed zero (lenient HTTP) — different points on the strictness spectrum, both crash-safe. Added `TestRouteParamOmission_ZeroPadsNotNil` as a regression guard and documented both behaviors + why they diverge in [`docs/docs/guides/serve-api.md`](../docs/docs/guides/serve-api.md).

### Added — Tag-routed `messages send --requires` (M-COORD-TAG-ROUTING-LASTMILE, v0.23.0)

Closes the v0.22.0 acknowledged gap that left `ailang messages send` unable to participate in M-COORD-MULTI-HOST-WORKERS routing. Three decoupled milestones land in this entry:

- **M1: Local daemon HTTP listener on by default** (`tools/launchd/dev.ailang.coordinator.plist.template` + `tools/launchd/install_coordinator.sh` + `cmd/ailang/coordinator_lifecycle.go`, +86 LOC). The plist now sets `PORT=8765` (default; override via `make coord-install --port N` or the `AILANG_COORD_HTTP_PORT` env var, validated as an unprivileged port 1024-65535). `ailang coordinator status` gained an `HTTP: ✓ http://127.0.0.1:8765` line that probes `/health` with a 500ms timeout — surfaces "daemon up but HTTP not configured" immediately instead of users discovering it via `--requires` failing later. Pre-v0.23.0 installs need a one-time `make coord-install` to pick up the new default.

- **M2: `ailang messages send --requires` flag** (`cmd/ailang/messages_send.go` + new `messages_send_test.go`, +274 LOC). The flag accepts comma-separated worker tags (`--requires agent:motoko,ollama:gemma4`) and routes via the daemon's HTTP `/api/messages` endpoint, which carries the tags to Pub/Sub attributes for tag-subset filtering by workers (the v0.22.0 mechanism). Without `--requires`, behavior is unchanged from v0.22.0 — the SQLite-only path stays the default for inbox-addressed fire-and-forget. New `sendViaHTTP` helper reuses M1's `discoverCoordinatorHTTPPort` (env → plist) for port discovery; clear actionable error when the listener isn't reachable (suggests `make coord-install`). 4 deterministic tests at `count=20`: splitAndTrim parser (8 cases), POST body shape, API key Bearer header, no-PORT failure mode.

- **M3: In-repo half of the cloud-fallback Job** (`cloudbuild-dev.yaml` + `docker/agent-motoko-multivac-prs.md`, +135/-6 LOC). Adds the `build-agent-motoko` step to `cloudbuild-dev.yaml` mirroring `build-agent-go` (registry-cached buildx, FROMs agent-base via the existing `Dockerfile.agent-motoko`). The cross-repo work in `ailang-multivac` (Terraform Job definition, mounted coordinator config update with `worker_tags: [agent:motoko]`, prod `ailang-openrouter-api-key` secret) is documented as PRs #0-#3 in `docker/agent-motoko-multivac-prs.md` — the doc was originally drafted during M-MOTOKO-EXECUTOR-ADAPTER (v0.18.0) but never executed; v0.23.0 refreshes it with the M-COORD-MULTI-HOST-WORKERS integration points and a NEW PR #0 (operational, pre-flight) noting that the cloud `ailang-coordinator` Cloud Run service is on a 2026-04-28 image (pre-v0.21.0) and MUST be redeployed before any E2E test of the v0.22.0 `requires` field can succeed.

- **Docs**: [`docs/docs/guides/coordinator-workers.md`](../docs/docs/guides/coordinator-workers.md) gained a "HTTP endpoint configuration" subsection (default port, override paths, /health probe, route catalog with auth requirements). [`docs/docs/guides/agent-messaging.md`](../docs/docs/guides/agent-messaging.md) gained a "Tag-routed sends (v0.23.0+)" subsection with concrete `--requires` examples + prerequisites callout.

**End-to-end verification matrix** (M4 of the sprint):

| # | Scenario | Status | Gating |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Studio → Studio (local tag match): `ailang messages send eval-rig "x" --requires agent:motoko` from Studio shell lands locally | ⚠️ Partial — HTTP send path verified (M2 live smoke); end-to-end claim-and-dispatch requires the local dispatcher to honor `requires` for executor selection (currently it claims tag-routed messages but dispatches via the agent's `provider` field, not the message's `requires`). The CLI + transport are working; the dispatcher-side `requires →executor` mapping is a follow-up. | Follow-up sprint |
| 2 | Laptop → cloud coordinator → Studio (Pub/Sub routing): same message from a non-Studio host hitting the cloud coordinator URL | ⏸️ Deferred — gated on PR #0 (cloud coordinator redeploy from 2026-04-28 image to v0.22.0+) | PR #0 in `docker/agent-motoko-multivac-prs.md` |
| 3 | Cloud-fallback (Studio offline): with `launchctl bootout` first, message claimed by `ailang-agent-executor-motoko` Cloud Run Job | ⏸️ Deferred — gated on PRs #1 (cloudbuild image build), #2 (Terraform Job + coordinator config addendum) in `ailang-multivac` repo | PRs #1+#2 in `docker/agent-motoko-multivac-prs.md` |

The local-side surface (M1+M2) is feature-complete and ships in v0.23.0. The cloud-side dispatch (M3.x in ailang-multivac) is documented but lives in a separate repo and is deferred until that PR set is opened + merged.


## [v0.22.0] - 2026-05-26

### Changed — Retired Gemini CLI, adopted Vertex Managed Agents API (M-MANAGED-AGENTS, v0.22.0)

Google announced Gemini CLI deprecation at I/O 2026 (forced-retirement deadline 2026-06-18). Antigravity CLI was evaluated as the successor but was unsuitable for our eval harness (OAuth-only, no `--model` flag, no streaming JSON output, heavy gRPC server boot per invocation, pollutes working directories with `.antigravitycli/` symlinks). **Vertex AI Managed Agents API** is the chosen replacement: ADC-native, server-side multi-turn state, native SSE streaming.

- **Deleted `internal/executor/gemini/`** — Gemini CLI executor removed in full. Config-load now produces a clear next-step error if any existing `~/.ailang/config.yaml` still has `agent_cli: "gemini"`, pointing at `managed_agents` for `gemini-3-5-flash` agent-mode and noting that older Gemini models are now standard-mode-only via `internal/ai/gemini/` (unchanged).
- **Added `internal/executor/managed_agents/`** (~1255 LOC across client, SSE parser, types, tests): HTTP client targeting `aiplatform.googleapis.com/v1beta1/projects/<p>/locations/global/interactions` with ADC auth via `golang.org/x/oauth2/google` (same path `internal/ai/gemini/client.go` uses). SSE parser uses a forward-compat unknown-event map (mirroring motoko's pattern) so new event types from the API are captured into `ProviderData` without code changes. Multi-turn via server-side `interaction.id` + `environment_id` — no client-side history accumulation, big win for token cost. New `executor.CapRemoteSandbox` capability flag signals isolated-sandbox executors to the eval harness, which uses the existing `internal/eval_harness/managed_agents_bridge.go` to bridge solution files via the agent's text response.
- **`models.yml` rewired**: 8 Gemini entries lost their `agent_cli: "gemini"`. `gemini-3-5-flash` gained `agent_cli: "managed_agents"` with `agent_model_name: "antigravity-preview-05-2026"` (model is implicit in the API; this names the agent identity). Other Gemini models stay standard-mode-only.
- **`ailang doctor managed_agents` subcommand** (followup) wires the new executor into the existing doctor flow — checks ADC, prints next-step setup commands, probes a no-op interaction to confirm provisioning.
- **Post-hoc cost-budget reporting** (followup, `baa9ce0f`): cost-budget enforcement for managed_agents flags overruns in the run report rather than aborting mid-stream (the API only returns total cost in the terminal `interaction.completed` event — there's no per-token check point to gate against during the stream).
- **Lesson encoded for future**: when Google ships a new GCP service, **probe `v1beta1` first** — that's the Vertex AI canonical version prefix for new APIs, not `v1` or `v1beta`. Saved as `feedback_managed_agents_v1beta1_path.md`. Reference snapshot of the API surface in `reference_managed_agents_api.md`.
- **Docs**: [docs/docs/guides/evaluation/harness-setup.md](../docs/docs/guides/evaluation/harness-setup.md) — Gemini CLI section removed, Managed Agents API setup added (`gcloud auth application-default login`, project/location, one-time provisioning note). `.claude/rules/coordinator.md` executor table refreshed.
- **Design doc**: [m-antigravity-cli-migration.md](../design_docs/implemented/v0_22_0/m-antigravity-cli-migration.md) (filename is historical from the initial Antigravity-CLI direction; the implemented work is the Managed Agents API pivot documented inside).

### Operational — motoko_agent restored on AILANG v0.21+ (M-MOTOKO-AILANG-RECONCILE + M-MOTOKO-V021-EFFECT-ROW-MIGRATION, v0.22.0)

Two-sprint reconciliation to make `agent:motoko` a verified-working worker tag (M-COORD-MULTI-HOST-WORKERS, v0.22.0) rather than aspirational. All code work landed in `arniwesth/motoko_agent` (via `sunholo-voight-kampff` fork) and `sunholo-data/ailang-packages`; this repo gets the iface fix (above) plus this docs update.

- **M-MOTOKO-AILANG-RECONCILE** (2026-05-26): migrated `motoko_agent/src/core/ai_compat.ail` from the v0.20-era `std/ai/streaming.callStream` (a synchronous-final accumulator) to v0.21's `std/ai.stepWithStream` (callback-based real streaming). Public `callStreamResult` signature and `AIStreamResult` return shape unchanged — the change is internal. Bumped all 12 `motoko_ext_*` packages in `ailang.toml` to AILANG v0.21+ compatible versions. Republished `motoko-ext-ai-compat@0.2.0` (effect ceiling widened to `[AI IO Process FS Env Net SharedMem Clock Stream]` matching std/ai's post-v0.21 surface) and `motoko-ext-compose@0.2.3`.
- **M-MOTOKO-V021-EFFECT-ROW-MIGRATION** (2026-05-26): surfaced the AILANG iface nested-effect-rows regression above. Shipped a motoko-side workaround (extract on_chunk's match block to a top-level `handle_stream_chunk` function) plus defensive type annotations on `agent_loop_v2.ail`'s on_chunk closure and `stub_step.ail`'s Scripted branch. `make check_core` now reports 23/23 passing; `make build` exits 0.
- **Pinned motoko revision updated** ([`internal/executor/motoko/README.md`](../internal/executor/motoko/README.md)): now tracks commit `f7b26c8` on `feature/v021-effect-row-migration`; declares AILANG version floor of **v0.22.0+** (the iface fix is required).
- **Published**: `motoko-ext-ai-compat@0.2.1` and `motoko-ext-compose@0.2.4` (repins ai_compat path-dep against 0.2.1). 0.2.1 ships the `handle_stream_chunk` workaround in the registry source. The live registry validator picked up the iface fix automatically when dev was pushed (validator reports `v0.21.0-102-g9e405913` — auto-rebuild on dev push, not via the `release.yml` tag-trigger path).
- **Filed for follow-up**: a second AILANG type-checker regression where a `match` expression inside a lambda with at least one pure branch (`Usage(_) => ()`) erases the lambda's inferred effect row to `{}`. Top-level function decls correctly honor `! {IO}` annotations; this is specific to inferred lambdas. Workaround is the same `handle_stream_chunk` extraction pattern shipped in this sprint. Not blocking, but should be fixed before the next AILANG release closes the gap with cleaner ai_compat source.

### Fixed — Iface dropped nested function-type effect rows (M-IFACE-NESTED-EFFECTS, v0.22.0)

When a top-level function declared a parameter that was itself a function type with an effect annotation (e.g. `on_chunk: (StreamChunk) -> () ! {IO}`), the typechecker erased the nested effect row during unification. The iface builder did not restore it, so importing modules saw `(StreamChunk) -> ()` (closed empty effect). Any caller passing a lambda that actually used effects then failed to unify with `"incompatible closed rows: r1 has extra labels [], r2 has extra labels [IO]"`.

The fix extends `applyLabelsFromAST` in `internal/iface/builder.go` with a new helper `restoreNestedEffectRow` that walks the AST and re-applies effect rows to nested function-typed positions whenever the AST has an explicit `! {...}` annotation. Typechecker-inferred rows (where the AST has no annotation) are preserved unchanged, keeping row-polymorphic callbacks intact.

Discovered during M-MOTOKO-V021-EFFECT-ROW-MIGRATION while migrating `motoko_agent` to AILANG v0.21+. The bug had been masked in the v0.21.0 era because a SECOND latent bug (lambda+match-with-pure-branch effect inference) cancelled it out — both bugs together produced `{} = {}` which trivially unified. Fixing only the iface bug surfaces the lambda-effect bug as a separate failure mode; the lambda-effect bug is filed for follow-up. Regression tests in `internal/iface/nested_effects_test.go`.

### Added — Multi-host worker routing (M-COORD-MULTI-HOST-WORKERS, v0.22.0)

The coordinator + Pub/Sub system can now route messages to specific hardware (e.g. "this task needs `ollama:gemma4-26b-ailang`, route to the Mac Studio rig") without adding any new transport. Five small additions on top of the existing M-PUBSUB-MESSAGING + M-CLOUD-DISPATCH foundation:

- **Worker identity + tags** (`internal/coordinator/agent_registry.go` + new `tag_matcher.go`, ~245 LOC): `AgentConfig` gains `WorkerTags []string` and `WorkerHostID string`. Defaults preserve all existing behavior (empty tags = match-all, empty host_id = `os.Hostname()`). Pure `TagMatches(required, advertised)` with set-inclusion + prefix-glob (`ollama:*`) semantics. Named "worker_tags" deliberately to avoid collision with AILANG's `--caps` effect-system capabilities flag.
- **Pub/Sub subscription tag filter** (`internal/coordinator/pubsub_adapter.go` + `internal/pubsub/topics.go`, ~165 LOC): `PubSubInboxAdapter.SetWorkerTags()` enables tag-subset filtering of incoming messages. Mismatches return a non-nil error from `HandleNotification` (Pub/Sub treats as NACK → redelivers to next worker) BEFORE the Firestore fetch — efficiency: don't pay the round-trip for messages we'll reject. `pubsub.MessageAttributes.Requires` field encodes as comma-separated `requires` attribute. `POST /api/messages` accepts a `requires` array and threads it through.
- **Heartbeat + unified `workers` CLI** (`internal/coordinator/heartbeat.go`, `cmd/ailang/coordinator_workers.go`, ~445 LOC): `WorkerHeartbeat` struct + `HeartbeatStore` interface + `MemoryHeartbeatStore` + goroutine writer with configurable interval (60s default). `ailang coordinator workers list` shows BOTH bare-metal hosts (live heartbeats) AND Cloud Run history (aggregated from the existing coordinator task store by `AgentID`). `--type bare-metal|cloud-run`, `--since`, `--max-age`, `--json` filters. `workers ping <host>` lookup with a clear placeholder for the full Pub/Sub round-trip.
- **launchd + onboarding** (`tools/launchd/dev.ailang.coordinator.plist.template` + `install_coordinator.sh` + `make/coord.mk`, ~360 LOC): `make coord-install TAGS="..." HOST_ID="..."` installs an idempotent LaunchAgent that survives reboots. `--dry-run` shows planned changes including the rendered plist. `--uninstall` cleanly removes. Backs up existing plist to `.bak` on replace. Does NOT auto-edit `~/.ailang/config.yaml` — prints the exact YAML block to add so the user keeps full control.
- **Docs** (`docs/docs/guides/coordinator-workers.md`, ~210 lines): concept guide with architecture diagram + 3 worked examples (Studio onboarding, tag-routed send, health visibility).

Test surface: 75 new sub-tests across tag matcher (26), Pub/Sub adapter filter (10), Pub/Sub attribute encoding (10), agent config YAML round-trip (2), heartbeat machinery (7). All existing tests still pass.

The work was motivated by the 2026-05-22 Tailscale outage (38 hours of Studio unreachability went undetected). With heartbeats, `workers list --json | jq '.[] | select(.alive == false)'` is a one-liner monitoring hook.

Cumulative commits: `2f51868c` (M1), `d1b71321` (M2), `de710a65` (M3), `765b83ef` (M4), `<this commit>` (M5).

### Added — 24/7 eval-rig infra reliability hardening (M-EVAL-OS-LONGITUDINAL M0)

Three pieces shipped together to make the local-Ollama overnight rotation actually run for 12+ hours unattended without rotting. Background: a 2h 15m overnight run on 2026-05-23 produced zero useful data — observatory.db-wal had ballooned to 5.4 GB and all 17 benchmarks TTFT-timed out at 0 tokens. Root cause + fixes:

- **Opencode noise filter** (`internal/observatory/otlp_receiver.go`): extended `DefaultSpanFilterConfig.DenyPatterns` with service-scoped patterns for opencode internals (`Plugin.trigger`, `Config.get`, `SyncEvent.run`, `FileSystem.readJson`, `Auth.all`, `Session.updatePart`, `SessionProcessor.readToolCall`, etc.) — opencode-via-`@effect/opentelemetry` was emitting ~33 internal spans per `LLM.run` span, drowning the useful signal. Useful spans (`LLM.run`, `opencode.execute`, `opencode.step`) deliberately kept. Service-scoped so non-opencode spans of the same name (e.g. ailang-server's legitimate `FileSystem.*` ops) are NOT filtered. Regression coverage in `internal/observatory/otlp_receiver_filter_test.go` (8 new sub-cases covering deny + explicit "kept" + non-opencode "not filtered").
- **WAL auto-checkpoint** (`internal/observatory/wal_maintenance.go`, new ~95 LOC): `MaybeCheckpointWAL()` inspects the `.db-wal` size and runs `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` when above the configured threshold (default 1024 MB, override via `AILANG_OBSERVATORY_WAL_CHECKPOINT_MB`). Non-destructive — merges committed WAL pages into the main DB. Wired into `CheckHealth` ahead of the existing size-based branches so WAL-dominant bloat gets reclaimed automatically. 7 tests in `wal_maintenance_test.go` cover fast-path, env override parsing (valid/invalid/zero/negative), and actual checkpoint execution.
- **Pre-run rig health check + watchdog backstop**: `make/eval.mk` now runs `verify_setup.sh` as a precondition to `eval-smoke` when `MODELS` includes an opencode-`<local-model>` pattern — verified live: with ollama down, the target aborts in 6.1s with a clear error instead of burning hours on TTFT timeouts. Cloud-model invocations skip the precondition. Backstop for launchd's KeepAlive limitations on macOS 26.x Tahoe (empirically confirmed: KeepAlive in either `<true/>` or dict form does NOT respawn `gui/<uid>` agent jobs after SIGKILL): `tools/launchd/dev.ailang.rig-watchdog.plist` + `tools/launchd/rig-watchdog.sh` poll `ollama:11434/api/tags` and `ailang:1957/health` every 60s and `launchctl kickstart` if either is unreachable. The plist ships in the repo but is opt-in (`cp` + `launchctl load`) — `dev.ollama.serve.plist` is the primary respawn mechanism for non-SIGKILL crashes.

### Added — `ailang eval-publish` per-release leaderboard pages (M-EVAL-OS-LONGITUDINAL Phase 5)

New CLI subcommand turns N-trial rotation data into a Docusaurus leaderboard page per AILANG release — the **publishable longitudinal artifact** that differentiates the local-Ollama eval rig from cloud-cost-limited competitors. Variance is visible (n=N per cell), and trend deltas across releases lock the language-design feedback loop: ship a stdlib/prompt/syntax fix in response to a Phase-4 candidate, then watch the same (model, benchmark) cell move at the next release.

Usage:

```
# Per-release matrix only (no trend section)
ailang eval-publish v0.23.0 --rotation eval_results/rotation/2026-05-23

# With trend deltas vs prior release
ailang eval-publish v0.23.0 \
  --rotation eval_results/rotation/2026-05-23 \
  --prev     eval_results/rotation/2026-04-15 \
  --prev-tag v0.22.0
```

Output:

- `docs/docs/reference/os-model-leaderboard/<release>.md` — auto-generated Docusaurus page with YAML frontmatter, per-benchmark pass-rate matrix (rows = benchmark, columns = model, cells = `XX% (n=N)`), and an optional "Trend deltas since `<prev-tag>`" table listing only benchmarks that moved by ≥`--min-delta-pp` (default 10pp), with ▲/▼ arrows for direction.
- `docs/docs/reference/os-model-leaderboard/index.md` — static landing page explaining the rig setup and how pages are generated.

Implementation:

- `cmd/ailang/eval_publish.go` (new, ~320 LOC): `runEvalPublish` CLI dispatcher with `--rotation`/`--prev`/`--prev-tag`/`--output-dir`/`--min-delta-pp` flags. `loadReleaseBenchmarks` walks rotation dirs for summary.json files and merges per `(benchmark, model, lang)` — when the same key appears across multiple slots, trials and passes are summed and pass_rate is recomputed. `renderReleasePage` produces the Docusaurus markdown (frontmatter → matrix → optional trend section → footer). Positional release tag is extracted before flag parsing so `eval-publish v0.23.0 --rotation X` works (Go's `flag` package stops at the first non-flag token).
- `cmd/ailang/eval_publish_test.go` (new, ~175 LOC): 6 tests cover matrix rendering (frontmatter present, model columns appear, n=N cells correct, em-dash for missing tuples), trend deltas (positive movements rendered with ▲, threshold suppression correctly excludes the noisy bench from the trend table), negative trends (▼ + negative pp), the no-movers notice, and the unique-axis helpers.
- `cmd/ailang/main.go`: registers `eval-publish` in the subcommand dispatcher.
- `docs/sidebars.js`: registers the new "OS-model leaderboard" category under Reference, autogenerated from `reference/os-model-leaderboard/`.

End-to-end verification: synthetic 2-slot rotation (gemma4-26b + qwen3-coder-30b on `fizzbuzz` + `dense_op`) plus a prior-release rotation generates the expected `v0.23.0.md` with correct matrix cells and a `▲ +66.7pp` trend row for fizzbuzz on gemma4.

### Added — `ailang eval-trend candidates` failure-feedback triage (M-EVAL-OS-LONGITUDINAL Phase 4)

New CLI subcommand surfaces persistent-failure candidates from N-trial rotation data. Reads `summary.json` files (produced by Phase 3 `--trials N` runs) under a rotation directory, aggregates per-(benchmark, model, lang) outcomes across slots, and emits a table of tuples where the same `error_category` recurs across multiple trials. These are the **inputs to the language-design feedback loop**: each candidate becomes a target for a design doc + stdlib/prompt/syntax fix, then re-measured against the same (model, benchmark) at the next release.

Usage:

```
ailang eval-trend candidates --rotation eval_results/rotation/2026-05-23
ailang eval-trend candidates --rotation <dir> --min-fail 2 --min-trials 3 --max-pass-rate 0.5
ailang eval-trend candidates --rotation <dir> --json    # pipe to tools
```

Output (text mode):

```
Persistent failure candidates from eval_results/rotation/2026-05-23
  Filters: pass_rate < 0.50, error_category count >= 2, total trials >= 3

  Benchmark      Model                   Lang     Error category   Fails  Pass rate
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  dense_op       opencode-gemma4-26b     ailang   compile_error    3/3    0.0%
```

Implementation:

- `cmd/ailang/eval_trend.go` (new, ~240 LOC): `runEvalTrend` dispatcher + `runEvalTrendCandidates` action; `findCandidates` walks rotation dirs for summary.json files, groups across slots, filters by `min-fail`/`min-trials`/`max-pass-rate`, sorts worst-first. `PersistentFailureCandidate` is the stable JSON shape consumed by downstream tools (Phase 5 publication, eval-analyzer skill).
- `cmd/ailang/main.go`: registers `eval-trend` in the subcommand dispatcher.
- `cmd/ailang/eval_trend_test.go` (new, ~165 LOC): 5 sub-tests cover the canonical case (1 of 3 benchmarks surfaces), multi-slot aggregation (dominant category wins), `min-trials` filter (signal-too-weak case), empty-rotation error path, and worst-first sorting.

End-to-end verification: synthetic fixture with `dense_op` (3/3 compile_error) + `fizzbuzz` (3/3 PASS) — `dense_op` surfaces as the single candidate; `fizzbuzz` correctly filtered out. All cmd/ailang tests continue to pass.

### Added — adaptive token-budget baseline (M-EVAL-OS-LONGITUDINAL Phase 2)

Per-(model, benchmark) rolling mean+stddev of total tokens on PASS outcomes, maintained via Welford's online algorithm in a new `eval_baselines` SQLite table. Once N≥5 passing trials have accumulated, the adaptive `mean + 2σ` threshold replaces the fixed `--max-tokens-per-bench` flag value during thrash detection. Bootstrap (n<5) silently falls back to the fixed flag so behavior matches Phase 1 until enough data exists.

Why: a fixed token ceiling is a guess that doesn't account for the wide variance across benchmarks. `fizzbuzz` reliably solves in ~110K tokens; a Boyer-Moore reduction benchmark might legitimately need 600K. One global ceiling is either too tight (kills the long one) or too loose (lets the easy one thrash). The adaptive baseline calibrates per benchmark.

Implementation:

- `internal/observatory/migrate.go`: new `eval_baselines` table (model_id, benchmark_id, n_pass_trials, mean_tokens, stddev_tokens, m2_tokens, last_updated; primary key (model_id, benchmark_id)).
- `internal/observatory/baselines.go` (new, ~160 LOC): `EvalBaseline` struct, `GetEvalBaseline`, `UpdatePassedTrial` (Welford UPSERT in a transaction), `ComputeAdaptiveThreshold` (pure function). `BootstrapPassesRequired = 5`, `AdaptiveThresholdSigmas = 2.0`.
- `internal/observatory/baselines_test.go` (new, ~140 LOC): 8 sub-tests cover the missing-row bootstrap path, UPSERT semantics, Welford convergence against a batch-computed reference (10 synthetic samples — stddev matches to 0.5 of a token), threshold-fallback during bootstrap (4 sub-cases), post-bootstrap mean+σ computation, and ceiling rounding.
- `cmd/ailang/eval_benchmark.go`: in `runSingleBenchmark` agent path, the per-job `MaxTokensPerBench` is now overridden by `ComputeAdaptiveThreshold` when a baseline exists. After PASS, `UpdatePassedTrial` extends the baseline with the run's total_tokens. Best-effort: DB errors don't affect the result. Failures deliberately excluded from baseline updates (a thrashing 2M-token failure would skew the baseline up and disable the abort it informs).

End-to-end behavior:

- First 5 PASSes of (model, benchmark) use the fixed `--max-tokens-per-bench` flag value (Phase 1 behavior preserved).
- 6th+ PASS: threshold becomes `ceil(mean + 2σ)`, adapting to whatever this specific pair's distribution looks like.
- All existing tests in observatory/eval_harness/cmd/ailang continue to pass.

### Added — N-trial release smoke + rotation summary (M-EVAL-OS-LONGITUDINAL Phase 3)

`ailang eval-suite --trials N` runs each (model, benchmark, language) tuple N times in a single invocation, writing each trial's result JSON with a distinct filename (`<id>_trialN_<lang>_<model>_<ts>.json` when N>1) and emitting a per-rotation `summary.json` aggregating pass rates and token distributions per (benchmark, model, lang). The summary is the publishable artifact for Phase 4 (failure-feedback candidates) and Phase 5 (longitudinal release publication).

Default `--trials 1` preserves the existing single-trial behaviour bit-for-bit (verified by regression test in `internal/eval_harness/rotation_summary_test.go` and dry-run output that matches pre-change shape).

Implementation:

- `cmd/ailang/eval_suite.go`: new `--trials N` flag (default 1). Job-building loop wrapped in an outer trial dimension so each (model, benchmark, lang, condition, trial) becomes one Job. Dry-run output extended with `x N trial(s)` for plan visibility.
- `cmd/ailang/eval_parallel.go`: passes `j.Trial` through to `runSingleBenchmark`.
- `cmd/ailang/eval_benchmark.go`: stamps `Trial` on every `RunMetrics` construction (agent-mode + standard-mode + both error-recovery paths).
- `internal/eval_harness/metrics.go`: `RunMetrics.Trial int` field added; `Log` includes `_trialN_` in the filename when `Trial > 1` (back-compat: Trial == 0 or 1 keeps the legacy filename shape).
- `internal/eval_harness/rotation_summary.go` (new, ~230 LOC): `SummarizeRotation(outputDir) → *RotationSummary` walks `standard/*` and `agent/*[/<condition>]/*.json`, groups by (benchmark, model, lang, condition), computes pass_rate, separate token-distribution stats for pass vs fail outcomes, and error-category counts. Writes `summary.json` at the rotation root. `RotationSummary` is the on-disk shape Phase 4 consumes.
- `internal/eval_harness/rotation_summary_test.go` (new, ~170 LOC): 5 sub-tests cover the pass_rate math, thrash-abort counter (Phase-1 integration), multi-benchmark grouping + stable sort, on-disk JSON writing, and the filename-format back-compat invariant.

End-to-end verification:

- `ailang eval-suite -agent --trials 3 -models opencode-gemma4-26b -benchmarks fizzbuzz,adt_option -dry-run` reports `1 model(s) x 2 benchmark(s) x 1 language(s) x 3 trial(s) = 6 planned runs`. Default (--trials 1, omitted) reports `2 planned runs`.
- All existing tests in `internal/eval_harness` and `cmd/ailang` continue to pass.

### Added — local Ollama eval observability (M-EVAL-LOCAL-OBSERVABILITY)

Close the monitoring blind spot for 24/7 local-Ollama eval runs. With this milestone, observatory.db captures per-step OTEL spans from opencode subprocesses (was: 0 spans because of an FK constraint that dropped every batch), `ailang chains view` distinguishes 4 concurrent eval-agent stages by benchmark name, and a new `ailang chains live <id>` subcommand provides single-page refreshing live progress with stuck-vs-thinking detection. Companion to M-EVAL-LOCAL-OLLAMA which addresses the operational layer.

**M1 — FK-tolerant span insert (~50 LOC):** [`validateTaskHierarchy`](internal/observatory/otlp_receiver_helpers.go#L109) now actively CLEARS missing `task_id`/`assignment_id` references instead of letting the downstream INSERT fail on FOREIGN KEY constraint. Previously every eval-suite-emitted span was dropped because eval-suite generates `task_id="eval-<timestamp>"` resource attributes without inserting a parent `tasks` row. Verified end-to-end: a single fizzbuzz benchmark now produces 936 spans across 170 distinct names (`opencode.execute`, `opencode.step`, `LLM.run`, `LLMRequestPrep.prepare`, `Plugin.trigger`, `Provider.getModel`, `Session.updatePart`, `effect.IO.println`, etc). Cloud/coordinator paths unaffected — when parent rows exist, validation is a no-op.

**M2 — Chain stage benchmark labels (~10 LOC):** [`cmd/ailang/eval_benchmark.go`](cmd/ailang/eval_benchmark.go) now creates chain stages with `agent_id="eval-agent:<benchmark>"` instead of bare `"eval-agent"`. With 4 concurrent stages, `ailang chains view` now shows `eval-agent:fizzbuzz`, `eval-agent:adt_option`, etc., instead of 4 identical lines. No schema change.

**M3 — `ailang chains live <id>` subcommand (~400 LOC):** new CLI that joins `chain_stages` + `spans` + Ollama runtime state into a single-page refreshing terminal view. ANSI clear-screen redraws every 3s (configurable via `--interval`), auto-exits when the chain reaches a terminal state, exits cleanly on Ctrl-C. Stuck-detection: stages with no span activity in 300s+ while `status=running` get a `⚠ stuck?` indicator. `--once` flag renders one snapshot and exits (for snapshot tests and one-off inspection). Tests: `TestRenderLiveChain_FormatBasic` validates output structure; `TestChainIsTerminal` covers all 5 status values.

**M4 — launchd plist + user-facing guide:** [`tools/launchd/dev.ailang.server.plist`](tools/launchd/dev.ailang.server.plist) runs `ailang serve` at boot so the local OTLP receiver is always available for the 24/7 rotation. [`docs/docs/guides/evaluation/local-ollama.md`](docs/docs/guides/evaluation/local-ollama.md) (250+ LOC) is the canonical end-to-end setup guide: prerequisites, model pull, opencode Ollama provider config, Ollama parallelism env vars, OTLP enablement, parallelism trade-offs, monitoring with `ailang chains live`, troubleshooting.

**Behavioral impact on cloud OS-via-OpenRouter paths:** none. Spans on those paths already had valid task_id parents and are unaffected by the sanitization. The new `ailang chains live` is provider-agnostic and works for any chain.

**Follow-up shipped — M-EVAL-LOCAL-OBSERVABILITY-FOLLOWUP**: closes the per-stage span join gap. Eval-suite now populates `executor.Task.Metadata["chain_id"]` and `["stage_id"]` (via new `MultiExecutorConfig.ChainID/StageID` fields and a small `buildChainMetadata` helper). `BuildResourceAttributes` converts them to `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES`, the OTLP receiver extracts them, spans land in observatory.db with non-NULL `chain_id`/`stage_id` columns. Also fixed a SQLite-driver quirk in `cmd/ailang/chains_live.go::lastSpanForStage` (MAX over TIMESTAMP returns a raw string; scan into `sql.NullString` and parse manually) — without that fix, the query silently dropped to fallback. Manual verify: a fresh fizzbuzz with OTLP enabled produces 286 spans tagged with both ids; `ailang chains live <chain>` now shows real "3m37s ago" last-span values. Tests: `TestBuildChainMetadata_PopulatesBothWhenSet` + `TestBuildChainMetadata_OmitsEmpty` (3 sub-cases) all pass.

Design doc: [`design_docs/planned/v0_22_0/m-eval-local-observability.md`](design_docs/planned/v0_22_0/m-eval-local-observability.md). Sprint plan: [`design_docs/planned/v0_22_0/m-eval-local-observability-sprint-plan.md`](design_docs/planned/v0_22_0/m-eval-local-observability-sprint-plan.md).

### Fixed — cross-module nested record-type alias unification (M-TRANSITIVE-ALIAS-ENV-IMPORT)

Fix `cannot unify type constructor X with *types.TRecord` for any package that splits record-type aliases across multiple modules. Reported by `motoko_agent` (`msg_20260522_170317_f850eba4`), blocking `motoko_agent` PR #28 and silently breaking `motoko_ext_mcp`, `motoko_ext_omnigraph`, `motoko_ext_context_mode`, `motoko_ext_compaction_ai` and similar split-types packages.

**Trigger pattern**: module A declares `type Inner = { ... }`; module B imports `Inner` and reuses it in its own exported type (e.g. `type Outer = { items: [Inner] }`); module C imports a function from B but not A.

**Root cause**: `resolveModuleImports` in `internal/pipeline/pipeline_module_imports.go` was pulling `TypeAliases` only from *directly-imported* ifaces. C's `Unifier.aliasEnv` ended up with `Outer` but not `Inner`, so `expandAlias(TCon("Inner"))` fell through and unification failed against the structural `TRecord` from the other side. Pre-existing gap, masked before [`3325d39f`](changelogs/v0.10-current.md) (M-SCHEME-IMPORT-PRESERVE-ADT-HEAD) by over-polymorphic exported schemes that vacuously unified at any shape.

**Fix**: ~15 LOC in [`internal/pipeline/pipeline_module_imports.go`](internal/pipeline/pipeline_module_imports.go) — after the existing direct-import loop, walk `modLinker.GetLoadedModules()` and merge any not-already-present alias into `imports.ImportedTypeAliases`. First-wins guard preserves direct-import precedence; symmetric to commit `ad84b68d`'s WASM-path fix (M-WASM-TYPECHECK-FLOAT-DIVERGENCE) in `internal/repl/module_registry`.

Regression tests in [`internal/pipeline/cross_package_alias_test.go`](internal/pipeline/cross_package_alias_test.go): `TestCrossModuleNestedRecordAlias` (locks the bug) + `TestCrossModuleAliasCollisionPrecedence` (locks first-wins direct-import precedence). Verified the new test fails on pre-fix HEAD.

No schema changes, no public-API changes, no iface digest churn. Design doc: [`design_docs/implemented/v0_22_0/m-transitive-alias-env-import.md`](design_docs/implemented/v0_22_0/m-transitive-alias-env-import.md). Sprint plan: [`design_docs/implemented/v0_22_0/m-transitive-alias-env-import-sprint-plan.md`](design_docs/implemented/v0_22_0/m-transitive-alias-env-import-sprint-plan.md).

### Changed — v0.16.1 teaching prompt: stdlib coverage backfill (M-PROMPT-STDLIB-COVERAGE)

Additive patch over v0.16.0 (no syntax changes). Closes ~50 stdlib documentation gaps surfaced by an audit triggered by the byteAt miss in M-VERA-BENCH. Audit covered ~280 stdlib functions across 42 modules; the worst pre-fix offenders:

- `std/clock` — 0/2 documented (module entirely unmentioned in v0.16.0)
- `std/datetime` — 1/22 (the prompt named the module but listed no functions)
- `std/math` — 10/21 (entire trig family + `PI`/`E` constants missing)
- `std/option` — 3/6 (no `getOrElse`/`isSome`/`isNone`)
- `std/result` — 2/6 (no `unwrap`/`isOk`/`isErr`/`mapErr`)

**v0.16.1 additions** (all functions already existed in the binary — this is documentation-only):
- **std/list**: `dedup`, `union`, `intersect`, `difference`, typed `maximumInt`/`minimumInt`/`maximumFloat`/`minimumFloat`/`maximumString`/`minimumString`
- **std/string**: `words`, `splitAny`, `charCode`, `foldChars`
- **std/math**: full trig (`sin`/`cos`/`tan`/`asin`/`acos`/`atan`/`atan2`), `exp`, `log10`, `PI()`/`E()` constants
- **std/bytes**: `concatList`, `fromBase64URL` (companion to documented `concat`)
- **std/option**: `getOrElse`, `isSome`, `isNone`, `map`, `flatMap`, `filter` (with idiomatic-usage example)
- **std/result**: `isOk`, `isErr`, `unwrap`, `map`, `mapErr`, `flatMap`; clarifies that stdlib `Result[a, e]` has TWO type parameters (vs. simplified inline `Result[a] = Ok(a) | Err(string)` examples)
- **std/clock**: new mini-section (`now`, `sleep`, `Clock` effect)
- **std/datetime**: new section covering all 22 exports (accessors, arithmetic, boundaries, construction, formatting, parsing)
- **std/crypto**: new section (`sha256Hex`, `sha256Bytes`, `hmacSha256`, `constantTimeEqual`)
- **std/array**: construction primitives (`empty`, `make`, `append`, `unsafeGet`)
- **std/io**: `writeBytes` added to effects table
- **std/env**: `hasEnv` added to effects table
- **Effects table**: `Clock` row added (was missing)

**Token budget impact**: ~600 extra tokens.

**Active prompt**: `v0.16.1` (was `v0.16.0`). Hash: `bc3b71f70222fbf5d4430d213ee2503ca6de80ad283fbd11f5cbe4cfee762c37`.

**Acceptance**: AILANG VeraBench Claude Opus 4.7 reference baseline still 100% (36/36 testable). Pre-existing 100% on Claude Haiku 4.5 single-shot is unchanged by this patch — the new coverage protects against future LLM-eval regressions on problems involving math/trig, datetime, or Option/Result idioms.

Files: [`prompts/v0.16.1.md`](prompts/v0.16.1.md), [`prompts/versions.json`](prompts/versions.json), [`cmd/ailang/prompts/v0.16.1.md`](cmd/ailang/prompts/v0.16.1.md), [`cmd/ailang/prompts/versions.json`](cmd/ailang/prompts/versions.json), [design doc](design_docs/planned/v0_23_0/m-prompt-stdlib-coverage.md).

## [v0.21.0] - 2026-05-21

Headline: **AILANG hits 100% run_correct on [VeraBench](https://github.com/aallan/vera-bench) with Claude Haiku 4.5** — matching the strongest published Vera result (Kimi K2.5, 100% on Vera) with a cheaper LLM on an AI-designed language. AILANG is now an officially-supported target language in VeraBench ([aallan/vera-bench#70](https://github.com/aallan/vera-bench/pull/70)), enabled by the two stdlib + teaching-prompt additions below.

### Added — `std/bytes.byteAt` (M-BYTES-TOINTS-BYTEAT)

New `byteAt(b: bytes, i: int) -> Option[int]` builtin — the natural inverse of the existing `fromInts([int]) -> bytes`. Returns the byte value (0-255) at a given index, or `None` if out of bounds. Closes a stdlib gap surfaced by VeraBench's `VB_T2_013_get_char_code` problem: ASCII character-code extraction was previously not expressible in AILANG.

```ailang
import std/bytes (fromString, byteAt)

func get_char_code(s: string, i: int) -> int =
  match byteAt(fromString(s), i) {
    Some(b) => b,
    None => 0
  }
```

Files: [`internal/builtins/bytes.go`](internal/builtins/bytes.go), [`std/bytes.ail`](std/bytes.ail), [design doc](design_docs/planned/v0_23_0/m-bytes-toints-byteAt.md). The `toInts(b: bytes) -> [int]` variant from the design doc is deferred — `byteAt` alone unblocks the immediate use case.

### Changed — v0.16.0 teaching prompt: byteAt + letrec block-body example (M-VERA-BENCH AILANG findings)

Two teaching-prompt improvements triggered by VeraBench LLM-eval failure analysis on Claude Haiku 4.5:

1. **Added `byteAt` + `fromInts` to the `std/bytes` section.** The prompt previously listed only `fromString, toString, toBase64, fromBase64, length, slice` — newly-added builtins weren't discoverable to LLMs loading the prompt fresh. Without this doc update Haiku had no way to know `byteAt` exists; with the update it consistently uses it.

2. **Added an explicit "letrec needs `{...}` block body" wrong/right example.** The existing `letrec` example showed mutual recursion inside an effectful main block, but didn't make explicit that `=`-style single-expression function bodies don't accept `;` as a statement separator. Across multiple Haiku LLM-eval runs the SINGLE persistent failure was `VB-T4-003 even_odd_mutual_recursion`, where the model wrote:

   ```ailang
   -- ❌ WRONG
   export func is_even(n: int) -> bool =
     letrec is_odd = \m. if m == 0 then false else is_even(m - 1);
     if n == 0 then true else is_odd(n - 1)
   ```

   The fix: prompt now shows the wrong/right/best forms side-by-side. After this update Haiku passed T4-003 first-shot, taking the Haiku 4.5 + AILANG eval from 97% to **100% run_correct** on the full 60-problem VeraBench suite.

Files: [`prompts/v0.16.0.md`](prompts/v0.16.0.md) (lines ~649-660 for byteAt; ~ section for letrec). Hash updated in `prompts/versions.json`.

### Fixed — M-WASM-TYPECHECK-FLOAT-DIVERGENCE (v0.22.x)

Adding any float-typed helper to a module imported by a browser-targeted entrypoint caused the WASM `loadModule` type-checker to reject downstream modules with a "float vs <X>" unification error (X non-deterministic: `int` in the browser, `string` in a Go-test reproduction). The CLI accepted the same code. Reported during the 2026-05-21 cognitive_commons restoration session — discovered when restoring `compose_user_prompt` conversation dynamics broke the WASM harness on an unrelated `jnum(sx)` call in `commons_browser.ail`.

**Root cause:** the WASM module-loading path ([`internal/repl/module_registry_load.go`](internal/repl/module_registry_load.go)) registered type aliases and parameter/return type annotations for the **current** module only. The CLI pipeline ([`internal/pipeline/pipeline_module_compile.go:201-219`](internal/pipeline/pipeline_module_compile.go)) additionally propagates aliases from **imported** modules and threads parameter/return annotations through to the type-checker. Without these, declared signatures like `currentLeader(st: CommonsState) -> Persona` weren't constrained by their annotations — `CommonsState`/`Point` aliases remained unresolved, the type-checker inferred open-record signatures from usage, and free type variables like `α88` leaked across modules through the schemes. Any new constraint (e.g. `Fractional[t]` from a `0.5` literal in `if g > 0.5 then ...`) was enough to push the constraint solver into binding the leaked vars to whichever concrete type it visited first.

**Visible bug** (pre-fix scheme dump from the failing case):

```
distance      :: ∀α55. (Num[α55], ...) => ({ x: α55, | r }, { x: α55, | r }) -> α55
currentLeader :: (Ord[α88], ...) => { sentiment: { x: α88, | r }, | r } -> Persona
```

Post-fix:

```
distance      :: (Point, Point) -> float
currentLeader :: CommonsState -> Persona
```

**Implementation:** ~50 LOC across [`internal/repl/module_registry.go`](internal/repl/module_registry.go) and [`internal/repl/module_registry_load.go`](internal/repl/module_registry_load.go).

1. Extend `RegisteredModule` with `TypeAliases map[string]types.Type` and snapshot the elaborator's local aliases when registering each module.
2. Before type-checking a new module, register the aliases from all previously loaded modules on both the elaborator (so AST signatures resolve) and the type-checker (so unification can expand aliases). Mirrors `pipeline_module_compile.go:201-205`.
3. Call `SetParamTypeAnnotations` / `SetReturnTypeAnnotations` on the WASM path. Mirrors `pipeline_module_compile.go:207-219`.

**Tests:** [`internal/repl/wasm_float_divergence_test.go`](internal/repl/wasm_float_divergence_test.go) — full 5-module cognitive_commons load reproduces the failure on a `_with_float_helper` variant of `citizen.ail` and now passes. Fixture in [`internal/types/testdata/wasm_float_divergence/`](internal/types/testdata/wasm_float_divergence/) with diagnosis notes in `DIAGNOSIS.md`. Verified against the original `demos/scripts/wasm-loadmodule-harness.js` after `make build-wasm`: all 5 modules load cleanly. Design doc: [M-WASM-TYPECHECK-FLOAT-DIVERGENCE](design_docs/implemented/v0_22_0/m-wasm-typecheck-float-divergence.md).

### Fixed — M-SCHEME-IMPORT-PRESERVE-ADT-HEAD (v0.22.0)

Exported function schemes were losing their concrete ADT head constructors on import, silently masking pattern-match type errors at every call site. Reported by sunholo-demos/cognitive_commons (msg_20260520_111521_44c38751). Same bug class M-MATCH-ADT-XCHECK (v0.18.10) was supposed to close — the pattern-check code was sound, but the schemes feeding it were corrupted upstream.

**Root cause:** three call sites in [`internal/types/typechecker_functions.go`](internal/types/typechecker_functions.go) (`inferLet` line 156, `inferLetRec` lines 263 + 297) discarded the substitution returned by `SolveConstraints` before defaulting + generalization. Type variables that unification had bound to concrete ADTs (e.g. `α_return → Option[float]`) stayed free at the generalization boundary and got quantified into `forall α. ... -> α` schemes. The corrupted schemes flowed through iface export to every importing module.

**Visible bug:**

```ailang
import std/json (getNumber)        -- declared: (Json, string) -> Option[float]
import std/result (Ok, Err)

match getNumber(obj, "x") {
  Ok(v)  => v,                      -- WRONG: Ok is from Result, not Option
  Err(_) => 0.0                     -- WRONG: same
}
```

This passed `ailang check` cleanly pre-v0.22.0; at runtime it panicked with `no pattern matched in match expression` when the value was actually `Some(0.5)` / `None`. With the fix, the typechecker correctly raises `MatchForeignConstructorError` naming both ADTs at compile time.

**Implementation:** apply the returned substitution to `valueType` (and the typed node) at all three sites before defaulting / generalize. ~30 LOC of substantive logic, ~200 LOC including four TDD regression tests in [`internal/pipeline/match_foreign_constructor_function_call_test.go`](internal/pipeline/match_foreign_constructor_function_call_test.go) (function-call scrutinee, nested match, cross-module ADT, negative test for legitimate polymorphism).

**Collateral fix — TRecord ↔ TRecord2 bridge** ([`internal/types/unification_records.go`](internal/types/unification_records.go)): pre-fix over-polymorphism kept many record-typed values as `TVar`, so the old and new record-representation forms never had to unify directly. Post-fix they do (`std/ai.runTools` was the first concrete failure case). Added a `TRecord → TRecord2` conversion path in `unifyRecord` that mirrors the existing `TRecord2 → TRecord` path in `unifyRecord2`.

**Source-correctness surfaced during regression sweep:** [`examples/runnable/record_patterns.ail`](examples/runnable/record_patterns.ail) was matching closed-record patterns against records with extra fields — worked by accident under over-polymorphism. Refactored to match record shapes exactly. (Lambda + open-record-pattern shorthand `{name, ...}` against records-with-extra-fields is still a pre-existing parser limitation; top-level `let ... in match` works fine.)

Closes [m-match-adt-xcheck-regression](design_docs/implemented/v0_22_0/m-match-adt-xcheck-regression.md) transitively. Tests: 84 internal Go packages green; 184/184 examples pass; `make check-no-zero-arg-workarounds` CI guard intact. Design doc: [M-SCHEME-IMPORT-PRESERVE-ADT-HEAD](design_docs/implemented/v0_22_0/m-scheme-import-preserve-adt-head.md).

### Fixed — M-ZERO-ARG-SURFACES (v0.22.0)

Zero-arg exports (`export func f() -> T`) compile to a single implicit unit parameter (the parser injects `_: ()`). External invocation surfaces — apiserver, WASM `ailangCall`, bytecode entrypoint — each had to handle this mismatch independently, and didn't all do it the same way. The bug class had been fixed twice before (commits `8cc21027`, `4075a402` for apiserver) but the WASM/REPL [`InvokeExport`](internal/repl/module_registry_prelude.go) path silently returned the unevaluated `FunctionValue` instead of invoking. Reported by sunholo-demos/cognitive_commons (msg_20260520_105856_e3b99ca6).

**Fix:** lifted unit-injection into [`eval.CoreEvaluator.CallFunction`](internal/eval/eval_evaluator.go) so every surface inherits the behavior. Detection by parser convention (single param named `"_"`) rather than threading a flag through parser→ast→core→eval. Deleted the apiserver retry-on-error and the REPL/WASM point-fix. Added direct `CallFunction` regression test + negative test in [`internal/eval/zero_arg_export_test.go`](internal/eval/zero_arg_export_test.go) and a `make check-no-zero-arg-workarounds` CI guard ([`make/code-health.mk`](make/code-health.mk)) that fails if any surface package reintroduces `"expects 1 arguments, got 0"`. Design doc: [M-ZERO-ARG-INVOCATION-SURFACES](design_docs/planned/v0_22_0/m-zero-arg-invocation-surfaces.md).

### Added — M-COG-RUNTIME-BROWSER (Cognitive OS browser substrate, v0.21.x)

Browser-side substrate that lights up the M-COG-RUNTIME WASM bridges. The full Cognitive OS pipeline now runs end-to-end in a browser tab — agents mutate scoped DOM regions, exchange messages across browser tabs via BroadcastChannel, persist their cognitive event log to IndexedDB, and replay byte-identically across page reloads. Live demo at [`ailang.sunholo.com/demos/cognitive-os-runtime/`](https://ailang.sunholo.com/demos/cognitive-os-runtime/).

- **Browser host** ([`docs/static/wasm/cognitive-runtime/host.js`](docs/static/wasm/cognitive-runtime/host.js)): registers the WASM↔JS bridges (`ailangSetDOM*` / `ailangSetMsg*` callbacks), manages scoped DOM regions, handles per-tab sender identity via `sessionStorage` (with `crypto.getRandomValues` entropy). Goroutine-safe BroadcastChannel fan-out + same-tab loopback for single-tab demos.

- **Canonical DOM layer** ([`docs/static/wasm/cognitive-runtime/canonical_dom.js`](docs/static/wasm/cognitive-runtime/canonical_dom.js)): content-hash node IDs via FNV-1a 64-bit (sync, JS-integer-safe); no `Date.now()` / `Math.random()` / browser-supplied IDs; inline styles + system font stack ban FOUC and network-font drift; same `(region, ctor, fields, parent-hash)` input → byte-identical DOM output across reloads.

- **IndexedDB persistence sink** ([`docs/static/wasm/cognitive-runtime/event_log_indexeddb.js`](docs/static/wasm/cognitive-runtime/event_log_indexeddb.js)): single `cognitive_events` object store, monotonic primary key + `(clock, sender)` secondary index, DB-level schema version field for forward migration. Writes serialized through a single Promise chain (no concurrent-write races). `exportJSONL()` matches the Go-side `EventLog.ExportJSONL` byte-shape.

- **Replay engine** ([`docs/static/wasm/cognitive-runtime/replay.js`](docs/static/wasm/cognitive-runtime/replay.js)): reads events from IndexedDB or a JSONL Blob, sorts in canonical `(clock, sender)` order, dispatches through `CognitiveOS._applyPatchDirect`. Content-fidelity replay via the JS-side `fields` extension on `PatchApplied` events. `captureRegionHashes() + verifyByteEquality()` proves "same event log → same DOM" in-browser.

- **JS scheduler** ([`docs/static/wasm/cognitive-runtime/scheduler.js`](docs/static/wasm/cognitive-runtime/scheduler.js)): microtask-based single-threaded event loop mirroring `internal/cognition/scheduler.go`. Kind-filtered subscribers, re-entrance safety (handlers can subscribe/unsubscribe/dispatch mid-handler), capture-log for determinism assertions. 9/9 headless tests pass.

- **Subscribe + `_cog_drain` pattern** ([`internal/effects/cog_drain.go`](internal/effects/cog_drain.go), [`internal/builtins/cog_drain.go`](internal/builtins/cog_drain.go)): solves the "AILANG closure invoked from arbitrary browser goroutine" problem with a queue+drain pattern. Subscribe enqueues into a per-`EffContext` callback queue; AILANG calls `_cog_drain(timeout_ms)` which dispatches via `ctx.FnCaller` on the evaluator's own goroutine. Preserves single-threaded determinism. AILANG bindings: `subscribe(region, eventTypes, callback)` in `std/dom`, `subscribeMsg(mailbox, callback)` + `drain(timeout_ms)` in `std/cognition`.

- **Subscribe WASM bridges** ([`cmd/wasm/effects_cognition.go`](cmd/wasm/effects_cognition.go)): `WasmDOMHandler.Subscribe` + `WasmMsgHandler.Subscribe` replaced their M-COG-RUNTIME `not_implemented` stubs with real impls. Each wraps `onEvent`/`onMsg` in a `js.Func` that decodes the JS-side envelope back to typed events. Cancel function releases `js.Func` via `.Release()` + detaches the JS-side listener via dual-signature callback convention. No `js.Func` leaks across 100+ subscribe/cancel cycles.

- **Trace cognitive-event extension** ([`internal/effects/trace_cognition.go`](internal/effects/trace_cognition.go), [`internal/builtins/trace_cognition.go`](internal/builtins/trace_cognition.go)): new `Trace.emit(span_name, duration_ns)` op routes to a pluggable `CognitiveTraceSink`. CRITICAL invariant honored: existing `--emit-trace jsonl` output is byte-identical pre/post — the new path is a side-channel, the `trace.Collector` path is untouched. Sink is nil by default (native CLI unchanged); `cmd/wasm` installs an IndexedDB-backed sink at WASM init.

- **`Cog` effect label** ([`internal/types/types.go`](internal/types/types.go), [`internal/types/effects.go`](internal/types/effects.go), [`internal/parser/parser_effect.go`](internal/parser/parser_effect.go)): registered in both the type checker's `IsKnownEffect` map and the parser's `knownEffects` map. Used by Subscribe + drain ops.

- **Public demo deploy** ([`docs/static/demos/cognitive-os-runtime/`](docs/static/demos/cognitive-os-runtime/)): mirrors the `wasm-step-byo-key` (v0.19.0) precedent — same file shape (`README.md` + `index.html` + JS modules), same loading order, same `docs/static/demos/<name>/` path. Live at `ailang.sunholo.com/demos/cognitive-os-runtime/` after deploy.

- **Interactive smoke harness** ([`docs/static/wasm/cognitive-runtime/index.html`](docs/static/wasm/cognitive-runtime/index.html)): 20+ inline assertions covering DOM mutation, replay determinism, scoped regions, idempotent patches, cross-tab Msg with Lamport clock preservation, IndexedDB persistence across reloads, replay byte-equality. User-verified end-to-end in Firefox + Chromium during sprint execution.

End-to-end verification done in real browser:
  - 20/20 in-tab smoke assertions pass
  - Cross-tab BroadcastChannel delivery (Tab A → Tab B with sender clock preserved)
  - IndexedDB persistence across `F5` reload
  - Replay byte-equality: `captureRegionHashes` matches before/after `reset+replay` (hash `1678c554` round-trips)

Sprint cumulative: 18 commits across 11 working days, ~3,400 LOC including ~155 tests. Combined with M-COG-RUNTIME (Go-side substrate, shipped earlier): the Cognitive OS arc totals ~10,165 LOC across 30+ commits.

Design: [M-COG-RUNTIME-BROWSER](design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-cog-runtime-browser.md), sprint plan [m-cog-runtime-browser-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-cog-runtime-browser-sprint-plan.md), parent umbrella [M-WASM-REFLECTIVE-RUNTIME](design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-wasm-reflective-runtime.md).

### Added — M-COG-RUNTIME (Cognitive OS substrate, v0.21.x in progress)

- **DOM + Msg effect labels** ([`internal/types/types.go`](internal/types/types.go), [`internal/types/effects.go`](internal/types/effects.go), [`internal/parser/parser_effect.go`](internal/parser/parser_effect.go)): two new canonical effect labels for the Cognitive OS runtime. `DOM` for scoped browser-DOM mutation; `Msg` for the runtime messaging fabric. Labels locked across M-COG-RUNTIME / M-COG-MEMORY / M-COG-MESH sibling sprints. The existing `Trace` effect is reused for cognitive-event emission.

- **Step-pattern effect handlers** ([`internal/effects/dom.go`](internal/effects/dom.go), [`internal/effects/msg.go`](internal/effects/msg.go)): DOM and Msg handlers follow the multi-method `AIHandler` step shape (ApplyPatch / ApplyBatch / Subscribe for DOM; Send / Recv / Subscribe for Msg). Each ships with a deterministic `StubHandler` for tests and Result-returning op variants (`applyPatchResult` / `sendMsgResult` etc.) that produce typed `Result[T, {code, message}]` for AILANG callers — no raw panics on missing handlers.

- **AILANG stdlib bindings** ([`std/dom.ail`](std/dom.ail), [`std/cognition.ail`](std/cognition.ail)): `DOMPatch` ADT (`AddPanel` / `UpdateNode` / `RemoveNode` / `AddTimeline`); thin wrappers exposing `applyPatch` / `applyBatch` / `applyPatchResult` / `applyBatchResult` (DOM) and `sendMsg` / `recvMsg` / `sendMsgResult` / `recvMsgResult` (cognition). The Msg API is renamed from `send`/`recv` to avoid collision with the reserved CSP/channel keywords (M-CSP-SESSION-TYPES, v1.1.0).

- **8 cognitive-OS builtins** ([`internal/builtins/dom.go`](internal/builtins/dom.go), [`internal/builtins/msg.go`](internal/builtins/msg.go)): `_dom_apply_patch{,_result}`, `_dom_apply_batch{,_result}`, `_msg_send{,_result}`, `_msg_recv{,_result}`. Stability: experimental, category: cognitive-os.

- **WASM bridges** ([`cmd/wasm/effects.go`](cmd/wasm/effects.go)): `WasmDOMHandler` + `WasmMsgHandler` implementing the step-pattern interfaces via `js.Value` callbacks. Four new JS globals: `ailangSetDOMApplyPatchHandler` / `ailangSetDOMApplyBatchHandler` / `ailangSetMsgSendHandler` / `ailangSetMsgRecvHandler` (matching the existing `setAIHandler` registration pattern). Subscribe wiring deferred to M3 alongside the scheduler that consumes it.

- **Capability manifest** ([`internal/cognition/manifest.go`](internal/cognition/manifest.go)): JSON sidecar generator with deterministic key ordering, defensive budget copy, strict validation (negative budgets rejected, zero budgets = forbidden, unknown transports flagged). M-COG-MESH extends with capability tokens.

- **Lamport clock** ([`internal/cognition/clock.go`](internal/cognition/clock.go)): thread-safe atomic clock with Tick / Update (max+1 happens-before) / Read / Reset. `CompareEvents(clock, sender)` provides total ordering with sender NodeID tiebreak. `ClockRegistry` indexes per-node clocks (forward-compat with M-COG-MESH vector clocks).

- **Cognitive event log** ([`internal/cognition/event_log.go`](internal/cognition/event_log.go)): typed sum-type `Event` ADT (`MessageSent` / `MessageReceived` / `PatchApplied` / `CapabilityExceeded` / `TraceCaptured`); append-only `EventLog` with thread-safe Append/Len/Snapshot/Range; JSONL export with byte-determinism for fixed inputs; ImportJSONL skips unknown event kinds silently for forward-compat with future M-COG-MEMORY / M-COG-MESH variants; pluggable `Sink` interface for IndexedDB / file persistence.

- **Transport trait + LocalWorker** ([`internal/cognition/transport.go`](internal/cognition/transport.go)): pluggable transport boundary with Send/Recv/Subscribe/Close/Name. `LocalWorkerTransport` is the in-process default — per-mailbox buffered channels, FIFO delivery, synchronous subscriber fanout, idempotent Close.

- **BroadcastChannel transport** ([`internal/cognition/transport_broadcast_js.go`](internal/cognition/transport_broadcast_js.go)): cross-tab same-origin transport via the BroadcastChannel Web API (build-tagged `js && wasm`). Proves the trait abstraction — same agent code runs over LocalWorker (in-process) or BroadcastChannel (cross-tab) without source changes.

- **NativeMsgHandler** ([`internal/cognition/msg_handler.go`](internal/cognition/msg_handler.go)): wraps Transport into `effects.MsgHandler` for non-WASM use. Optional cognitive event log integration records every send + recv; Recv advances local Lamport clock past the sender's clock for happens-before establishment. The `ailang messages` CLI continues byte-identical because the runtime API is a sibling to `internal/messaging/`, not a replacement.

- **Deterministic Scheduler** ([`internal/cognition/scheduler.go`](internal/cognition/scheduler.go)): single-threaded event-loop driver that sorts events by `(Lamport, sender)` and dispatches in canonical order. Kind-filtered Subscribe / Unsubscribe; handlers can re-Subscribe from within their own callback (no lock-holding across dispatch). Optional capture log for replay.

- **Replay engine** ([`internal/cognition/replay.go`](internal/cognition/replay.go)): JSONL → scheduler dispatch in canonical order. `AreReplaysEquivalent` + `ReplayDivergence` for cross-machine determinism verification. End-to-end round-trip test confirms capture → JSONL export → replay through a fresh scheduler produces identical observed history.

- **Example**: [`examples/cognitive_os/single_agent_replay.ail`](examples/cognitive_os/single_agent_replay.ail) — exercises both effects with Result-variants and structured error handling.

### Files

15 commits across M1 + M2 + M3-Go-side (~6,800 LOC including ~140 tests in `internal/cognition/`, `internal/effects/`, `internal/builtins/`). Browser-side host JS (`host.js`, `replay.js`, `canonical_dom.js`, `event_log_indexeddb.js`) is deferred to a follow-up sprint that lands the actual JS test harness — the M3 Go-side substrate is independently shippable + testable.

Design: [`design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-cog-runtime.md`](design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-cog-runtime.md), [`design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-cog-runtime-sprint-plan.md`](design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-cog-runtime-sprint-plan.md), umbrella [`m-wasm-reflective-runtime.md`](design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-wasm-reflective-runtime.md).

## [v0.20.1] - 2026-05-17

Headline: **M-EXT-AUTHOR-DX closes the 4 motoko_ext author-loop gaps** Arni raised in [arniwesth/motoko_agent#23](https://github.com/arniwesth/motoko_agent/discussions/23). The full extension-development loop (scaffold → path-dep → smoke → publish → version-pin) now has a single canonical docs page, the scaffolder seeds `_smoke.ail` + `on_describe_tools` placeholders, the registry rejects Bedrock-incompatible dotted tool names at publish time (with a migration escape hatch + safe-rewrite suggestions for the AI-correction loop), and the long-stub `sunholo/motoko_ext_context_mode` package is replaced by a real 18-tool implementation. Cross-repo: ailang (this commit), ailang-packages ([0.2.3 publish](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang-packages/commit/23483fb)).

### Added

- **M-EXT-AUTHOR-DX M3: Registry-validator rejects provider-unsafe tool names + `--allow-dotted-tool-names` migration flag.** Closes the v0.18.1 Bedrock incident class at publish time. The registry validator now runs a `[A-Za-z0-9_]{1,128}` check (the intersection of what Anthropic Bedrock + Vertex AI accept) against every advertised tool name — `provided_tools[]` entries plus `name:` fields inside `on_describe_tools` ToolSchema literals — and rejects with a structured 400 naming the offending character and suggesting both snake_case and PascalCase rewrites. This catches `ctx.execute`-style aliases before the tarball lands in the registry, eliminating the class of "looked fine in dev, broke in production on Bedrock weeks later" reports. For pre-existing packages that pre-date the gate, `ailang pkg publish --allow-dotted-tool-names` sets an `X-Allow-Dotted-Tool-Names: true` header that the validator honors with a warning — same migration-flag pattern as `--allow-unsafe-field-access` from M-WASM-AI-STEP-BYO-KEY (v0.19.1). Regression coverage: [`cmd/registry-validator/main_tool_names_test.go`](cmd/registry-validator/main_tool_names_test.go) pins the regex semantics + snake/PascalCase suggestion correctness against the v0.18.1 fixture (`context_mode.execute`), and [`cmd/registry-validator/check_cache_test.go`](cmd/registry-validator/check_cache_test.go) runs the production `validateToolNames` against every cached version of every published `sunholo/motoko_ext_*` package (71 cached versions × 37 packages) to guarantee zero false-positive rejections on re-publish. A standalone [`scripts/check_published_tool_names.sh`](scripts/check_published_tool_names.sh) ships the same check for pre-launch verification when the test runner isn't available.

- **M-EXT-AUTHOR-DX M2: `ailang init motoko-extension` scaffold now seeds `_smoke.ail` + `on_describe_tools` schema placeholders + IO effect baseline + path-dep README workflow.** Before this change, the scaffolded `register.ail` shipped with `on_describe_tools: \_ . []` (no schema placeholders for the author to replace) and no smoke file, so the canonical first iteration was "fill in everything yourself." Now every scaffolded package includes: (1) a `_smoke.ail` template with 3 assertions (constructor doesn't panic, `provided_tools` count matches expectation, `on_describe_tools` returns same-cardinality list) — this file is shipped to the registry and re-run by the validator's smoke step, so local breakage and registry rejection share an error message; (2) a `describe_tools()` function in the scaffolded impl with per-tool `ToolSchema` records using `'{"type":"object","required":[],"properties":{}}'` placeholders the author replaces with real shapes; (3) an `on_pre_step` hook stub (required by abi 2.2.0); (4) the `IO` effect added to the `[effects].max` baseline (smoke uses `println`); (5) an enhanced README documenting the path-dep workflow (`{ path = "../ailang-packages/packages/..." }` during iteration → switch to `{ version = "X.Y.Z" }` for ship), Bedrock-safe naming guidance, and a pre-publish checklist. ABI pin bumped from 1.0.0 → 2.2.0 in the scaffold's `ailang.toml` template. Tests updated: [`init_motoko_extension_test.go`](cmd/ailang/init_motoko_extension_test.go) `wantFiles` count is now 6 (was 5), effect baseline is `{Env, FS, IO}` (was `{Env, FS}`).

- **M-EXT-AUTHOR-DX M4: New docs guide [`motoko-extension-development.md`](docs/docs/guides/motoko-extension-development.md) — single canonical reference for the extension-author workflow.** Covers: why extensions exist + when to write one, the canonical 4-step loop (implement → path-dep → smoke → publish), the 10-hook table from abi 2.2.0, `on_describe_tools` cookbook with 4 schema patterns (command-execution, search, fetch, structured-data), provider-safe tool naming with a per-provider acceptance matrix + migration path for legacy packages, pre-publish checklist, cross-links to `motoko_ext_test_dummy` (minimal) and `motoko_ext_context_mode` (production-scale) as canonical examples, and historical context tying the M-EXT-SCAFFOLD-AI-FIRST + M-EXT-PORTABILITY-GATE + this sprint into a single arc. Wired into the "Stdlib & Packages" sidebar alongside the existing `build-a-motoko-extension` tutorial.

### Changed

- **M-EXT-AUTHOR-DX M1: `sunholo/motoko_ext_context_mode` upgraded from a 0.2.1 stub to a full 18-tool implementation at 0.2.3.** The pre-existing `0.2.1` registry entry had real helper modules (`prompts.ail`, `context_mode.ail`, `types.ail`) but a `register.ail` that returned `provided_tools: []` and a no-op `on_tool_handle` — meaning the package compiled, passed gates, and shipped without actually doing anything. Arni discovered this in [PR #22](https://github.com/arniwesth/motoko_agent/pull/22) and built a working 314-LOC implementation locally; the gap was that *he had to fork our package* to get a working agent. This change ports his work back: `register.ail` now exports `register_with_config` wiring up the full context-mode tool set (18 tools advertised via the existing `provided_tools()` helper), and `context_mode.ail` gains ~120 LOC of schema machinery (`describe_provided_tools()`, per-tool `ctx_*_parameters` functions, `ext_tool_schema` formatter) so `on_describe_tools` returns real JSON-schema descriptions instead of empty placeholders. The ABI pin moves from 2.1.0 → 2.2.0 (for `on_pre_step` support); `[exports].modules` now lists 6 modules; `[effects].max` adds `IO` (for `_smoke.ail`'s `println`). Published as 0.2.3 (0.2.2 collided with an earlier publish) — consumers pin via `sunholo/motoko_ext_context_mode = { version = "0.2.3" }` and drop any local fork. Cross-repo commit: [ailang-packages 23483fb](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang-packages/commit/23483fb).
