Design doc: [`design_docs/implemented/v0_20_1/m-ext-author-dx.md`](design_docs/implemented/v0_20_1/m-ext-author-dx.md). Sprint plan: [`design_docs/implemented/v0_20_1/m-ext-author-dx-sprint-plan.md`](design_docs/implemented/v0_20_1/m-ext-author-dx-sprint-plan.md).

## [v0.20.0] - 2026-05-16

Headline: **AILANG ships an LSP** (`ailang lsp --stdio`) with one-command VS Code install (`ailang editor install vscode`), plus diagnostics-on-save / hover types / go-to-def / find-references / document symbols for `.ail` files in any LSP-aware editor. Also includes the docparse-driven `serve-api` strict-by-default fail-fast on dropped `@route` modules, and the M-DX26 contract verification fixes so `ensures`/`requires` clauses actually run. 27 commits, 86 files, ~9.2k LOC.

### Benchmark Results (M-EVAL)

**Standard eval** (544 runs across 8 models × 34 benchmarks × {AILANG, Python}, $18.34 spend):

| Metric | v0.20.0 |
|--------|---------|
| Overall success rate | 72.4% |
| AILANG 0-shot | 63.2% (172/272) |
| AILANG final (with self-repair) | 68.7% (187/272) |
| AILANG repair effectiveness | +5.5pp |
| Python (final) | 76.1% (207/272) |
| **AILANG vs Python gap** | **−7.4pp** |

**Top performers**: claude-opus-4-7 (86.8%), gpt5-5 (85.3%), claude-sonnet-4-6 (79.4%), gpt5-4-mini (76.5%), gemini-3-1-pro (73.5%), gemini-3-flash (67.6%), or-glm-5 (58.8%), or-minimax-m2-7 (51.5%).

**AILANG wins where it beats Python by ≥10pp**: 25 (benchmark × model) wins — notable tags `contracts` (+10.7pp) and `records` (+25.0pp). 5 models agree on `contract_bst_validate`; 4 agree on `contract_matrix_determinant`; 3 agree on `cli_args`. The contracts cluster is direct payoff from the M-DX26 ensures/requires verification fix shipped in this release.

**Gaps to watch**: `type_safety` (−41.7pp), `string_algo` (−21.9pp), `data_transform` (−21.6pp) — all areas where AILANG lags. None are regressions from v0.19.2; they're persistent gaps tracked in benchmarks/CURATION.md.

**Agent eval** (multi-turn, **core tier only**, 414 runs across 9 models × 23 benchmarks × {AILANG, Python}, $26.23 spend):

| Model | AILANG agent | Python agent | Gap | Cost |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **claude-sonnet-4-6** | **23/23 (100%)** | 21/23 (91%) | **+8pp** | $3.88 |
| motoko-glm-5 | 22/23 (95%) | 21/23 (91%) | +4pp | $2.16 |
| opencode-or-glm-5 | 22/23 (95%) | 21/23 (91%) | +4pp | $2.05 |
| motoko-claude-haiku-4-5 | 20/23 (86%) | 21/23 (91%) | −5pp | $2.14 |
| opencode-or-minimax-m2-7 | 19/23 (82%) | 21/23 (91%) | −9pp | $0.56 |
| gemini-3-flash | 17/23 (73%) | 17/23 (73%) | 0pp | $5.22 |
| gpt5-4-mini | 17/23 (73%) | 22/23 (95%) | −22pp | $9.97 |
| opencode-or-glm-4-7-flash | 9/23 (39%) | 15/23 (65%) | −27pp | $0.20 |
| motoko-gemma-4 | 0/23 (0%) | 0/23 (0%) | n/a | $0.00 |
| **AGENT TOTAL** | **149/207 (71%)** | **159/207 (76%)** | **−5pp** | **$26.23** |

**Headline findings:**
- **claude-sonnet-4-6 scores 100% on AILANG in agent mode** — perfect 23/23 across core benchmarks, beating its own Python at 91% by +8pp. The anchor model + multi-turn iteration fully exploits AILANG's compile-feedback loop.
- **Three models put AILANG ahead of Python in agent mode**: claude-sonnet-4-6 (+8), motoko-glm-5 (+4), opencode-or-glm-5 (+4). The AILANG-native motoko harness and open-source GLM via OpenRouter both match frontier on AILANG.
- **Multi-turn agent mode narrows the AILANG/Python gap by 2.4pp** vs standard (−5pp agent vs −7.4pp standard). The iterative compile-error feedback loop benefits AILANG disproportionately — AILANG errors are more "fixable" than Python's silent logic bugs.
- **motoko-gemma-4 hit 0/23 on both languages** — model/executor failure, NOT an AILANG issue. Filed for follow-up.
- **gpt5-4-mini gap widens in agent mode** (−18pp standard → −22pp agent) — codex's tool-use pattern doesn't fit AILANG's compile-first workflow as well as other harnesses.

**Saturation audit** (agent ≥95% on every dim with data → demote candidates): `api_call_json`, `float_eq`, `fold_reduce`, `higher_order_functions`, `mini_interpreter`, `state_machine_elevator`, `tree_transformation_pipeline`. These 7 benchmarks should move from `core` to `stretch` (or retire) in the next benchmark curation pass.

Cross-release context: v0.20.0 success rate of 72.4% (standard) is **+2.8pp** above the v0.18.4 baseline of 69.6% — no regression, slight improvement, above the trend of the v0.15.x → v0.18.4 sequence which was drifting downward.

### Fixed

- **`ailang editor install vscode` now invalidates VS Code's `~/.vscode/extensions/extensions.json` manifest cache.** Without this, VS Code reads the *previous* version's manifest from cache even after the on-disk `package.json` has been updated — so a 0.2.0 → 0.3.0 upgrade (the LSP-bundling change shipped below) would appear to "install" successfully but the LSP wouldn't activate because the cached entry lacked the `main` field. The new `invalidateVSCodeExtensionCache` helper drops the matching entry on every install (and every uninstall) so VS Code rescans the extension dir on next process start. Failures are non-fatal and logged. The install command's "Next steps" output now also explicitly says **fully quit VS Code (Cmd+Q)** rather than just "reload window" — the cache file is read at process startup, not window load.

### Added

- **VS Code extension (`ailang editor install vscode`) now bundles the AILANG language server.** The embedded `cmd/ailang/editor_assets/vscode/` extension previously shipped only syntax highlighting + bracket matching. With this change it also spawns `ailang lsp --stdio` via the standard `vscode-languageclient` runtime, so a single command — `ailang editor install vscode` — gives users colors AND real-time diagnostics, hover types, go-to-definition, find-references, and document symbols. Extension version bumped to `0.3.0`; engines pinned to `^1.75.0` to match the `vscode-languageclient@9.x` requirements. New `extension.js` is an esbuild-minified bundle (~354 KB, source at [`tools/vscode-extension-build/extension-src.js`](tools/vscode-extension-build/extension-src.js)); rebuild via `make vscode-extension-bundle` after editing the source or bumping the LSP-client dep. `cmd/ailang/editor.go` now copies `extension.js` alongside the manifest + grammar files and prints a richer "Next steps" + "Troubleshooting" section. Closes the friction of the previous two-step `glspc + settings.json` install path documented in `docs/docs/guides/lsp.md`, which is now demoted to an "other LSP clients" footnote.

- **M-AILANG-LSP-FOR-AI M6: `ailang-lsp` Claude Code plugin + user guide + token-cost eval harness.** The repo's local Claude Code marketplace ([`.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`](.claude-plugin/marketplace.json)) now lists `ailang-lsp` alongside the existing `ailang-go-lsp`. Install with `/plugin install ailang-lsp@ailang-tools` after `/plugin marketplace add /path/to/ailang/checkout`. The LSP config follows the `.lsp.json`-not-inline pattern empirically established in commit `235a5afc` for the gopls plugin: `.claude/plugins/ailang-lsp/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` carries metadata only; `.claude/plugins/ailang-lsp/.lsp.json` carries the `command: ailang lsp --stdio` config and the `.ail → ailang` extension binding. New user-facing guide [`docs/docs/guides/lsp.md`](docs/docs/guides/lsp.md) documents both the Claude Code install path AND the generic LSP-client path (VS Code, Emacs, Helix, nvim), the workspace-setup note (avoid MOD010 by opening the project root, not an absolute file path), and the token-cost rationale for AI-agent users. CLAUDE.md updated with the install-once command. Token-cost eval harness shipped at [`bench/lsp_token_cost/`](bench/lsp_token_cost/) — runs the same fixed cross-module-edit task once with the LSP plugin enabled and once without, captures `input_tokens` / `output_tokens` / `duration_ms` / `num_turns` from `claude --print --output-format json`, and writes a `results.json` with the `delta.pct_reduction` headline number. Single-trial / single-task / single-model — proof-of-impact, not statistically robust; the multi-trial follow-up is tracked as M-LSP-EVAL-FOLLOWUP. **This concludes the M-AILANG-LSP-FOR-AI sprint** (6 milestones, ~3700 LOC, 17 tests, all five MVP LSP capabilities — diagnostics, hover, definition, references, documentSymbol — functional end-to-end).

- **M-AILANG-LSP-FOR-AI M5: `textDocument/documentSymbol` returns the file outline (functions, ADTs+constructors, type classes).** A Claude Code session opening a `.ail` file now gets a structured outline view: each top-level function maps to `SymbolKindFunction`, each `type X = A | B | C` ADT maps to `SymbolKindClass` with the constructors as `SymbolKindConstructor` children, and each `class X` declaration maps to `SymbolKindInterface`. Imports are intentionally omitted from the outline (clutter; LSP outline is for navigation, not dependency listing). Implementation: `internal/lsp/symbols.go` parses the buffer and walks `file.Funcs` plus `file.Statements` (the `*ast.TypeDecl` and `*ast.TypeClass` cases). All Range/SelectionRange values are corrected to point at the symbol NAME (not the parser's `pure`/`type`/`class` keyword anchor) via the same `findIdentAfter` helper M4 introduced. `DocumentSymbolProvider` capability flipped to true. Tests added: `TestDocumentSymbolReturnsFuncAndADT` (verifies `Color` ADT has 3 constructor children with correct Kinds), `TestDocumentSymbolEmptyFile` (no panic on empty buffer).

- **M-AILANG-LSP-FOR-AI M4: `textDocument/definition` and `textDocument/references` for top-level identifiers.** Cursor on any identifier-use now resolves to its FuncDecl across same-file, session-open documents, and (best-effort) stdlib imports. Implementation: `internal/lsp/definition.go` looks up the cursor's identifier via the M3 PositionIndex, then walks (1) the root file's `*ast.FuncDecl`s, (2) `pipeline.Run().Modules` for transitive imports, (3) stdlib imports via `loader.NewStdlibResolver`. Returned `Location.Range` corrects `FuncDecl.Pos` (which points at the `pure`/`func` keyword the parser consumes first) by scanning the source for the function name as a bare identifier — done via `findIdentAfter` with proper boundary checks so e.g. `add` inside a longer name like `addAll` doesn't false-match. `internal/lsp/references.go` walks every `didOpen`-d / `didSave`-d document's PositionIndex via the session's `s.docs` `sync.Map`, gathering matching identifiers; the LSP `includeDeclaration` flag is honored. Workspace-wide scan (parsing every `.ail` under the project root) is deferred to the M-AILANG-LSP-WORKSPACE-SCAN follow-up. Server now advertises `DefinitionProvider: true` and `ReferencesProvider: true`. Fixture committed at [`examples/lsp_xref_fixture/`](examples/lsp_xref_fixture/) (a.ail declares `add`, b.ail imports + uses it). Tests added: `TestSameFileDefinition` (decl-name-not-keyword position correctness), `TestSessionScopedReferences` (cross-document gather including declaration), `TestReferencesExcludesDeclarationWhenAsked`.

- **M-AILANG-LSP-FOR-AI M3: `textDocument/hover` returns inferred type signatures for top-level identifiers.** Hovering over a callsite of an exported function now returns a Markdown hover body with the function name and its full type signature, e.g. `add : (int, int) -> int`. Implementation: `internal/lsp/index.go` builds a sorted `PositionIndex` over every `*ast.Identifier` in the parsed file (binary-searchable by `(line, col)`, with `endCol = startCol + len(name)` since AILANG identifiers don't span lines). `internal/lsp/hover.go` looks up the identifier under the cursor, re-runs `pipeline.Run` against the in-memory buffer, and resolves the type via `Result.Interface.GetExport(name)` or any imported module's iface. Locals (let-bound, lambda parameters) hit a documented fallback message — full local-type plumbing through Core node IDs is the M-AILANG-LSP-LOCAL-TYPES follow-up. Pre-implementation gate (the sprint plan's "Day 3 09:30 position-fidelity probe", `position_probe_test.go`) confirmed AILANG's parser preserves fine-grained line+column on every identifier across 51 idents in three real `examples/` files (zero `(0,0)` positions, zero duplicate positions, sub-token resolution working — `string_replace.ail:27` carries 5 distinct identifiers at columns 3, 13, 16, 21, 28). End-to-end smoke against `ailang lsp --stdio` returns the expected hover JSON over the wire. Tests added: `TestPositionIndexFindsTopLevelIdent`, `TestPositionIndexReturnsNilOutsideIdent`, `TestPositionIndexHandlesEmptyFile`, `TestHoverOnTopLevelExport`, `TestHoverOnWhitespaceReturnsNil`, `TestProbeIdentPositionFidelity`.

- **M-AILANG-LSP-FOR-AI M2: `textDocument/didOpen` and `didSave` now publish AILANG-checker diagnostics over LSP.** With this milestone an AI agent (or human IDE) opening or saving a `.ail` file under the LSP gets pipeline errors back as structured `publishDiagnostics` notifications without invoking `ailang check` separately. Implementation: `internal/lsp/diagnostics.go` runs `pipeline.Run` on the buffer, extracts structured `*Report` errors via `errors.AsReport` (with positions when present, file-start fallback when not), maps them to `protocol.Diagnostic{Severity: Error, Source: "ailang"}`, and dedupes against the top-level error return; `internal/lsp/document_sync.go` adds `DidOpen`/`DidChange`/`DidSave`/`DidClose` handlers. `didSave` is the diagnostic trigger; `didChange` only stores the buffer (per-keystroke incremental re-typecheck deferred to a follow-up milestone). The `Initialize` handshake now advertises `TextDocumentSyncOptions{OpenClose: true, Change: Full, Save: {IncludeText: true}}` so clients send the events we need. End-to-end smoke against `examples/runnable/parser_block_trailing_record.ail` returns a real `publishDiagnostics` over the wire with the file URI, severity 1 (Error), source `ailang`, and a structured AILANG error message. 6 tests pass: handshake (M1) updated for the new sync options; M2 adds didOpen-well-typed (empty diagnostics), didOpen-type-error (≥1 Error diagnostic), didSave-republishes (open clean → rewrite-on-disk → save → new diagnostics).

- **M-AILANG-LSP-FOR-AI M1: `ailang lsp` subcommand + Language Server Protocol skeleton.** Reverses the prior "AILANG LSP is for humans, not AI" stance now that Claude Code (and other coding agents) consume LSP servers via plugins. M1 ships the wire-protocol foundation: `ailang lsp [--stdio]` accepts the LSP `initialize` handshake and advertises only `TextDocumentSync` (subsequent milestones flip the diagnostics, hover, definition, references, documentSymbol providers as they ship). The server is built on `go.lsp.dev/protocol` + `go.lsp.dev/jsonrpc2` (same library family as gopls). Internal layout: `internal/lsp/server.go` is the real handler, `internal/lsp/unimplemented.go` stubs the 56 LSP methods we don't implement yet so the `protocol.Server` interface is satisfied at compile time; future milestones override stubs as features land. Smoke test: `printf 'Content-Length: 58\r\n\r\n{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"initialize","params":{}}' | ailang lsp --stdio` returns a valid `InitializeResult` with `serverInfo.name: "ailang-lsp"`. Design doc: [`design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-ailang-lsp-for-ai.md`](design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-ailang-lsp-for-ai.md). Sprint plan: [`design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-ailang-lsp-for-ai-sprint-plan.md`](design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-ailang-lsp-for-ai-sprint-plan.md).

### Fixed

- **M-SERVEAPI-GET-QUERY-SHADOW: GET query args no longer silently shadowed by zero-value padding for handlers with declared params.** In v0.19.2, any `@route("GET", "/...")` handler with declared parameters discarded `?args=X&args=Y` (and `?name=X&age=Y`) entirely — the handler was invoked with empty strings (or other type-zero values) regardless of what the client sent. Root cause: two well-intentioned safety nets composed destructively. `parseArgsWithNames` ([internal/apiserver/handler.go:248-262](internal/apiserver/handler.go#L248-L262)) padded empty bodies with zero-values to match declared arity (preserving the M-SERVE-API-EMPTY-BODY-NAMED-ZEROPAD fix for empty-POST validation paths), producing `["", ""]` instead of `nil`. The query-arg fallback in `routes.go` then checked `len(args) == 0` to decide whether to call `parseQueryArgs(r.URL.Query())` — but the slice was now pre-padded, so the guard was always false and the query string was silently discarded. Confirmed broken in production on `/billing/me/entitlements` (dashboard usage gauge) and `/api/v1/debug/usage`. Reported by the `docparse` agent after a five-round messaging bisect (inbox thread `e1814c9f` → `597f3ae9` → `952d6ef0` → `9044f0cf` → `3375cbf5` → `8154269d` → `79c86f8e` → `e60442d7` → `dbf01230`). Fix introduces an `ArgSource { None, Real, ZeroPadded }` provenance enum in `internal/apiserver/argresolve.go` and a single `resolveArgs(r, body, paramNames, paramTypes)` function that owns the precedence: body parsing → query fallback (when body source is NOT `Real`) → zero-pad. The fallback guard now keys on *provenance*, not *shape*, which closes the class of bug where defensive padding shadows defensive fallback. When the final source is `ZeroPadded`, an INFO breadcrumb is logged so synthesized handler inputs become visible to operators rather than silent. Companion to M-CHECK-STRICT-FALLBACKS (planned) which catches the *downstream* `Err(_) => Ok(emptyDefault)` anti-pattern at the AILANG layer; this fix catches the *upstream* dispatcher shadowing in Go. New tests: `TestArgResolution_AllSources` (16-case table exercising the cross-product of method × body × query × paramTypes), `TestArgResolution_ZeroPadDoesNotShadowQuery` (direct repro of the docparse curl), `TestParseArgsWithNamesEx_SourceLabels`. Design doc: [`design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-serveapi-get-query-shadow.md`](design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-serveapi-get-query-shadow.md).

- **M-SERVEAPI-SURFACE-DROPS: `serve-api` no longer silently degrades when an `@route`-bearing module is dropped by the under-basePath filter.** Before this change, `serve-api` would silently reject any module whose symlink-resolved physical path resolved outside the server's basePath ([internal/apiserver/module_entry.go:52-69](internal/apiserver/module_entry.go#L52-L69)). A common deployment layout — running `serve-api` from inside a published package's cache directory, with the package's handlers under basePath but a transitively-imported helper module *outside* — would silently drop the helper. Handlers compiled (the loader resolved the import) but at runtime emitted structurally-valid empty-defaulted records to customer traffic. This was the root cause of the docparse v0.14.1 billing-service "all-zero entitlements" bug (inbox `e1814c9f`). The partial fix in commit `a629a129` (v0.18.4) added a stderr log line; this change promotes it to a startup-time fail-fast: if any dropped module carries `@route`, `serve-api` prints a structured error naming each fatal drop (declared path, basePath, resolved path) and exits 1. `AILANG_SERVE_API_ALLOW_DROPS=1` is an env-var-only escape hatch for migration scenarios. Non-annotation drops (e.g. stdlib resolution edges) still emit only a `⚠  Dropped N module(s) outside basePath: ...` banner WARN. `/api/_health` always populates a `dropped_modules: [...]` field when drops occurred, but `status` only flips to `"degraded"` when at least one drop carries `@route` — non-annotation drops keep `status: "ok"` so readiness probes don't route traffic away from an otherwise-functional revision that hit a routine stdlib resolution edge. Reported by the `docparse` agent in `sunholo-data/docparse`. Design doc: [`design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-serveapi-surface-drops.md`](design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-serveapi-surface-drops.md).

- **`ailang messages reply <id>` no longer panics on missing message.** When the target message wasn't found in the local store, `Store.GetInboxMessage` returns `(nil, nil)` on `sql.ErrNoRows` ([internal/messaging/inbox.go:355-358](internal/messaging/inbox.go#L355-L358)), and the reply command dereferenced `msg.GitHubIssue` without a nil-check — producing a nil-pointer panic at `cmd/ailang/messages_send.go:248`. Other CRUD commands (`messages read`, `messages forward`) already had the guard; only the reply path was missed. Patch: add the `msg == nil` guard, and pipe the id through `resolveMessageID` for short-prefix support consistent with the other commands. Reported by the `docparse` agent (inbox `952d6ef0`).

- **M-PARSER-BLOCK-TR: `{ stmt; ...; recordLiteral }` blocks now parse correctly.** A block expression with `;`-separated statements followed by a trailing record literal previously failed with `PAR_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN at line N: expected next token to be else, got } instead`. The root cause was in `parseRecordLiteral`'s IDENT-branch block parser ([internal/parser/parser_literals.go:478](internal/parser/parser_literals.go#L478)) — it used `curTokenIs(RBRACE)` for loop termination, which incorrectly treated an inner record literal's closing `}` as the outer block's closing `}`. The outer `}` was then never consumed, so the if-then-else parser failed to find its `else`. Fix: rewrite the loop using the same peek-based pattern as `parseBlockOrExpression`. Same architectural bug class as the implemented [M-DX16](design_docs/implemented/v0_6_1/m-dx16-inline-record-match-arms.md) (which fixed it for match arms), but in a different LBRACE entry point. Demo: `examples/runnable/parser_block_trailing_record.ail`. Reported by `cli` agent in `sunholo/ailang-parse` (inbox `c8813647`). Design doc: `design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-parser-block-trailing-record-literal.md`.

- **M-DX26 Phase 5 + 5.1 + 5.2: `ensures` AND `requires` clauses now actually verify, including arithmetic in predicates.** Pure functions with `ensures { ... }` postconditions previously failed at test time with `evaluation failed: empty program` (or, for some body shapes, `PAR_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN at _test.ail:10:25`). The runner now extracts the function binding, generates parameter values per parameter type, calls the function with those values, binds the special name `result` to the return value (and each parameter to its generated value, so predicates may reference both), and evaluates the predicate. On violation, the test reports `ensures violated for input: name=value, ...` as a counterexample. Phase 5.1 follow-up: the harness now pulls the *already-lowered* Core predicate from `Core.Meta[funcName].Contracts` (which has been through OpLowering — the same path that powers `--verify-contracts`), so arithmetic operators (`+`, `*`, `-`, `/`, `%`) inside ensures predicates work too. Phase 5.2 follow-up: `requires` clauses get the same Core-based treatment, but skip the function call (the predicate runs *before* the call, references parameters only). Generated inputs that violate `requires` are reported as `Skipped` with the offending input — out-of-contract inputs aren't a function bug, they're a generator-coverage signal. Forall-style `properties [...]` blocks remain on the (still broken) source-synthesis path — full Option A refactor of `EvaluateExpression` is tracked separately. Demo: `examples/runnable/contracts/ensures_violation_demo.ail`. Reported by the `cli` agent in `sunholo/ailang-parse` (inbox `cdd55f9f`). Parent design doc: `design_docs/planned/v0_13_0/m-dx26-property-test-empty-program.md` (Updates 2 + 3). Sprint plan: `design_docs/planned/v0_21_0/m-dx26-ensures-result-binding-sprint-plan.md`. Builds on M-CONTRACTS-OPLOWERING (v0.8.0).

## [v0.19.2] - 2026-05-14

Patch release shipping two stdlib additions that landed on `dev` after v0.19.1:

- **M-STDLIB-XML-WALK-PERF**: six new `std/xml` builtins to cut FFI / allocation cost of `XmlNode` tree walks (`foldChildren`, `foldChildrenStep`, `getAttrMap`, `nodeKind`, plus the M4 list-output primitives `flatMapChildren` and `mapChildren`). Driven by `sunholo/ailang-parse`'s profile data on a 79 KB sunholo.com page (21,651 fn calls / parse) and the follow-up regression report on the Mollie 1.7 MB fixture.
- **M-STDLIB-URL-ENCODE**: `urlEncode` and `urlEncodeForm` in `std/net`. Closes the gap surfaced by the LinkedIn OAuth2 token-exchange flow.

Design docs: `design_docs/implemented/v0_19_2/`.

### M-STDLIB-URL-ENCODE: URL percent-encoding helpers in `std/net`

Two new pure builtins addressing the stdlib gap surfaced by `demos/linkedin`
(feedback msg `9e25539f`): the LinkedIn OAuth2 token-exchange flow had to
hand-roll a 6-line, order-sensitive `formUrlEncode` via chained
`std/string.replace` calls because no URL-encoding primitive existed.

**New surface in `std/net`:**
- `urlEncode(s: string) -> string` — percent-encode a single value per RFC
  3986. Space becomes `%20`; reserved characters (`=`, `&`, `+`, `/`, etc.)
  are encoded; unreserved (`A-Za-z0-9-_.~`) pass through. Safe in any URL
  position (path, query, fragment, form value).
- `urlEncodeForm(params: List[{name: string, value: string}]) -> string` —
  build an `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` body. Space encodes as `+`
  (WHATWG form spec — what HTTP servers expect for form bodies). Keys are
  sorted alphabetically. KV shape matches the existing `headers` parameter
  of `httpRequest`.

Both functions are pure (no `! {Net}` effect row).

Implementation note: `urlEncode` uses Go's `url.QueryEscape` followed by
`+`→`%20` substitution rather than `url.PathEscape`, because PathEscape
leaves `=`, `&`, `+` untouched (path-safe but unsafe inside an OAuth
secret).

Example (`examples/url_encode.ail`):

```ailang
import std/net (urlEncode, urlEncodeForm)

let body = urlEncodeForm([
  {name: "grant_type",    value: "authorization_code"},
  {name: "code",          value: code},
  {name: "client_id",     value: clientId},
  {name: "client_secret", value: clientSecret},
  {name: "redirect_uri",  value: redirectUri}
])
```

Files:
- `internal/builtins/net.go` — registers `_net_url_encode` and
  `_net_url_encode_form` (pattern: `registerBytesToBase64`).
- `internal/builtins/net_url_encode_test.go` — 16 tests including OAuth
  edge cases, UTF-8, determinism under `-count=20`, and a regression
  fixture matching the exact LinkedIn token-exchange payload.
- `std/net.ail` — `export func urlEncode` and `urlEncodeForm` wrappers.
- `examples/url_encode.ail` — runnable demo of both functions.

Design doc: `design_docs/implemented/v0_19_2/m-stdlib-url-encode.md`.

### M-STDLIB-XML-WALK-PERF: Tree-walk performance builtins for `std/xml`

Four new builtins addressing the FFI/allocation cost of `XmlNode` tree walks,
profiled by `sunholo/ailang-parse` (21,651 function calls per 79 KB HTML page
dominated by `getChildren`/`getTag`/`flatMap` round-trips). All four operate
on the existing `XmlNode` ADT, so `std/html` callers get them for free.

**New surface in `std/xml`:**
- `foldChildren[a](node, init, f) -> a` — fold over direct children without
  materialising `[XmlNode]`. Replaces `foldl(f, init, getChildren(node))`.
- `foldChildrenStep[a](node, init, f) -> a` — bounded variant; handler returns
  `FoldStep[a] = Continue(a) | Stop(a)`. Useful for short-circuit searches.
- `getAttrMap(node) -> Map[string, string]` — all attributes in one FFI call.
  Replaces N separate `getAttr` calls when extracting multiple attrs from one
  node. Last-write-wins on duplicate names.
- `nodeKind(node) -> NodeKind` — classify a node as `KindElement | KindText
  | KindComment` instead of testing `getTag(n) == ""`. Pattern-matchable.

**New ADT in `std/xml`**: `NodeKind = KindElement | KindText | KindComment`.
Kind-prefixed constructors avoid colliding with `XmlNode`'s existing
`Element`/`Text`/`Comment` constructors.

**Measured wallclock impact** (from `examples/runnable/xml_walk_perf.ail`,
2,000 `<row>` children × 10 attributes each, AILANG-level timing via
`clock.now()`):

| Walker | Time (median of 5) |
|---|---:|
| Classic (`foldl` + `getChildren` + 10× `getAttr`/child) | ~130 ms |
| `foldChildren` + `getAttrMap` + 10× pure map lookups | ~103 ms |

≈ 20 % wallclock reduction; ~18,000 fewer AILANG↔Go crossings per parse.
The win scales with attributes-probed-per-node: the design doc's 30 % target
assumed a wider attribute fan-out than this example exercises. For the
profile that drove this work (`<img>` with 7 attrs, 100+ images per page),
expected improvement is closer to the 30 % range.

Go-level microbenchmarks (`go test -bench Walk_|Attr_`) are **not**
representative — `FnCallerN` in test mode is a direct Go call with no
closure-invocation overhead, so the AILANG↔Go cost the design targets does
not appear. Quoted numbers come from the AILANG-level example, not from the
Go benches.

Files:
- `internal/builtins/xml_query.go` (+281 LOC): 4 register/impl pairs
- `internal/builtins/xml_walkperf_test.go` (+580 LOC): 19 unit tests + 4 floor-cost benches
- `std/xml.ail` (+33 LOC): `NodeKind` ADT + 4 `pure func` declarations
- `examples/runnable/xml_walk_perf.ail` (+91 LOC): wallclock comparison
- `internal/pipeline/testdata/builtin_types.golden`: regenerated (303 sigs)

Design doc: `design_docs/implemented/v0_19_2/m-stdlib-xml-walk-perf.md`.
Refs: ailang-messages msg `cd45490b` (sunholo/ailang-parse, 2026-05-14).

### M-STDLIB-XML-WALK-PERF M4 hotfix: `flatMapChildren` / `mapChildren`

sunholo/ailang-parse retested `foldChildren` on the Mollie 1.7 MB regression
fixture (msg `f639ad74`) and observed **alloc regression 229 MB → 395 MB**
when using `foldChildren` as a `flatMap(_, getChildren(_))` replacement. The
diagnosis is correct: producing a flat-list output via AILANG-side fold
requires a cons-then-reverse pattern, which allocates more than `flatMap`'s
existing Go-iterative builtin (`_list_takeFlatMap`-style; native slice append,
O(1) amortised). `foldChildren` was the wrong primitive for the list-output
case.

**Two new builtins shipped on `dev`:**

```ailang
flatMapChildren[a](node: XmlNode, f: (XmlNode) -> [a]) -> [a]
mapChildren[a](node: XmlNode, f: (XmlNode) -> a) -> [a]
```

Both iterate inside Go using native slice append, reading children directly
from the `*eval.TaggedValue`'s children field — no `[XmlNode]` materialisation
AND no AILANG-side accumulator penalty.

**Measured impact** (Go bench, 1,900-child tree, list-output workload):

| Pattern | ns/op | B/op | allocs/op |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `foldChildren` + prepend (forced by list output) | 6,394,825 | 30,665,913 | 7,603 |
| `flatMapChildren` (M4) | 165,449 | 169,593 | 7,602 |

**~39× faster, ~180× lower bytes-allocated**, matching the shape of the
229 MB → 395 MB user regression. Same alloc count interestingly — the
foldChildren penalty is purely in *bytes per alloc* (O(N²) total bytes from
the prepend pattern vs O(N) from native slice append).

**`foldChildren` is kept** — it is correct for scalar accumulators (count,
sum, map, string). The doc comments on each new function now steer callers
to the right primitive for their result shape.

**Files (M4):**
- `internal/builtins/xml_query.go`: +151 LOC (2 register/impl pairs)
- `internal/builtins/xml_walkperf_test.go`: +263 LOC (12 unit tests + 2 benches)
- `std/xml.ail`: +17 LOC (2 `pure func` declarations)

Ship reply: `msg_20260514_110215_21f88d8f` (ack), follow-up reply on
M4 commit pending.

## [v0.19.1] - 2026-05-13

### fix(wasm): `awaitPromise` extracts real `.message` from JS rejection objects

`cmd/wasm/effects.go::awaitPromise` called `args[0].String()` on the JS rejection value passed to its `.catch` handler. Per Go's `syscall/js` semantics, calling `.String()` on a `js.Value` of type Object returns the literal string `"<object>"` — not the `.message` field. Result: any AILANG WASM demo using a Promise-returning JS handler that threw an Error would surface as `ERROR(Internal): <object>` on the AILANG side, swallowing the real provider/network error message.

Surfaced during the M-WASM-AI-STEP-BYO-KEY browser smoke-test: an OpenRouter 401 (wrong API key for provider) showed as `<object>` instead of the actual auth-failed message.

New `jsRejectionToString` helper handles all three cases: TypeString → return as-is; TypeObject → prefer `.message` (standard Error), fall back to `.toString()`; other types → existing `.String()` behavior. Drop-in replacement inside `awaitPromise` — affects `ai.call` BYO-key, `ai.step` BYO-key, and every JS-effect-handler flow. Files: [`cmd/wasm/effects.go`](../cmd/wasm/effects.go) (+27 LOC).

### docs(reference): added `std/package`, `std/extension`, `std/smoke` to the Stdlib Index

The Stdlib Index drift checker (`docs/scripts/check-stdlib-index.sh`) flagged three modules present in the binary but missing from the index page. Added a new "Package & Extension Authoring" section grouping them, since they're all utilities for code that ships *as* a package or *hosts* package code — distinct from the I/O / Network / AI sections. Files: [`docs/docs/reference/stdlib.md`](../docs/docs/reference/stdlib.md) (+8 LOC).

### M-WASM-AI-STEP-BYO-KEY: browser AILANG can call `ai.step` / `stepWithCache` / `stepWithStream`

(Originally drafted under v0.19.0, moved to v0.19.1 since it shipped after the v0.19.0 tag.)

Browser-AILANG demos (the existing `ailangSetAIHandler` BYO-key pattern) were limited to the legacy `ai.call` (string -> string) shape. The typed `ai.step` API — and its caching and streaming variants — returned the stub error `"ai.step is not yet supported in WASM (M-AI-WASM-STEP)"` because `WasmAIHandler.Step / StepWithCache / StepWithStream` were intentional stubs. Demos that wanted typed multi-turn flows, prompt caching, or token-by-token streaming had to give up on AILANG-in-the-browser entirely.

**Three new global JS hooks**:

- `ailangSetAIStepHandler(fn)` — `(model, messages, tools) -> Promise<Response>`
- `ailangSetAIStepWithCacheHandler(fn)` — `(model, messages, tools, breakpoints) -> Promise<Response>`
- `ailangSetAIStepWithStreamHandler(fn)` — `(model, messages, tools, breakpoints, onChunk) -> Promise<Response>`

`Response` is the same shape as the typed `StepResult`: `{ message: { role, content, tool_calls, tool_call_id }, tool_calls, input_tokens, output_tokens, cache_read_input_tokens, cache_creation_input_tokens, finish_reason, model }`. `StreamChunk` round-trips via a `kind` discriminator (`"ContentDelta"` / `"ThinkingDelta"` / `"Usage"`). `CacheBreakpoint{Position, TTL}` becomes a plain JS object the handler can forward to e.g. Anthropic's `cache_control: { type: "ephemeral" }` field. `ToolCall` decoding accepts both the canonical flat shape (`{id, name, arguments}`) and the OpenAI-nested shape (`{id, function: {name, arguments}}`).

**Singleton pattern**: `WasmAIHandler` is now a package-level singleton that all four setters (legacy `ailangSetAIHandler` + the three new hooks) mutate in place — handlers can be installed in any order without clobbering each other, and the first install wires the handler into the REPL's effect context exactly once. Browser smoke-test confirmed the legacy `ai.call` BYO-key flow still works after the refactor (no regression).

**Implementation**: [`cmd/wasm/effects.go`](../cmd/wasm/effects.go) (+377 LOC, then -68 LOC after Round 2 helper extraction: replaces three error stubs with real JS-callback implementations + WASM-side helpers `jsToResponse`, `jsToToolCalls`, `jsToStreamChunk`), [`cmd/wasm/effects_helpers.go`](../cmd/wasm/effects_helpers.go) (Round 2: pure-Go conversion helpers `messagesToJSCompat`, `toolsToJSCompat`, `cacheBreakpointsToJSCompat` extracted to a non-build-tagged file so they're host-testable without `syscall/js`), [`cmd/wasm/effects_helpers_test.go`](../cmd/wasm/effects_helpers_test.go) (8 unit tests covering empty inputs, single/multi-message round-trips, tool-call serialization, and the explicit M2 acceptance criterion that empty cache_breakpoints serialize to JS empty array, not null), [`cmd/wasm/main.go`](../cmd/wasm/main.go) (+3 LOC: register the three new globals).

**Tests**: 8 host-side unit tests for the pure-Go conversion helpers (run under `make test`). The `js.Value`-consuming helpers (`jsToResponse`, `jsToToolCalls`, `jsToStreamChunk`) stay WASM-only and are verified end-to-end by the `examples/wasm_step_byo_key/` demo. **Browser smoke-test passed 2026-05-13** against real provider endpoints: `ai.step` / `ai.stepWithCache` / `ai.stepWithStream` all return real LLM responses via Anthropic + OpenRouter.

**Demo**: [`examples/wasm_step_byo_key/`](../examples/wasm_step_byo_key/) — single-page chat demo with three buttons (`ask`, `askCached`, `askStreaming`) exercising all three Step variants. Per-provider `localStorage` key separation (`ailang-step-byo-key-{anthropic,openrouter,gemini}`) prevents the wrong-key-for-provider trap. Three providers wired: Anthropic (with `anthropic-dangerous-direct-browser-access` header), OpenRouter (`openrouter/auto`), Gemini AI Studio (`gemini-3-flash-preview` with native `streamGenerateContent?alt=sse`). Demo is wired into the docs site at [`docs/static/demos/wasm-step-byo-key/`](../docs/static/demos/wasm-step-byo-key/) and published live at `https://ailang.sunholo.com/demos/wasm-step-byo-key/index.html` after the next deploy.

**Docs**: [`docs/docs/guides/wasm-ai-step-byo-key.md`](../docs/docs/guides/wasm-ai-step-byo-key.md) — full guide covering the three new hooks, Response/StreamChunk/CacheBreakpoint shapes, provider notes (Anthropic / OpenRouter / Gemini / OpenAI / Ollama), the SSE streaming drain pattern, and a troubleshooting matrix. Wired into the docs sidebar under "Deploy & Embed" with a "Try it live" callout linking to the deployed demo.

**WASM binary delta**: +44KB (39,818,990 → 39,863,224 bytes), under the 50KB target.

**Limitations**: tool calls aren't exercised by the demo (we pass empty `tools=[]`). The cross-origin browser fetch to Anthropic requires the `anthropic-dangerous-direct-browser-access: true` header — production deployments should proxy through their own domain. The companion message-bus path (M-WASM-AI-STEP-VIA-MESSAGES) is still planned for sprints that want server-side tool dispatch and zero-trust key handling.

**Sprint evaluator**: 2 rounds, scored 78 → 91/100 PASS. Reports at `.ailang/state/evaluations/eval_M-WASM-AI-STEP-BYO-KEY_round_{1,2}.json`. Design doc + sprint plan: [`design_docs/implemented/v0_19_0/m-wasm-ai-step-byo-key.md`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_19_0/m-wasm-ai-step-byo-key.md), [`m-wasm-ai-step-byo-key-sprint-plan.md`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_19_0/m-wasm-ai-step-byo-key-sprint-plan.md).

### M-STDLIB-HTML: `std/html` — lenient HTML5 parser returning the `std/xml` `XmlNode` ADT

Real-world HTML (Word exports, CMS output, marketing pages) is rarely well-formed XML, so `std/xml` rejects it. AILANG projects that needed to extract content from HTML — most visibly `sunholo/ailang-parse` v0.13.0 — had to ship ~300 LOC of HTML5-tolerance shims in AILANG: boolean-attribute normalization for 23 HTML5 booleans, a tag-stack auto-closer for unclosed/overlapping tags, `<script>` stripping, `<!--[if IE]>` stripping. Working but accruing edge cases (Word `<o:p>` namespaces, mixed-case tags, embedded quotes…) indefinitely.

**New stdlib module `std/html`**:

- `parse(html: string) -> Result[XmlNode, string]` — Lenient WHATWG HTML5 parse via `golang.org/x/net/html`. Handles unclosed tags, boolean attributes, overlapping nesting, mixed-case tags. Returns the `<html>` element at the top level (DOCTYPE and the synthetic document root are stripped).
- `parseFragment(html: string) -> Result[[XmlNode], string]` — Fragment parse for snippets like email bodies or CMS content; no `<html>`/`<head>`/`<body>` wrapper is synthesized.

Both functions return the same `XmlNode` ADT that `std/xml` produces (`Element | Text | Comment`), so every existing `std/xml` query — `findAll`, `findFirst`, `getText`, `getAttr`, `getTag`, `getChildren`, `serialize` — works identically on HTML trees. Downstream code can swap parsers without touching tree-walking logic.

**Security**: 256 depth limit and 50MB input-size limit, same caps as `std/xml`.

**Implementation**: [`internal/builtins/html.go`](../internal/builtins/html.go) (+~250 LOC: two builtins + recursive `*html.Node` → `XmlNode` converter), [`std/html.ail`](../std/html.ail) (module wrapper that `import std/xml (XmlNode)`), [`examples/runnable/html_parser.ail`](../examples/runnable/html_parser.ail) (golden example covering unclosed tags, boolean attrs, mixed-case roots, attribute extraction).

**Tests**: 12 unit tests in [`internal/builtins/html_test.go`](../internal/builtins/html_test.go) covering simple parse, unclosed paragraphs becoming siblings, boolean attributes, lower-cased tags, comment preservation, `<script>` content passthrough, namespaced tags (Word `<o:p>`), oversized-input rejection, empty input, fragment parsing with multiple roots, wrong-arg-type rejection.

**Dependencies**: `golang.org/x/net` promoted from indirect to direct (no version bump — pinned to existing v0.53.0).

**Design**: [`design_docs/implemented/v0_19_1/m-stdlib-html.md`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_19_1/m-stdlib-html.md) + sprint plan. Inbox source: msg_20260513_132546_62a9b106 from `sunholo/ailang-parse`. Unblocks deletion of the ~300 LOC `htmlSanitize` pipeline in ailang-parse v0.13.0.

## [v0.19.0] - 2026-05-12

(See M-FS-SANDBOX-DIAGNOSTICS and M-MOTOKO-EXECUTOR-ADAPTER below — both target future releases and are still under [Unreleased] above the v0.18.5 ship marker.)

### M-PKG-INTRA-PACKAGE-IMPORTS: intra-package imports work without `ailang.lock`

A multi-module package like `sunholo/linkedin` (with `types.ail`, `auth.ail`, `posts.ail`) could not be authored or published using the natural code shape — every intra-package import form failed during authoring. Reported by `demos/linkedin` (inbox `msg_20260512_144239_913c0aa4`): `ailang publish sunholo/linkedin@0.1.0` rejected the package because `auth.ail`'s `import sunholo/linkedin/types` was unresolvable, and workarounds (`./types`, `pkg/sunholo/linkedin/types`) all required a pre-existing `ailang.lock`. Inspection of every published `sunholo/*` package showed they had all inlined their type definitions to dodge this — the natural "one types module, several service modules" pattern was unreachable in practice.

**Three forms now work, identically, with or without a lock file:**

```ailang
-- 1. Bare canonical (matches the module declaration — the form authors naturally write)
import sunholo/linkedin/types (Profile, AuthHeader)

-- 2. Relative (shortest; recommended for siblings)
import ./types (Profile, AuthHeader)

-- 3. Canonical with explicit pkg/ prefix
import pkg/sunholo/linkedin/types (Profile, AuthHeader)
```

**Implementation:**
- New `pkg.NewSelfOnlyPackageLoader(rootDir)` constructs a package loader from `ailang.toml` alone — no lock file required. External `pkg/<other>/...` imports under this loader surface a clear "run `ailang lock`" error instead of generic LDR001.
- The pipeline ([`internal/pipeline/pipeline_module.go`](../internal/pipeline/pipeline_module.go)) wires this as a fallback when `tryLoadPackageResolver` returns nil, so a freshly-initialized package can be type-checked before `ailang lock` runs.
- The module loader gains `SetCurrentPackageName` ([`internal/loader/loader.go`](../internal/loader/loader.go)). When set, bare canonical imports whose first two segments match the current package route through the package resolver's self-reference path — closing the natural-form UX gap. Boundary check on the prefix (`pathMatchesPackagePrefix`) prevents `sunholo/linkedin_other` from matching `sunholo/linkedin`.
- The registry validator (which shells out to `ailang check --package .`) inherits the fix automatically, so multi-module package publishing now works without inlining types.

**Files**: `internal/pkg/loader.go`, `internal/pkg/loader_self_only_test.go` (4 new tests), `internal/pipeline/package_resolver.go`, `internal/pipeline/pipeline_module.go`, `internal/pipeline/pipeline_module_intra_pkg_test.go` (4 new tests covering all three forms + external-import regression guard), `internal/loader/loader.go`, `internal/loader/loader_intra_pkg_test.go` (5 new tests), `cmd/registry-validator/main_intra_pkg_test.go` (4 E2E tests against the actual validator), `examples/intra_package_imports/` (canonical example demonstrating all three forms). Design + sprint plan: [`m-pkg-intra-package-imports.md`](../design_docs/planned/v0_19_0/m-pkg-intra-package-imports.md).

### M-NET-BINARY-BODIES: `httpRequestBytes` + `bodyBytes` field

`std/net.httpRequest` only accepted a `string` body, so any flow that needed to PUT raw binary (image upload, PDF, audio, S3-style object PUT, gRPC binary) was hard-blocked — `toString(bytes)` corrupts non-UTF-8 data, and base64-in-string is rejected by servers expecting raw payloads. Reported by `demos/linkedin` (inbox `msg_20260512_101641_12a77549`): step 2 of LinkedIn's image-attach API is `PUT <uploadUrl>` with raw PNG/JPG bytes, which had no AILANG path.

**Added** (`std/net`):
- `httpRequestBytes(method, url, headers, body: bytes) -> Result[HttpResponse, NetError] ! {Net}` — same shape as `httpRequest`, but the body is raw bytes. Defaults `Content-Type: application/octet-stream`, sets explicit `Content-Length` (no chunked encoding — required by S3, LinkedIn, etc.).
- `HttpResponse.bodyBytes: bytes` — new field on the existing `HttpResponse` record alongside `body: string`. Both are populated for every response, so callers can pick the UTF-8 view (existing) or the raw payload (binary downloads). Field addition is backward-compatible — existing `resp.body` / `resp.status` / `resp.ok` / `resp.headers` callers are untouched.

**Refactored** ([`internal/effects/net.go`](../internal/effects/net.go)): extracted `buildSecureRequest` shared helper so the new bytes path can't drift on security checks (DNS rebinding prevention, IP block, header validation, redirect handling). Both `NetHTTPRequest` and `NetHTTPRequestBytes` now go through the same validation pipeline.

**Bridge from `readFileBytes`** (no breaking changes to existing fs API): `readFileBytes` still returns `Result[string, string]` (base64-encoded). To send a file as a binary upload, decode via `std/bytes.fromBase64`:

```ailang
import std/fs (readFileBytes)
import std/bytes (fromBase64)
import std/net (httpRequestBytes)

match readFileBytes("photo.png") {
  Ok(b64) => match fromBase64(b64) {
    Some(imgBytes) => httpRequestBytes("PUT", uploadUrl, [], imgBytes),
    None => Err(...)
  },
  Err(e) => Err(...)
}
```

**Files**: `internal/effects/net.go`, `internal/builtins/net.go`, `internal/builtins/registry.go`, `internal/builtins/registry_codegen.go`, `std/net.ail`, `examples/runnable/http_put_bytes.ail` (verified working against `httpbin.org/put`), `internal/effects/net_bytes_test.go` (7 new tests). Design + sprint plan: [`m-net-binary-bodies.md`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_19_0/m-net-binary-bodies.md).

### Tooling: official `setup-ailang` GitHub Action (v1.0.0)

Shipped as a separate repo: **[sunholo-data/setup-ailang](https://github.com/sunholo-data/setup-ailang)**. Replaces ~15 lines of bash boilerplate with one step:

```yaml
- uses: sunholo-data/setup-ailang@v1
  with:
    version: latest
    cache: true
    github-token: ${{ github.token }}
```

Platform auto-detect (`linux.x64`, `darwin.x64`, `darwin.arm64`), SHA256 verification, binary caching (drops install ~25s → ~5s on hit), and `GITHUB_TOKEN`-aware downloads (dodges the 60/hr anonymous API limit). Closes the discoverability + ergonomics gap reported in agent message `43553198`. Design doc: [`m-ci-setup-ailang-action.md`](../design_docs/planned/v0_19_0/m-ci-setup-ailang-action.md).

## [v0.18.11] - 2026-05-11

### M-EXT-PORTABILITY-GATE follow-up: close evaluation gaps

The independent sprint evaluation of the M-EXT-PORTABILITY-GATE base work (PASS at 92/100) identified five follow-up items. v0.18.11 closes them.

**F1 — `std/smoke.dispatchAllTools` helper**

The v0.18.11 pre-publish smoke gate catches register-time crashes. The original `motoko_ext_omnigraph 0.2.0` bug shape (registers fine, crashes on first tool dispatch) is structurally adjacent — the gate doesn't enforce "tool calls don't crash in empty workdir". v0.18.11 adds the canonical helper for package authors to exercise every advertised tool from `_smoke.ail`:

```ailang
import std/smoke (dispatchAllTools)
import std/io (println)

export func main() -> () ! {Env, FS, IO, Process, AI, Net, SharedMem, Clock, Stream} {
  let hooks = register_with_config(0);
  println("OK: register_with_config returned without panic");
  dispatchAllTools(hooks.provided_tools, \name. {
    let _ = hooks.on_tool_handle(synthetic_ctx, mk_envelope(name));
    ()
  })
}
```

A panic inside the dispatcher closure aborts the smoke run — the gate marks the publish as failed and prints the captured output. Also exports `dispatchTool(name, dispatch)` for opt-in / opt-out per tool, and `okSuite(name)` for the canonical `^OK:` marker that `make verify-extensions` greps for.

The dispatcher is decoupled from `pkg/sunholo/motoko_ext_abi/types` — `std/*` cannot import `pkg/` paths, so callers wire up `ToolCallEnvelope` construction themselves.

**Row-polymorphic callback effects (M-PARSER-ROW-POLY-EFFECTS Phase 1)**

`dispatchAllTools` and `dispatchTool` ship with row-polymorphic callback effects:

```ailang
dispatch: (string) -> () ! {e}     -- callback's actual effects
) -> () ! {IO, e}                   -- caller pays IO + whatever the callback uses
```

A caller whose dispatcher only does `Process+FS` work needs `--caps IO,Process,FS` — not the wide union of every host effect. This restores the harness promise that capability requirements at the CLI mirror the program's actual effects.

**M-PARSER-ROW-POLY-EFFECTS Phase 2 (also v0.18.11) — `|` row-extension sugar**

The Koka-style `! {IO | e}` syntax now parses (in addition to the comma form `! {IO, e}` that already worked). Surfaced during F1 implementation when the original wide-effect-row workaround was tracked back to a parser-grammar gap. ~60 LOC parser change in `internal/parser/parser_effect.go::parseEffectAnnotation`: after parsing concrete effects, accept `PIPE` followed by exactly one row variable (a lowercase identifier that isn't a case-insensitive match to a known effect name — typos like `! {IO | io}` are rejected with `PAR_EFF015_ROW_VAR_AFTER_PIPE`).

Both forms now work and are interchangeable:

```ailang
-- All three of these are equivalent and parse to the same EffectAnnotation slice:
func f(g: (int) -> int ! {e}) -> int ! {IO, e}      = g(1)
func f(g: (int) -> int ! {e}) -> int ! {IO | e}     = g(1)
func f(g: (int) -> int ! {e}) -> int ! {IO, FS | e} = g(1)  -- multi-label + tail
```

`std/smoke.dispatchAllTools` and `dispatchTool` adopt the `| e` form as the canonical pattern. 5 new parser tests in `TestEffectRowExtensionPipeSugar` cover both the happy path and typo-rejection. Existing parser tests unchanged.

Bare `! e` (no braces) syntax is **not** added in this release — comma and pipe forms inside `{ }` cover every use case. Tracked in design doc as a possible future addition.

See [`design_docs/planned/v0_18_11/m-parser-row-poly-effects.md`](../design_docs/planned/v0_18_11/m-parser-row-poly-effects.md) for the full discovery story (the original "wide-effect-row workaround" was unnecessary — comma syntax already worked, and the parser limitation was only the `|` sugar).

**F2 — Example files for v0.18.11 APIs**

`.claude/rules/coding-standards.md` requires every language feature to ship an example. v0.18.11 added two stdlib APIs without examples; v0.18.11 ships:

- `examples/asset_path.ail` — demos `assetPath("nonexistent/pkg", "foo.txt")` with both Ok and Err branches printed (defaults to the Err branch so the example is self-contained).
- `examples/extension_self_disable.ail` — demos `requireWorkdirFile("/tmp", "missing.yaml")` with a tiny stand-in `Hooks` record. Shows the canonical `motoko_ext_omnigraph 0.2.1` self-disable pattern.

Both verified by `ailang run`. `examples/manifest.json` is auto-generated (auto-metadata-v0.6.2) so the new entries are discovered on next regen.

**F3 — Configurable `[smoke] timeout_seconds`**

The v0.18.11 design doc's mitigations table listed `[smoke] timeout_seconds = 60` per-package override; v0.18.11 hardcoded 30s. Slow-CI extensions (cold-start AI calls, large module graphs) could hit the cap without recourse. v0.18.11 ships:

- `SmokeConfig{TimeoutSeconds int}` struct + `Smoke` field on `PackageManifest` (TOML key `smoke`)
- `PackageManifest.Validate()` rejects timeouts outside `[1, 300]` when set; 0 (or unset) means "use `pkg.DefaultSmokeTimeout` (30s)"
- `cmd/ailang/pkg_publish.go::runPrePublishSmoke` honours `manifest.Smoke.TimeoutSeconds` when > 0
- New tests: `TestManifest_SmokeTimeoutValidation` (7 boundary cases), `TestRunSmokeInTempDir_RespectsCustomTimeout` (500ms timeout against a 30s sleep verifies elapsed < 5s — the runner honours the per-call timeout, not just the default)

```toml
[smoke]
timeout_seconds = 60   # extension with cold-start AI calls
```

**F4 — Cross-platform tarball path test**

v0.18.11 added `filepath.ToSlash` to the tarball walker so cross-platform hash determinism would hold, but only within-platform determinism was tested. v0.18.11 adds `TestCreateTarball_PathSeparatorIsForwardSlash`: creates a package with nested `assets/scripts/bin/run.sh`, decodes the resulting tarball, and asserts every `header.Name` uses `/` even for nested paths. Also asserts both nested and root-level assets actually shipped (guards against a regression where the walker silently stops descending). On Unix this is a regression-pin; on Windows it would catch a real cross-platform hash divergence.

**F5 — Stdlib reference doc page**

The v0.18.11 design doc M1 acceptance list called for a stdlib doc page; the CHANGELOG covered the API but no website reference page landed. v0.18.11 ships `docs/docs/reference/std-package.md`:

- When to use `std/package` vs ordinary `import pkg/...`
- Asset bundling via `[assets]` block in `ailang.toml`
- `assetPath` signature: `pkgName`, `relPath`, return shape (Ok/Err variants)
- Version selection (lexically-highest)
- Security: FS effect + double-layer path validation + stat-before-return
- Two complete usage examples (resolve-and-exec + self-disable)
- Cross-link to M-EXT-PORTABILITY-GATE design doc + example file

- `examples/asset_path.ail` (NEW) + `examples/extension_self_disable.ail` (NEW)
- `internal/pkg/manifest.go` — `SmokeConfig` + `Smoke` field + boundary validation
- `cmd/ailang/pkg_publish.go::runPrePublishSmoke` — honours `manifest.Smoke.TimeoutSeconds`
- `internal/pkg/tarball_test.go` — `TestCreateTarball_PathSeparatorIsForwardSlash` + `TestManifest_SmokeTimeoutValidation`

### M-EXT-PORTABILITY-GATE: AILANG packages can ship assets + pre-publish smoke gate

Closes the bug class that motoko_ext_compaction_ai 0.1.1 demonstrated in production: a package passes `ailang publish`, gets installed by a consumer (motoko_agent), and then crashes on first tool dispatch with `no pattern matched in match expression` (or panicking-readFile, or any other "looks fine in the dev workdir, panics in a fresh workdir" failure mode). Three changes ship together:

**1. Asset bundling** — packages can ship arbitrary helper files

```toml
# ailang.toml
[assets]
files = ["mcp-call.mjs", "schemas/tool-call.json"]
```

`internal/pkg/tarball.go::CreateTarball` now walks the package's `assets/` subdirectory and bundles its contents (lexically sorted, zero ModTime — same determinism guarantee as before). At publish time, `internal/pkg/VerifyDeclaredAssets` rejects manifests whose declared assets are missing from disk, so a typo fails loud rather than shipping an installed package whose runtime `assetPath()` lookups all return Err.

New AILANG API in `std/package`:

```ailang
import std/package (assetPath)
import std/result (Ok, Err)

match assetPath("sunholo/motoko_ext_mcp", "mcp-call.mjs") {
  Ok(p)  => spawnNode(p),
  Err(e) => Delegate
}
```

`assetPath(pkgName, relPath)` returns `Result[string, string] ! {FS}` — `Ok(absolutePath)` when the asset exists in `~/.ailang/cache/registry/<vendor>/<name>/<version>/assets/<rel>`, `Err(message)` for invalid input (single-segment names, absolute or `..`-containing paths), missing package, or missing asset. The lexically-highest installed version wins. Backed by the new `_pkg_asset_path` builtin (effects: FS).

**2. Pre-publish smoke gate** — broken packages never reach the registry

`ailang publish` now runs the package's `_smoke.ail` in an isolated temp directory before uploading the tarball:

```
$ ailang publish
Publishing sunholo/motoko_ext_compaction_ai@0.1.2...
  Running _smoke.ail in temp workdir (timeout 30s)...
✓ _smoke.ail passed (0.35s)
  Tarball: 4306 bytes (sha256:af14212ab59bc31a0...)
```

`internal/pkg/RunSmokeInTempDir(packageDir, ailangBin, timeout) (*SmokeResult, error)` stages the publishable file set (manifest, *.ail, AGENT.md, assets/, lock) into a fresh tmpdir and spawns ailang as a process-group leader. On timeout the entire group is SIGKILL'd via POSIX `setpgid`, so orphaned `find` / `sleep` / child shells don't outlive the publish command (no-op on Windows). Captured stdout/stderr is truncated to 8KB and printed on failure for triage.

Behaviour matrix:
- `_smoke.ail` present + passes → publish proceeds
- `_smoke.ail` present + fails → publish blocked, captured output printed
- `_smoke.ail` present + timeout → publish blocked, process group killed
- `_smoke.ail` absent → warn (back-compat); HARD FAIL when manifest declares `[extension]`
- 30s default timeout (`pkg.DefaultSmokeTimeout`)
- `AILANG_RELAX_MODULES=1` set for the run so canonical module names work even though the file lives in a tmpdir

**3. `std/extension.requireWorkdirFile` helper**

The canonical "require this file or self-disable" pattern for AILANG extension packages:

```ailang
import std/extension (requireWorkdirFile)

export func register_with_config(_cfg: a) -> ExtensionHooks ! {Env, FS} {
  let workdir = getEnvOr("MOTOKO_WORKDIR", ".");
  let tools = match requireWorkdirFile(workdir, "omnigraph/omnigraph.yaml") {
    Ok(_)  => provided_tools(),  -- active
    Err(_) => []                  -- self-disabled; host won't dispatch tools
  };
  ...
}
```

Better than calling the panicking variant of `std/fs.readFile` and bringing the host down. Returns `Result[(), string] ! {FS}`.

### Why this matters for AI coding agents

The exact failure mode this closes:

```
agent writes  → ailang check passes → ailang publish accepts
              → consumer installs → first tool call panics
              → agent has to wait for the consumer crash signal
                to even know something is wrong
```

The new gate moves the failure detection from "consumer runtime, days later" to "publish time, while the writer still has full context loaded". Combined with v0.18.10's `M-MATCH-ADT-XCHECK` (which catches Option-vs-Result match-arm typos at type-check time), the AI-agent feedback loop is now: write → typecheck-error fires for ADT mismatches → write → publish-smoke fires for runtime crashes → write → ship.

### Files changed

**AILANG repo** (~570 LOC + ~100 LOC tests):
- `internal/pkg/manifest.go` — `AssetConfig` + `Assets` field + traversal validation
- `internal/pkg/tarball.go` — `AssetsDir` const, walks assets/, ToSlash for cross-platform determinism, `VerifyDeclaredAssets()`
- `internal/pkg/publish_validator.go` (NEW) — `RunSmokeInTempDir`, `SmokeResult`, `SmokeFile`, `DefaultSmokeTimeout`, `HasExtensionBlock`
- `internal/pkg/process_unix.go` + `process_windows.go` (NEW) — build-tag-split `setProcessGroup` / `killProcessGroup` helpers
- `internal/effects/pkg.go` (NEW) — `pkgAssetPath` effect handler with input validation + version selection
- `internal/builtins/pkg.go` (NEW) — `_pkg_asset_path` builtin registration `((string, string) -> Result[string, string] ! {FS})`
- `cmd/ailang/pkg_publish.go` — wires `VerifyDeclaredAssets` + `runPrePublishSmoke` before `CreateTarball`
- `std/package.ail` (NEW) — `assetPath` public wrapper
- `std/extension.ail` (NEW) — `requireWorkdirFile` public helper
- `internal/pkg/tarball_test.go` — 4 new tests (assets bundled, deterministic, declared-asset verification, manifest traversal)
- `internal/pkg/publish_validator_test.go` (NEW) — 5 tests (pass, fail, timeout, isolation, no-smoke-file)
- `internal/effects/pkg_test.go` (NEW) — 5 tests (resolves, picks-latest, package-missing, asset-missing, rejects-bad-inputs)
- `internal/pipeline/testdata/builtin_types.golden` — refreshed for new builtin

**ailang-packages repo** (committed to `feat/portability-v0.18.11` branch, awaits this AILANG release before publish):
