### M-EVAL-SWEET-SPOT-WEBSITE-INTEGRATION: dashboard surfaces for sweet-spot data

Brings the typed-category, bucket, and $/pass-economics data from M-EVAL-SWEET-SPOT (CLI-only at v0.18.10) onto the public benchmark dashboard. Headline visibility goal achieved: **the 12.4× deepseek-vs-gemma $/pass spread from the validation session is now visible on first dashboard load**.

**Embedded in `docs/static/benchmarks/latest.json`** (driven by the Go exporter, no client-side recomputation):

- `models[name].sweet_spot` — per-model block with `pass_rate`, `median_tts_ms`, `p90_cost_per_success`, `dollars_per_pass`, `pareto_frontier`, `buckets`, `finish_reasons`, `error_categories`.
- `models[name].sweet_spot_by_harness` — same shape keyed by executor when a model ran via multiple harnesses (e.g. claude-haiku-4-5 via claude AND opencode).
- `sweet_spot_global.champions[]` — per-benchmark cheapest-pass + fastest-pass winners, plus `slow_threshold_ms` and `total_runs`.

**New JSX components**:

- `DollarsPerPassTable` — per-model `$/pass` leaderboard, default-sorted ascending. Toggle for "Show as ratio vs cheapest" surfaces large absolute spreads. Pareto-frontier ✓ badge marks models for which no alternative is both cheaper AND faster.
- `BenchmarkChampionsTable` — per-benchmark cheapest / fastest winners with same-winner collapse ("↑ same") to reduce visual noise.
- `FailureCategoryBars` — stacked horizontal bars per model showing the 4-way outcome family breakdown (success / budget / capability / provider). Color-coded with Docusaurus CSS vars so dark mode adapts.

**Operational drift guard**: `sweet_spot_parity_test` asserts every numerical field round-trips identically between `BuildSweetSpot` (CLI side) and `renderSweetSpotRow` (dashboard JSON side). Validated against all 3 M-EVAL-SWEET-SPOT validation datasets (56 runs across validation/hard/stretch tiers). Float tolerance 1e-4; ints/bools exact. Subtests per dataset for granular failure isolation.

**Sweet-spot data-model enrichments** (also reusable by future tools):

- `SweetSpotRow.DollarsPerPass` — totalCost / passes for this (model × harness).
- `SweetSpotRow.ParetoFrontier` — Pareto-frontier membership computed across all rows in the report.
- `SweetSpotRow.FinishReasons` — `map[reason]int` tally across the model's runs.

Pre-v0.18.10 baselines (no `finish_reason` / typed `error_category` data) still produce a valid mostly-zero `sweet_spot` block. JSX components gate-check and render a "regenerate with v0.19.0+" hint when missing.

**Deferred to follow-up**: tier filter on the existing QualityScatter (would surface the frontier-inversion phenomenon — deepseek dominates hard tier, claude+gemma share stretch). Requires per-tier `sweet_spot.tiers[tier]` data, which depends on M-BENCHMARK-DATA-INTEGRITY Issue #5.

Refs:
- Design doc: `design_docs/planned/v0_19_0/m-eval-sweet-spot-website-integration.md`
- Sprint plan: `design_docs/planned/v0_19_0/m-eval-sweet-spot-website-integration-sprint-plan.md`

## [v0.18.10] - 2026-05-11

### M-MATCH-ADT-XCHECK: typechecker rejects match arms with foreign-ADT constructors

Closes the bug class that crashed motoko_ext_compaction_ai 0.1.1 in production ([arniwesth/motoko_agent#16](https://github.com/arniwesth/motoko_agent/pull/16)). The package matched an `Option[string]` value with `Result`-shaped arms (`Err`/`Ok` instead of `None`/`Some`); the typechecker accepted it and the runtime crashed with `no pattern matched in match expression` only when the field was actually present.

```ailang
-- Before v0.18.10: typechecks cleanly, crashes at runtime
match getString(json, "model") {  -- returns Option[string]
  Err(_) => "fallback",            -- Err is from Result, not Option
  Ok(m)  => m
}
```

**v0.18.10 typechecker now rejects this with a structured error** (when the scrutinee's ADT is concretely resolved):

```
Error: type error in test (decl 0): match arm constructor 'Err'
belongs to ADT 'Result', not 'Option' (the scrutinee's type).
  Option's constructors are: None, Some
  Result's constructors are: Err, Ok
  Suggestion: did you mean one of: None, Some?
```

**Coverage**:
- `match Option[T] { Err(_) => ..., Ok(_) => ... }` rejected
- `match Result[T,E] { Some(_) => ..., None => ... }` rejected (symmetric)
- `match list[T] { Some(_) => ..., None => ... }` rejected (no list-vs-Option confusion)
- Nested patterns: inner foreign constructor in `match Option[Option[T]] { Some(Err(_)) => ... }` rejected at the inner position
- Wildcard / variable arms alongside foreign-ctor arms still trigger the error (no suppression)
- Valid matches (`match Option { Some(_) => ..., None => ... }`) compile unchanged

**Why this matters for AI workflows**: AI coding agents (motoko, Claude Code, etc.) iteratively write code → `ailang check` → declare done. Pre-v0.18.10, the loop accepted buggy code that crashed at runtime, forcing multi-version package iterations to discover the bug downstream. Now the typechecker rejects immediately, the AI gets a precise error pointing at the wrong constructor with the right one suggested, and self-correction happens in one iteration.

**Implementation** (~310 LOC across 4 source files + 9 unit tests + 2 expected-fail examples + new docs page):
- `internal/types/errors.go` — new `MatchForeignConstructorError` kind + `NewMatchForeignConstructorError` constructor producing the rich multi-line message with sorted constructor lists.
- `internal/types/typechecker_core.go` — `lookupADTConstructors` helper reverses the existing `constructorTypes` map.
- `internal/types/helpers.go` — `extractADTName` helper extracts the named ADT from `*TCon`/`*TApp[*TCon]`, returning empty for unresolved type variables (so polymorphic scrutinees fall through to the existing constraint-based unification path).
- `internal/types/typechecker_patterns.go` — cross-check in `checkPattern`'s `ConstructorPattern` case fires the new structured error when the scrutinee's ADT is concretely resolved at typecheck time.
- `internal/pipeline/match_foreign_constructor_test.go` (new, 9 tests) — covers Option↔Result, list↔Option, valid matches (regression), nested patterns (valid + inner-foreign), and wildcard/variable-arm-doesn't-suppress-error.
- `examples/expected_fail/match_foreign_constructor_{option,result}.ail` (new) + README entry — codifies that these MUST fail; if `make verify-examples` ever stops rejecting them, the typechecker has regressed.
- `docs/docs/reference/option-vs-result.md` (new) — canonical "which is which" reference page with full table of std/json + std/fs + std/list + std/ai functions and which return Option vs Result.

**Dual-path detection**: the cross-check fires the rich error eagerly when the scrutinee's ADT is resolved (explicit annotations like `let x: Option[string] = ...`). For inferred-type cases where the scrutinee type is still a fresh type variable at pattern-check time (e.g. `match getString(...) { Err(_) ... }`), the existing constraint-based unification path catches the bug — the error message there is generic ("type constructor mismatch: Option vs Result") but includes the constructor name + both ADTs in the path. Both paths together close the bug class. Richer formatting for the inferred path is a v0.18.11 follow-up that requires intercepting the unification error formatting.

**Acceptance verified**:
- All 9 unit tests pass
- Full `go test ./...` suite: zero new failures
- `make verify-examples`: 178 passed, 0 failed (the 2 new expected_fail examples are correctly rejected)
- WASM build clean

Refs:
- Source bug: [arniwesth/motoko_agent#16](https://github.com/arniwesth/motoko_agent/pull/16)
- Design doc: `design_docs/implemented/v0_18_10/m-match-adt-xcheck.md`
- Sprint plan: `design_docs/implemented/v0_18_10/m-match-adt-xcheck-sprint-plan.md`

### M-EVAL-SWEET-SPOT: typed failure categories + cost-vs-time-vs-success sweet-spot CLI

**Problem**: 76% of recent agent-mode failures (51 of 67 across `eval_results/v0_18_*`) were lumped into an undifferentiated `api_error` bucket — OpenRouter monthly quota kills, transient 429s, hard timeouts, and motoko cost-cap exhaustion all looked identical. Operators couldn't tell whether a cheap-but-slow model would pass with more budget vs was genuinely incapable. Separately, v0.15.1 already captured per-result speed metrics (`SuccessAtMs`, `TokensPerSec`, `CostKilledAt`) and rendered them on the dashboard JS, but the CLI had no surface for it.

**Failure-category foundation** (M1+M2+M3):

- `internal/eval_harness/metrics.go`: 5 new `ErrorCategory` constants — `timeout`, `quota_exhausted`, `rate_limit`, `cost_killed`, `step_exhausted` — and a new `FinishReason string` field on `RunMetrics` (omitempty, backward-compatible).
- `internal/eval_harness/error_categorizer.go`: `CategorizeAgentError(err, finishReason)` matches against real OpenRouter / OpenAI / motoko error strings. Structured `finishReason` wins over string matching; falls back to `api_error` for genuine unknowns. 17 table tests + dataset reclassification test against shipped `eval_results/v0_18_*` JSONs.
- `internal/executor/executor.go`: top-level `FinishReason` field on `Result`. Motoko parser promotes `motoko_finish_reason` from `ProviderData` to this canonical field.
- `cmd/ailang/eval_benchmark.go`: classifies API-error path via `CategorizeAgentError(err, "")`; in success path with non-zero `FinishReason`, promotes the typed category over the generic compile/runtime/logic classification.
- `internal/eval_analysis/capability.go`: new `ShouldExcludeFromCapability(cat)` helper replaces 12 inline `r.ErrorCategory == "api_error"` checks across `dashboard_io.go`, `export_json.go`, `export_json_executors.go`, `export_json_matrix.go`. Excludes `quota_exhausted`, `rate_limit`, `api_error` from capability scoring (keeps `timeout` / `cost_killed` / `step_exhausted` visible — they're real signal).

**Sweet-spot reporting** (M4+M5):

- `ailang eval-sweet-spot <results_dir> [--format=text|csv|json|mdx] [--slow-ms=N]` — ranked per-(model, harness) view of pass rate × median TTS × p90 $/success, plus per-benchmark "cheapest pass" / "fastest pass" champions table.
- `internal/eval_analysis/sweet_spot.go`: `BuildSweetSpot` with 5-way bucketing per benchmark: `fast_pass` (≤ slowMs), `slow_pass`, `budget_blocked` (`cost_killed`/`step_exhausted`), `capability_blocked` (compile/runtime/logic/timeout), `provider_blocked` (quota/rate_limit/api_error — excluded from pass rate). Text/CSV/JSON/MDX formatters. 12 unit tests.
- `internal/eval_analysis/efficiency.go`: extracted `EfficiencyByModel(results)` helper shared between sweet-spot and the existing JSON dashboard exporter.

**Empirical finding from rollout**: re-running the new categorizer offline against `eval_results/v0_18_*/agent` reclassifies 15 of 51 `api_error` rows (29%) into the new `quota_exhausted` bucket. The remaining 36 (71%) are genuine executor crashes ("motoko terminated without emitting run_summary") and provider 400s for malformed `tool_use_id` — neither matches quota/timeout/429 patterns and legitimately stays as `api_error`. The design doc's "≥90% reclassification" target was based on the incorrect assumption that all `api_error` rows were noise; reality is that ~30% are noise and ~70% are real executor failures that need richer signals (e.g. a future `executor_crashed` category).

**Deviation from design**: `ShouldExcludeFromCapability` includes `api_error` in the exclusion set (the doc proposed only quota/rate_limit). Documented rationale in `capability.go`: until legacy result JSONs that pre-date M1 categorization age out, dropping `api_error` from the exclusion set would silently broaden dashboard pass-rate denominators for runs whose genuine quota kills are still tagged the old way.

**Post-implementation validation against real OpenRouter traffic** (32 agent runs, 2026-05-11):

Three new `motoko-or-*` model entries added (`deepseek-v4-flash`, `kimi-k2-6`, `gemma-4-26b`) plus existing `claude-haiku-4-5` strong baseline. Two suites — 4 easy benchmarks (`balanced_parens`, `list_comprehension`, `csv_to_json_converter`, `state_machine_vending`) and 4 hard (`config_file_parser`, `mini_interpreter`, `lambda_calc`, `expression_evaluator`):

Hard-suite frontier (16/16 runs, all passed by claude + deepseek):

| Model | Pass% | Med TTS | $/win | Pareto |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| claude-haiku-4-5 | 100% | 33.9s | $0.074 | ✓ frontier (speed) |
| motoko-or-deepseek-v4-flash | 100% | 46.7s | $0.042 | ✓ frontier (cost) |
| motoko-or-kimi-k2-6 | 67% | 111.5s | $0.095 | dominated |
| motoko-or-gemma-4-26b | 75% | 364.9s | $0.122 | dominated |

deepseek-v4-flash via motoko is **cheapest on every hard benchmark** ($0.016–0.042/win) and Pareto-optimal alongside claude-haiku-4-5. Validates the headline thesis: a cheap model with adequate iteration matches a frontier model at half the cost.

Most striking single data point — gemma on `balanced_parens` (a trivial benchmark): **2590s wall-clock (43 min), 222 turns, $0.94 cost, finish_reason="stop"** (succeeded). Without typed categorization and cost-vs-time reporting this would historically have looked like an `api_error`.

**Discovered limitation**: the `motoko-or-gemma-4-26b` entry's `max_cost_usd: 0.50` cap was exceeded ($0.94) but the run was **not killed** — the v0.15.1 cost-budget primitive is not actually wired to terminate motoko runs when the cap is exceeded. The field is recorded as metadata but not enforced. Added as P1 to M-EVAL-SWEET-SPOT-FOLLOWUP — without enforcement, `cost_killed` category cannot fire in production, blunting the `budget_blocked` bucket's value.

**Post-P1 validation against stretch tier** (24 additional runs, 2026-05-11):

Same 4 models on the 6 hardest stretch benchmarks (`polymorphic_ord_defaulting`, `log_file_analyzer`, `contract_rle_roundtrip`, `run_length_encode`, `type_unify`, `contract_sorted_merge` — historically 43–66% pass rate). Gemma's cap dropped to `$0.30`.

| Model | Easy | Hard | **Stretch** | Pass overall |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| claude-haiku-4-5 | 100% | 100% | **100%** | 14/14 |
| motoko-or-deepseek-v4-flash | 75% | 100% | **83%** | 12/14 |
| motoko-or-kimi-k2-6 | 50% | 50% | **50%** | 7/14 |
| motoko-or-gemma-4-26b | 50% | 75% | **17%** | 6/14 |

Stretch tier surfaces the real capability gradient — clean 100/83/50/17 split.

**The Pareto frontier *inverted* between hard and stretch**: on hard, deepseek dominated cost; on stretch, claude wins everything except `contract_sorted_merge` ($0.017 on deepseek). Gemma becomes Pareto-optimal not for being good but for being the **only** cheap option that ever passes (`run_length_encode` at $0.035).

**`cost_killed` STILL did not fire even at $0.30 cap** — but for an informative reason: gemma fails *fast* on stretch (compile_error in 4–18 turns, max spend $0.05) instead of marathoning. The model knows when it's beaten. The marathon-fail pattern only appears on easy benchmarks where gemma thinks it's almost there ($1.10 / 98 min / 144 turns on `state_machine_vending`, fail with compile_error).

**Per-pass economics across all 56 session runs**:

| Model | $ total | Wall-clock | Passes | $/pass |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| motoko-or-deepseek-v4-flash | $0.46 | 25 min | 12/14 | **$0.038** ⭐ |
| claude-haiku-4-5 | $1.24 | 9 min | 14/14 | $0.089 |
| motoko-or-kimi-k2-6 | $0.76 | 36 min | 7/14 | $0.109 |
| motoko-or-gemma-4-26b | $2.84 | 221 min | 6/14 | **$0.473** (12.4× deepseek) |

Headline: **deepseek via motoko is 12.4× cheaper per success than gemma via OpenRouter** — but a locally-hosted gemma would have $0 marginal cost, flipping the entire ranking. M-EVAL-SWEET-SPOT makes that trade-off legible.

**Typed failure categories firing in production**: kimi 2× `api_error` (OpenRouter denial), gemma 1× `rate_limit`. Categorizer works against real provider noise.

Total session spend: $5.30 across 56 runs (~9.4¢/run avg). 221 of 273 total minutes of wall-clock were gemma alone.

**Followup shipped same-day**: P1 cost-cap enforcement for motoko executor (M-EVAL-SWEET-SPOT-FOLLOWUP-P1, see below).

**Still deferred**:
- `FormatMatrix` "By Model" efficiency-column extension (needs `PerformanceMatrix` to carry per-model efficiency).
- `eval-compare` ΔTTS / Δcost_killed / Δp90$ deltas (needs `ComparisonReport` field additions).
- `step_exhausted` emission from CLI subprocess executors (claude/gemini/opencode) — they have no harness-side max-turns budget today; the agent decides when to stop.

### M-EVAL-SWEET-SPOT-FOLLOWUP-P1: cost-cap enforcement for motoko

**Problem**: validation found that `Budgets.MaxCostUSD` was parsed from `models.yml` but never reached motoko — gemma-4-26b ran to $0.94 against a $0.50 cap on `balanced_parens` (43 min, 222 turns) because motoko had no idea about the cap. The eval-side `CostBudget.MaxUSD` was metadata-only on the motoko code path.

**Fix**: motoko already reads an `AI_MAX_COST_USD_CENTS` env var (in `motoko_agent/src/core/config.ail:549`) and enforces it internally — emitting `finish_reason="cost_exhausted"` when crossed. The eval harness just wasn't passing it.

- `internal/executor/motoko/motoko.go`: when `task.Budget.MaxUSD > 0`, export `AI_MAX_COST_USD_CENTS=<USD × 100>` on the subprocess env. Same pattern as the existing `MOTOKO_COST_INPUT_PER_1M_MILLICENTS` forwarding.
- `internal/executor/motoko/parser.go`: when `finish_reason == "cost_exhausted"`, populate `res.CostKilledAt = res.CostUSD` so the per-result JSON's `cost_killed_at` field is non-zero. Drives the new `budget_blocked` sweet-spot bucket.
- `internal/executor/motoko/execute_test.go`: extended `TestExecute_BudgetEnvVarPassthrough` to assert `AI_MAX_COST_USD_CENTS=50` flows through when `MaxUSD=0.50`. Extended `TestExecute_NoBudget_NoEnvVar` to assert it's UNSET when budget is nil.
- `internal/executor/motoko/parser_test.go`: `TestParseSessionJSONL_CostExhausted` now also asserts `CostKilledAt == 0.046` on the cost_exhausted fixture.

Flow now end-to-end: `models.yml budgets.max_cost_usd` → `CostBudget.MaxUSD` → `AI_MAX_COST_USD_CENTS` → motoko cost gate → `finish_reason="cost_exhausted"` → `CategorizeAgentError` → `ErrorCategoryCostKilled` → `sweet_spot.go` `budget_blocked` bucket. Closes the P1 gap discovered by validation.

## [v0.18.9] - 2026-05-10

### M-AI-OPENROUTER-REASONING-FIELD: surface reasoning from OR-routed thinking models

Follow-up to v0.18.8's M-AI-STEP-STREAMING-THINKING. Caught when Arni reported DeepSeek thinking traces weren't appearing in motoko despite Gemma working. Direct-curl probe of `openrouter.ai/api/v1/chat/completions` against `deepseek/deepseek-r1` and `anthropic/claude-opus-4.5` revealed the field-name mismatch:

| Path | Reasoning field |
|---|---|
| OpenAI o1/o3 direct API | `delta.reasoning_content` (per OpenAI spec) |
| **OpenRouter (any routed thinking model)** | **`delta.reasoning`** (OpenRouter normalization) |

Verified by curl: deepseek-r1 (via Novita) AND anthropic-via-OR (via Bedrock) BOTH emit through `delta.reasoning` with optional `delta.reasoning_details` array. v0.18.8's parser only read `delta.reasoning_content`, so EVERY OpenRouter-routed reasoning model silently dropped reasoning chunks.

**Fix (additive)**:
- `internal/ai/openai/streamstep.go` — `ChatStepStreamDelta` grew a `Reasoning` field alongside `ReasoningContent`. Per-chunk loop fires `ai.StreamThinkingDelta` for either non-empty value (in practice only one fires per chunk; dual-field support is provider-shape defensive).
- `internal/ai/openrouter/streamstep.go` — wire body now includes `"include_reasoning":true` (OpenRouter's opt-in flag — without it, OR drops reasoning silently regardless of underlying provider's native field name). Also refactored `marshalStepBodyWithProvider` into a more general `marshalStepBodyWithExtras` that takes a list of pre-marshalled `"key":<value>` fragments to splice into the JSON body.

**Why "Gemma worked but DeepSeek didn't"**: Gemma 3 isn't a thinking model — it doesn't emit any reasoning fields. Motoko's `[think]` panel for Gemma was actually rendering tag-convention `<thinking>...</thinking>` content from its prompt format (via the v0.18.7 ContentDelta + TUI tag-stripping path). DeepSeek-R1 IS a thinking model and its reasoning should have been on the `[reason]` panel via the v0.18.8 ThinkingDelta path — but that path was field-name-blocked at the OpenAI parser layer.

**Tests**:
- `TestStreamStep_ParsesOpenRouterReasoningField` (openai): fakes a deepseek-r1 SSE stream with `delta.reasoning` chunks, asserts `ai.StreamThinkingDelta` fires AND `Response.Text` contains only the answer (reasoning excluded).
- `TestStreamStep_IncludeReasoningOnTheWire` (openrouter): asserts the wire body includes `"include_reasoning":true` so OR doesn't drop reasoning at the source.
- All 8 v0.18.8 streaming tests still pass.
- WASM build clean.

**Caller migration**: zero. Existing v0.18.8 motoko-side `ThinkingDelta(text)` match arms automatically receive the now-flowing chunks.

## [v0.18.8] - 2026-05-09

### M-AI-STEP-STREAMING-THINKING: surface API-level reasoning as separate stream variant

Extends `std/ai.stepWithStream` (v0.18.7) with a third `StreamChunk` variant that exposes model reasoning/thought content as a first-class stream — distinct from assistant-visible content. Closes the gap for thinking-mode models (Anthropic claude-opus-4.5+ extended-thinking, OpenAI o1/o3 reasoning, Gemini 2.5+ thought parts) where reasoning was previously dropped by the v0.18.7 SSE parsers.

```ailang
type StreamChunk =
    ContentDelta(string)         -- assistant-visible text (v0.18.7)
  | ThinkingDelta(string)        -- model reasoning (NEW, v0.18.8)
  | Usage({...})                 -- terminal token summary (v0.18.7)
```

**Variant semantics**:
- `ContentDelta` — assistant-visible text fragment. Concatenation equals `StepResult.message.content`.
- `ThinkingDelta` — model reasoning fragment. **NOT included** in `StepResult.message.content`. Reasoning-token count surfaces via existing `StepResult.reason_tokens`. Render in a separate UI surface or log channel; safe to drop entirely.
- `Usage` — unchanged from v0.18.7.

**Per-provider plumbing**:

| Provider | Source | Surface |
|---|---|---|
| Anthropic (direct + Bedrock + Vertex) | `content_block_delta` events of `delta.type:"thinking_delta"` | Native (claude-opus-4.5+ with extended thinking) |
| OpenAI | `delta.reasoning_content` field on chat completion chunks | Native (o1/o3 models) |
| OpenRouter | Inherits whichever upstream shape the routed-to model uses | Pass-through |
| Gemini (AI Studio + Vertex) | `parts[].thought:true` flag on streamed parts | Native (gemini-2.5+ thinking models) |
| Ollama, config-driven | NO-OP fallback unchanged from v0.18.7 | Reasoning not surfaced |

**Backward compatibility**: This is an **additive ADT extension**. Existing v0.18.7 callers that pattern-match on only `ContentDelta`/`Usage` continue to work — `ThinkingDelta` simply doesn't fire for non-thinking models, and for thinking models it's silently dropped on unmatched arms (AILANG's match exhaustiveness is checked at type-check time, but missing-variant warnings can be suppressed with a `_` arm). Upgrade to v0.18.8 is risk-free.

**Important non-coverage**: This DOES NOT cover the convention where models embed `<thinking>...</thinking>` text tags inline in their content output (glm-5, deepseek-r1, qwen-thinking, etc.). Those models deliver reasoning via `ContentDelta` with embedded tag markers; consumers should split via a tag-aware helper on their side. The v0.18.8 surface only covers API-level reasoning (where the provider tells us the chunk IS reasoning via a separate field).

**Implementation**:
- `internal/ai/provider.go` — new `StreamThinkingDelta` struct with marker method on the sealed `StreamChunk` interface (~25 LOC including doc).
- `internal/effects/ai_step.go` — `encodeStreamChunk` extended with `StreamThinkingDelta → ThinkingDelta(string)` AILANG-side encoding (~5 LOC).
- `std/ai.ail` — `ThinkingDelta(string)` constructor added to `StreamChunk` ADT; doc comment updated with per-provider matrix and tag-convention disclaimer (~50 LOC of doc).
- `internal/ai/anthropic/streamstep.go` — `streamContentBlockDelta` grew a `Thinking` field; dispatcher fires `ai.StreamThinkingDelta` on `delta.type:"thinking_delta"` (~10 LOC).
- `internal/ai/openai/streamstep.go` — `ChatStepStreamDelta` grew `ReasoningContent` field; per-chunk loop fires `ai.StreamThinkingDelta` on non-empty `delta.reasoning_content` (~10 LOC).
- `internal/ai/gemini/types.go` + `internal/ai/gemini/streamstep.go` — `part` grew `Thought bool` field; parser fires `ai.StreamThinkingDelta` for `thought:true` parts and excludes their text from `Response.Text` (~20 LOC).

**Acceptance verified**:
- `TestStreamStep_ParsesThinkingDelta` (Anthropic): 2 thinking deltas + 1 content delta → 2 ThinkingDelta callbacks fire, content callback fires once, Response.Text equals only "Answer" (reasoning does NOT leak).
- `TestStreamStep_ParsesReasoningContent` (OpenAI o1-mini): same pattern with `delta.reasoning_content`.
- `TestStreamStep_ParsesThoughtParts` (Gemini 2.5 Pro): same pattern with `parts[].thought:true`; also verifies `Response.ReasonTokens` reflects `usageMetadata.thoughtsTokenCount`.
- `TestAIStepWithStream_EncodesThinkingDeltaCorrectly` (effect op): round-trip integration verifying the `TaggedValue{CtorName:"ThinkingDelta", Fields:[StringValue]}` encoding lands at the AILANG callback.
- `examples/runnable/ai_streaming.ail` updated to include `ThinkingDelta(text) => print("[think] ${text}")` arm; runs cleanly against `--ai-stub`.
- WASM build verified: `GOOS=js GOARCH=wasm go build ./cmd/wasm` clean.

**Caller migration**: just add a third match arm:
```ailang
match chunk {
  ContentDelta(text)  => print(text),
  ThinkingDelta(text) => print("[think] ${text}"),  -- NEW
  Usage(_)            => println("")
}
```

### parser: helpful error for `\x -> body` lambda (correct: `\x. body`)

Common mistake when migrating from other ML-family languages: writing lambdas as `\x -> body` (Haskell-ish) instead of AILANG's `\x. body`. Previously the parser surfaced this as a cascading `expected '.' after lambda parameter` followed by 4-5 noisy `PAR_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` errors at every subsequent token. Caught while wiring v0.18.7 streaming into motoko_agent (`agent_loop_v2.ail` originally written with `\chunk -> ...` syntax).

Now: single targeted error on the `->` token with a corrected suggestion.

```
Error: lambda body separator is '.' not '->' at file.ail:42:18
       write: \x. <body>
```

`internal/parser/parser_expr.go` (+5 LOC), `internal/parser/expr_test.go` (+22 LOC test cases).

### planning hygiene: archive M-DX-MATCH-IN-HOF-BLOCK-LAMBDA design doc

The match-in-lambda parser quirk that motivated this design doc (v0.13.0 planned) is sidestepped by using the period syntax (`\x. match x { ... }`) instead of arrow syntax (`\x -> match x { ... }`). The arrow form is now caught early by the new parser error above; period-form match-in-lambda works fine. Doc moved to `design_docs/archive/`.

## [v0.18.7] - 2026-05-09

### M-AI-STEP-STREAMING: typed-StepResult streaming variant for std/ai

Source: [arniwesth/motoko_agent#10 Discussion](https://github.com/arniwesth/motoko_agent/discussions/10) — motoko's TUI lost token-by-token streaming when migrating from the AILANG fork (which had `std/ai_motoko.callStreamResult`) to upstream AILANG (PR #3, v0.15.2 → v0.18.x). All upstream `std/ai.step*` calls block 5-30s with no per-chunk visibility, despite motoko's TUI having token-streaming infra wired (event protocol, incremental markdown renderer, seq tracking).

The narrower-than-it-looks gap: significant streaming infra already shipped in v0.15.0 (`std/ai/streaming.openaiCompatStream` / `anthropicStream`) and v0.15.1 (`_ai_call_stream` / `std/ai/streaming.callStream`), but the diagonal "typed StepResult + per-chunk callback in BUILT-IN providers" was missing. v0.18.7 fills that diagonal with a single additive opt-in API mirroring the v0.18.4 stepWithCache shape exactly.

**New: `std/ai.stepWithStream`** — same shape as `stepWithCache` plus a 5th argument:

```ailang
type StreamChunk =
    ContentDelta(string)
  | Usage({input_tokens, output_tokens, cache_read_input_tokens, cache_creation_input_tokens})

func stepWithStream(
  model, messages, tools, cache_breakpoints,
  on_chunk: (StreamChunk) -> () ! {IO}
) -> Result[StepResult, AIError] ! {AI}
```

The callback's effect row is open — typically `{IO}` for stdout streaming but you can wire whatever side-channel you need (a websocket, a TUI buffer, a metrics sink, etc.). Errors thrown by the callback are recorded as trace events but do NOT abort the SSE drain.

**Per-provider scope (Phase 1)**:
- Anthropic (direct/Bedrock/Vertex): native SSE, `ContentDelta` per `content_block_delta` event + final `Usage` from `message_delta`.
- OpenAI / OpenRouter (anthropic/* prefix routes through Anthropic shape): native SSE, `ContentDelta` per delta + final `Usage` from `chat.completion.chunk` (when `stream_options.include_usage:true`).
- Gemini (AI Studio + Vertex): native SSE via `streamGenerateContent?alt=sse`, `ContentDelta` per text part + single `Usage` at end-of-stream (Gemini emits running totals on every chunk; we surface only the final authoritative value to match Anthropic/OpenAI semantics).
- Ollama / config-driven providers: NO-OP fallback at the Handler layer — calls `StepWithCache`, fires one synthetic `ContentDelta(full_text)` + one `Usage(...)` chunk. Same `StepResult` shape, no incremental visibility.

**Out of scope (deferred to Phase 2)**:
- `ToolCallDelta` variant — requires per-provider JSON-fragment buffering (Anthropic input_json_delta, OpenAI tool_call function.arguments fragments). Tool calls ARE captured for the final StepResult.ToolCalls; they're just not streamed individually.
- `ThinkingDelta` variant — Anthropic-specific extended-thinking deltas.

**New AIHandler interface method**: `StepWithStream(model, messages, tools, breakpoints, onChunk)`. Adds to the existing 6 implementations on `AIContext`, `StubAIHandler`, `ai.Handler`, `WasmAIHandler`, plus `fakeStepHandler` + `routingStubHandler` test stubs. Existing `step()` / `stepWithCache()` callers are unchanged (regression: all 22 v0.18.4 unit tests pass).

**New optional Provider interface**: `ai.StreamingProvider` (single method `StreamStep(ctx, req, onChunk) (*Response, error)`). Providers that implement it get native streaming via type-assertion in `Handler.StepWithStream`; those that don't get the NO-OP fallback. Currently implemented by `*anthropic.Client`, `*openai.Client`, `*openrouter.Client`, `*gemini.Client` (compile-time `var _ ai.StreamingProvider = (*Client)(nil)` checks in each).

**Acceptance verified**:
- 4 unit tests on Anthropic SSE parser (content+usage, tool_use input_json_delta accumulation, HTTP error envelope hoisting, stream:true wire flag).
- 4 unit tests on OpenAI SSE parser (content+usage, tool_call fragment reassembly, stream_options.include_usage wire flag, HTTP error envelope).
- 3 unit tests on OpenRouter (content+usage through anthropic/* routing, AIRoutingPolicy + stream composition, HTTP error envelope).
- 4 unit tests on Gemini SSE parser (content+final-only usage, functionCall → ToolCall with deterministic IDs, streamGenerateContent + alt=sse URL, HTTP error envelope).
- 4 round-trip integration tests on the effect op (encoded ContentDelta+Usage delivery, handler-error → typed Err, missing FnCaller → typed Err, cache_breakpoints plumbing).
- Pipeline golden snapshot: 295 builtin signatures (`_ai_step_with_stream` added).
- WASM build clean (`GOOS=js GOARCH=wasm go build ./cmd/wasm`).
- End-to-end `examples/runnable/ai_streaming.ail` runs against `--ai-stub` with the synthetic ContentDelta + Usage path.

Cross-package wiring:
- `std/ai.ail` — new `StreamChunk` ADT + `stepWithStream` function (~40 LOC additive).
- `internal/ai/provider.go` — `StreamChunk` + `StreamContentDelta` + `StreamUsage` + optional `StreamingProvider` interface (~35 LOC).
- `internal/effects/ai.go` — `AIHandler.StepWithStream` interface widening + `AIContext.StepWithStream` + `StubAIHandler.StepWithStream` synthetic chunks (~25 LOC).
- `internal/ai/handler.go` — `Handler.StepWithStream` with NO-OP fallback for non-streaming providers (~50 LOC).
- `internal/builtins/ai_step.go` — `_ai_step_with_stream` builtin registration (~110 LOC including doc).
- `internal/effects/ai_step.go` — `aiStepWithStream` op + `encodeStreamChunk` helper (~130 LOC).
- `cmd/wasm/effects.go` — `WasmAIHandler.StepWithStream` returns "not supported in WASM".
- `internal/ai/anthropic/streamstep.go` — Anthropic native SSE → typed StepResult (~310 LOC).
- `internal/ai/openai/streamstep.go` — OpenAI native SSE → typed StepResult (~280 LOC).
- `internal/ai/openrouter/streamstep.go` — composes openai SSE parser + OpenRouter provider field + attribution headers (~110 LOC).
- `internal/ai/gemini/streamstep.go` — Gemini native SSE → typed StepResult, including functionCall → ToolCall translation (~210 LOC).
- `internal/ai/gemini/generate.go` — `buildStreamURL` helper that emits `:streamGenerateContent?alt=sse` URLs for both AI Studio (api-key) and Vertex AI (ADC) auth modes.

Refs design doc: `design_docs/planned/v0_18_7/m-ai-step-streaming.md`
Refs sprint plan: `design_docs/planned/v0_18_7/m-ai-step-streaming-sprint-plan.md`
Refs source: arniwesth/motoko_agent#10


## [v0.18.6] - 2026-05-09

### M-BENCHMARK-DATA-INTEGRITY: dedup runs + per-tier breakdown + cross-harness fixes

- `loader.go`: deduplicate LoadResults by `(model, id, lang, seed)` keeping newest-first. Prevents debug/scaffold runs from polluting aggregate stats — motoko went from 209→38 final runs (52%→95% pass rate).
- `export_json_executors.go`: per-tier breakdown in harness aggregates (`harnesses[name].tiers[tier][lang]`); motoko display name added.
- `export_json.go`: thread `benchmarkTier` map through to `buildHarnessAggregates`.
- `BenchmarkExplorer/index.jsx`: suppress `successRateAdjusted` when `apiErrorRate > 50%` (formula `rate/(1-apiErrorRate)` is unreliable above that threshold — Gemini stretch was showing 100% adjusted from 9% raw); CrossHarnessTable uses `agentSuccessRate` (not blended) as baseline; tier filter chips scoped to harness table.
- `latest.json` regenerated with dedup + motoko + tier data.

### Fix: skip POSIX sandbox path tests on Windows

`/tmp/sandbox` is not absolute on Windows so `filepath.IsAbs` returns false, causing the sandbox containment check to silently join the path instead of rejecting it, breaking 4 tests in CI. The sandbox feature is POSIX-only (paths come from `AILANG_FS_SANDBOX` which callers set as Unix paths), so Windows skips are correct.

### Fix: `ailang init motoko-extension` auto-includes Env+FS in effect ceiling

Bug caught by post-release verification of v0.18.5. When `--effects` omitted `Env` (e.g. `--effects "FS,Process"`), the generated package's `[effects].max` didn't permit Env, but `register.ail`'s `register_with_config` declares `! {Env, FS}` (matching the canonical motoko-ext-* shape) — so `ailang check register.ail` failed with:

```
Error: in function register_with_config in register: effect ceiling violation in package <name>: effects [Env] not in max [FS Process]
```

Fix: `ensureEffects()` helper auto-adds `Env` + `FS` to the user's `--effects` list before generation. User-supplied effects extend this baseline, never replace it. Generated packages now type-check with zero edits regardless of what the user passes for `--effects`.

7 new sub-tests cover the auto-include path; existing tests updated for the new effect-list length expectation.

(`cmd/ailang/init_motoko_extension.go`, `cmd/ailang/init_motoko_extension_test.go`)

## [v0.18.5] - 2026-05-09

### M-EXT-SCAFFOLD-AI-FIRST: one-command motoko extension scaffolder (target v0.18.5)

Closes the AI-driven extension authoring gap surfaced by [arniwesth/motoko_agent#8](https://github.com/arniwesth/motoko_agent/pull/8). Today, scaffolding a working motoko_agent extension by hand requires reading ~840 lines of docs + a ~100-LOC reference impl, then writing ~120 LOC of canonical boilerplate before any actual logic. ~30,000 tokens of doc context per extension. Pure axiom-A7 (Machines First) violation.

**New: `ailang init motoko-extension`** — single command produces a working package:

```bash
ailang init motoko-extension \
  --name arniwesth/motoko_ext_openkb \
  --tools "OpenKBSearch,OpenKBList" \
  --effects "FS,Process,Env"
```

Generates 5 files at `packages/motoko-ext-<short>/`:
- `ailang.toml` — package metadata, **registry-version dep on `motoko_ext_abi`** (NEVER `path = "../..."`), exports list, effects, `[stability] level = "experimental"`
- `register.ail` — canonical `register_with_config` wrapper
- `types.ail` — placeholder type stub (TODO comment, user fills in)
- `<short>.ail` — full `make_hooks()` with all 8 `ExtensionHooks` fields populated as no-op defaults so the package compiles immediately
- `README.md` — links to tutorial + publishing guide

**The four PR #8 failure modes are structurally impossible from generated output:**

| Mistake | Auto-prevented |
|---|---|
| Extension nested in host's `src/core/ext/` | ✅ Output dir is always `packages/motoko-ext-<name>/` |
| Package name missing `motoko_ext_` infix | ✅ `--name` validation rejects names without it |
| Hand-edited `registry_generated.ail` | ✅ Scaffolder never writes a registry file in the package |
| `path = "../..."` in production `ailang.toml` | ✅ Generated `ailang.toml` uses registry version of `motoko_ext_abi` |

**Token-cost impact** (the load-bearing motivation): scaffolding via this command takes ~500 tokens (read generated stubs) vs ~30,000 today (read multi-doc context + reference impl). **~60× reduction per extension authored.** Particularly important for AI agents creating extensions on the fly inside motoko_agent or similar hosts.

**Implementation:**
- `cmd/ailang/init_motoko_extension.go` (234 LOC) — flag parsing, validation, dispatch
- `cmd/ailang/init_motoko_extension_templates.go` (~280 LOC) — 5 file templates rendered via `text/template`
- `cmd/ailang/init_motoko_extension_test.go` (~210 LOC) — 19 unit tests + integration test (gated behind `AILANG_INTEGRATION_TESTS=1` for the full `ailang lock + ailang check` cycle)
- `cmd/ailang/init_webapp.go` — wired the new type into `initCommand` dispatch

**Tutorial doc updated** ([build-a-motoko-extension.md](../docs/docs/guides/build-a-motoko-extension.md)): Step 1 collapses from manual 4-file scaffolding to a single `ailang init motoko-extension` command. Old manual walkthrough preserved as Appendix A (for users on AILANG &lt; 0.18.5 or who want to understand the structure).

**Out of scope** (deferred to future sprints):
- Tier 2 generic `[extension_template]` block in host packages — `M-EXT-SCAFFOLD-GENERIC-TEMPLATES`, target v0.19.0+. Future-proofs scaffolding for non-motoko extension hosts
- Templates for non-motoko hosts — out until we have a 2nd extension host
- Interactive TTY prompts — flag-only first version is AI-friendly; humans get `--help`
- Auto-publish — `ailang publish` stays a separate explicit step

(`cmd/ailang/init_motoko_extension*.go`, `cmd/ailang/init_webapp.go`, `docs/docs/guides/build-a-motoko-extension.md`, `docs/docs/guides/package-publishing.md`, `design_docs/implemented/v0_18_5/m-ext-scaffold-ai-first*.md`)

<!-- The two entries below target future releases (v0.19.0 / v0.18.0). They were
     left under [Unreleased] at the time v0.18.5 shipped — see the marker above
     this section. -->

### M-FS-SANDBOX-DIAGNOSTICS: FS sandbox path rejection diagnostics (target v0.19.0)

Fixes a recurring production gotcha where `AILANG_FS_SANDBOX` caused programs to silently degrade to defaults with no diagnostic signal. The root cause: `exists`, `isDir`, and `isFile` return `false` (not an error) for out-of-sandbox paths — correct public contract, but invisible to the program using fallback logic.

**New: `AILANG_FS_SANDBOX_DEBUG=1`**  
Set alongside `AILANG_FS_SANDBOX` to log every sandbox path rejection to stderr:
```
[ailang/sandbox] REJECT exists("/home/mark/.motoko/config/default/config.json") → escapes sandbox "/tmp/task-abc" (returns false)
```
Zero overhead when unset. Covers `exists`, `isDir`, and `isFile`. (`internal/effects/fs.go`)

**New: OTEL trace events under `AILANG_TRACE=deep`**  
When deep tracing is active, sandbox rejections appear as `FS.<op>.sandbox.reject` events in the trace collector, surfacing in `ailang trace list` without any extra env vars. (`internal/effects/fs.go`)

**New: `ailang sandbox-check <path>`**  
Diagnose path resolution against the current sandbox configuration:
```
$ AILANG_FS_SANDBOX=/tmp/task ailang sandbox-check /home/mark/.motoko/config/default/config.json
sandbox:  /tmp/task
path:     /home/mark/.motoko/config/default/config.json (absolute)
result:   REJECT — escapes sandbox "/tmp/task"
          exists/isDir/isFile → false
          readFile/writeFile/etc → error
```
Exit 0 = ALLOW, exit 1 = REJECT. Scriptable for shell-level debugging. (`cmd/ailang/sandbox_check.go`)

**`ailang --help` updates**: Both `sandbox-check` and `AILANG_FS_SANDBOX_DEBUG` appear in help output. (`cmd/ailang/help.go`)

### Package auto-update bus DX fixes (M-PKG-AUTO-UPDATE-DX, target v0.18.0)

Four DX gaps surfaced during the motoko-ext-* publish session that together blocked the autonomy story (msgs 4a16ab61, 202d5eef).

**Fixed:**
- `ailang pkg notify-upgrade` now works for path-based dependencies. Previously failed with `"requires from_interface_hash"` for all packages consumed via `path = "..."` (the entire motoko-ext-* fleet). Fix: when running from inside the package directory, `pkg.InterfaceHash(manifest)` is called directly; when running from a consumer, the path dep's manifest is loaded and hashed in place. (`cmd/ailang/pkg_msg.go`)
- `ailang publish` path-dep rewrite now handles TOML-aligned whitespace. Previously `rewritePathDepsForPublish` used an exact string match, silently skipping column-padded deps and shipping tarballs with unresolvable paths. Replaced with a `regexp`-based match that tolerates any whitespace variant. (`cmd/ailang/pkg_publish.go`)
- `ailang lock` now warns when a path dep resolves to a higher version with no `upgrade-available` message in the inbox — the "silent ratchet" failure mode where bumping `[package].version` without running `ailang publish` bypasses `M-PKG-AUTONOMOUS-CASCADE-SAFE`. Warning is best-effort; exit code stays 0. (`cmd/ailang/pkg_commands.go`)

**Improved:**
- `ailang pkg` help now cross-references `ailang publish` as the preferred publish path; `notify-upgrade` is annotated as a manual fallback. (`cmd/ailang/main.go`)

### M-MOTOKO-EXECUTOR-ADAPTER: motoko_agent joins the eval harness (target v0.18.0)

Adds the **sixth CLI-subprocess executor** to AILANG's eval harness, alongside `claude` / `gemini` / `codex` / `opencode` / `pi`. motoko_agent is the first AILANG-native agent on the leaderboard — ~5,200 LOC of `.ail` modules + 9 published `motoko-ext-*` packages, wrapped in a bun TUI. Sprint design: [`design_docs/planned/v0_18_0/m-motoko-executor-adapter.md`](design_docs/planned/v0_18_0/m-motoko-executor-adapter.md). Conforms to [EXECUTOR_SHAPE.md](docs/internal/EXECUTOR_SHAPE.md) two-pillar contract.

**Pillar 1 — local executor (~900 LOC, all shipped):**
- New `internal/executor/motoko/` package: `motoko.go` (subprocess driver) + `parser.go` (schema-v1 JSONL → `executor.Result`) + 4 captured fixtures + 19 tests at **86.2% coverage**.
- `parseSessionJSONL` reads motoko's session JSONL at `${WORKDIR}/.motoko/logfile/<session_id>.jsonl`, prefers `run_summary` totals (authoritative) but falls back to summed per-step `thinking` events when crash-mid-run leaves no summary. Disambiguates the union-typed `tool_calls` field (int in `thinking`, array in `native_tool_calls`).
- `internal/executor/factory.go::Config` gains `MotokoPath` / `MotokoModel` / `MotokoProfile` with `dogfood` / `openrouter/anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5` defaults.
- `internal/coordinator/provider_executor.go`: **one blank-import line** (the EXECUTOR_SHAPE auto-discovery contract — zero dispatch/coordinator code change).
- `internal/eval_harness/models.yml`: 4 new model entries (`motoko-claude-haiku-4-5`, `motoko-claude-sonnet-4-6`, `motoko-glm-5`, `motoko-gemma-4`) all routing via OpenRouter, all in `agent_suite`. Cost-control rule per EXECUTOR_SHAPE.md §8: `env_var: "OPENROUTER_API_KEY"` (NOT `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` — pi-precedent: motoko's Anthropic models route via OpenRouter to keep pay-per-token billing exposure off the cloud Job).
- `internal/executor/motoko/README.md`: ~150 lines covering CLI flags, auth, schema-v1 event mapping, cache token telemetry, known limits, trust boundary, pinned commit (`84fa449`).
- New tests: `TestProviderResolution_Motoko` (proves blank-import auto-discovery), `TestMotokoModelsResolve` (validates models.yml entries), `TestMotokoModelsInAgentSuite` (proves agent_suite membership).

**Pillar 2 — cloud deployment (in-repo done; cross-repo queued):**
- `docker/Dockerfile.agent-motoko`: clones `sunholo-data/motoko_agent@84fa449`, installs bun + motoko-ext-* packages, symlinks `scripts/run-agent.sh` to `/usr/local/bin/motoko`. Mirrors `Dockerfile.agent-pi` structure (CLI-only, no Go toolchain).
- `internal/dispatch/cloudrun/dispatcher.go::knownVariants`: `"motoko": true` added.
- `docker/agent-motoko-multivac-prs.md`: full step-by-step for the two ailang-multivac PRs (cloudbuild + cloudbuild-images sync per EXECUTOR_SHAPE §6, and the `agent_executor_motoko` Cloud Run Job with cost-controlled secret bindings). Cross-repo PRs require ailang-multivac access — queued for the user.

**M5 — threshold measurement (queued, NOT in this commit):**
- The paired comparison `motoko-claude-haiku-4-5` vs vanilla `claude-haiku-4-5` (THE strategic measurement: does motoko's harness lift cheap models above the AILANG-correctness threshold?) requires either (a) the cloud Job from the queued multivac PRs OR (b) a local run with a real `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` budget. Once available:
  ```bash
  ailang eval-suite \
    --models motoko-claude-haiku-4-5,claude-haiku-4-5 \
    --benchmarks <agent-tier>
  ```
  Numbers will be appended to this changelog under a follow-up entry.

**Strategic payoff** (from sprint design doc): motoko on the leaderboard enables the cost-arbitrage thesis test — does cheap-model + tuned-harness ≈ frontier-model on AILANG-specific tasks? Without these adapter rows, the question stays a hypothesis. With them, it becomes a result.

### std/ai.step surfaces upstream prompt-cache telemetry (motoko-driven)

`StepResult` gains `cache_read_input_tokens` + `cache_creation_input_tokens`. Pure-additive — Anthropic populates both (from `usage.cache_read_input_tokens` + `usage.cache_creation_input_tokens` in the Messages API response); OpenAI populates `cache_read` from `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` (no separate cache-write count); Gemini populates `cache_read` from `usageMetadata.cachedContentTokenCount`; providers without prompt caching leave both at 0.

**Why**: the AILANG eval harness's planned motoko executor adapter needs cache-hit telemetry to distinguish cold-cache vs warm-cache cost profiles per benchmark step. Without these fields, "this run was cheap because the prompt cached" looks identical to "this run was cheap because the model is fast" — the wrong signal for cost-arbitrage analysis. Driven by motoko's M-MOTOKO-EVAL-INSTRUMENTATION schema-v1 which deferred cache plumbing to AILANG-side; this commit unblocks the deferred motoko M4.

**Internal refactor**: extracted `anthropicUsage` as a named struct (was an inline struct literal) so future field additions don't break test-side `Usage:` literals — caught when adding the new fields broke 2 test files. Also added `ChatStepPromptTokDetails` for OpenAI's nested `prompt_tokens_details` shape.

**Files**: `internal/ai/provider.go` (Response: +CacheReadInputTokens, +CacheCreationInputTokens; existing `CachedTokens` field deprecated to OpenRouter cost-normalization back-compat), `internal/ai/anthropic/{client,step}.go`, `internal/ai/openai/step.go`, `internal/ai/gemini/{step,types}.go`, `internal/builtins/ai_step.go`, `internal/effects/ai_step.go`, `std/ai.ail` (StepResult type), golden snapshot at `internal/pipeline/testdata/builtin_types_golden.txt` regenerated.

### serve-api: surface silently-dropped modules (msg 94b4d7fa)

When a hosted package declares `module sunholo/<pkg>/main` and the file lives directly under the basePath, the loader resolves the deeply-namespaced path against a cached/symlinked location instead of the local file, causing `registerModule` to reject it via the under-basePath filter — silently. Operators see a 0-modules server, `skills:null` on the agent card, and no diagnostic. `module_entry.go` now logs `Skipped: <file> (declared "<path>" resolves outside basePath ...)` for any rejection where the declared module path is non-empty and not stdlib/cache, so the operator immediately sees what was dropped and why.

### Extension registry generator (M-AILANG-EXT-REGISTRY-GEN, target v0.17.1)

New `ailang generate-extension-registry` command reads an `[extensions]` section from `ailang.toml` and emits a static-import AILANG dispatch file, unblocking the extensions-as-packages migration in `motoko_agent` (M-MOTOKO-EXTENSIONS-AS-PACKAGES). Because AILANG resolves all imports at compile time, runtime extension lookup requires a generated file that lists each extension package with an explicit static import — the generator is the build step that produces it.

- **`[extensions]` TOML section.** New `ExtensionRegistryConfig` struct added to `PackageManifest` (following the `[[ai_provider]]` pattern from v0.16.0). Fields: `packages` (list of `name@version` refs), `config_import` (module/path.TypeName for the config arg), `hooks_import` (module/path.TypeName for the hooks return type), `output` (generated file path; default `registry_generated.ail`), `module_name` (optional module declaration override). Validation: if any package is listed, `config_import` and `hooks_import` are both required. 5 new manifest tests.

- **`ailang generate-extension-registry` command.** New `cmd/ailang/ext_registry_gen.go` (~155 LOC). Reads `ailang.toml`, verifies every listed package is present in `ailang.lock` (errors with the missing name and a `ailang lock` hint), derives short identifiers (`motoko-ext-exa-search@0.4.1` → `exa_search`), renders a `text/template` dispatch file, and writes atomically via `.tmp` + `os.Rename`. Flags: `--config`, `--output`, `--dry-run`. Module name always derived from the TOML's `output` field (relative path), not the write location, so the generated module declaration is stable across different build roots. Wired into `main.go` dispatch and `ailang help` Packages section.

- **6 golden-file tests** (`cmd/ailang/ext_registry_gen_test.go`): `TwoPackages` (matches golden file), `DryRun` (stdout, no file written), `PackageNotLocked` (named error), `MissingConfigImport` (validation error), `EmptyPackages` (no-op), `DeriveShortName` (table-driven). All pass with `-count=3` (determinism verified).

- **Guide page:** `docs/docs/guides/extension-packages.md` — convention, TOML schema, workflow, short-name derivation, commit strategy.

- **`registry_import` option (M-MOTOKO-EXTENSION-INTEGRATION).** Optional `registry_import = "module/path.TypeName"` field in `[extensions]` block. When set, the generator additionally emits `parse_core_ext_order()` + supporting helpers (`with_id`, `parse_tokens`) using `std/string (split, trim)` — making the generated file a complete standalone registry entry point. The `resolve()` function becomes unexported. Without `registry_import`, output is unchanged (backward compatible). Enables deletion of hand-written `registry.ail` files in host projects.

## [v0.18.4] - 2026-05-08

### M-AI-PROMPT-CACHING: model-independent prompt-cache hints across all AI providers

Closes a structural ~10× input-cost gap on long-context AILANG agent loops. AILANG's `internal/ai/anthropic/step.go` already read `cache_read_input_tokens` from Anthropic responses (telemetry side wired since v0.17.0) but never set `cache_control` markers on outgoing requests — every step paid full input cost, even when the system prompt was identical across turns. Programs going through claude-code/opencode CLIs got auto-caching for free via the Anthropic SDK; programs going through `std/ai.step` did not.

**New: `std/ai.CacheBreakpoint` type + `stepWithCache(...)` function.**
Opt-in cache hints with model-independent contract — same field works across Anthropic, OpenAI, Gemini, OpenRouter, Ollama. Each provider interprets per its own caching contract:

```ailang
import std/ai (stepWithCache, Message, CacheBreakpoint)

let breakpoints = [{ position: "system", ttl: "ephemeral" }];
match stepWithCache(model, messages, tools, breakpoints) {
  Ok(r) => -- r.cache_read_input_tokens > 0 on warm turns
  Err(e) => ...
}
```

**Per-provider behavior:**

| Provider | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Anthropic (direct + Bedrock + Vertex) | Stamps `cache_control:{type:"ephemeral"}` on the system content block. Phase 1 supports `position="system"` only |
| OpenRouter `anthropic/...` | Forwards Anthropic-style cache_control (mutates system message Content into a content array) |
| OpenRouter `openai/...` / `google/...` | NO-OP + once-per-session warning |
| OpenAI direct | NO-OP (auto-caches prompts ≥1024 tokens) + once-per-session warning |
| Gemini direct | NO-OP + once-per-session warning (Context Caching API integration deferred) |
| Ollama | Silent NO-OP (local model, no caching API) |

**Back-compat preserved bit-for-bit.** `cache_breakpoints: []` (or omitted) produces wire bytes identical to today. The Anthropic `system` field switches from JSON string to JSON content-array ONLY when a `{position:"system"}` hint is present. Existing callers of the legacy `step(model, messages, tools)` signature are unaffected — `stepWithCache` is a new function, not a replacement.

**Implementation:**
- `internal/ai/provider.go` — adds `Request.CacheBreakpoints` field + `CacheBreakpoint` struct
- `internal/ai/cache_warnings.go` — `WarnOnceCacheHintIgnored(provider, reason)` with `sync.Map`-based dedup; concurrent calls produce exactly one stderr line per `(provider, reason)` pair
- `internal/ai/anthropic/cache.go` — `systemFieldFromPrompt` mapper switching string vs content-array shape
- `internal/ai/openrouter/cache.go` — model-prefix routing dispatch (`anthropic/...` → stamp; `openai/`/`google/...` → warn-and-no-op; unknown → silent)
- `internal/effects/ai_step.go` — new `aiStepWithCache` effect op + `decodeCacheBreakpoints` decoder
- `internal/builtins/ai_step.go` — new `_ai_step_with_cache` builtin (4-arg)
- `std/ai.ail` — exports `CacheBreakpoint` + `stepWithCache`
- `effects.AIHandler` interface gains `StepWithCache(...)` method (5 implementations updated, all minimal — stubs delegate to `Step`)

**Tests added:** 22 new unit tests covering: bare-string back-compat (Anthropic + OpenRouter), system-cache wire-shape correctness, empty-prompt + cache-hint corner case, missing system message no-op, model-prefix routing for all 5 categories, concurrent warning dedup (100 goroutines → 1 emission), and Request-plumbing round-trip through builtin → effect op → handler.

**Out of scope** (deferred to future sprints):
- `position="last_user"` and `position="tool_result"` — Phase 2 after telemetry confirms ROI
- Gemini's async Context Caching API — separate design (different shape than synchronous hints)
- Auto-detection of "should we cache?" based on prompt size — explicit opt-in only
- motoko_agent opt-in (cross-repo PR after this lands; design doc references the wiring point in `agent_loop_v2.ail`)

**Honest framing on motoko's 1000× input-token observation (2026-05-08):** The dominant cause of motoko's 1000× input bloat in the v0.18.3 3-harness comparison was a motoko-internal config bug (BashExec storm via `delegated_backend_not_wired`, [arniwesth/motoko_agent#225](https://github.com/arniwesth/motoko_agent/issues/225)), NOT this caching gap. Once #225 lands, this milestone closes the residual ~10× structural gap on healthy long loops.

(`internal/ai/`, `internal/effects/`, `internal/builtins/`, `std/ai.ail`, `examples/runnable/ai_caching.ail`)
