## [v0.18.3] - 2026-05-08

### M-MOTOKO-HYBRID-TOOL-CORRELATION: fix Bedrock tool_use_id rejection in motoko hybrid mode (target v0.18.3)

Surfaced during v0.18.2's parallel-4 acceptance testing — `numeric_modulo` benchmark hit:

```
Provider returned error: messages.18.content.0:
unexpected `tool_use_id` found in `tool_result` blocks: hybrid-step-8.
Each `tool_result` block must have a corresponding `tool_use` block in the previous message.
```

When motoko's hybrid-bash mode extracts a fenced ` ```bash` block from the model's prose response and synthesizes a fake `BashExec` ToolCall (id `hybrid-step-N`), the corresponding `tool_use` block was NOT being injected into the prior assistant message. The subsequent `tool_result` referenced an orphaned tool_use_id.

**Direct Anthropic API + Google Vertex are permissive** and accept this. **Amazon Bedrock is strict** and rejects with HTTP 400. Same cross-provider validation asymmetry as the v0.18.1 dotted-tool-name bug. OpenRouter routes Anthropic models among multiple backends; if it picks Bedrock for a request that involves hybrid-bash extraction, the request fails.

**Fix** (motoko commit `d767b1c`): in the hybrid-mode `Some(cmd)` branch of `agent_loop_v2.ail`, build a separate `augmented_assistant_msg` that includes the synthetic ToolCall in its `tool_calls` field, then thread that as the prior assistant message instead of the original (which had empty tool_calls because the model emitted prose, not a structured tool call). The model didn't generate this `tool_use`, but Bedrock doesn't care about authorship — only about the `tool_use_id` being correlatable.

**Verified**: `numeric_modulo` benchmark went from "Bedrock 400 reject" to `Success: true, Duration: 33.2s, Cost: $0.089`. Full smoke regression deferred — OpenRouter monthly quota was exhausted by the day's testing. ~26 LOC, ~30 minutes wall-clock vs the design doc's 2-4 hour estimate.

## [v0.18.2] - 2026-05-08

### M-MOTOKO-PARALLEL-EXECUTION-ISOLATION: lessons-learned + defensive isolation (target v0.18.2)

The first paired comparison after v0.18.1 shipped (3 harnesses × 15 smoke benchmarks × `--agent-parallel 2`) hit 5/15 motoko failures, all `dur_s=0` "no run_summary" — a different failure mode from anything v0.18.1 addressed. We hypothesized a parallel-execution race (H1 cache-write race + H4 bun-startup race), planned a worktree-based per-task isolation sprint, then **discovered both hypotheses were wrong**.

The actual root causes — found by capturing per-spawn motoko stderr to files instead of relying on the AILANG-side parser:

- **Stale bun processes from this session's debugging** holding ports 18080-18099 (motoko wrapper's `pick_free_port` range). PIDs from various interrupted earlier runs were never killed; the wrapper's `lsof -i ":$port"` probe raced against them, producing the dur=0 + EADDRINUSE pattern.
- **Stale `ailang.lock` after publishing v0.1.1 of ext packages.** When I ran `ailang publish` for `motoko-ext-{omnigraph,context-mode,exa-search}@0.1.1` earlier that day, motoko's lock still recorded the v0.1.0 sha256 — AILANG resolved against the stale lock but type-checked against the new packages on disk, producing "incompatible closed rows" on `on_build_system_prompt`'s effect signature.

**In a clean state** (stale processes killed + `ailang lock` + `ailang generate-extension-registry` to refresh), parallel-4 motoko runs now hit **27/30 (90%) over 30 runs** — statistically equivalent to serial 42/45 (93.3%). The 3 failures across the 30-run aggregate were all model-quality misses or motoko-internal bugs, not parallel-execution races.

**What v0.18.2 actually ships** (much smaller scope than originally planned):

- **`AILANG_CACHE_DIR` env override** ([commit 7cd0c743](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/commit/7cd0c743)) — `internal/pipeline/cache_store.go`'s `NewCacheStore` honors this env var. The motoko adapter sets it per-task in `${TMPDIR}/motoko-task-<sessionID>/cache`. Defensive isolation against future regressions of the same shape; not the immediate fix this sprint hunted but architecturally correct. ~120 LOC + 8 tests.
- **`HealthCheck sync.Once` dedup** ([commit b6fe1410](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/commit/b6fe1410)) — `motoko --version` now spawns once per executor lifetime instead of once per task. Halves bun startup overhead at parallel-N.
- **HealthCheck stale-process warning** ([commit b6fe1410](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/commit/b6fe1410)) — scans lsof at startup for bun processes holding ports 18080-18099, prints PID list and `pkill -9 -f 'bun.*src/tui'` remediation. Catches the "stale debugging processes" gotcha that wasted ~2 hours of investigation this sprint.
- **HealthCheck stale-lock warning** ([commit b6fe1410](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/commit/b6fe1410)) — compares motoko's `ailang.lock` mtime to sibling `ailang-packages/*/ailang.toml` mtimes; if any package toml is newer, prints `cd $MOTOKO_REPO && ailang lock && ailang generate-extension-registry` remediation. Catches the cross-repo lock-drift gotcha that wasted ~1 hour.

**Worktree-based per-task isolation: cancelled.** No architectural race exists; the symlink-management complexity wasn't justified. The cross-executor audit in the design doc (motoko vs claude/gemini/codex/opencode/pi) showed motoko WAS architecturally different (long-lived bun + embedded env-server) but those differences turned out NOT to cause races at parallel-4.

**Follow-up logged for v0.18.3:** [m-motoko-hybrid-tool-correlation.md](design_docs/planned/v0_18_3/m-motoko-hybrid-tool-correlation.md) — motoko's hybrid-bash mode generates `tool_use_id="hybrid-step-N"` for synthesized `tool_result` blocks but doesn't inject the corresponding `tool_use` into the prior assistant message. Anthropic Bedrock rejects with "tool_result block must have a corresponding tool_use block". 1 of 30 parallel-4 runs hit this; ~50 LOC fix planned for v0.18.3.

**Lessons learned** (for AILANG project institutional memory):
1. Process pollution from interactive debugging is a category of failure that confounds investigation — clean-slate testing should be the FIRST step when debugging mysterious failures, not the last.
2. Cross-repo lock-file drift is invisible until type errors appear — publishing a new package version while a downstream project's lock is unrefreshed produces cryptic AILANG errors, not "lock is stale" warnings.
3. Investigation-first didn't fully prevent the wrong-hypothesis trap. The Phase 1 lsof captures correctly recorded stale PIDs in the dying-spawn vicinity, but the natural read (H4 = bun race) ignored those PIDs as "noise from prior tests". Lesson: when instrumentation includes "noise", treat that noise as a candidate explanation, not just a filter target.

## [v0.18.1] - 2026-05-08

### M-MOTOKO-EVAL-HARNESS-HARDENING: ten gaps closed (target v0.18.1)

Patch release on top of v0.18.0's M-MOTOKO-EXECUTOR-ADAPTER. Local smoke testing on 2026-05-08 surfaced ten interconnected integration gaps that prevented clean eval-harness runs. Fixed in five coordinated phases. Touches both repos (AILANG + motoko_agent).

**Phase 1 — gap #1 (run_summary truncation, motoko `7997c8f`):** Bisected via 12 debug:checkpoint markers in `agent_loop_v2.ail` to a TS-side race: the `done` event handler called `process.exit(0)` synchronously, dropping any unflushed bytes from the JSONL WriteStream's buffer — including `run_summary`. Fix: `SessionLogger.close()` now returns `Promise<void>` resolving on stream finish; both runtime callbacks await drain before exit. Subsumes gap #10 (TS reorder audit).

**Phase 2 — gaps #6, #7, #8 (extensions visibility, --headless, --version, motoko `7d595a4` + AILANG `20f97b00`):**
- **#6 — `loaded_extensions: []` always:** Three-layer fix. (a) `src/core/config.ail` adds `resolve_profile_dir` with MOTOKO_REPO fallback. (b) TS `runtime-process.ts` forwards MOTOKO_REPO in childEnv whitelist. (c) TS `mirrorProfileFromRepo` copies `MOTOKO_REPO/.motoko/config/<profile>/` into `<workdir>/.motoko/config/<profile>/` because AILANG_FS_SANDBOX otherwise blocks reading from outside the workdir.
- **#7 — `--headless` CLI flag:** `parseMotokoFlags()` runs at top of `main()`, sets `MOTOKO_HEADLESS=1`. More discoverable than the env var.
- **#8 — `--version` mode:** Prints structured `key=value` lines (`tui_version`, `git_rev`, `ailang_built`, `motoko_repo`) and exits 0. AILANG-side `HealthCheck` calls it with a 5s timeout and stashes the metadata for telemetry + drift detection. Older motoko binaries hang on any flag; the timeout caps that worst case.

**Phase 3 — gaps #2, #5 (success fallback, repo discovery, AILANG `06c28546`):**
- **#2 — Success gated on run_summary presence:** `parseSessionJSONL` now treats `lastFinishReason="stop"` as success even when `run_summary` is missing. Sets `ProviderData["motoko_run_summary_missing"]=true` so consumers can distinguish from a clean run. Defends against future tail-truncation classes (network drop, signal kill).
- **#5 — MOTOKO_REPO discovery:** `findSessionJSONL` accepts a third arg `discoveredRepo`. When MOTOKO_REPO env is unset but HealthCheck populated `e.motokoRepo` from `motoko --version`, the search falls back there. Zero-config: operator can run smoke without any env-var setup.

**Phase 4 — gap #4 (session_id unification, motoko `1055976`):** Pre-fix, three independent IDs coexisted: (a) adapter env var, (b) TS ISO-timestamp filename, (c) AILANG `derive_session_id`. Only (a) and (c) matched. Post-fix: TS `SessionLogger` reads `MOTOKO_SESSION_ID` and uses it as the filename stem when set. Defensive `sanitizeSessionID()` strips path-traversal, collapses dots, rejects degenerate inputs with a `session_<timestamp>` sentinel. ISO timestamp remains the fallback for interactive runs.

**Phase 5 — gaps #3, #9 (cost rates source-of-truth, AILANG `a5d33677` + motoko `77e7de0`):** Pre-fix, every motoko profile had to declare cost_rates for every model, leading to `CostUSD=0` across every motoko-* model when missed. New flow: `internal/eval_harness/models.yml` (input_per_1k, output_per_1k) → `Task.Budget` → adapter converts to per-1M millicents → `MOTOKO_COST_INPUT_PER_1M_MILLICENTS` / `MOTOKO_COST_OUTPUT_PER_1M_MILLICENTS` env vars → motoko `load_cost_rates` reads env first, profile config as fallback. Single source of truth; profile cost_rates blocks remain valid for interactive use.

**Acceptance gate verification (7 of 7 conditions met):**
- ✅ run_summary present on every termination path
- ✅ Success=true on the no-tool-calls success path (truncated-success fallback)
- ✅ All 3 session_ids match (filename = MOTOKO_SESSION_ID = AILANG-side derive)
- ✅ loaded_extensions field accurate (mirrors profile dir, FS sandbox respected)
- ✅ MOTOKO_REPO discovered automatically (via `motoko --version`)
- ✅ CostUSD>0 (verified end-to-end after ailang-packages `5e2eaef` Bedrock-compat fix + motoko `af9cd68` TS childEnv passthrough)
- ✅ End-to-end smoke success: `Success=true, Cost USD=$0.000730, Finish reason=stop`

**Bedrock compat (final unblocker):** Live smoke initially failed because three extensions (omnigraph, context-mode, exa-search) advertised tool aliases with dots (`omnigraph.query`, `ctx.execute`, `exa.search`, etc — 28 aliases total). Direct Anthropic API + Vertex accept dots; OpenRouter routes to Amazon Bedrock which validates against `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,128}$` and rejects them. Fix in [ailang-packages `5e2eaef`](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang-packages/commit/5e2eaef) drops the dotted aliases from the OUTPUT (advertised) tool lists; INPUT normalizers still accept the dotted forms for back-compat. Pair with motoko `af9cd68` (forward `MOTOKO_COST_*` in TS childEnv whitelist, same gotcha as MOTOKO_REPO had).

**LOC tally:** ~80 LOC AILANG-side (parser fallback, HealthCheck enhancement, env-var emit), ~250 LOC motoko-side (TS profile mirror, --headless/--version flags, sanitizer, cost env override, AILANG resolve_profile_dir + env_int). 11 new tests across both repos (3 parser fallback, 6 session-logger sanitization, 2 budget passthrough). One new design doc captures the v0.19.0 follow-up: M-FS-SANDBOX-DIAGNOSTICS for the silent-false `fileExists` issue.

## [v0.17.0] - 2026-05-07

### External consumer DX: MOD013, effect-row provenance, error_codes.json (M-EXTERNAL-CONSUMER-DX, target v0.17.0)

Three diagnostic improvements sourced from `motoko_agent/.agent/learnings/` — the first production-scale external AILANG consumer. The local-OpenAI-endpoint item from the same learning set shipped earlier as v0.16.2.

- **MOD013: module_prefix overlap diagnostic.** When the root project and a dependency both declare the same `module_prefix` value (e.g. both use `module_prefix = "src"`), imports of root-only modules can silently cross the package boundary and resolve against the dep — producing an opaque "not exported by package" error with no pointer to the underlying ambiguity. `detectModulePrefixOverlap` (called after MOD011 in `internal/pipeline/pipeline_module.go`) fires `Error MOD013` naming both claimants, the shared prefix, and all three fix paths: (1) remove the dep from root, (2) change one side's prefix, (3) use explicit `pkg/` imports. New `MOD013` constant in `internal/errors/codes.go`. Doc page: `docs/docs/reference/errors/mod013.md`. 6 tests covering the motoko scenario, different-prefix clean, root-only clean, dep-only-no-root clean, empty-map clean, and fix-hint completeness. Source: [`motoko_agent/.agent/learnings/2026-05-03-motoko-core-package-sync.md`](https://github.com/sunholo-data/motoko_agent/blob/dev/.agent/learnings/2026-05-03-motoko-core-package-sync.md).

- **Effect-row mismatch error names the contributing call site (M2).** `Row` gains a `Provenance map[string]ast.Span` field (nil by default, excluded from `Equals` so unification is unaffected). When populated, `newEffectRowError` annotates each extra label with `(introduced at file:line:col)` and each missing label with `(slot at file:line:col)`, letting the developer immediately see which call site forced an undeclared effect and where in a type signature the effect is expected. `combineEffects` merges provenance maps when combining rows. 4 tests: `ExtraLabel_NamesCallSite`, `MissingLabel_NamesSlot`, `NoProvenance_StillWorks` (backward compat), `RowEquals_ProvenanceIgnored` (unification safety). Doc: `docs/docs/reference/errors/typ_effect_row_mismatch.md`.

### Multi-turn AI tool dispatch (M-AI-TOOL-LOOP, target v0.17.0)

Adds the multi-turn / tool-aware leg to the `std/ai` surface: agents written in pure AILANG can now drive OpenAI-style tool-use loops without dropping into Go. Five new public functions (`step`, `runTools`, `callResult`, `callJsonResult`, plus the existing `call`/`callJson` for back-compat) and five new types (`AIError`, `Message`, `ToolCall`, `ToolSchema`, `StepResult`). All Result-returning surfaces share the existing v0.15.0 `AIError {code, message, retryable}` shape — no new error type, no schema migration. Closes the last gap blocking [`motoko_agent`](https://github.com/arniwesth/motoko_agent) from retiring its hand-rolled `tool_contract.ail` + `tool_runtime.ail` (~200 LOC of agent-loop boilerplate over `_ai_call_json`). Design doc: [m-ai-tool-loop.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_17_x/m-ai-tool-loop.md). Sprint plan: [m-ai-tool-loop-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_17_x/m-ai-tool-loop-sprint-plan.md).

- **M0: Dispatch callback signature confirmation.** Read motoko_agent's `tool_contract.ail` + `tool_runtime.ail` to lock the `dispatch: (ToolCall) -> string` shape against actual external usage. AILANG's row-polymorphic callbacks (proven via `std/stream.onEvent` iface.json — surface `(T) -> U` compiles to `(T) -> U ! ε`) mean motoko_agent's `! {FS, Process}` dispatch composes with `runTools` without changing the surface signature.
- **M1: Provider interface + AIError extension.** New `internal/ai/errors.go` (~175 LOC) hosts the canonical `AIError` Go struct (mirrors `std/ai/streaming.AIError`) plus 14 code constants (8 pre-existing + 6 new: `RateLimit`, `ContextLength`, `SchemaValidation`, `ToolsNotSupported`, `ModelNotFound`, `Internal`), `IsRetryable` taxonomy, and `ClassifyHTTPError` / `ClassifyError` (HTTP-status + body disambiguation, transport-error pattern matching). Extended `internal/ai/provider.go` with `Message`/`ToolSchema`/`ToolCall` types, `Messages`/`Tools` Request fields, `ToolCalls`/`FinishReason` Response fields, and a `Step` method on the Provider interface. Stub `step.go` in all 6 implementations (anthropic/gemini/openai/openrouter/ollama/configdriven) returns `*ai.AIError` per design.
- **M2: Anthropic Step (parallel sub-agent).** Real implementation against the Messages API tool-use format. System prompt top-level (NOT in messages array); content-array for `tool_use` / `tool_result` blocks; JSON-OBJECT tool inputs (Anthropic quirk vs OpenAI string); `stop_reason → FinishReason` mapping (`end_turn → stop`, `tool_use → tool_calls`, `max_tokens → length`). 30 tests via `httptest.NewServer`, 85.6% coverage on `step.go`. Inner `error.message` hoisted into `AIError.Message` for cleaner downstream messages on 4xx/5xx.
- **M3: Gemini Step (parallel sub-agent).** Real implementation against the `generateContent` function-calling API. `system_instruction` top-level; `contents[].parts[]` with `functionCall` / `functionResponse` parts; JSON-OBJECT tool args. **Tool-call ID generation:** Gemini does NOT assign IDs natively — adapter generates deterministic `<turn_index>_<call_index>` IDs (turn_index = count of prior assistant messages) so the round-trip back to the model is stable across loop turns. `FinishReason` override rule: any `functionCall` part forces `FinishReason="tool_calls"` regardless of upstream `STOP`. 28 tests, 91.9% coverage (split into `step_test.go` + `step_edge_test.go` to stay under the 800-line file-size budget).
- **M4: OpenAI + OpenRouter Step (parallel sub-agent) + Ollama (inline).** OpenAI `step.go` (~503 LOC) is the canonical home for shared Chat Completions tool-use helpers (`BuildChatStepRequest`, `ParseChatStepResponse`, `MapChatFinishReason`, `ClassifyChatHTTPErrorFor`); OpenRouter `step.go` imports them and composes with `translatePolicy(req.Routing)` for the `provider` field plus `HTTP-Referer`/`X-Title` env-var headers — provider field spliced into body bytes (no JSON round-trip). Routing+Tools composition verified end-to-end. **OpenAI quirk:** `tool_calls[].function.arguments` is a JSON STRING (not object) — passed through verbatim. Ollama `step.go` (~115 LOC) rejects tools at boundary with `AIError{ToolsNotSupported}`; for no-tools no-Messages delegates to existing `Generate`; for no-tools multi-turn translates `Messages` to `ollamaapi.Message[]` and calls `client.Chat` directly with `[tool result]` prefix flattening for `Role="tool"` (Ollama lacks native tool-result semantics). 41 OpenAI+OpenRouter tests + 7 Ollama tests; coverage: 83.9% / 81.2% / 79.3%.
- **M5: callResult / callJsonResult / step builtins + AILANG types.** Three new builtins in `internal/builtins/ai_step.go` (~245 LOC) with full `Result[T, AIError]` type signatures. New ops in `internal/effects/ai_step.go` (~290 LOC) with AILANG-record ↔ Go-struct converters; `classifyOpError` maps `ErrNoAIHandler` to `AIError{ProviderNotFound}` (matches v0.15.1 `callStream`'s code shape). `AIHandler` interface gained `Step` reusing `ai.Message`/`ai.ToolSchema`/`ai.Response` directly (one-way `internal/effects → internal/ai` import; zero new transitive deps). `std/ai.ail` adds 5 type exports (`AIError`, `ToolCall`, `Message`, `ToolSchema`, `StepResult`) + 3 function exports. 9 effect-op tests cover happy/error/typed-pass-through/nil-handler/tool-roundtrip/schema-validation paths. Pipeline golden snapshot regenerated for the 3 new builtin signatures.
- **M6: runTools loop driver + worked example.** Pure-AILANG loop driver in `std/ai.ail` (~70 LOC including `dispatchToolCalls` helper) — calls `step` until `finish_reason != "tool_calls"` or `step_budget` hits 0. Returns the FULL final transcript on `Ok` (caller extracts last assistant text or full conversation per their needs). Effect row `! {AI}`; dispatch callback's effects propagate via row polymorphism. New [examples/runnable/ai_tool_loop.ail](examples/runnable/ai_tool_loop.ail) (~95 LOC) with 2-tool catalog (`read_doc`, `list_docs`), canned dispatch for offline determinism, demonstrates the typed-error retry-decision branch (`if e.retryable then ...`).
- **M7: Trace events + finalization.** New `TestAIStep_RecordsTraceEvent` and `TestAIStep_RecordsErrorEvent` verify the new ops emit `AI/step` events with `tool_calls:N finish:X` and `err:Code` shapes via the same `RecordAIEffect` machinery as `call`/`callJson` — so dashboards and replay get a uniform view across single-shot and tool-loop calls. motoko_agent compat-swap migration guide ships separately; the API is verified compatible with `motoko_agent/src/core/tool_runtime.ail`'s existing dispatch contract (M0 finding).
- **Parallelization wins.** M2/M3 ran concurrently as worktree-isolated Task sub-agents (~12 min each vs ~25 min sequential = ~12 min saved). M4 mixed parallel/inline (OpenAI+OpenRouter via sub-agent ~19 min while Ollama done inline in ~6 min = ~22 min vs ~30 min sequential).
- **Final numbers:** ~6,400 LOC across 8 milestones, ~6 hours wall-clock against a 7-day plan estimate. The compression came from (a) v0.15.x already having shipped AIError + streaming + provider-config + OpenRouter, so the sprint reuses rather than reinvents; (b) parallel sub-agent execution on M2/M3/M4. Sprint refresh on 2026-05-05 trimmed the original 8d / 2,950 LOC plan to 7d / 2,200 LOC; actual delivery beat the refreshed plan.

## [v0.16.4] - 2026-05-06

### Bug Fixes

- **fix(testing): inline tests resolve imported ADT constructors** (M-TESTING-ADT-IMPORT-RESOLUTION). `ailang test` no longer fails with `failed to resolve global $adt.make_Option_Some: module $adt not found` when a test body uses ADT constructors imported from another module (e.g. `Some`/`None` from `std/option`, `Ok`/`Err` from `std/result`, any custom ADT). Root cause: `CombinedResolver` handled the `$builtin` synthetic module but had no case for `$adt` — the other synthetic module name the elaborator generates for imported constructor calls. Fix: add a `$adt` case to `CombinedResolver.ResolveValue` that dispatches to a new `resolveAdtFactory` method (~30 LOC); the method parses `make_Option_Some` → `typeName="Option"`, `ctorName="Some"`, searches `r.Modules[*].Iface.Constructors` for a matching entry, and returns a `TaggedValue` (nullary, arity=0) or `ConstructorClosure` (n-ary). Mirrors what `internal/runtime/resolver.go:resolveAdtFactory` already does for production execution. Pre-existing gap since `CombinedResolver` was introduced in M-DX25 (v0.7.4); surfaced by `motoko_agent` `make test_core` (`parse_test.ail` 19/19 failures). Regression tests: `TestADTConstructorFromImportedModule` (harness path, nullary + n-ary) and `TestADTConstructorInCluster` (cluster path, imported ADT in local helper) in `internal/testing/executor_regression_test.go`. Design doc: [m-testing-adt-import-resolution.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_16_4/m-testing-adt-import-resolution.md).

## [v0.16.3] - 2026-05-06

### Bug Fixes

- **fix(testing): inline tests for local helpers using stdlib imports** (M-MOTOKO-INLINE-TEST-HARNESS M1). `ailang test` no longer fails with `cluster harness evaluation failed: cannot apply non-function value: <nil>` when a tested function calls a local helper that itself calls an imported stdlib function (e.g. `substring` from `std/string`). Root cause: `EvaluateInlineTestsWithCluster` in `internal/testing/executor.go` was using `BuiltinOnlyResolver` — which resolves only builtins, not imported module functions — and `ExtractPureClusterForFunction` did not cache `result.Modules` into `e.modules` (unlike `ExtractFunctionBinding`, which already did). Fix: (1) `ExtractPureClusterForFunction` now saves `e.modules = result.Modules` after the pipeline run so the cluster path has module data available; (2) `EvaluateInlineTestsWithCluster` now uses `CombinedResolver` + `injectModuleBindings`, matching the pattern in `EvaluateInlineTestsWithHarness`. No API surface change. Regression test: `TestClusterEvalWithImportedHelper` in `internal/testing/executor_regression_test.go`. Source: `motoko_agent` `agents_md.ail` — the `find_char` helper called `substring` from `std/string`, causing all 11 inline tests to fail with `<nil>` apply error.

- **fix(testing): aliased import collision in CombinedResolver** (M-MOTOKO-INLINE-TEST-HARNESS M2). `ailang test` no longer fails with `_list_length: expected List, got *eval.StringValue` when a module imports the same function name from two stdlib packages, one with an alias (e.g. `import std/string (length as str_len)` alongside `import std/list (length)`). Root cause: `injectModuleBindings` injected all functions into a flat env keyed by bare name — when both `std/string.length` and `std/list.length` were injected, the last-loaded module overwrote the first. When `CombinedResolver.ResolveValue` resolved a module-qualified reference `{module="std/string", name="length"}`, it called `r.Env.Get("length")` and got the list version instead. Fix: `PendingLambdaBinding` now captures `modulePath`; Pass 2 of `injectModuleBindings` sets both the bare key (`"length"`) for backward compatibility and a module-qualified key (`"std/string.length"`); `CombinedResolver` Case 2 now prefers the qualified key before falling back to bare-name lookup. Regression test: `TestAliasImportCollision` in `internal/testing/executor_regression_test.go`. Design doc: [m-motoko-inline-test-harness.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_16_3/m-motoko-inline-test-harness.md).

## [v0.16.2] - 2026-05-06

### Fixed

- **`OPENAI_API_KEY` no longer required when `OPENAI_BASE_URL` is set** (M-AI-OPENAI-LOCAL-ENDPOINT-RELAX). Both `cmd/ailang/ai_handlers.go` (`setupAIHandlerFromConfig`) and `cmd/ailang/exec.go` (`executeAPI`) previously required a non-empty `OPENAI_API_KEY` for any OpenAI-provider model — including models pointed at a local/self-hosted endpoint (vLLM, TGI, llama.cpp, LM Studio, Ollama-compat) that doesn't authenticate. The guard now fires only when both `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `OPENAI_BASE_URL` are empty; if `OPENAI_BASE_URL` is set, the empty key is passed through and `openai.NewClient` receives the custom base URL via `WithBaseURL`. Error message updated to include the fix hint: `"(or set OPENAI_BASE_URL for a custom unauthenticated endpoint)"`. 8 tests (4-case matrix per code site). Source learning: [`motoko_agent/.agent/learnings/2026-05-03-local-openai-endpoint-key-relaxation.md`](https://github.com/sunholo-data/motoko_agent/blob/dev/.agent/learnings/2026-05-03-local-openai-endpoint-key-relaxation.md) — this workaround was carried in the motoko_agent fork and lost during the v0.16.x fork-retirement; this commit brings it upstream. Design doc: [`design_docs/planned/v0_17_0/m-ai-openai-local-endpoint-relax.md`](design_docs/planned/v0_17_0/m-ai-openai-local-endpoint-relax.md).

## [v0.16.1] - 2026-05-06

Patch release with three small Vertex/Gemini fixes that had been sitting in the working tree since the M-AI-TOOL-LOOP M3 sprint (~20 hours before v0.16.0 cut). They predate but logically belong with v0.16.0; bundling as v0.16.1 keeps the Vertex story complete.

### Fixed

- **`gemini.NewVertexAIClient`: honor `GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION` env var**. The constructor was hardcoding `location: "global"`. For users whose `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` lives in a non-global region (e.g. `europe-west1`, `us-central1`), this caused `gemini-2.5-flash` to return "model not found" because the model is only registered in the global endpoint. Surfaced during the [M-MOTOKO-RPC-LOOP-FULL-MIGRATION](../motoko_agent) M9 provider matrix run — the workaround was `export GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION=global`. This release makes the env var actually take effect (falls back to `"global"` when unset, matching prior behavior).
- **Gemini safety-blocked error surfacing**. When Gemini blocks a prompt for safety reasons, the previous code returned the cryptic `"response contained no candidates"`. The error now includes the `blockReason` from `promptFeedback`: `"response contained no candidates (blocked: SAFETY)"`. Adds the `promptFeedback` struct to `stepRawResponse` for the parse path. Affects all providers using `std/ai.step` against Gemini.

### Added

- **`gemini-3-flash-preview` model alias** in `internal/eval_harness/models.yml`. The `api_name` was already `"gemini-3-flash-preview"` but the user-friendly model id was only registered as `"gemini-3-flash"`. Added an explicit alias matching the API name verbatim so `--ai gemini-3-flash-preview` works without surprise.

## [v0.16.0] - 2026-05-06

Minor release bundling two stdlib additions and one upstream-AI bug fix that landed during the M-MOTOKO-RPC-LOOP-FULL-MIGRATION end-to-end testing arc. Both stdlib additions are **purely additive** (no breaking changes to existing builtins) and unblock real-world consumers: M-AILANG-FS-RESULT lets agent runtimes catch fs failures at the dispatch boundary; M-STD-DEFLATE-ZLIB unblocks PDF FlateDecode parsing for `ailang-parse` consumers.

### Added

- **M-AILANG-FS-RESULT — Result-returning std/fs builtins.** Five new variants of the void-returning fs operations let agent runtimes recover from filesystem syscall failures (missing parent directory, permission denied, sandbox violation, disk full, file vanished mid-read) without crashing the process via the effect system's runtime panic. The legacy panicking variants (`readFile`, `writeFile`, `appendFile`, `removeFile`, `mkdirAll`) are kept untouched.
  - `readFileResult: string -> Result[string, string] ! {FS}`
  - `writeFileResult: string -> string -> Result[(), string] ! {FS}`
  - `appendFileResult: string -> string -> Result[(), string] ! {FS}`
  - `removeFileResult: string -> Result[(), string] ! {FS}`
  - `mkdirAllResult: string -> Result[(), string] ! {FS}`

  Implementation pattern mirrors the existing `fsReadFileBytes` shape: each Go handler ([`internal/effects/fs.go`](internal/effects/fs.go)) returns `(eval.Value, error)` where the inner value is `fsMakeOk(...)` or `fsMakeErr(message)`. The outer Go error is reserved for arity / type-mismatch (which the AILANG typechecker prevents at compile time). Builtins registered in [`internal/builtins/fs.go`](internal/builtins/fs.go), exports in [`std/fs.ail`](std/fs.ail). 11 paired Ok/Err Go tests in [`internal/effects/fs_result_test.go`](internal/effects/fs_result_test.go) — all pass with `-count=20` (determinism verified). Bug surfaced by: M-MOTOKO-RPC-LOOP-FULL-MIGRATION live testing — the `motoko_agent` TUI crashed when the model emitted `WriteFile` to a path whose parent dir didn't exist; legacy `writeFile` escaped through the effect system as a runtime panic. Companion motoko commit: [be10cab](https://github.com/sunholo-data/motoko_agent/commit/be10cab) migrates the dispatcher. Design doc: [m-ailang-fs-result.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_16_0/m-ailang-fs-result.md). Sprint plan: [m-ailang-fs-result-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_16_0/m-ailang-fs-result-sprint-plan.md).

- **M-STD-DEFLATE-ZLIB — Raw zlib/deflate primitives.** New [`std/deflate`](std/deflate.ail) module exposing four pure builtins (`inflate`, `inflateZlib`, `deflate`, `deflateZlib`) backed by Go's `compress/flate` and `compress/zlib`. Unblocks AILANG modules that need to decompress raw zlib streams without the gzip/zip wrappers — primary driver is PDF FlateDecode in `/ObjStm` streams (used by all PDF 1.5+ readers for compressed object tables and annotations), with HTTP `Content-Encoding: deflate`, PNG `IDAT`, and WebSocket `permessage-deflate` as adjacent use cases. Source request: GitHub issue [#223](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/issues/223) from `cli` agent (ailang-parse PDF annotation extractor). Naming: `inflate`/`deflate` are raw RFC 1951 (no header, no trailer); `inflateZlib`/`deflateZlib` are RFC 1950 wrapped (2-byte zlib header + adler32 trailer). Cross-format negative test confirms feeding zlib-wrapped data to `inflate` returns `Err` rather than silently swallowing the header. Same 100MB output cap as `std/gzip` (`io.LimitReader`-based guard prevents zip-bomb decompression). Worked example: [`examples/runnable/std_deflate_pdf_objstm.ail`](examples/runnable/std_deflate_pdf_objstm.ail) (decodes a hand-rolled FlateDecode payload representing three PDF annotation dictionaries). Final numbers: ~830 LOC across 3 milestones (~2 hours wall-clock against a 1-day plan). Design doc: [m-std-deflate-zlib.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_16_0/m-std-deflate-zlib.md). Sprint plan: [m-std-deflate-zlib-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_16_0/m-std-deflate-zlib-sprint-plan.md).

### Fixed

- **OpenAI gpt-5 / o-series `max_tokens` rejection** ([commit `daaa595b`](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/commit/daaa595b)). `internal/ai/openai/step.go` was sending `max_tokens` for GPT-5+ and o-series reasoning models (o1, o3) where these models reject the field with HTTP 400 — `"Unsupported parameter: 'max_tokens' is not supported with this model. Use 'max_completion_tokens' instead."` `chat.go` already handled the routing via `usesMaxCompletionTokens()`; `step.go` (the typed tool-use path used by `std/ai.step`) was missed and unconditionally set the legacy field. Fix routes `req.MaxTokens` to either `MaxTokens` or `MaxCompletionTokens` based on the same model classifier. Surfaced by the M-MOTOKO-RPC-LOOP-FULL-MIGRATION M9 provider matrix run — the openai cell errored on every step until the fix landed. Regression test in [`step_edge_test.go`](internal/ai/openai/step_edge_test.go) covers 5 model ids: `gpt-4o` (legacy), `gpt-5`, `gpt-5-mini`, `o1-preview`, `o3-mini`.

## [v0.15.2] - 2026-05-05

Patch release with two regression fixes surfaced by running [motoko_agent](https://github.com/arniwesth/motoko_agent) end-to-end against the upstream toolchain. Both regressions had shipped in earlier upstream releases and stayed invisible because internal AILANG usage self-selected away from the affected patterns; external-consumer evidence found them. Migration PR: [arniwesth/motoko_agent#3](https://github.com/arniwesth/motoko_agent/pull/3).

### Stdout-buffering regression fix: real-time streaming for piped consumers (M-PERF6B-PIPE-FLUSH)

Fixes another regression caught while running [motoko_agent](https://github.com/arniwesth/motoko_agent) end-to-end through its TUI: small JSON events emitted by long-running AILANG programs (`thinking_stream_start`, `thinking_delta`, `session_start`, etc.) accumulated in the M-PERF6B 64KB stdout buffer and never reached downstream consumers. The TUI showed "Input locked: task still running" with zero progress for the entire turn — the buffer flushed only at process exit, but agent loops don't exit during a turn.

Root cause: M-PERF6B (a perf optimization) wrapped `os.Stdout` in `bufio.NewWriterSize(os.Stdout, 64*1024)` unconditionally, replacing the normal Go `os.Stdout` semantics (writes pass through to the file descriptor without intermediate buffering). For batch programs that print frequently, this reduces syscall overhead. For long-running daemons piping events to a parent process, it makes them invisible until exit.

Fix: gate the buffer on `term.IsTerminal(os.Stdout.Fd())`. Terminal users still get the M-PERF6B syscall reduction; piped users get unbuffered, line-by-line delivery — the standard Unix convention (`stdbuf -oL` semantics, matching `tee`, `journalctl`, `bun spawn` etc.). Files: [`cmd/ailang/main_run.go`](cmd/ailang/main_run.go) (~10 LOC: import `golang.org/x/term`, add `isStdoutTTY()`, wrap the buffer assignment in a TTY check). Regression test [`TestRunCommand_PipedStdoutFlushesPerLine`](cmd/ailang/main_run_pipe_test.go) (~150 LOC) runs the locally-installed `ailang` binary as a subprocess emitting 3 events with `sleep(500)` between each, asserts the events arrive ~500ms apart (not all batched at exit). The load-bearing assertion is the gap between EVENT_1 and EVENT_2 ≥ 200ms — if the buffer is broken, both arrive within milliseconds of each other near process exit. Verified actual timings: 101ms / 602ms / 1103ms — clean per-line streaming.

This is exactly the kind of regression the new [Conflict Surface design-doc gate](.claude/skills/design-doc-creator/resources/design_doc_structure.md) is meant to catch: M-PERF6B's design assumed batch-style programs ("how do I reduce syscall overhead from frequent println?") without enumerating that long-running event-streaming programs use the same `os.Stdout` and have the opposite latency requirement. AILANG's internal test corpus was self-selected toward batch programs; an external consumer (motoko_agent's TUI subprocess) hit it. Same pattern as [M-PARSER-REFINEMENT-LOOKAHEAD](#parser-regression-fix-refinement-vs-function-body-disambiguation-m-parser-refinement-lookahead) earlier in this changelog — the reusable lesson is "look at what *else* uses this surface before changing its semantics."

### Parser regression fix: refinement vs function-body disambiguation (M-PARSER-REFINEMENT-LOOKAHEAD)

Fixes a parser regression introduced by [M-TAINT-TYPES (commit `9e9db626`, v0.14.3)](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/commit/9e9db626) where `func name(...) -> bool { not <call>(args) }` mis-parsed as a malformed refinement type and emitted `PAR_REFINE_MVP`. Caught while migrating [motoko_agent](https://github.com/arniwesth/motoko_agent) off its AILANG fork — the fork is based on v0.13.0 (predates M-TAINT-TYPES) so the syntax was idiomatic in motoko_agent's bool-returning helpers (`is_extension_tool_call`, `is_native_tool_name`, etc., ~14 sites across `rpc.ail`, `tool_runtime.ail`, `parse.ail`, `agents_md.ail`, `compress.ail`, `config.ail`).

Root cause: M-TAINT-TYPES added `T{not LABEL}` refinement-type syntax. The original parser used 2-token lookahead (`peek=LBRACE && peek2=NOT`) to enter the refinement path, but a function body opening `{ not <call>(...)` is structurally identical at that depth. The refinement parser committed prematurely and complained about the trailing `(` not being a `}`.

Fix: extend parser lookahead to **4 tokens** (`peek3` + `peek4`) and tighten the entry guard to require the exact `{not IDENT}` shape — `peek=LBRACE && peek2=NOT && peek3=IDENT && peek4=RBRACE`. Function bodies and other constructs cleanly defer to their respective parsers. Files: [`internal/parser/parser.go`](internal/parser/parser.go) (struct + `nextToken` + helpers, ~15 LOC), [`internal/parser/parser_type.go`](internal/parser/parser_type.go) (entry guard, ~10 LOC). Regression test [`TestRefinementVsFunctionBodyDisambiguation`](internal/parser/parser_types_label_test.go) pins the exact `motoko_agent::is_extension_tool_call` shape plus three sibling cases. The well-formed `T{not LABEL}` syntax continues to parse via [`TestRefinementSyntaxParam`](internal/parser/parser_types_label_test.go) and a new belt-and-braces `TestRefinementStillParsesAfterDisambiguation`. The previously-tested malformed `{not a && not b}` conjunction case still produces a parse error — just a different one (the parser declines to enter refinement and the surrounding parameter-list parser catches the malformed input instead).

Verified: motoko_agent's 5 affected files (`rpc.ail`, `tool_runtime.ail`, `parse.ail`, `agents_md.ail`, `compress.ail`, `config.ail`) all type-check cleanly post-fix. The remaining type-check error in `rpc.ail` is a separate concern (`_io_poll_stdin` builtin existed only in the fork).

## [v0.15.1] - 2026-05-05

### Cost-and-speed budgets at the Executor interface (M-EVAL-COST-AND-SPEED-BUDGETS, target v0.15.1)

Replaces wall-clock-as-cost-proxy with explicit cost budgets at the shared `executor.Executor` interface, so all 5 harnesses (claude/gemini/codex/opencode/pi) enforce uniform cost-vs-time semantics. Pre-v0.15.1 the harness used 60-180s wall-clock timeouts as a *proxy* for cost control — this systematically excluded cheap-but-slow OS models (DeepSeek V4 Flash, Kimi K2.6, GLM 4.7 Flash) even though they cost fractions of a cent per benchmark. Meanwhile expensive models (`opencode-sonnet-4-6` at $13.38 across 68 v0.15.0 runs) weren't effectively constrained by wall-clock anyway. **Cost is the right dimension; v0.15.1 makes it explicit.** Design doc: [m-eval-cost-and-speed-budgets.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_15_1/m-eval-cost-and-speed-budgets.md). Sprint plan: [m-eval-cost-and-speed-budgets-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_15_1/m-eval-cost-and-speed-budgets-sprint-plan.md).

- **M1: Schema + cost helper.** New [internal/executor/cost.go](internal/executor/cost.go) (~115 LOC + ~200 LOC tests) — `CostBudget` struct with `atomic.Int64` token counters and `atomic.Uint64` killedAt (bit-cast float64 for first-write-wins via CompareAndSwap). All 4 public methods (`NewCostBudget`, `Add`, `Current`, `KilledAt`) at 100% coverage; race-clean under N=50×100 concurrent goroutines. New `Budgets` struct in [internal/eval_harness/models.go](internal/eval_harness/models.go) with `ResolvedMaxCostUSD()` (default formula: `min($0.50, input × 64K + output × 32K)`) and `ResolvedHardTimeoutSecs()` (default 600s, was 60-180s). Real models.yml back-compat verified by `TestRealModelsYmlBackCompat`.
- **M2 prep: Task/Result schema.** Added `Task.Budget *CostBudget` (nullable — nil preserves byte-identical legacy behaviour) and `Result.{CostKilledAt, FirstAttemptMs, SuccessAtMs, TokensPerSec}` to the [internal/executor/executor.go](internal/executor/executor.go) interface. Zero values mean "not measured" — additive schema only.
- **M2: Wire 5 executors.** Per-executor token-tally hooks landed via Task sub-agent (~150 LOC across claude/opencode/gemini/codex/pi). Hook events: claude=`message_delta` cumulative→delta + result reconcile; opencode=`step_finish` (naturally incremental); gemini=`result` event post-hoc (no incremental usage from Gemini CLI — fallback estimator deferred per Deferred Decisions); codex=`turn.completed`/message+result cumulative→delta; pi=`message_end` per-turn delta. Speed timestamps (`FirstAttemptMs`, `TokensPerSec`) populated; `SuccessAtMs` left at -1 (eval harness handles success detection). All 5 executors race-clean.
- **M3: Result schema + JSON exporter.** New [internal/eval_analysis/efficiency.go](internal/eval_analysis/efficiency.go) (~150 LOC + ~200 LOC tests) emits per-model `efficiency` block in `dashboard/latest.json`: median TTFA / TTS / turns / tokens-per-sec, p90 cost-per-success, dimensionless `speed_efficiency_score = success_rate / (1 + median_TTS_seconds / 60)`, and `cost_killed_count`. `cost_killed` is a distinct error category in the Top Error Codes table. `BenchmarkResult` schema is additive only (`json:omitempty`) — pre-v0.15.1 baselines (v0.14.x, v0.15.0) replay with identical aggregates when re-exported. TTS falls back to `DurationMs` when `SuccessAtMs <= 0`.
- **M4: Dashboard charts.** Two new components plus one augmentation, ~400 LOC React via Task sub-agent. New `SpeedRadar.jsx` — outlier-clipped median time-to-success radar (mirrors v0.15.0 cost-radar pattern). New `CostSpeedFrontier.jsx` — Pareto scatter (x=cost/success log-scale, y=sec/success log-scale, marker=success rate, color=harness) using `recharts.ComposedChart` so a Line series can draw the dashed frontier connector. `PerModelTrend.jsx` augmented with metric dropdown: Success Rate / Time to Success / Cost per Success — graceful latest-snapshot fallback when historic data lacks the new metrics (resolves once M5 reruns the suite). All 7 dashboard formatters use the unified `formatModelName` from v0.15.0 hotfix.
- **M5: Re-test 4 OS near-misses — 1 PROMOTED.** Added `budgets:` blocks (`max_cost_usd: 0.30`, `hard_timeout_secs: 600`) to `opencode-or-deepseek-v4-flash`, `opencode-or-kimi-k2-6`, `opencode-or-glm-4-7-flash`, `opencode-or-gemma-4-26b` in `models.yml`. Wired [internal/eval_harness/agent_runner_multi.go](internal/eval_harness/agent_runner_multi.go) to populate `Task.Budget` from `cfg.ResolvedMaxCostUSD()` and override CLI `--agent-timeout` with `budgets:hard_timeout_secs` (per-benchmark `spec.Timeout` still wins). **Smoke outcome 2026-05-05**: `opencode-or-glm-4-7-flash` promoted from 2/3 NEAR-MISS to **3/3 PASS** — csv_to_json took 131s, would have failed at the old 60s ceiling. Added to `agent_suite`. DeepSeek V4 Flash + Kimi K2.6 stay 2/3 (csv_to_json `api_error` is upstream OpenRouter route issue, not capability). Gemma 4 26B regressed to 1/3 (single-run variance — stays on watchlist). **The primary sprint success metric — at least 1 OS near-miss promotes to PASS — is met.** This validates the core thesis: timeout-as-cost-proxy was systematically excluding capable-but-slow OS models.
- **M6: Docs + retro.** New [docs/docs/guides/evaluation/cost-and-speed-budgets.md](docs/docs/guides/evaluation/cost-and-speed-budgets.md) (~150 LOC) covers conceptual overview, per-executor hook events, `models.yml` `budgets:` reference, Result schema reference, migration guide for legacy baselines. CHANGELOG entry references this milestone with M5 outcome summary.
- **Axiom net: +10** (A9 Cost Visibility +2, A10 Composability +2, A12 System Boundary +2, A11 Structured Failure +1, A2 Replayability +1, A3/A4/A6/A7 +0/+1, no hard violations). This milestone IS a capability budget for cost — direct A9 alignment.
- **Deferred to follow-up sprints.** Per-tier cost ceilings (smoke=$0.10, core=$0.50, stretch=$1.00) — possible follow-up if uniform $0.50 proves wrong shape; cost-aware routing (route to cheapest provider mid-flight) — needs M-AI-OPENROUTER M5+ work; live budget alerting via macOS notify daemon — separate milestone; per-org/per-day cumulative budgets — needs persistence layer; AILANG-side `!{AI[budget=$X]}` parameterised effect — separate v1.0.0 axis (M-EFFECT-REFINEMENT Phase 6+).

### AI streaming accumulator helper (M-AI-CALL-STREAM-HELPER, target v0.15.1)

Closes the streaming-boilerplate gap that surfaced in the [motoko_agent v0.15.0 migration plan](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/blob/dev/design_docs/planned/motoko-agent-v0.15.0-migration.md): consumers wanting only the accumulated final string from an SSE LLM call had to assemble ~80–120 LOC of `onEvent` + `runEventLoop` + `StringBuilder` + delta extraction in user code, even though every consumer wrote substantially the same loop. Ships a thin synchronous wrapper `callStream(provider, model, messagesJson) -> Result[string, AIError] ! {AI, Stream, Net}` that opens the stream, drives the event loop in Go, accumulates content deltas via the provider's existing `[ai_provider.streaming]` JSONPath config, and flattens `StreamErrorKind` variants into a structured `AIError` record. Pure layering work over v0.15.0 primitives — no new effect, no new schema, no new transport. Single new builtin (`_ai_call_stream`) follows the existing `_ai_call_*` family pattern. Design doc: [m-ai-call-stream-helper.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_15_1/m-ai-call-stream-helper.md). Sprint plan: [m-ai-call-stream-helper-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_15_1/m-ai-call-stream-helper-sprint-plan.md).

- **M1: Go-side `aiCallStream` op + `_ai_call_stream` builtin + tests.** New `aiCallStream` op section in [cmd/ailang/configdriven_streaming.go](cmd/ailang/configdriven_streaming.go) (~280 LOC) registers via `RegisterOp("AI", "callStream", aiCallStream)` alongside the existing `streamCall` op. The accumulator opens the connection via `effects.Call(ctx, "AI", "streamCall", args)` (so the underlying primitive's trace span fires exactly once and the AI cap budget tracks normally), drains events through a new `StreamConnection.DrainEventsToFunc(cb)` method (~50 LOC) added to [internal/effects/stream.go](internal/effects/stream.go), and accumulates content deltas via an inline minimal JSONPath walker mirroring `internal/ai/configdriven/jsonpath.go`. Termination handles all three taxonomies: `[DONE]` sentinel (OpenAI), `message_stop` event type (Anthropic), and Closed events (clean upstream finish). `wrapErrAsAIError` parses the `[<code>]` prefix from `ProtocolError` messages so `ProviderNotFound` / `CapabilityNotSupported` codes survive the StreamErrorKind→AIError flattening. Reasoning fields (`reasoning_content`, `thinking`) are READ but DISCARDED in v0.15.1 — only visible content accumulates. New `_ai_call_stream` builtin in [internal/builtins/ai.go](internal/builtins/ai.go) (~95 LOC) returns `Result[string, AIError] ! {AI, Stream, Net}`. 8 integration tests in [cmd/ailang/configdriven_callstream_test.go](cmd/ailang/configdriven_callstream_test.go) cover OpenAI happy path, Anthropic-shape termination via `message_stop`, provider-not-found, streaming-not-enabled, reasoning-discard verification, malformed-JSON / path-miss skipping, the no-double-`callStream`-span snapshot, and `[DONE]` clean termination.
- **M2: `callStream` AILANG wrapper + recipe page restructure + cross-references.** New `callStream` export in [std/ai/streaming.ail](std/ai/streaming.ail) (~25 LOC + docstring; module grows from 149 → ~180 LOC, under the 200 LOC v0.15.1 soft cap raised by the design doc). [internal/pipeline/testdata/builtin_types.golden](internal/pipeline/testdata/builtin_types.golden) regenerated to include the new builtin signature. Recipe page [docs/docs/recipes/ai-token-streaming.md](docs/docs/recipes/ai-token-streaming.md) restructured: "Quick start: callStream" leads with the zero-boilerplate path; the manual event-loop pattern moves to "Advanced: token-by-token control flow" for chat-UI / per-delta use cases. "When to use which" decision matrix now lists `callStream` first as the most-common case. [docs/docs/guides/custom-ai-providers.md](docs/docs/guides/custom-ai-providers.md) gains one-line cross-references to `callStream` in both the limitations table and the capabilities table. Migration estimate in [motoko-agent-v0.15.0-migration.md](design_docs/planned/motoko-agent-v0.15.0-migration.md) revised down from 4–5 hours to ~2 hours.
- **Trace span semantics**: exactly ONE `AI/streamCall` span per `_ai_call_stream` invocation. The accumulator deliberately does NOT call `RecordAIEffect` — it relies on the underlying `streamCall` op's existing span. Verified by snapshot test asserting no event with `OpName="callStream"` is emitted.
- **Total LOC**: ~120 implementation + ~25 AILANG + ~330 tests + ~80 docs = ~555 LOC; ~5 hours wall-clock against the design-doc's 4–6 hour estimate.

## [v0.15.0] - 2026-05-04

### AI token streaming helper (M-AI-STREAMING-HELPER, target v0.15.0)

Closes the AI streaming discovery + ergonomics gap surfaced by the [arniwesth/ailang motoko fork](https://github.com/arniwesth/ailang/tree/motoko), which built ~1,500 LOC of Go-side streaming infrastructure (`StreamingProvider` interface, OpenAI SSE client, `AI.callStreamResult` effect op, dedicated builtin) when upstream `std/stream` already provided generic SSE-via-POST primitives — but the AI-domain agent searches never surfaced them. This milestone ships a thin AILANG module at `std/ai/streaming.ail` that composes the registered `[[ai_provider]]` config (M-AI-PROVIDER-CONFIG) with the existing `_stream_sse_post` builtin into a typed AI-streaming surface. Effect signature `! {AI, Stream, Net}` preserves AI cap gating + budget tracking (D11/D3) — no Net-bypass shortcuts. Pulled forward from v0.17.0 to v0.15.0 because all prerequisites had shipped in parallel threads. Design doc: [m-ai-streaming-helper.md](design_docs/planned/v0_17_0/m-ai-streaming-helper.md). Sprint plan: [m-ai-streaming-helper-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/planned/v0_17_0/m-ai-streaming-helper-sprint-plan.md).

- **M1: Go bridge + new `_ai_stream_call` builtin + tests.** New [internal/ai/configdriven/streaming.go](internal/ai/configdriven/streaming.go) exposes `BuildStreamRequest(spec, model, messagesJSON) -> StreamRequest{URL, Body, Headers}` reusing `buildRequestBody` from `shapes.go` and a new `buildStreamHeaders` mirroring `applyAuth` but emitting a header list. New [cmd/ailang/configdriven_streaming.go](cmd/ailang/configdriven_streaming.go) registers the `AI.streamCall` effect op (placed in cmd/ailang to break a cycle: configdriven→telemetry→effects→configdriven). New `_ai_stream_call(provider, model, messages_json)` builtin in [internal/builtins/ai.go](internal/builtins/ai.go) returns `Result[StreamConn, StreamErrorKind] ! {AI, Stream, Net}`. The builtin dispatches via `effects.Call(ctx, "AI", "streamCall", args)` — same pattern as all other AI ops. Single new builtin; no new effect; preserves the design doc's hard constraint. New `Spec()` accessor on `configdriven.Provider` exposes the registered `AIProviderSpec` for the streaming dispatch path. 12 tests in [cmd/ailang/configdriven_streaming_test.go](cmd/ailang/configdriven_streaming_test.go) cover the full happy path (OpenAI + Anthropic shapes), provider-not-found, streaming-not-enabled, missing Stream cap, env-var-unset, invalid messages JSON, models allow-list, and `BuildStreamRequest` unit cases including the `query-param`-auth limitation.
- **M2: `std/ai/streaming.ail` module + cross-links.** New stdlib module at [std/ai/streaming.ail](std/ai/streaming.ail) (uses nested module path; first nested stdlib module — the resolver at `internal/loader/stdlib_resolver.go:181` already supports it). Exports `openaiCompatStream(provider, model, messagesJson)`, `anthropicStream(provider, model, messagesJson)`, plus reserved `TokenDelta` and `AIError` record types. Caller passes a registered provider name + model + pre-serialised messages JSON — never a URL or API key. Cross-link docstrings landed in [std/ai.ail](std/ai.ail) ("## Token streaming") and [std/stream.ail](std/stream.ail) ("## See also") so agent searches starting from either domain surface the right entry point. Verified type-checks via synthetic-import test.
- **M3: docs + runnable example + design-doc reconciliation.** New runnable example [examples/runnable/ai_stream_openai.ail](examples/runnable/ai_stream_openai.ail) (~70 LOC) with full setup instructions for any OpenAI-compatible provider; type-checks cleanly. New recipe page [docs/docs/recipes/ai-token-streaming.md](docs/docs/recipes/ai-token-streaming.md) (~250 LOC) covers OpenAI, OpenRouter, Anthropic recipes plus reasoning-model handling (`reasoning_content` / `thinking` fields) and an explicit dispatch decision tree. Design doc amended with the architectural-deviation note (one new builtin; cycle-resolution placement; v1 simplifications). Master sequence doc [motoko-integration-sequence.md](design_docs/planned/motoko-integration-sequence.md) status board flipped to ✅.
- **v1 simplifications (intentionally deferred).** `messagesJson` is a pre-serialised string rather than a typed `[Message]` list (deferred to v1.1 once `std/json` gains record-of-records encoding sugar). `parseDelta` typed extraction is deferred to v1.1 — recipe page documents shape-specific manual extraction for v1. `query-param` auth not supported for streaming (no streaming-capable LLM provider uses query-param auth in practice; bearer / x-api-key / `auth_headers` cover the universe). Built-in providers (`openai`/`anthropic`/`gemini`/`ollama`/`openrouter`) not routable through the helper in v1 — they have their own streaming code paths in future milestones.
- **Total LOC**: ~700 net new (Go bridge + builtin + AILANG module + 12 tests + recipe + example), under the planned 870 budget. ~5.5 hours wall-clock against ~6 hour sprint estimate.

### Config-driven AI providers via package manifests (M-AI-PROVIDER-CONFIG, target v0.15.0)

Closes the AI extension surface: packages can register new AI providers via `[[ai_provider]]` blocks in `ailang.toml` — no Go code, no binary fork. Until v0.15.0 the five built-in providers (`openai`, `anthropic`, `gemini`, `ollama`, `openrouter`) were the entire universe; adding a sixth required editing the hardcoded if/else chain in `cmd/ailang/{exec,ai_handlers}.go` and submitting an upstream PR. This was the dynamic that produced the [arniwesth/ailang motoko fork](https://github.com/arniwesth/ailang/tree/motoko) (~1,500 LOC of Go to add OpenRouter routing + custom OpenAI base-URL — most of it now obsolete with M-AI-OPENROUTER landing upstream and this milestone opening the registration surface). Config-driven providers integrate fully with the AI effect: budget tracked, `--caps AI` gated, trace spans emitted with the same shape as built-ins. Architectural decisions (D1: reject pure-AILANG-via-`std/net` because it would bypass AI cap; D2: reject Go plugin / WASM as v1 mechanism; D4: built-ins stay built-in; D11: config-driven providers reject `AIRoutingPolicy`; D12: maps onto `AI[mode=fixed]` once the AI port of M-EFFECT-REFINEMENT lands) are captured in [motoko-integration-sequence.md](design_docs/planned/motoko-integration-sequence.md). User guide: [docs/docs/guides/custom-ai-providers.md](docs/docs/guides/custom-ai-providers.md). Reference example: [examples/configdriven_provider_demo/](examples/configdriven_provider_demo/). Design doc: [m-ai-provider-config.md](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-ai-provider-config.md).

- **M1: schema + per-manifest validation.** New [internal/pkg/ai_provider.go](internal/pkg/ai_provider.go) (~270 LOC) defines `AIProviderSpec` and the v1 catalogues — three request shapes (`openai_chat`, `anthropic_messages`, `simple_completion`) plus `request_template` escape; four auth shapes (`bearer`, `x-api-key`, `query-param`, `none`) plus `auth_headers` escape with `${ENV_VAR}` interpolation restricted to `[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*`; per-token / per-call / both cost declarations; capability flags (`tool_calling`, `json_mode`, `streaming`, `vision`, `structured_outputs`) whose TOML keys match the wire identifiers in `internal/ai/routing.go AICapability` so registration-side declarations and request-side `AIRoutingPolicy.Require` share one vocabulary. Streaming sub-block reserved for M-AI-STREAMING-HELPER (v0.17.0). 31 tests with golden TOML fixtures cover required-field omission, schema-version drift, name conflicts within a single manifest, malformed env-interpolation patterns, ISO 4217 currency-code shape, and capability-flag round-trip.
- **M2: generic provider implementation.** New `internal/ai/configdriven/` package (~700 LOC + ~450 LOC tests). `Provider` implements `ai.Provider` (`Generate`, `Name`); routes the request through `buildRequestBody` (shape templates), `applyAuth` (auth shape application + `${ENV_VAR}` interpolation), `extractPath` (minimal internal JSONPath subset — no external dep), and emits trace spans identical in attribute schema to built-in providers (`ai.provider`, `ai.model`, `ai.tokens_in`, etc., plus `ai.provider_kind = "config_driven"` for filtering). Cost calculation supports per-token, per-call, both, or none (empty string lets downstream consumers distinguish "no cost data" from "free"). Routing-policy rejection (D11) and capability gating (image-without-vision, JSON-mode-without-capability) fail fast before any HTTP. Models allow-list optionally constrains which model identifiers route to a given provider. Endpoint redaction strips secrets from trace attributes when query-param auth is used. 33 integration tests against `httptest.Server` cover all three request shapes, all four auth shapes, success / 401 auth-fail / 5xx-retryable / malformed-JSON / response-path-miss / error-path-fallback paths.
- **M3: registry + dispatch wiring.** New [internal/ai/registry.go](internal/ai/registry.go) (~150 LOC) exposes `ProviderRegistry` with `Register`/`Lookup`/`Names`/`SourceOf`/`Reset`/`Diagnostics` and `IsBuiltinName` helper. Concurrency-safe via `sync.RWMutex`; cross-package duplicate-name conflicts produce a structured error naming both source manifests so users can resolve. Built-in name shadowing produces a stderr warning, not a hard error — built-ins win at dispatch (D4). New [cmd/ailang/configdriven_init.go](cmd/ailang/configdriven_init.go) (~120 LOC) provides `RegisterConfigDrivenProviders` (harvest from a list of manifests with cross-manifest conflict detection) and `HarvestAndRegisterFromDir` (load `ailang.toml` + walk the lock file's deps). Source-based idempotency in `Register` makes repeated `setupAIHandler` calls safe. Dispatch wiring in [cmd/ailang/exec.go](cmd/ailang/exec.go) and [cmd/ailang/ai_handlers.go](cmd/ailang/ai_handlers.go) consults the registry after the built-in switch falls through; refactored `setupAIHandlerFromConfig` extracted from `setupAIHandler` for testability. The direct path handles `<provider>/<model>` prefix matching for users invoking without a `models.yml` entry. 25 tests across the registry, harvest, and dispatch layers including end-to-end `httptest.Server` integration verifying `setupAIHandler → registry → generic provider → mock HTTP → response text` works through the full effect-handler chain.
- **M4: docs, reference example, startup wiring.** Harvest invoked from `setupAIHandler` so `ailang run --caps AI --ai my-llm ...` automatically picks up `[[ai_provider]]` blocks from the project's `ailang.toml` plus its dependency manifests. New [examples/configdriven_provider_demo/](examples/configdriven_provider_demo/) with `ailang.toml`, `main.ail`, and README — runnable demonstration of two providers (vLLM-style local + OpenRouter-via-config) registered without Go. New user-facing guide [docs/docs/guides/custom-ai-providers.md](docs/docs/guides/custom-ai-providers.md) with three real-world recipes (vLLM, llama.cpp, Anthropic-via-config), full schema reference, and an explicit limitations table. 6 integration tests cover ancestor-manifest lookup, malformed-manifest harmlessness, idempotent re-harvest, and full end-to-end dispatch through registry → provider → mock HTTP.
- **Total tests passing**: 31 (M1) + 33 (M2) + 25 (M3) + 6 (M4) = **95 tests** across `internal/pkg/`, `internal/ai/`, `internal/ai/configdriven/`, `cmd/ailang/`. Race detector clean.
- **Deferred to follow-up sprints (intentionally).** Custom auth flows (AWS SigV4, Azure AD, OAuth) — stay Go-side or escape via `auth_headers` for static tokens; non-HTTP transports (gRPC, persistent WebSocket) — Go-side; `request_shape = "custom"` with `request_template` Go templates — schema reserved, runtime support deferred to schema v2; tool-use templating, image-input templating, batch endpoint templating — schema v2 work; provider-plugin marketplace / `ailang search --tag ai-provider` — separate v0.18+ design once the format stabilises; cost-aware routing (multiple providers serving the same model, route to cheapest) — depends on cost declarations being broadly populated. Streaming for config-driven providers ships in this same v0.15.0 release alongside [M-AI-STREAMING-HELPER](design_docs/planned/v0_17_0/m-ai-streaming-helper.md).

### Parameterised effect syntax (M-EFFECT-REFINEMENT Phase 1, target v0.15.0)

Lays the language-feature scaffolding for parameterised effects `!{E[k=v, ...]}` — the foundation for the full M-EFFECT-REFINEMENT taxonomy planned for v1.0.0, where Rand/Clock/Net/FS/AI all gain `[mode=...]` and `[scope=...]` parameters in follow-up sprints. Phase 1 ships parser, AST, row algebra, and invariant unification with one pilot effect (`Rand → mode=os`). Existing programs that use bare `!{Rand}` are byte-identical pre-/post-sprint via the default-mode desugar; 332/332 example `.ail` files produce identical typecheck output. The runtime is unchanged in Phase 1 — mode-aware dispatch lands in Phase 3 of the parent doc. Phase-1 design doc: [m-effect-refinement-phase1.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_15_x/m-effect-refinement-phase1.md). Sprint plan: [m-effect-refinement-phase1-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_15_x/m-effect-refinement-phase1-sprint-plan.md). Parent design (canonical, 8 phases): [m-effect-refinement.md](design_docs/planned/v1_0_0/m-effect-refinement.md). User guide: [docs/docs/guides/parameterised-effects.md](docs/docs/guides/parameterised-effects.md).

- **M1: parser + AST.** Commit `a90ada21`, ~573 LOC. Lexer disambiguation kept the existing `LBRACKET` token (parser-level context check is sufficient — effect rows are already a known parser context). New `EffectParam{Key, Value}` AST node and `EffectAnnotation.Params []EffectParam` field. Pretty-printer emits parameters alphabetically by key (deterministic for golden files; `-count=20` guard against map-iteration nondeterminism). 22 new tests covering positive forms, malformed inputs, and parser→pretty-printer→parser round-trip identity. Six structured parse errors with stable codes `PAR_EFF010`–`PAR_EFF014` covering empty `[]`, missing key/value, missing `=`, wrong separator (`:` instead of `=`), missing comma, and duplicate key.
- **M2: row algebra + invariant unification.** Commit `320b2a6c`, ~565 LOC. `Row.Params map[string]map[string]string` extends the effect row representation. `defaultEffectModes` table in `internal/types/effects.go` ships the single Phase 1 entry: `Rand → ("mode", "os")`. `DefaultModeFor(name)` exposes it; `effectiveParamsOf` is the back-compat bridge that normalises during comparison — rows constructed by `validate_effects.go::stringSliceToEffectRow` have nil `Params`, while elaborator-built rows have desugared `Params`, and the bridge fills the gap so both sources unify cleanly. Unification is invariant on parameter values: `!{Rand[mode=os]}` and `!{Rand[mode=seeded]}` are distinct rows; bare `!{Rand}` unifies with `!{Rand[mode=os]}` via the default-desugar match; row-tail polymorphism and row swap continue to work unchanged. JSON round-trip uses `omitempty` on `Params` so older iface caches continue to load. 12 test functions / ~30 sub-cases run with `-count=20`. Zero-diff back-compat verified: 332/332 example `.ail` files byte-identical pre-/post-sprint.
- **M3: worked example.** Commit `72859118`, ~58 LOC. New [examples/modal_rand.ail](examples/modal_rand.ail) demonstrating bare `!{Rand}`, explicit `!{Rand[mode=os]}`, and `!{Rand[mode=seeded]}` side by side; runs end-to-end under `ailang run --caps Rand,IO`. The CryptoRand alias zero-diff regression originally scoped for M3 was **skipped** because `CryptoRand` does not exist in this codebase — the parent doc reference was aspirational. Stdlib `std/rand` is the only Rand path today; M-CRYPTORAND landed as a separate effect token rather than an alias, so there are no callers of `type CryptoRand = Rand[mode=crypto]` to validate against. The default-mode desugar still gives the back-compat property the alias check would have demonstrated.
- **M4: docs + release.** ~310 LOC docs only (no Go changes). New user-facing guide [docs/docs/guides/parameterised-effects.md](docs/docs/guides/parameterised-effects.md) covering syntax, default-mode table, closed-mode-set rationale, unification matrix, trace/replay status, and the forward pointer to the parent doc's eight phases. Registered in `docs/sidebars.js` under Reference > Language. Teaching prompt sections added to [prompts/v0.16.0.md](prompts/v0.16.0.md) and [cmd/ailang/prompts/v0.16.0.md](cmd/ailang/prompts/v0.16.0.md). Design doc + sprint plan moved from `design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/` to `design_docs/implemented/v0_15_x/` with status updated to Implemented and an implementation report appended.
- **Deferred to follow-up sprints (intentionally).** Replay contract registry / mode-aware runtime dispatch (Phase 3 of parent doc). Capability scoping `scope=...` (Phase 4). Clock / Net / FS / AI ports — each adds one row to `defaultEffectModes` and ports its stdlib (Phase 5). The AI port specifically subsumes [M-AI-OPENROUTER](design_docs/implemented/v0_16_0/m-ai-openrouter-provider.md)'s runtime `--allow-routing` gate as a type-level marker `!{AI[mode=routeable]}`. M-ENTROPY mode constraints (Phase 6). Open / user-extensible mode sets (v1.0+ research). Total Phase 1 LOC across the four milestones: ~1,506 (M1: 573 + M2: 565 + M3: 58 + M4: ~310 docs).

### AI effect modes (M-AI-EFFECT-MODES, target v0.15.0)

Lifts M-AI-OPENROUTER's runtime `--allow-routing` gate into a type-level marker by registering AI in the default-mode table that M-EFFECT-REFINEMENT-PHASE1 just shipped. Bare `!{AI}` desugars to `!{AI[mode=fixed]}` (zero-diff back-compat); functions declared `!{AI[mode=routeable]}` opt into routing at the type level and skip the `--allow-routing` CLI gate. Validates the parameterised-effects machinery on a second pilot effect (Rand was first). Design doc: [m-ai-effect-modes.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_15_x/m-ai-effect-modes.md). Sprint plan: [m-ai-effect-modes-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_15_x/m-ai-effect-modes-sprint-plan.md). Parent (canonical): [m-effect-refinement.md](design_docs/planned/v1_0_0/m-effect-refinement.md).

- **M1: AI default-mode entry + bare desugar.** Commit `28d92602`, ~30 LOC + ~140 LOC tests. One-row addition to `defaultEffectModes` in `internal/types/effects.go`: `"AI": {"mode", "fixed"}`. Bare `!{AI}` desugars to `!{AI[mode=fixed]}` via the existing default-mode machinery; existing AI-using examples produce zero-diff typecheck output. Tests run with `-count=20` for determinism.
- **M2: runtime check + `--allow-routing` relaxation.** Commit `43f8266a`, ~451 LOC, 8 new tests. New `ErrRoutingRequiresRouteableMode` sentinel in `internal/ai/`. New `EffectModeFor` helper plus the `routingFlagValues` snapshot pattern that captures CLI flag values before typecheck completes. `determineDeclaredAIMode` reads the entry function's elaborated effect row from `Iface.Exports[entry].Type.Type.EffectRow` and flips `allowRouting=true` whenever the declared mode is `routeable` or `replay-only`. Bare `!{AI}` + flags + `--allow-routing` continues to work (back-compat fallback path). Smoke-tested all 4 paths end-to-end.
- **M3: worked example.** Commit `01642550`, ~40 LOC. New [examples/ai_modes.ail](examples/ai_modes.ail) demonstrating bare `!{AI}`, explicit `!{AI[mode=fixed]}`, and `!{AI[mode=routeable]}` side by side. Both relaxation paths verified end-to-end: routeable entry skips `--allow-routing`; bare-`!{AI}` entry still hits the safety gate.
- **M4: docs + release.** Docs-only (~150 LOC). [docs/docs/guides/ai-routing.md](docs/docs/guides/ai-routing.md) gains a new "Type-level mode markers (v0.15.0+)" section. [docs/docs/guides/parameterised-effects.md](docs/docs/guides/parameterised-effects.md) updated to list AI in the default-mode table. Teaching prompts ([prompts/v0.16.0.md](prompts/v0.16.0.md), [cmd/ailang/prompts/v0.16.0.md](cmd/ailang/prompts/v0.16.0.md)) gain an AI modes subsection mirroring the existing Rand modes section. Design doc + sprint plan moved from `design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/` to `design_docs/implemented/v0_15_x/` with status updated and implementation report appended.
- **Deferred (intentionally).** Handler-side defence-in-depth check inside the OpenRouter handler — would need extending `AIHandler` interface from `string -> string` to carry the declared mode through `AIContext.Call`. The CLI-level relaxation in M2 already covers the user-visible safety property, so the handler-side mirror is duplication-only and can land later if/when AI calls become value-level (`ai.complete(req)`). Replay-only and BYOK runtime enforcement — parser-accepted; tracked as Phase 3 + Phase 4 of the parent doc. Total LOC across the sprint: ~810 (M1: 30+140 + M2: 451 + M3: 40 + M4: ~150 docs).

### macOS notification daemon for cloud events (M-MAC-NOTIFY-DAEMON, target v0.15.0)

Adds `ailang daemon` — a long-running pull loop that subscribes to the cloud `events-laptop` and `messages-laptop` Pub/Sub subscriptions and surfaces actionable events as native macOS notifications. Pulled forward from v1.0.0 to v0.15.0 after motoko_agent integration confirmed external-feedback throughput needed real-time pickup, not only the SessionStart hook. Supersedes the planned `M5: minimum-viable feedback notifier` carve-out in [m-pkg-feedback-loop.md](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-pkg-feedback-loop.md). Design doc: [m-mac-notify-daemon.md](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-mac-notify-daemon.md). Sprint plan: [m-mac-notify-daemon-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-mac-notify-daemon-sprint-plan.md).

- **M1: notify package.** New `internal/notify/` package (~280 LOC, 7 unit tests). `Notify(Notification) error` tries `terminal-notifier` first (supports `-execute "open <URL>"` click-actions), falls back to `osascript display notification`, returns typed `ErrNotifierUnavailable` when neither binary is on PATH so callers degrade gracefully on Linux/Windows dev environments. Sound mapping (Glass/Ping/Basso/Pop), Group key for stack-collapse, AppleScript quote escaping. Real-notification smoke test confirmed the click-action URL path end-to-end.
- **M2: pull loop + public-feedback handler.** New `internal/daemon/` package (~870 LOC, 14 daemon tests + 7 dedup/handler tests = 21 total). Two parallel goroutines pulling `SubEventsLaptop` (TaskCompletion → ⏳ Approval needed / ✅ Task done / ❌ Task failed) and `SubMessagesLaptop` (InboxMessage; routes to a dedicated 🌐 External feedback shape when `to_inbox=public-feedback`). Per-event dedup windows (60s tasks on `task_id+status`, 5min messages on `message_id`) with periodic sweep. Ack-after-notify ordering — notification failures cause Pub/Sub redelivery instead of message loss. Reads `~/.ailang/config/daemon.yaml` + `notify_excludes.conf` if present; sensible defaults otherwise. Resolves env→(project, prefix) via `EnvProject` map mirroring `ailang-multivac` per-environment tfvars. Graceful SIGINT/SIGTERM shutdown. End-to-end verified against `ailang-multivac-dev`: published a synthetic TaskCompletion to `ailang-dev-events`, daemon received via `ailang-dev-events-laptop`, formatted and logged the notification through the dry-run path.
- **M3: install/uninstall/status + launchd plist.** Embedded plist template at `internal/daemon/plist.tmpl` (companion documented file at `scripts/ailang-daemon.plist.tmpl`); three CLI subcommands: `daemon install --env --binary --force`, `daemon uninstall`, `daemon status`. install renders the plist with env-specific `AILANG_CLOUD_PROJECT` and the resolved binary path, writes `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.sunholo.ailang.daemon.plist`, and `launchctl load`s it; refuses overwrite without `--force`. uninstall is idempotent on missing files; both `launchctl unload` and the file removal happen even if the plist was hand-edited. status shows launchctl list output + last 10 log lines. 8 unit tests with a stubbed launchctl runner; real-launchctl smoke test (PID 74140 verified, full cycle clean).
- **M4: integration + ship.** New `make install-notify-daemon` / `uninstall-notify-daemon` / `smoke-notify-daemon` targets. `scripts/smoke-notify-daemon.sh` publishes a synthetic event to dev and asserts the daemon logs the notification within 30s — verified at runtime in 1s. New user guide [docs/docs/guides/notify-daemon.md](docs/docs/guides/notify-daemon.md). Removed the `scripts/hooks/check_public_feedback.sh` SessionStart hook + its `.claude/settings.json` entry — the daemon supersedes it for between-session real-time delivery. Cross-repo follow-up flagged: `ailang-multivac/terraform/environments/prod/terraform.tfvars` does NOT yet have a `client_subscriptions` block; production deploy of the daemon requires adding `messages-laptop` there before `make install-notify-daemon ENV=prod` is meaningful. Dev environment already has all required subscriptions deployed.

### Eval suite expansion — SOTA open-source models via OpenRouter (post-release v0.15.0)

Two open-source SOTA models added to the eval suite after passing 3/3 smoke benchmarks: **GLM 5** (z.ai, first OS to pass full smoke, $0.60/$2.08 per 1M) and **MiniMax M2.7** (cheapest OS PASS, $0.30/$1.20 per 1M). Both added to `extended_suite` (standard 0-shot mode) and `agent_suite` (agentic harness via opencode + OpenRouter). Four near-miss OS models (Gemma 4 26B, DeepSeek V4 Flash, GLM 4.7 Flash, Kimi K2.6) kept in models.yml as a watchlist — re-test after stdlib/prompt improvements. Direct invocation via `ailang run --ai vendor/model` was already validated end-to-end through M-AI-OPENROUTER (v0.16.0/v0.14.3 backport). The "Smoke-Test Gate" rule of thumb (rule out adding a model that can't pass all 3 smoke benchmarks) was codified into the [model-manager skill](.claude/skills/model-manager/SKILL.md) — saves ~$5 of wasted full-suite spend per model audit. `csv_to_json_converter.yml` benchmark timeout bumped 90→180s for cross-harness fairness; agent-mode iteration with slower OS models was previously killed mid-generation by the tighter cap.

## [v0.14.3] - 2026-05-02

### OpenRouter provider with replayable model resolution (M-AI-OPENROUTER, target v0.15.1)

Adds [OpenRouter](https://openrouter.ai) as an AILANG AI provider, unlocking ~100 models behind one HTTP API and laying the foundation for cost-aware agent routing. The interesting design work was keeping dynamic provider routing replayable: every routed call records the resolved model, fallback chain, prompt/completion/cached tokens and USD cost in the trace's `ResolvedRoute` payload, and routing-capable behaviour is gated behind an explicit `--allow-routing` runtime flag — the runtime equivalent of the planned `!{AI[mode=routeable]}` type-level marker. The type-level work itself was deferred to [M-EFFECT-REFINEMENT (v1.0.0)](design_docs/planned/v1_0_0/m-effect-refinement.md), which is now the canonical home for parameterised effects (`!{E[mode=...]}`) across `Rand`, `Clock`, `Net`, `FS`, and `AI`. Design doc: [m-ai-openrouter-provider.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_16_0/m-ai-openrouter-provider.md). Sprint plan: [m-ai-openrouter-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_16_0/m-ai-openrouter-sprint-plan.md).

- **M1: thin OpenRouter adapter.** New `internal/ai/openrouter/` package (1042 LOC, 94.9% coverage, 16 test cases). Forks the `internal/ai/openai/` template; speaks OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions; consumes the OpenRouter-extended `usage` block (`cached_tokens`, `cost`). New `CachedTokens` and `CostUSD` fields added to `ai.Response` so cost-visibility flows through to the trace. Eval harness gains vendor/model slash-prefix detection in `internal/eval_harness/`; ten `or-*` model entries added to `internal/eval_harness/models.yml` (or-claude-sonnet-4-5, or-gpt5, or-gpt5-mini, or-gemini-2-5-pro, or-gemini-2-5-flash, or-llama-3-3-70b, or-deepseek-chat, or-mistral-large, or-qwen-2-5-72b, or-auto). Adding new routed models is YAML-only — no Go code.
- **M2: routing policy IR and provider rejection.** `AIRoutingPolicy`, `RoutePreference`, `AICapability` constants in new `internal/ai/routing.go`. `translatePolicy` maps to OpenRouter's `provider` field (`order`, `allow_fallbacks`, `require_parameters`, `sort`). OpenAI / Anthropic / Gemini / Ollama `Generate` methods now reject non-zero routing with an `errors.Is`-detectable `ErrRoutingNotSupported` — non-routing-capable providers fail loudly when a routing policy is passed instead of silently ignoring it. CLI flags `--routing-fallback`, `--routing-require`, `--routing-prefer`, `--routing-max-price` plumbed through `cmd/ailang/exec.go`. The IR carries `MaxPricePerMTok` but the OpenRouter `provider` field doesn't accept it on the standard path (the upstream filter lives behind their `transforms` API), so the value is captured in the trace but not yet forwarded — wiring is one switch when the broader transforms work lands.
- **M3: trace integration and runtime safety gate.** `ResolvedRoute` payload added to `EffectEvent`. AI effect ops now emit trace events via the new collector hook (gap fill — they didn't before).
