New `AIHandlerWithRouting` interface lets the OpenRouter handler surface resolution metadata (`requested_model`, `resolved_model`, `resolved_provider`, `fallback_chain`, all token fields, `cost_usd`). `--allow-routing` runtime safety gate substitutes for the deferred `!{AI[Routeable]}` type-level marker — any `--routing-*` flag without `--allow-routing` returns a typed error before submitting the request. New [examples/ai_openrouter_routing.ail](examples/ai_openrouter_routing.ail) demonstrates the flow with both stub (deterministic, no API key) and live (OpenRouter key + routing policy) modes. Backward-compat verified: existing trace events without the route field still parse correctly. Trace package never imports the effects package — the collector interface lives where it's consumed.
- **M4: docs + cost-comparison benchmark.** New user-facing guide [docs/docs/guides/ai-routing.md](docs/docs/guides/ai-routing.md) covering Quick Start, routing policies (each `--routing-*` flag with its OpenRouter mapping), trace and replay (with `jq` recipes), supported models, the BYOK / `AI[Routeable]` future, and the deferred-by-design list. New cost-comparison benchmark [benchmarks/openrouter_cost_compare/](benchmarks/openrouter_cost_compare/) — `scenario.md`, `run.sh`, `README.md`. The runner issues one Chat Completions call per model (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5`, `openai/gpt-5-mini`, `google/gemini-2.5-flash`, `meta-llama/llama-3.3-70b-instruct`), extracts the `route` payload from the trace via `jq`, and emits a CSV with cost / tokens / latency per model. The benchmark is **live-API only** — it makes real billable calls, is gated behind `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, and is intentionally NOT wired into `make ci` (`run.sh` exits 0 with a friendly skip message when the env var is unset). Design doc and sprint plan moved from `design_docs/planned/v0_16_0/` to `design_docs/implemented/v0_16_0/` with implementation report appended; cross-references in M-EFFECT-REFINEMENT updated.
- **Deferred to follow-up sprints (intentionally).** Type-level effect-row markers `!{AI[mode=routeable|fixed|replay-only]}` and `!{AI[scope=byok]}` — moved to [M-EFFECT-REFINEMENT (v1.0.0)](design_docs/planned/v1_0_0/m-effect-refinement.md) as the canonical home; the v0.16.0 `--allow-routing` runtime gate continues as the back-compat fallback. Replay-engine pin-to-resolved + `--reroute` flag — trace data is captured today, the replay engine itself is still trace-naive (a separate replay-engine refactor). AILANG-side `ai.complete(req)` entry point and `stdlib/std/ai/providers/openrouter.ail` constructor — needs new builtins for value-level provider construction, scoped to v0.17.0. Streaming responses. OpenRouter `transforms` / web-search / image features. AILANG-side cost-budget enforcement (trace records cost; halt-on-exceed is additive on top).

### Information-flow control labels (M-TAINT-TYPES, target v0.15.1)

Adds an information-flow control (IFC) label system to AILANG so functions can express where untrusted data is and is not allowed to flow. The annotation `T<label>` marks a value as carrying a source-level taint; `T{not LABEL}` marks a parameter position as a sink that refuses values labelled with `<LABEL>`. The new `! {Declassify}` effect must be present on any function that intentionally lowers a label. Adapted from Erik Meijer's "Guardians of the Agents" (CACM Jan 2026) — the language-level half of the static-check-before-tool-runs idea, sitting alongside the Z3 contract layer that v0.9.x already provided. Design doc: [m-taint-types.md](design_docs/planned/v0_16_0/m-taint-types.md). Sprint plan: [m-taint-types-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/planned/v0_16_0/m-taint-types-sprint-plan.md).

- **M1: label lattice.** New `internal/types/labels.go` package with `Label` interface and four implementations (`labelBottom`, `labelConst`, `labelVar`, `labelJoin`). The join constructor canonicalises (sorted, deduplicated, flat) so equality is structural and commutative-/associative-/idempotent-clean. `EvalNot(L, ℓ)` evaluates the refinement predicate `{not ℓ}` against label `L` (true iff `ℓ` is not subsumed by `L`). 12 unit tests run with `-count=20` to catch map-iteration nondeterminism in the canonical sort.
- **M2: AST + parser.** New AST nodes `LabelExpr`, `RefinementExpr`, `LabelledType`. Parser gains 2-token lookahead (`peek2Token`) so `{not IDENT}` after a base type is unambiguous from a function body's `{`. `T<label>` and `T{not LABEL}` work in any type position (params, returns, record fields, list/tuple element types). Pretty-printer emits canonical forms. Structured parse errors with hints for non-MVP shapes (`{!email}`, `{¬email}`, `{not a && not b}`, `{label = email}`).
- **M3: type-system label primitives.** New `TLabelled{Inner, L}` wrapper type and helpers `LabelOf`, `StripLabel`, `WithLabel`, `PurePropagateLabel` (APP-PURE: output label = join of input labels), `JoinLabels`, `JoinLabelList`. `WithLabel` strips before re-wrapping to prevent double-nesting.
- **M4: sink-refinement check + DECLASS rule.** `CheckSinkRefinement(argType, refinement)` evaluates `EvalNot(LabelOf(argType), <ℓ>)` and returns a structured `SinkError` if the argument's label flows into a `{not ℓ}` position. `CheckDeclassify(in, out, effectRow)` enforces the DECLASS rule: a function that changes the input/output label must have `Declassify` in its effect row, with identity (same label) treated as a no-op. The `Declassify` effect is registered alongside `IO`, `Net`, `FS`. `*ast.LabelledType` cases added to all 7 `ast.Type` switches (typechecker, iface builder, elaborate, gen/lower, apiserver, verify).
- **M5: single-module demo + Z3 sort handler.** [examples/runnable/contracts/inbox_injection_v2.ail](examples/runnable/contracts/inbox_injection_v2.ail) rewrites the v1 demo using real labels — `string<email>` source, `string<sanitized>` declassified output. `ailang verify` reports `5 functions: 3 verified, 2 violations`. `cmd/ailang/verify.go` gains `*ast.LabelledType` cases in `astTypeToSMTSort` and `convertASTTypeToType` so Z3 sees the underlying sort. All 20 existing contract examples produce identical pre-/post-sprint outcomes.
- **M6: iface JSON serialisation.** Tagged-union JSON for `Label` (`bottom`, `const`, `var`, `join`) plus a `tlabelled` envelope on `Type`. The iface builder restores labels from the original `*ast.FuncDecl` onto its `*types.Scheme` before serialisation — the type checker erases labels for unification, so `applyLabelsFromAST` walks the AST and re-wraps each param/return position. Backwards-compatible: ifaces without label fields decode as plain types with `LabelOf == ⊥`. New `internal/types/label_json.go` + 9 round-trip tests.
- **M7: cross-module demo.** New [inbox_v2_lib.ail](examples/runnable/contracts/inbox_v2_lib.ail) exports a `Mail` record with labelled fields (`body: string<email>`); [inbox_v2_app.ail](examples/runnable/contracts/inbox_v2_app.ail) imports it and shows the same 3-verified / 2-violation split with the label flowing across the module boundary. Required adding `*TLabelled` to the unifier (strip + recurse). M-SMT-CROSS-MODULE-TYPES regression check passes (`cross_module_types{,_lib}.ail` still 4 verified / 1 named SKIP).
- **M8: prompt-injection benchmark.** [benchmarks/prompt_injection/](benchmarks/prompt_injection/) — scenario.md, expected_ailang_safe.ail (3 verified), expected_ailang_injected.ail (1 violation caught), expected_python_naive.py (no static check available), score.sh CSV scorer, README.md. YAML benchmark spec at [benchmarks/prompt_injection.yml](benchmarks/prompt_injection.yml) for `ailang eval-suite` integration.
- **M9: prompt + CHANGELOG + sprint finalisation.** New [prompts/v0.16.0.md](prompts/v0.16.0.md) (active version) teaches `T<label>`, `T{not LABEL}`, the lattice naming convention (lowercase, kebab-case for compounds), the DECLASS rule, and a 30-line worked example.

**Follow-up resolved:** M-SMT-CROSS-MODULE-FUNCTIONS (see below) now teaches the encoder to handle imported function calls via two paths — inlining (define-fun) for pure non-recursive callees and contract-as-spec (declare-const + assert) for recursive ones. The M7 cross-module types example and `inbox_v2_app.ail` continue to verify without regressions.

### Autonomous package cascade — validated + hardened (M-PKG-AUTONOMOUS-CASCADE-SAFE)

Closes the loop on the v0.10.0 [M-PKG-AUTONOMOUS-UPDATES](design_docs/implemented/v0_10_0/m-pkg-autonomous-updates.md) work that built the cascade infrastructure but had never been exercised end-to-end in cloud — partly because cloud dispatch was sending raw message content as the agent prompt instead of the templated workflow until commit `228d5c0a` (M-PKG-FEEDBACK-LOOP M2). This sprint validates the cascade end-to-end AND hardens the cascade-trigger channel via topic-IAM separation.

- **New `ailang-{env}-cascade` Pub/Sub topic.** Publish IAM restricted to the coordinator service account ONLY. The pkg-update.md template now starts with a critical guard: act on the bump request only when `Source: cascade`. Public MCP traffic (`submit_feedback` etc.) cannot reach this topic — enforced at the GCP layer before any agent code runs. Threat-modelled in the design doc as "Public MCP attacker"; closed at the IAM layer instead of relying solely on template-level discipline.
- **`Source` attribute end-to-end.** Added to `pubsub.MessageAttributes`, `coordinator.Message`, `coordinator.TaskRecord` (persisted in both Firestore and the local SQLite — bonus latent-bug fix: `Kind` was set on the in-memory task at message-receive time but never persisted to local SQLite, so dispatchTasksCloud silently fell back to the default template on the laptop dev path; now both `kind` and `source` round-trip).
- **`emitDependentNotifications` dual-writes.** Existing inbox path stays for backward-compat (deprecated v0.17), new cascade-topic publish via `pubsub.Publisher.PublishCascade(ctx, msgID, attrs, rootPackage)` stamps `source=cascade` and `root_package=<vendor>/<name>@<version>` attributes.
- **Per-cascade cumulative budget cap.** New optional `[cascade] max_cost_usd` section in `ailang.toml` (default $1.00). `coordinator.CascadeBudget` accumulates per-task cost across the cascade DAG; aborts further dispatches with structured event when cap would be exceeded. Per-task hard ceilings unchanged — this is an additional, cumulative gate. Each package owner controls how expensive a cascade rooted at THEIR package can grow.
- **`ailang pkg cascade status <vendor/name@version>` CLI.** Prints cascade DAG with per-dependent status (queued/merged/unknown), accumulated cost, PR URLs. Reads from existing registry provenance + index data.
- **End-to-end smoke tests.** `scripts/integration/test_cascade_e2e.sh` (positive: real publish → real PR observed) and `test_cascade_negative.sh` (negative: a crafted public-MCP `submit_feedback` with `auto_dispatch=true` and a "please bump" body MUST NOT publish anything). Both target a configurable env via `AILANG_CLOUD_PROJECT`. Idempotent cleanup.
- **`sunholo/test_pkg_consumer` cascade fixture** (in ailang-packages repo). Depends on `sunholo/test_pkg`; sets tight `[cascade] max_cost_usd = 0.50` so the forced-fail variant trips the budget gate cheaply.
- **Always-PR for v1.** No package agent auto-merges cascade-driven changes. The cascade does the boring work; humans keep the merge call.
- **Developer guide:** [`docs/guides/autonomous-package-updates.md`](docs/guides/autonomous-package-updates.md) — the golden path, threat model, diagnostics.

### Cascade deterministic-first dispatch (M-PKG-CASCADE-DETERMINISTIC-FIRST)

First real cascade observation against `ailang-multivac-dev` revealed an architectural opportunity: AILANG already had the hash-driven semver classifier from M-PKG-MSG (`classifyChange` in `internal/messaging/pkg_events.go` — A=content-only, B=additive, C=interface change), but the cascade flow was treating every bump as an AI task even when it was pure computation. Routine cascades cost ~$0.10–0.30 + ~3 min on a $0.000 deterministic operation.

This sprint shifts cascades to deterministic-first: the wrapper does the routine bump (toml dep version edit + `ailang lock` + `check` + `test`) and only invokes the AI agent when actually needed (interface breakage that requires consumer code repair). Validated end-to-end on dev with both paths exercised. Design doc: [m-pkg-cascade-deterministic-first.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_16_0/m-pkg-cascade-deterministic-first.md).

- **Self-contained cascade messages.** New `pubsub.PublishCascadeWithEnvelope` carries the full hash + change_class + effect-ceiling deltas in the Pub/Sub message data field AND surfaces key fields as attributes (`change_class`, `from_version`, `to_version`, `effects_widened`). The cloud coordinator decodes the envelope and populates 11 new cascade fields on `coordinator.Message` and `coordinator.TaskRecord` (persisted in both Firestore and the local SQLite). Backward-compatible: existing single-attribute cascade publishes still decode via fallback.
- **Wrapper-side deterministic dispatch.** `cmd/ailang/coordinator_cloud.go` now calls `classifyDispatchPath(change_class)` before invoking the AI executor: class A/B → `deterministicCascadeBump` (read consumer's ailang.toml, regex-rewrite the dep pin, `ailang lock`, `ailang check`, `ailang test`, commit). On success: skips AI executor entirely, the wrapper's existing push + `openCascadePullRequest` flow runs. On failure: falls through to AI with the check output for repair.
- **AI escalation context.** Nine new template variables (`{{.RootPackage}}`, `{{.RootChangeClass}}`, `{{.FromVersion}}`, `{{.ToVersion}}`, `{{.FromInterfaceHash}}`, `{{.ToInterfaceHash}}`, `{{.EffectsWidened}}`, `{{.PrevEffectCeiling}}`, `{{.NewEffectCeiling}}`) so when the AI is invoked it sees exactly what changed instead of having to reverse-engineer it from the message body. Plumbed through DispatchParams → Cloud Run Job env vars → `stage_execution.go::buildTemplateDirective`. The `pkg-update.md` template was rewritten action-first (assumes the wrapper attempted the bump first; the agent's job is breakage repair).
- **Conservative C-classifier.** `mapChangeClassToSchema` in `pkg_publish.go` now returns C whenever interface hash differs unless we can prove the change was a pure new-module addition. The previous logic returned B for "module list unchanged" cases, missing function-level removals (the registry sees module-level exports, not function signatures).
- **Cascade PRs auto-tagged.** Wrapper detects the cascade context via `AILANG_CASCADE_ROOT_PACKAGE` env var and opens the PR with `[cascade] bump <root> (<task-id>)` title and `cascade` label. Skips the `injectAgentsMD` step for cascade tasks (kept the cascade PR diff focused on the actual bump).
- **End-to-end smoke verified on dev:**
  - **Class A deterministic success** (PR #12): cascade fired → wrapper detected class A → bumped toml + regenerated lock + check passed → committed → wrapper pushed + opened PR. Total: **7.5 seconds, $0.00, zero AI invocations** (`turns=0, tokens=0+0`).
  - **Class A → check fails → AI escalation** (PR #11): cascade fired → wrapper bumped + locked → `ailang check` failed (consumer wrap.ail had deprecated `++` syntax) → wrapper escalated to AI with full cascade context → AI repaired wrap.ail → PR opened with cascade label.
- **Test+prod deployment deferred.** Cascade infrastructure live in `ailang-multivac-dev` only. `ailang-core-test-release` triggers on tag push (`^v.*`); promotion to test+prod requires a v0.14.3 release tag plus a manual `promote-to-prod` trigger run. Multivac config changes (templates, agent registrations) deploy via `git push origin dev:test/prod` once a release lands.

### Z3 cross-module type verification (M-SMT-CROSS-MODULE-TYPES)

Closes the gap that prevented `ailang verify` from working on real-world multi-module AILANG code. Phase 1 (demand-driven ADT filtering) shipped in v0.9.x; this pass lands Phase 2 (record aliases + inline records), Phase 3 (mutual recursion + parameter prefix), and the Issue 7 follow-up (named SKIP messages). Design doc: [m-smt-cross-module-types.md](design_docs/planned/v0_13_0/m-smt-cross-module-types.md). Sprint plan: [m-smt-cross-module-types-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/planned/v0_13_0/m-smt-cross-module-types-sprint-plan.md).

- **M1: per-function record-alias and inline-record filter.** Extends the v0.9.x ADT-only filter so the verifier passes only the named record aliases (`type Cell = {…}`) and inline records (e.g., `Record_blocks_kind`) that a function actually references via its params, return type, or body — including transitively through inline-record bodies. Eliminates the cascade where one unrelated alias's missing dependency would skip every function in the module. New helper `buildNeededSortSet` walks ADT variants, alias field types, and ExtraDeclaration bodies to compute the closure.
- **M2: mutual recursion via `declare-datatypes` (plural).** When the multi-pass declaration loop ends with sorts that reference each other (e.g., `Block ↔ Record_blocks_kind`), the verifier now runs Tarjan's SCC over the dep graph and emits each cycle as a single `(declare-datatypes ((Sort1 0) (Sort2 0)) (variants1 variants2))` block — Z3's only supported encoding for mutual recursion. Self-recursive singletons stay in the singular `declare-datatype` form (which Z3 also accepts). New file `internal/smt/codegen_mutual.go` (~200 LOC) plus 6 unit tests covering split helpers, plural emission, and SCC detection.
- **M3a: `$p_` parameter prefix.** Parameters are declared as `(declare-const $p_NAME SORT)` and rewritten in body references via the new `activeParamRenames` table. Eliminates Z3's "ambiguous constant reference" error when a parameter shares a name with a record accessor function (e.g., `text` parameter alongside `(text TableCell) -> String`). Bounded-recursion top-level call (`(factorial_5 $p_n)`) and the body's `*core.Var` encoder both honour the prefix.
- **M3b deferred.** Qualified field-accessor names (`RecordType_fieldName`) for cross-record collisions (Issue 6) are not in this sprint — the current docparse codebase has no real Issue 6 case, and the eval.ail breakage that surfaces "applicable" errors is a separate HOF-inlining + let-binding shadowing concern. Tracked as future work.
- **M4: named SKIP messages.** `firstUnencodableBuiltin` walks the body and reports the first unencodable builtin by name — both internal (`_str_trim`, `_str_upper`) and stdlib (`std/list.filter`, `std/string.toLower`). Replaces the generic "Function X uses types not encodable in SMT (list, unsupported string operations)" with actionable diagnostics like "Function X uses an unencodable builtin: std/list.filter". Generic fallback preserved for unencodable shapes (e.g., `core.Array`) without a named builtin.
- **M5: cross-module example + downstream validation.** New `examples/runnable/contracts/cross_module_types{,_lib}.ail` exercises all four capabilities in a verifiable form: 4 functions VERIFIED (record alias from another module, recursive ADT via `Tree`, parameter `text`/`count` colliding with accessor, cross-function call), 1 SKIPPED with a named blocker (`std/string.trim`).
- **Net effect on docparse:** 3/28 → 20+/115 verified functions across the project (~7× more functions reaching Z3). `types/document.ail` alone goes from 0 verified to 5 verified. Existing `format_router.ail` maintains 3/3 (no regression). All 20 existing AILANG contract examples produce identical verify outcomes to pre-sprint baseline.

### Z3 cross-module function verification (M-SMT-CROSS-MODULE-FUNCTIONS)

Extends `ailang verify` to reason about imported function calls, not just imported types. When a function in module A calls a function from module B, the verifier now resolves the callee via two paths. Design doc: [m-smt-cross-module-functions.md](design_docs/planned/v0_17_0/m-smt-cross-module-functions.md). Sprint plan: [m-smt-cross-module-functions-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/planned/v0_17_0/m-smt-cross-module-functions-sprint-plan.md).

- **M1: `ImportedPrograms` field.** `EncodeFunctionOpts` gains an `ImportedPrograms map[string]*core.Program` field. `cmd/ailang/verify.go` populates it from all transitively resolved modules before calling the encoder. The callee resolver reads from this map to locate bodies of imported functions without round-tripping through the file system.
- **M2: inline path (define-fun).** For pure, non-recursive imported callees, the resolver emits a `(define-fun <name> ...)` block exactly as for same-module callees. The encoder substitutes the callee name at every call site. `verify.go` now also applies the `IsPure=true` fixup to imported module functions (previously only applied to the main module) so functions declared with `! {}` are correctly recognised as inlineable.
- **M3: contract-as-spec fallback (declare-const + assert).** For recursive or unencodable imported callees, the resolver falls back to the callee's `ensures` contracts: it declares a fresh constant `$xmod_result_<callee>_<idx>` and asserts each ensures clause as a Z3 axiom. Callers that need only a consequence of the postcondition (e.g., `result >= 0`) can be fully verified from this axiom alone. `substituteContractExpr` handles the full Core AST — including `*core.DictApp` (type-class dispatch for comparison operators like `>=`) — substituting param names and the `result` pseudo-variable correctly. `activeContractCallees` wires the result constant into call-site encoding.
- **`AILANG_DUMP_SMT` debug flag.** When set, the temp `.smt2` file is preserved instead of deleted — useful for inspecting what Z3 actually receives.
- **New examples.** `examples/runnable/contracts/cross_module_functions{,_lib}.ail` demonstrates both paths: `quadruple` (inline — `double` is pure + non-recursive, verified via define-fun), `sumBound` (contract — `sumTo` is recursive, verified from ensures axiom `result >= 0`), and `mixed` (both paths simultaneously). All 3 functions verify. All pre-existing contract examples produce identical outcomes.

### Multivac CI speed (M-CI-BUILD-SPEED, ailang-multivac repo)

Cut Cloud Build wall-clock from 16-22 min to 60 sec for YAML/config-only pushes (16-22× faster) and to 6:27 for full source pushes (~3× faster). Real measurements from real builds — see [docs/sprint-retros/m-ci-build-speed.md](docs/sprint-retros/m-ci-build-speed.md).

- **BuildKit registry cache + parallel agent variants (M2+M3).** All 11 agent variants (`agent-base` + `agent` + `agent-go` + `agent-codex` + `agent-codex-go` + `agent-opencode` + `agent-pi` + `agent-gemini` + `agent-gemini-go` + `agent-eval` + `agent-eval-go`) now use `docker buildx build --cache-from --cache-to --push`. Per-image `:cache` tags in Artifact Registry survive between runs. waitFor restructured so the 6 level-1 variants fan out from `build-agent-base` in parallel (vs serial chain via `push-agent-base`). Agent build phase: ~14 min serial → max(any single variant) ≈ 30s when cached.
- **Path-based trigger filters + config-only fast path (M4).** New `cloudbuild-config-only.yaml` and 3 new triggers (`ailang-multivac-config-{dev,test,prod}`) fire only on `config/**` or `terraform/**` changes and skip the entire image rebuild. Existing 4 triggers (`ailang-multivac-{dev,test,prod}`, `ailang-core-dev`) gained `includedFiles`/`ignoredFiles` so a docs-only push runs nothing and a code-only push doesn't fire the unrelated multivac pipeline.
- **Terraform skip-when-clean (M5).** `terraform plan -detailed-exitcode -out=plan.out` writes the exit code to `/workspace/tf-exitcode`. Apply step skips entirely when plan returned 0 (no changes). `deploy-services` always runs — Cloud Run revisions need to roll for new image digests even when terraform has nothing to do (terraform tracks `:latest` tag, not the digest).
- **Eliminated Cloud Run terraform drift.** Added `lifecycle { ignore_changes = [client, client_version] }` to all 14 `google_cloud_run_v2_job` and 6 `google_cloud_run_v2_service` resources. Every `gcloud run {services,jobs} update` (CI deploy step + manual force-refresh) was stamping `client="gcloud"` / `client_version="565.0.0"` on resources, causing terraform to perpetually try to null them out. Plan now stable across consecutive runs in dev/test/prod (was 0/9/0 with ping-pong, now 0/0/0).
- **Eliminated Identity Platform drift.** Removed the explicit `email{ enabled=false, password_required=false }` block from `google_identity_platform_config.default` (provider's read returns null for the block when disabled, so explicit values caused permanent diff). Belt-and-braces `lifecycle { ignore_changes = [sign_in] }` for future provider quirks.
- **Side-fix:** Cloud Build's `images:` declaration is incompatible with `buildx --push` (daemon can't find the locally-built images to push at build end). Dropped from all three `cloudbuild*.yaml` configs.
- **Net effect:** Fix-a-bug-and-ship-to-prod cycle drops from ~75 min (3 hops × 25 min) to ~15 min (3 hops × 5 min, fast paths). Major DX win for the agent-driven dev loop.

### Security & Supply Chain (M-SUPPLY-CHAIN-HARDENING-2)

Second pass on OpenSSF Scorecard findings. Predecessor: [M-SUPPLY-CHAIN-HARDENING](design_docs/implemented/v0_14_1/m-supply-chain-hardening.md) (v0.14.1). Design doc: [m-supply-chain-hardening-2.md](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-supply-chain-hardening-2.md).

- **Go toolchain bumped 1.25.0 → 1.25.9 (M1).** Clears 12 reachable stdlib vulnerabilities: `crypto/x509` chain-building bugs, `crypto/tls` TLS 1.3 KeyUpdate DoS, `archive/tar` unbounded allocation, `html/template` XSS, plus net/url, net/http, encoding/asn1, encoding/pem, os patches. `go.mod` toolchain pinned; all CI workflow `go-version` strings updated to `'1.25.9'`.
- **MCP SDK bumped v1.3.1 → v1.4.1 (M2).** Clears `GO-2026-4773` (cross-site tool execution on HTTP server without authorization) and `GO-2026-4770` (null-Unicode JSON parsing). Real attack surface for users running `ailang-microrag-mcp` locally.
- **SLSA Build Level 3 provenance added to release.yml (M3).** Uses `slsa-framework/slsa-github-generator/.github/workflows/generator_generic_slsa3.yml@v2.1.0`; outputs `multiple.intoto.jsonl` attached to each release. Composes with existing cosign keyless signing — cosign proves identity, SLSA proves source commit + build steps. Verifier example documented in [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md#slsa-build-provenance) using `slsa-verifier verify-artifact`.
- **Third-party GitHub Actions pinned to SHA (M4).** 7 references across 6 workflow files: `docker/build-push-action`, `docker/setup-buildx-action`, `ossf/scorecard-action`, `SonarSource/sonarqube-scan-action`, `astral-sh/setup-uv`, `sigstore/cosign-installer`, `softprops/action-gh-release`. First-party `actions/*` and `github/*` deliberately stay on tags — trust boundary is GitHub itself, SHA-pinning would force Dependabot churn on identical-looking SHA bumps which is itself the rubber-stamping attack vector. Policy documented in [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md#build-trust-boundary).
- **`govulncheck` CI gate + suppression file (M5).** New `.govulncheck-allow.yml` and `tools/govulncheck-filter/` Go tool catch new stdlib regressions same-day rather than waiting for the weekly Scorecard cron. Allowlist entries require `id`/`reason`/`expires` fields; expiries fail CI when stale. Initial allowlist covers 9 unfixed Ollama vulns (upstream tracked via Dependabot). The filter handles govulncheck's pretty-printed JSON stream (NOT JSONL — common gotcha) via `json.Decoder`.
- **Net effect:** govulncheck reachable findings 23 → 9 (all 9 allowlisted). Projected next-Scorecard score: Vulnerabilities 0 → ~7, Pinned-Dependencies 0 → ~3, Token-Permissions 0 → ~8, Signed-Releases 3 → 7 (full 10 needs ≥3 releases with provenance).

### Public MCP rate limit (M-MCP-EDGE-THROTTLE Path A)

The public unauthenticated `submit_feedback` MCP tool was unthrottled — a single client could fan out a flood through the coordinator into Sonnet-driven package agents. Path A ships an in-process per-IP token-bucket limiter; Path B (Cloud Armor + HTTPS LB cutover) and the deeper triage gate are documented separately.

- **`internal/apiserver/ratelimit.go`** — per-IP token bucket; nil = disabled (rpm=0 escape hatch); idle buckets swept after 1h to bound memory under flood. Concurrency-safe; verified under `go test -race`.
- **`X-Forwarded-For` parsing** — uses the rightmost entry, the only one Google's frontend trusts (clients can prepend, GFE always appends the real TCP source as the last hop). `X-Real-Ip` fallback; missing headers bucket as `"unknown"` rather than bypassing the cap.
- **Defaults: 5 rpm, burst 3.** Override via `AILANG_RATELIMIT_RPM` and `AILANG_RATELIMIT_BURST` on the Cloud Run service. Junk values fall back to defaults.
- **Wired only on `submit_feedback`.** Read tools (stdlib introspection, examples, benchmarks, etc) stay uncapped — they're idempotent and cacheable.
- **Wire shape:** rate-limited calls return the structured envelope `{"error": "rate_limited", "detail": "...retry after 60s"}` with `IsError=true`. No Firestore write, no Pub/Sub publish, no agent dispatch — denial happens at the tool boundary.
- **Tests:** 8 cases in `ratelimit_test.go` + `feedback_tool_test.go` cover allow/deny/refill/per-IP isolation/stdio safety/sweep/env-var override/concurrent access. All pass under race detector.
- **Limitations:** state is per Cloud Run instance, so under autoscale the cap is "soft" up to `instances × rpm`. Path B (Cloud Armor) and M-FEEDBACK-TRIAGE-GATE are the strict-enforcement and cost-killer follow-ups respectively.

Design docs: [m-mcp-edge-throttle.md](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-mcp-edge-throttle.md), [m-feedback-triage-gate.md](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-feedback-triage-gate.md).

### Earlier in window — M-AGENT-MCP



**Design docs**:
- [M-AGENT-MCP](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-agent-mcp-website.md) — Cloud Run-hosted MCP server (mcp.ailang.sunholo.com) exposing versioned AILANG docs/stdlib/examples/benchmarks via the existing `internal/apiserver/mcp.go` framework. CLI fresh-fetch fallback (M5), bootstrap skill simplification (M6), public feedback channel reusing `ailang-messages` Pub/Sub (M7).
- [Sprint plan](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-agent-mcp-sprint-plan.md) — 8 milestones, ~7.5 days, ~2520 LOC

### Added (M-AGENT-MCP M1)
- **`mcp_tools/` directory** — 21 MCP tools authored in AILANG (≤30 LoC each), 6 files: `language.ail` (7 tools), `examples.ail` (3), `design.ail` (3), `benchmarks.ail` (4), `discovery.ail` (3), `feedback.ail` (1 placeholder, full impl in M7), plus `lib.ail` (shared helpers for snapshot reads, version resolution, `{served_for, data}` envelope)
- **`make snapshot`** — builds the `build/snapshot/{versioned/<ver>/, unscoped/}` tree (M1: stub data; M2 expands with real builders)
- **`make mcp-local`** — runs `ailang serve-api --mcp-http --routes-only --caps FS,Env --port 8080 ./mcp_tools/` against the local snapshot
- **All 21 tools register and respond.** `tools/list` returns the full catalog with valid schemas; sample `tools/call effects_catalog` returns the `{served_for: "latest", data: {effects: [...]}}` envelope.

### Friction surfaced (worth tracking — see sprint retrospective)
- Module path strictness rejects hyphens (`mcp-tools/` → `mcp_tools/`) — design doc references the old name; doc cleanup follow-up
- `++` is list-only on strings — used `"${var}"` interpolation instead (μRAG didn't catch this proactively)
- `module` is a reserved keyword (rejected as parameter name) — renamed to `name`
- `!` not a valid prefix unary operator — wrote `invalidCategory(c)` helper instead

### Fixed (M-AGENT-MCP, follow-up to M1)
- **`std/json.Json` values now serialize as plain JSON over HTTP/MCP.** Previously, AILANG functions returning `Json` (built via `jo`/`kv`/`js`/etc.) leaked the AILANG ADT envelope `{__type, __tag, fields}` into responses, making outputs ~10x larger and unreadable. `internal/embed.ToGo` now special-cases `TaggedValue` where `TypeName=="Json"` and `ModulePath=="std/json"`, unwrapping each constructor (JNull/JBool/JNumber/JString/JArray/JObject) to its natural Go shape. Other ADTs keep the envelope so callers can still discriminate by tag. Regression test in `internal/embed/embed_test.go::TestToGo_JsonUnwrap`.
  - **Downstream impact:** ailang-parse's MCP server (`docparse/services/tools.ail`) returns `Json` for several tool defs; those responses are now plain JSON too. Existing integration tests pass — the new shape matches what their docs already document.

### Added (M-AGENT-MCP M2)
- **`tools/build-snapshot/`** — Go binary that builds `build/snapshot/` from the current repo state in ~70ms. Reads `std/*.ail`, `eval_results/baselines/`, `cmd/ailang/prompts/`, `changelogs/`, `design_docs/`, `docs/LIMITATIONS.md`, and `examples/examples_report.json`. Emits stable, sorted JSON (deterministic across runs).
- **Snapshot output** (~1.3 MB, 150 files):
  - `versioned/0.14.2/`: stdlib_summary.json (35 modules), effects.json (8 effects), limitations.json, prompts/{full,agent,devtools}.json, examples_index.json
  - `versioned/latest -> 0.14.2/` symlink
  - `unscoped/`: versions_index.json (46 versions), benchmarks_index.json (24 baselines), benchmarks/by_model/<model>.json (21 models), changelog/<ver>.json (146 versions), design_docs_index.json
- **`make snapshot`** rebuilt to call `bin/build-snapshot`; **`make mcp-local`** unchanged.
- **`build/snapshot/`** added to `.gitignore` — generated artifact, regenerated per build (Cloud Run image bakes it in; local dev runs `make snapshot`).
- **Verified end-to-end:** `tools/call stdlib_modules` over MCP returns all 35 modules with summaries and function counts in plain JSON — agents see real data, not stubs.

### SCOPE NOTE for M2 (vs design doc)
The design doc M2 specifies `benchmarks.sqlite` + `docs.sqlite` (FTS5). We chose JSON because the AILANG-side `mcp_tools/` modules read via `std/fs` and there is no `std/sqlite` builtin yet. SQLite is a clean v2 optimization — either via a new builtin or by registering query-shaped tools as Go-side MCP handlers. The current snapshot is small enough (1.3 MB) that JSON-with-grep is fine for v1 traffic.

### Added (M-AGENT-MCP M3 — Cloud Run + image build)
- **`docker/Dockerfile.mcp`** — multi-stage Docker build (`golang:1.25-bookworm` → `gcr.io/distroless/static-debian12:nonroot`) that bakes binary AND snapshot into one image. `WORKDIR=/srv` so the AILANG loader resolves `import mcp_tools/lib` correctly inside the container.
- **ailang-multivac `terraform/cloud_run_mcp.tf`** — new Cloud Run service `${prefix}-mcp` with min-instances 0 (scale-to-zero), max 10, CPU 1, mem 512Mi. Public ingress (no auth in v1). Custom domain mapping for `mcp.ailang.sunholo.com` (prod only — dev/test use `*.run.app`).
- **ailang-multivac `cloudbuild.yaml` + `cloudbuild-images.yaml`** — new `build-mcp` step in both pipelines (auto-trigger and manual).
- **Live URLs:**
  - dev: `https://ailang-dev-mcp-ejjw6zt3bq-ew.a.run.app/mcp/` — confirmed serving 21 tools
  - prod (after DNS): `https://mcp.ailang.sunholo.com/mcp/`

### Added (M-AGENT-MCP M4 — docs site discovery)
- **`docs/docusaurus.config.js`**: `<link rel="mcp" href="https://mcp.ailang.sunholo.com/mcp/">` in `<head>` for agents that scan it.
- **`docs/src/components/AddToAgent.jsx`** + matching CSS — three deeplink buttons on the landing page (Claude Desktop, Cursor, Cline) plus a "copy manual config" fallback. Inserted into the index page between BenchmarkMini and DemosShowcase.
- **`docs/docs/guides/agent-mcp.md`** — full guide: why use it, connection flow (deeplink + manual JSON), tool catalog (all 21 tools grouped by category), versioning model, caps, source code links.
- **`llms.txt`** — header now points scraping agents at the MCP endpoint as the preferred path for structured queries.

### Added (M-AGENT-MCP M5 — CLI fresh-fetch via MCP)
- **`internal/mcp_client/`** — minimal MCP Streamable HTTP client (~250 LOC). `initialize → notifications/initialized → tools/call` flow with 1.5s default timeout. Validates `served_for` matches the CLI's compile-time version; returns `ErrVersionMismatch` for cross-version content leak.
- **`internal/prompt/fresh.go`** — orchestration layer with three sources (`auto|mcp|embedded`). `auto` tries cache first, then MCP, then falls back to embedded silently. Cache lives at `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/ailang/prompts/<version>/<kind>.md`.
- **`ailang prompt --source <auto|mcp|embedded>`** — defaults to `auto`. Prints a one-line stderr provenance note (`source=mcp version=... sha=... (fresh from MCP)`); suppress with `AILANG_MCP_QUIET=1`. **`embedded` is the required source for reproducible eval runs.**
- **`ailang mcp status`** — new subcommand that reports CLI version, MCP endpoint, reachability, server's view of the CLI's version, embedded vs deployed SHA, and drift state. `--json` for machine-parseable output.

### Added (M-AGENT-MCP M6 — bootstrap skill thin shim)
- **`.claude/skills/use-ailang/SKILL.md`**: 354 → 160 LoC (-55%). Removed embedded stdlib reference table that went stale per release; now points at the MCP server for `prompt_get`, `stdlib_modules`, `stdlib_search`, `examples_for_concept`, `limitations_list`, `effects_catalog`, `submit_feedback`. Local CLI commands kept as offline fallback.
- **`ailang_bootstrap` repo** (the plugin advertised on the website + auto-loaded into Cloud executors): `skills/ailang/SKILL.md` 251 → 164 LoC (-35%); README "MCP Server" section rewritten to document BOTH the local stdio server (handles "run the user's code") and the new remote HTTP server (handles "look up authoritative AILANG knowledge"). Pushed through `dev → preview → stable` with back-merge of orphaned auto-sync commits.

### Added (M-AGENT-MCP M7 v1 — public feedback channel)
- **`mcp_tools/feedback.ail`**: validates anonymous bug/feature/docs reports (title required, body ≤10 KB, snippet ≤4 KB, category enum). Returns structured errors for bad input plus an `attributes_would_be` response shape so callers can verify routing intent.
- **ailang-multivac `terraform/cloud_run_mcp.tf`**: topic-scoped `google_pubsub_topic_iam_member` granting MCP SA `roles/pubsub.publisher` on the `ailang-messages` topic ONLY (tighter than coordinator/agent project-level publisher — appropriate for a public anonymous service).
- **Deferred to M7.1**: the actual Pub/Sub publish wire (Go builtin `_publish_feedback` wrapping `messaging.Store.InsertInboxMessage` + `PubSubNotifier.Notify`). Estimated ~80 LOC of focused work in one follow-up commit. Submissions currently validate + advertise the right tool surface but don't yet reach the `public-feedback` inbox.

### Added (M-AGENT-MCP M8 — CI integration)
- **`make verify-mcp-tools`** — type-checks every `.ail` file under `mcp_tools/` (parallel-friendly, exits non-zero on any failure).
- **`make ci`** + **`make ci-strict`** now include `verify-mcp-tools` so PRs that break MCP module code can't merge.

### Pending (post-sprint follow-ups)
- **M7.1**: wire the actual Pub/Sub publish in `submit_feedback` (~80 LOC) — **shipped post-sprint, see M-AGENT-MCP M7.1 entry**
- **M8.1**: end-to-end agent benchmark (run a fixed task with vs without MCP attached, measure pass-rate / token delta) — design doc says ≥10% pass-rate lift OR ≥30% token reduction is the success metric
- **Snapshot SQLite upgrade**: when `std/sqlite` builtin lands, migrate `docs_search` and benchmark queries to FTS5 for speed
- **Doc cleanup**: design doc references `mcp-tools/` (with hyphen) — should rename to `mcp_tools/` to match what shipped

### Added (M-AGENT-MCP-ONBOARDING — follow-up sprint)

Closes 3 regressions from M-AGENT-MCP and adds 2 new tools. MCP catalog grows from 21 → 23.

**Design doc**: [m-agent-mcp-onboarding](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-agent-mcp-onboarding.md). **Sprint plan**: [m-agent-mcp-onboarding-sprint-plan](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-agent-mcp-onboarding-sprint-plan.md).

#### M1 — Fix 3 broken indexes + cloudbuild auto-redeploy
- **`docs_search`, `example_for_concept`, `stdlib_search` now return real data.** All three were defined in M-AGENT-MCP M1 but the corresponding snapshot index files were never generated, so every prod call returned `{error: "snapshot_read_failed"}`.
- New snapshot generators in `tools/build-snapshot/main.go`:
  - `writeStdlibSearchIndex` — 131 stdlib exports indexed with module, signature, doc, keywords (single regex parses each `export func` declaration)
  - `writeDocsSearchIndex` — 107 docs pages indexed (frontmatter stripped, body lowercased & capped at 4 KB)
  - `writeExampleConceptIndex` — 320 examples indexed via direct `examples/` walk (deliberately does NOT depend on `examples_report.json`, which gets corrupted when `make verify-examples` fails — bit us in M-AGENT-MCP M2)
- **ailang-multivac `cloudbuild.yaml`**: extends the existing `deploy-services` step to include `ailang-mcp` so the Cloud Run revision auto-updates when `:latest` resolves to a new SHA. Kills the manual `gcloud run services update --image=:latest` we needed twice during M-AGENT-MCP execution.

#### M2 — `install_guide(harness, platform)` tool
- New `mcp_tools/install.ail` returns harness-aware install commands for Claude Code, Gemini CLI, Codex CLI, manual install (with platform variants), and build-from-source. Includes a `remote_mcp` config block with copy-paste JSON for both stdio-bridged and HTTP-native MCP harnesses.
- New `tools/build-snapshot/install_guide_overrides.json` is the hand-curated source. New `make verify-install-guide` target diffs it against canonical sources (`docs/docs/guides/getting-started.mdx` + `ailang_bootstrap/README.md`) — wired into `make ci` and `make ci-strict` so drift fails CI.

#### M3 — `onboarding_guide(role)` tool
- New `mcp_tools/onboarding.ail` returns a stepwise flow per role (`agent` | `developer`). 8 steps each. Agent flow includes mcp_setup in step 2 (the catch-22 fix: the tool that tells you to add the MCP is itself reachable only via the MCP, so anyone who CAN call it has already done it correctly).
- **`common_pitfalls`** section pre-populated with the AILANG-syntax gotchas surfaced during M-AGENT-MCP execution — paid for once, encoded in the snapshot so future agents don't pay again:
  - String concat with `++` (list-only — use `${var}` interpolation)
  - `module` as a reserved keyword (don't use as parameter name)
  - Hyphens in module paths (`module mcp-tools/lib` parses as subtraction)
  - `ailang run` flag ordering (flags MUST come before filename)
  - `!` is not a valid prefix unary operator (write a named helper or invert branches)
  - Missing effect declarations (`! {IO}` etc. is mandatory for side-effecting code)

#### Sprint outcome
- MCP catalog: **21 → 23 tools**
- Three regressions closed; never need to manually `gcloud run services update` again
- All four pieces verified end-to-end against the dev MCP (smoke test in M4)

## [v0.14.2] - 2026-04-27 (hotfix)

**Design docs**:
- [M-CLI-ARGS-WIN](design_docs/planned/v0_14_2/m-cli-args-file-stdin.md) — Windows hotfix + Windows runtime CI
- [M-EXEC-PI](design_docs/implemented/v0_14_2/m-exec-pi-harness.md) — fifth CLI-subprocess executor (pi minimal harness, multi-provider)

### Added (M-EXEC-PI)
- **Pi coding harness as a fifth executor.** `internal/executor/pi/` (~520 LOC + 680 LOC tests, 78.8% coverage) wraps the `@mariozechner/pi-coding-agent` CLI as a CLI-subprocess executor following [docs/internal/EXECUTOR_SHAPE.md](docs/internal/EXECUTOR_SHAPE.md). `executor.GlobalFactory().ListAvailable()` now returns `["claude", "codex", "gemini", "opencode", "pi"]`.
- **Pi NDJSON parser.** Parses 10 top-level event types (`session`, `agent_start`, `turn_start`, `message_start`, `message_update`, `message_end`, `tool_execution_start`, `tool_execution_end`, `turn_end`, `agent_end`) plus 9 `assistantMessageEvent` sub-types (text/thinking/toolcall × start/delta/end). Per-turn token deltas summed from `message_end` (role=assistant); cost taken directly from `usage.cost.total`.
- **Four `pi-*` model entries in [models.yml](internal/eval_harness/models.yml)** — `pi-claude-haiku-4-5`, `pi-claude-sonnet-4-6`, `pi-gpt5-4`, `pi-gemini-3-flash-preview`. Each pairs with an existing same-`model_family` entry (claude-* + opencode-*) for 3-way cross-harness comparison via `eval-matrix --by-harness` / `--group-by=model-family`. `pi-claude-sonnet-4-6` added to `harness_suite` as the headline cross-harness signal.
- **Pi Cloud Run Job deployment.** `docker/Dockerfile.agent-pi` (mirrors `Dockerfile.agent-opencode`); `agent-pi` added to `Dockerfile.agent-eval` (multi-CLI eval image). In `ailang-multivac`: `cloudbuild-images.yaml` + `cloudbuild.yaml` build steps, `terraform/cloud_run_jobs.tf` resources for `agent_executor_pi` (project keys) and `agent_executor_pi_apikey` (BYOK). `"pi"` added to `knownVariants` in `internal/dispatch/cloudrun/dispatcher.go`. Cloud smoke verified with `pi-smoke-openai-1777275552`: 2 turns, $0.0046 with `openai/gpt-5.4-mini`.
- **Cost-control policy for pi cloud deployment.** `agent_executor_pi` binds only `OPENAI_API_KEY` + `GEMINI_API_KEY` (NOT `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) per `ailang-multivac/CLAUDE.md` §5. Pi-claude-* models work LOCALLY (developer's own key in env) but fail fast in cloud — preventing surprise Anthropic API bills. Documented at [EXECUTOR_SHAPE.md §8](docs/internal/EXECUTOR_SHAPE.md).

### Fixed (M-EXEC-PI bonus)
- **`ailang-multivac/cloudbuild.yaml` now builds all 16 images (was 8).** The auto-trigger CI/CD pipeline previously built only the core 8 images (coordinator, dashboard, agent, agent-go, docparse, billing, website-builder, agent-base) and skipped the 8 executor variants (codex/codex-go/opencode/pi/gemini/gemini-go/eval/eval-go), which lived only in the manual `cloudbuild-images.yaml`. The drift surfaced when M-EXEC-PI's terraform-apply tried to create Cloud Run Jobs for variant images that hadn't been pushed by the same pipeline, producing `Image 'agent-X:latest' not found.` for ALL eight variants. Pre-existing Cloud Run Jobs survived only because someone had previously run `cloudbuild-images.yaml` manually — first deploy of any new variant would silently break until that drift was fixed. Now both files agree on the image set; `cloudbuild-images.yaml` stays as a manual image-only rebuild path.

### Contract refinement (M-EXEC-PI M7)
- [docs/internal/EXECUTOR_SHAPE.md](docs/internal/EXECUTOR_SHAPE.md) updated with the cloudbuild.yaml/cloudbuild-images.yaml drift lesson (§6: both files must be updated for any new variant), the cost-control rule for cloud secret bindings (§8), and an explicit "add to `knownVariants`" step (recipe step 9). Pi precedent now documented inline as the canonical multi-provider example.

### Fixed (M-CLI-ARGS-WIN, refs #199)
- **`ailang run -args-json` is now usable on PowerShell.** Two new non-quoting input paths bypass shell quote mangling:
  - `--args-file <path>` — read JSON arguments from a file (mirrors `--allow-env-file`)
  - `--args-json -` — read JSON arguments from stdin
  - Both strip a leading UTF-8 BOM (common from PowerShell `Set-Content`) and trim trailing whitespace
  - Specifying both `--args-json <non-default>` and `--args-file <path>` exits 2 with a clear error
  - Reported by docparse maintainer (ailang msg `msg_20260426_221627_0d287ba3`, GH #199)
  - Existing inline `--args-json '<json>'` invocations are unchanged
- **CI now runs on Windows.** New `test-windows` job runs `go test ./...` plus a PowerShell smoke that exercises both new input forms against `examples/docs/module_greet.ail`. Sibling to the existing Linux `test` job (not a matrix) to keep the change scoped to a hotfix; broader matrix conversion is future work.
  - **Maintainer follow-up:** mark `test-windows` as required in branch protection so Windows breakage blocks merges.

### Benchmark Results (M-EVAL)

Baseline run on the v0.14.2 tag plus subsequent infrastructure improvements
(Pi harness, codex CLI 0.125 upgrade, opencode-sonnet-4-6 swap, two benchmark
spec fixes). 1,178 total runs across standard + cross-harness + lang-harness
suites; **$93.23 cost**.

**Overall Performance**: 77.5% success rate (396 standard runs, 782 agent runs)

**Standard Eval (extended_suite, 6 models)**:

| Model | 0-shot | With Repair | Tokens |
|---|---|---|---|
| claude-opus-4-7 | 86.4% | 86.4% | 1.12M |
| **gpt5-5** (new flagship) | **83.3%** | **86.4%** | 763K |
| claude-sonnet-4-6 | 72.7% | 81.8% | 989K |
| gpt5-4-mini | 69.7% | 72.7% | 628K |
| gemini-3-1-pro | 60.6% | 71.2% | 894K |
| gemini-3-flash | 56.1% | 66.7% | 875K |

**Cross-Harness Agent Eval (harness_suite, 7 model/harness pairs)** — adjusted rates exclude API errors:

| Harness | AILANG | Python | API err | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Pi** (new) | **97%** | **97%** | **0.0%** | Cleanest harness — minimal-CLI approach pays off |
| opencode | 95% adj | 97% adj | 4.5% | 10x improvement vs v0.14.1 (was 48% api err) |
| codex (gpt5-5) | 100% adj | 100% adj | 16.7% | Codex CLI 0.125 upgrade dropped api errors from 47% → 17% |
| Claude CLI | 96% adj | 97% adj | 16.7% | Stable; small quota dips |
| Gemini CLI | 89% adj | 88% adj | 28.8% | Quota issues persist; -38pp api err vs v0.14.1 |

**Cross-Language (lang_harness_suite, JS+Go added)** — first release with full 4-language coverage:

| Harness | JavaScript | Go |
|---|---|---|
| **Claude CLI** | 83% | **94%** ⭐ |
| **opencode** | **83% (0 api_err)** | **89% (0 api_err)** ⭐ |
| Codex | 50% | 44% |
| Gemini CLI | 22% | 22% |

**Key Findings**:
- **Pi harness** delivers the best raw + adjusted pass rates with zero API errors — proving that minimal-CLI agents can outperform feature-rich ones on benchmark workloads
- **API error rates collapsed** vs v0.14.1: codex 47%→17%, opencode 48%→5%, gemini 67%→29% (codex CLI 0.125 + opencode-sonnet-4-6 swap as planned)
- **gpt5-5** ties claude-opus-4-7 on standard eval (86.4%) at significantly fewer tokens (763K vs 1.12M)
- **6 saturation candidates** identified for next release demotion: `effect_composition`, `effect_tracking_io_fs`, `expression_evaluator`, `higher_order_functions`, `merge_sort`, `state_machine_elevator` (all ≥95% adjusted on every dimension with data)

**μRAG A/B (`lang_harness × core × ailang`, 90 runs each arm)**:
- microrag=ON:  **97.4% adjusted pass rate** (38/39 non-api runs)
- microrag=OFF: **90.0% adjusted pass rate** (36/40 non-api runs)
- **Δ = +7.4pp from μRAG knowledge injection** on AILANG syntax learning
- Largest per-benchmark deltas: `config_file_parser` (+50pp), `cli_args` (+50pp). μRAG should remain on by default.

**Eval-driven prompt + parser improvements (M-EVAL-GAP-FIXES, planned for v0.15.0)**:
- Strengthened `prompts/v0.12.1.md` Rule #3 with bidirectional "function body style" guidance — `{ }` body uses `;`, `=` body uses `in`, mixing them causes the most common AILANG parse error in 0-shot. Added explicit "no markdown fences" rule (was silently breaking ~14 runs).
- Filed [M-EVAL-GAP-FIXES](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-eval-gap-fixes.md) for: Pi `config_file_parser` regression (only Pi failure observed), match-arm-block parser corner case (already noted as known bug), and folding the prompt change into the next active prompt revision.

## [Unreleased] - v0.15.1 (in progress)

**Design docs**:
- [M-BENCHMARK-SECTION-REDESIGN](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-benchmark-section-redesign.md)
- [M-EXECUTOR-VARIANTS](design_docs/planned/v1_1_0/m-executor-variants.md)

### Added (M-EXECUTOR-VARIANTS)
- **Per-agent Docker image variants** — replaces monolithic `agent:latest` with a matrix of slim images, each containing only the executor CLIs the agent actually needs:
  - `agent-base` — Node 22 + git + ailang binary. No executor CLIs. Shared layer for all variants.
  - `agent` — `agent-base` + Claude Code CLI only (~220MB vs ~540MB monolithic)
  - `agent-go` — `agent` + Go 1.25 toolchain. For sprint-executor on the ailang repo.
  - `agent-codex` — `agent-base` + OpenAI Codex CLI (`@openai/codex`)
  - `agent-codex-go` — `agent-codex` + Go toolchain
  - `agent-opencode` — `agent-base` + opencode CLI (`opencode-ai`)
  - `agent-gemini` — `agent-base` + Google Gemini CLI (`@google/gemini-cli`, no `|| true`)
  - `agent-gemini-go` — `agent-gemini` + Go toolchain
  - `agent-eval` — `agent-base` + all four CLIs (claude, gemini, codex, opencode)
  - `agent-eval-go` — `agent-eval` + Go toolchain
- **`executor_variant` field in agent config** — `AgentConfig.ExecutorVariant` wires per-agent variant selection through `DispatchParams` to the Cloud Run dispatcher
- **`jobSuffixForVariant(variant, authMode)`** in dispatcher — strict allowlist of known variants, returns descriptive error for unknowns. Replaces ad-hoc dual-suffix logic.
- **Cloud Run Job templates for all variants** (`ailang-multivac`) — each variant gets its own job template with the appropriate image and API key secrets (CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN, GOOGLE_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY as needed)
- **CI build DAG** (`cloudbuild-images.yaml`, `cloudbuild-trigger-ailang.yaml`) — sequential intermediate pushes ensure `FROM` references resolve: `agent-base → push → agent → push → agent-go`; codex, gemini, eval chains run in parallel after `push-agent-base`

### Added (M-BENCHMARK-SECTION-REDESIGN)
- **Multi-page benchmark section** — website benchmark section expanded from 1 page to 6:
  - `/benchmarks/overview` — headline metrics + per-language + per-harness summary cards
  - `/benchmarks/by-language` — heatmap table: languages × models, pass rate colour-coded, JS/Go rows appear automatically when results land
  - `/benchmarks/by-model` — focused model leaderboard with tier filter (uses existing `BenchmarkDashboard` with `view="model"`)
  - `/benchmarks/by-harness` — grouped by `model_family`, shows Δ delta rows (pass/cost/duration) between harnesses; degrades gracefully when no cross-harness results yet
  - `/benchmarks/performance` renamed to "Standard Eval Details" in sidebar
- **`DashboardJSON.Harnesses`** — new `harnesses` key in `latest.json` grouped by `agent_cli` value. Each entry: `name`, `display_name`, `models[]`, `total_runs`, `success_rate`, `avg_cost_usd`, `avg_duration_ms`, `languages{}`. Generated by `buildHarnessAggregates()` in `export_json_executors.go`.
- **`provider_type` + `timeout_scale` on model entries** — `"cloud"` for all non-Ollama models, `"local"` for `provider: "ollama"`. `timeout_scale` = `ttft_timeout / 30` (cloud default). Also surfaces `agent_cli` and `model_family` in model JSON entries.
- **`LocalCloudBadge` component** — inline ☁ Cloud / 🖥 Local (×N) badge driven by `provider_type` + `timeout_scale`.
- **`DimensionSelector` component** — shared tier/language/harness chip filter bar used across by-language and by-harness pages.
- **`LanguageLeaderboard` component** — pass-rate heatmap, reads language list dynamically from JSON (no hardcoding).
- **`HarnessComparisonTable` component** — groups by `model_family`, renders Δ rows. Ungrouped fallback when `model_family` absent.
- **`BenchmarkOverview` component** — landing page with headline MetricCards, per-language cards, per-harness cards, and links to detail pages.
- **Fixed broken links** in `docs/guides/microrag.md` (`./evaluation/README.md` → `./evaluation/`, skill SKILL.md links removed from docs tree).

**Design docs**:
- [M-BRAIN-BOOTSTRAP](design_docs/implemented/v0_15_1/m-brain-bootstrap.md)
- [M-EVAL-LANGREG](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-eval-langreg-sprint-plan.md)
- [M-EVAL-LANG-JSGO](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-eval-lang-jsgo-sprint-plan.md)

### Fixed
- **`tiers: {}` empty in dashboard JSON** — agent-only baselines (harness comparison runs with no standard-mode results) now correctly populate the `tiers` section. `ExportBenchmarkJSON` falls back to all results when `standardResults` is empty, so tier/tag aggregates are generated from agent results instead of being silently omitted.
- **`LanguageLeaderboard` data path** — `getRate()` was reading `ms?.languages?.[lang]?.success_rate` but `model_stats` entries store lang stats directly at `ms[lang].successRate` (no `.languages` wrapper, camelCase field). Fixed path corrects tier-filtered pass rates for the language heatmap.
- **Go/JavaScript language prompts** — added `prompts/go.md` and `prompts/javascript.md` so agents receive idiomatic language guidance (stdlib-only, correct entry point, execution command). Both wired via `prompts/versions.json` and loaded by `langreg.LoadSyntaxRef`.
- **Codex v0.1+ success detection** — `turn.completed` event sets `sawResult=true`; prior code only recognised old-format terminal events (`result`, `turn_complete`, `message_stop`), causing every codex run to return `Success=false` despite correct output.

### Refactored
- **Language registry (`internal/eval_harness/langreg`)** — new package replaces all
  `switch language { case "python": ... case "ailang": ... }` dispatch in the eval
  harness. `Language` interface encapsulates per-language template paths, solution
  filenames, display names, default prompts, syntax-ref loading, and runner
  construction. Registering a new eval language is now one file + one `init()` call
  instead of 8 scattered switch edits.
- **`internal/prompt.LoadPromptWithVersion`** — new helper returns `(content, versionUsed, error)`
  so callers no longer need to separately resolve the active version ID from the manifest.
- 8 `switch lang` blocks removed from `agent_prompt.go`, `agent_runner.go`, `runner.go`,
  `spec.go`. All dispatch now routes through `langreg.Get(name)`.
- Circular import avoided via factory vars (`SetPythonRunnerFactory` /
  `SetAILANGRunnerFactory`) wired from `langreg_wire.go` in the `eval_harness` package.

### Added (M-EVAL-LANG-JSGO)
- **JavaScript eval language** — `JSRunner` executes `node solution.js` in a temp dir with
  process-group isolation, LimitedWriter, and configurable timeout. Registered as `"javascript"`
  in langreg via `SetJSRunnerFactory`.
- **Go eval language** — `GoRunner` executes `go run solution.go` in a temp dir. Prepends
  `package main\n\n` automatically when absent. Registered as `"go"` via `SetGoRunnerFactory`.
- **`runSubprocess` helper** — shared subprocess runner used by JSRunner and GoRunner,
  eliminating code duplication (tmpDir isolation, SetProcessGroup, LimitedWriter, timeout).
- **Agent task templates** — `templates/agent_task_javascript.txt` and
  `templates/agent_task_go.txt` give agents language-specific verification steps and tips.
- **10 benchmark YAMLs updated** — fizzbuzz, recursion_fibonacci, graph_bfs, binary_tree_sum,
  balanced_parens, csv_to_json_converter, expression_evaluator, gcd_lcm, fold_reduce,
  higher_order_functions now include `"javascript"` and `"go"` in their `languages:` arrays.
- **20 reference solutions** — `examples/reference/<bench>/main.{js,go}` for all 10
  benchmarks; each produces byte-identical output to the YAML `expected_stdout`.
- **`TestReferenceSolutions_JS` / `TestReferenceSolutions_Go`** — integration tests that
  execute every reference solution through JSRunner/GoRunner and verify output exactness.

> **μRAG bootstrap for fresh installs.** v0.15.0 shipped the engine + hooks
> but left fresh installs (Claude Code plugin / Gemini CLI extension) with
> empty `ailang-syntax` / `ailang-builtins` namespaces — the hooks fired
> against an empty corpus and silently returned no content. The new
> `ailang micro-rag bootstrap` subcommand populates both namespaces using
> ONLY resources bundled in the binary (`//go:embed all:prompts` + the
> compiled-in builtin registry), so plugin users get a working μRAG
> immediately after `install.sh`. Source-repo developers continue to use
> the full-fat `tools/index_ailang_syntax.sh` for the optional
> `ailang-examples` and LIMITATIONS namespaces.

### Added
- **`ailang micro-rag bootstrap` subcommand** ([cmd/ailang/microrag.go](cmd/ailang/microrag.go)) —
  populates `ailang-syntax` (h2 chunks of the active embedded prompt) and
  `ailang-builtins` (one frame per registered builtin with formatted
  signature + module + effect + description). Default `--scope user`
  writes to `~/.ailang/state/brain.db` so a single bootstrap services
  every project on the host. Flags: `--reset`, `--scope user|project`,
  `--no-embed`, `--json`. Idempotent (stable `syntax-<version>-<slug>` /
  `builtin-<sanitized-name>` keys upsert on rerun). Graceful when Ollama
  is down: writes frames with `embedding_dim=0`, retrieval falls back to
  SimHash + FTS.
- **Auto-bootstrap during plugin install** ([ailang_bootstrap/skills/ailang/scripts/install.sh](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang_bootstrap/blob/main/skills/ailang/scripts/install.sh)) —
  appends `ailang micro-rag init && ailang micro-rag bootstrap --scope user --no-embed`
  after the binary lands. So `gemini extensions install …` /
  `/plugin install ailang@…` ends with a populated brain corpus and a
  working μRAG out of the box. No source repo required.
- **Unit tests** ([cmd/ailang/microrag_test.go](cmd/ailang/microrag_test.go)) —
  `TestChunkPromptByH2` (multi-section + slug normalisation + body-size filter),
  `TestSanitizeKey`, `TestBootstrapEmitsBuiltins` (against in-memory SQLite),
  `TestBootstrapGracefulNoOllama` (no embedder configured).
- **Guide updates** ([docs/docs/guides/microrag.md](docs/docs/guides/microrag.md)) —
  new "Bootstrap (Fresh-Install Corpus Population)" section documenting
  the subcommand, what gets indexed (and what doesn't — examples + LIMITATIONS
  deferred), default-scope rationale, idempotency guarantees, and the
  auto-install flow.

### Notes
- This is a strict subset of `tools/index_ailang_syntax.sh`. The shell
  script remains the "full-fat" indexer for source-repo developers (it
  can read `examples/runnable/*.ail` and `docs/LIMITATIONS.md` from disk,
  which the binary cannot).
- Examples corpus (`ailang-examples`) intentionally deferred — the
  `examples/` directory is not `go:embed`'d. Embedding it deserves its
  own discussion (binary size + whether examples should be release-pinned).
- The `ailang_bootstrap` plugin needs a sympathetic version bump
  (0.10.0 → 0.11.0) so the new install-script logic ships to plugin users.

## [Unreleased] - v0.15.0

**Design docs**:
- [M-BRAIN-MICRORAG](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-brain-microrag.md)
- [M-EXEC-EXPAND](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-exec-expand-codex-opencode.md)
- [M-MICRORAG-EXPAND](design_docs/planned/v0_15_0/m-microrag-hook-expansion.md) — μRAG hook coverage expansion (post-ADR-002)

### Added (M-MICRORAG-EXPAND)
- **`ailang micro-rag user-prompt` CLI** — UserPromptSubmit-driven μRAG entry
  point. Reuses the engine's search / dedup / session-budget logic, but the
  query is the user's natural-language prompt instead of file content. This
  is the embedding-query shape cosine similarity actually excels at — single
  dense intent-bearing sentence, no averaging over noisy code body.
  `internal/microrag/userprompt.go` (~190 LOC) + 13 unit tests.
- **Claude Code UserPromptSubmit hook** —
  `.claude/skills/microrag/frontends/claude-code/microrag_userprompt.sh`
  registered in `.claude/settings.json` alongside the existing
  `brain_on_prompt.sh`. Both fire and join their `additionalContext`.
- **opencode `postToolUse` builtin-lint** — extended the existing
  `internal/executor/opencode/plugins/microrag-plugin.ts` to call
  `ailang micro-rag lint-builtin` after Edit/Write/MultiEdit on `.ail`
  files and return the joined nudge text. Closes the harness-fairness gap
  the plugin's earlier `postToolUse` stub left open. 6 new tests.
- **Pi gap documented** — Pi's NDJSON event stream is observation-only with
  no plugin/extension surface for context injection; cross-harness fairness
  with the builtin-first-use μRAG nudge is currently unachievable on Pi.
  Tracked in `internal/executor/pi/README.md` Known Limits.

### Notes (M-MICRORAG-EXPAND)
- UserPromptSubmit relevance floor is 0.50, calibrated against the bootstrap
  corpus: clearly off-topic prompts ("what's the weather?") score ~0.45 from
  baseline English-vocabulary similarity; real syntax questions land 0.7+.
- ADR-002 stands. PreToolUse μRAG injection on `.ail` file content remains
  disabled in `~/.ailang/microrag.yaml` (`kb: skip`).

> **μRAG: harness-agnostic just-in-time knowledge injection.** Extends
> M-BRAIN (v0.9.2) and M-BRAIN-CONTEXT (v0.10.0) with a single engine in
> `internal/microrag` that all frontends — Claude Code hooks, MCP server,
> CLI — funnel through. Token-window dedup keeps the AI prompt-cache warm,
> per-namespace relevance bypass surfaces high-urgency snippets even when
> recall is fresh, and a first-use builtin lint nudges callers with
> signature + module before they guess wrong.

### Added
- **`internal/microrag` engine** — config-driven glob router (`~/.ailang/microrag.yaml`),
  per-namespace dedup windows + relevance bypass, search-result cache with
  4-min TTL aligned to Anthropic's 5-min prompt-cache TTL, dryrun mode for
  safe rollout.
- **`ailang micro-rag context` CLI** — primary engine entry point. Reads
  `tool_name`/`file_path`/`content` flags or stdin JSON; emits
  `ContextResult` JSON envelope.
- **`ailang micro-rag lint-builtin` CLI** — first-use builtin nudges. Regex
  candidate extraction → `ailang builtins show --json` → ≤80-token signature
  block. Per-session `builtins_seen.txt` ledger.
- **`ailang micro-rag index` CLI** — bootstrap script for the
  `ailang-syntax` corpus. Indexes prompt corpus + `docs/LIMITATIONS.md` +
  all 253 builtins. Idempotent (stable keys), re-runnable on prompt-version
  bump.
- **Claude Code hooks** — `~/.ailang/hooks/microrag_context.sh`
  (PreToolUse: Edit | Write | Read | MultiEdit) and
  `~/.ailang/hooks/microrag_lint.sh` (PostToolUse: Edit | Write | MultiEdit).
  macOS-friendly `gtimeout` → `timeout` → unbounded fallback. Exit-0 on any
  internal failure (graceful degradation — never block tool calls).
- **MCP server `cmd/ailang-microrag-mcp`** — MCP stdio frontend exposing
  the same engine to Cursor / Continue / Cline / any MCP-aware harness.
  Two tools: `microrag_context_for_file`, `microrag_lint_builtin`.
- **Eval-suite `--microrag=on|off|auto` flag** — A/B comparison primitive.
  `on`/`off` strips inherited `AILANG_MICRORAG_ENABLED` env and forces the
  value in subprocess env (both direct fork paths and executor.BuildEnvironment
  paths). `auto` honours caller env. New `microrag_state` field in
  `RunMetrics` records the resolved value per benchmark for downstream
  analysis.
- **OTEL telemetry** — `microrag.context` and `microrag.lint` spans on
  every engine call, attributes for state, reason, namespace, tokens, score.
  Flow through the existing telemetry pipeline (no new exporter wiring).
- **User docs** — `docs/docs/guides/microrag.md` covering Claude Code
  setup, Cursor/Continue/Cline setup, configuration, eval toggle,
  reindexing, and troubleshooting.

### Notes
- The 5,000-token session budget is calibrated against the bumped
  per-namespace dedup windows (15K / 30K / 80K) — much higher than the
  v0.10.0 brain_context defaults — to avoid cache-thrashing under the
  larger windows.
- One-week dogfooding window starts when `.claude/settings.json` ships.
  AI prompt-cache hit rate ≥0.6 over 5-edit bursts and manual relevance
  ≥70% on a 50-sample audit are the gating metrics for promotion to
  global `~/.claude/settings.json`.
- **Codex + opencode executors** — Added `internal/executor/codex` and
  `internal/executor/opencode` as first-class CLI-subprocess executors
  following the uniform EXECUTOR_SHAPE.md contract. Both use `init()` →
  `executor.GlobalFactory().Register()` self-registration; the coordinator
  and eval harness pick them up with zero coordinator-code changes (one
  blank import each). `agent_suite` now routes benchmarks through all four
  harnesses: claude / gemini / codex / opencode.
- **`internal/executor/codex`** — Codex CLI executor: NDJSON stream parser
  (schema: `session`, `message`, `tool_use`, `tool_result`, `result`); cumulative
  token accumulation (MAX semantics per OpenAI API); `--json` /
  `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` / `--skip-git-repo-check` flags;
  75% test coverage; HealthCheck via `codex --version`.
- **`internal/executor/opencode`** — opencode executor: NDJSON stream parser
  (schema: `step_start`, `text`, `tool_use`, `step_finish`); per-step delta token
  accumulation (SUM semantics); `--format json` / `--dangerously-skip-permissions`
  flags; session resume via `--session <sessionID>` (M-TRANSCRIPT pattern);
  `provider/model` format supports Anthropic / OpenAI / Google / local Ollama;
  80% test coverage; HealthCheck via `opencode --version`.
- **Ollama local model support** — opencode executor routes to local Ollama
  models via `ollama/<model>:latest` model strings (e.g. `ollama/gemma4:latest`).
  Config template at `internal/executor/opencode/testdata/opencode_ollama_config.jsonc`.
- **`docs/internal/EXECUTOR_SHAPE.md`** — uniform CLI-subprocess executor contract
  documenting the 4-element recipe (package layout, required symbols, coordinator
  wiring, models.yml wiring) so future executors (aider, cline, etc.) plug in
  trivially.
- **μRAG frontends for Gemini CLI, Codex CLI, and opencode** (M4A + M7A):
  - Gemini CLI: bash hooks (`microrag_context.sh` / `microrag_lint.sh`) at
    `.claude/skills/microrag/frontends/gemini/`; registered via `.gemini/settings.json`.
  - Codex CLI: same bash hooks at `.claude/skills/microrag/frontends/codex/`;
    registered via `.codex/hooks.json`.
  - opencode: TypeScript plugin at `internal/executor/opencode/plugins/microrag-plugin.ts`
    exporting `preToolUse` / `postToolUse` / `sessionStart`; registered in
    `~/.config/opencode/opencode.jsonc`. Install to `~/.config/opencode/plugins/`.
- **`agent_suite` composite** — `ailang eval-suite --models agent_suite` now
  enumerates 4 harness runs (claude-sonnet-4-6 / gemini-3-flash / gpt5-4 /
  opencode-haiku). `--dry-run` flag shows routing table without execution.
- **7 Codex model entries in models.yml** resolved from `agent_cli: null` to
  `agent_cli: "codex"` (gpt5, gpt5-mini, gpt5-1, gpt5-1-instant, gpt5-2,
  gpt5-2-instant, gpt5-4).

## [v0.14.1] - 2026-04-21

**Design docs**:
- [M-SUPPLY-CHAIN-HARDENING](design_docs/implemented/v0_14_1/m-supply-chain-hardening.md)
- [M-SONAR-GATE-CLEANUP](design_docs/implemented/v0_14_1/m-sonar-gate-cleanup.md)

> **Supply-chain hardening release.** OpenSSF Scorecard response: release
> artifacts are now signed keyless with Sigstore cosign and verified
> automatically by the installer. Provenance is independently auditable via
> the Rekor transparency log. Checked-in binaries removed; branch protection
> and least-privilege workflow permissions enforced.

### Added
- **Sigstore cosign keyless signing on release artifacts** — `.github/workflows/release.yml`
  installs cosign v2.4.1 via `sigstore/cosign-installer@v3.7.0`, signs every
  `.tar.gz` / `.zip` and the aggregate `SHA256SUMS` via GitHub Actions OIDC
  → Fulcio CA, and uploads `<archive>.sig` + `<archive>.pem` alongside the
  archives. The signing identity is bound to
  `https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/.github/workflows/release.yml@refs/tags/v.*`
  — a valid signature proves the binary was built by this repo's release
  workflow for a `v*` tag, not "some Action somewhere".
- **Installer cosign-first verification (`docs/static/install.sh`)** —
  verification order is cosign → SHA256 → abort (unless `NO_VERIFY=1` is set
  explicitly). If cosign is installed and the release has `.sig`/`.pem`,
  signature is verified with certificate identity + OIDC issuer pinning.
  Otherwise falls back to per-archive `<archive>.sha256`. Missing both
  refuses to install rather than silently skipping.
- **SECURITY.md** — vulnerability reporting policy (GitHub Security
  Advisories preferred, email fallback), scope + out-of-scope, SLA
  (acknowledge 3d / assess 7d / critical-fix 14d), manual
  `cosign verify-blob` verification recipe.
- **OpenSSF Scorecard workflow** — `.github/workflows/scorecard.yml`
  triggers on push to `dev` + weekly schedule, publishes results to the
  Security tab.
- **CodeQL Go SAST** — `.github/workflows/codeql.yml` runs on push/PR +
  weekly schedule.
- **Core tier is the headline metric (from v0.14.0 prep)** —
  `tiers.core.ailang_success_rate` in `docs/static/benchmarks/latest.json`.
  Dashboard tier selector defaults to Core. See v0.14.0 section below for
  the full eval-suite reorganisation (tiers + 12-tag taxonomy).

### Changed
- **Module path renamed** `github.com/sunholo/ailang` →
  `github.com/sunholo-data/ailang` so the module resolves via
  proxy.golang.org and Go Report Card works. All 767 Go files updated
  atomically.
- **Installer refuses to install unverified archives** — previously
  continued with a warning; now aborts unless `NO_VERIFY=1`. A silent-skip
  check is worse than no check.
- **Least-privilege workflow permissions** — all workflows declare
  top-of-file `permissions: contents: read` and only grant `write` on
  specific jobs that need it (`dependency-submission`, `create-release`
  with `id-token: write` for cosign OIDC).

### Removed
- **Checked-in binaries** — `docs/static/wasm/ailang.wasm`,
  `web/ailang.wasm`, `examples/sim_stub/sim_stub`,
  `examples/sim_stub/sim_stub_release`. Regenerated by build workflows
  (`make build-wasm` in `docusaurus-deploy.yml`; `make test` rebuilds
  sim_stub in `examples/sim_stub/`). Closes Scorecard Binary-Artifacts.
- **README auto-commit step in `ci.yml`** — the
  `stefanzweifel/git-auto-commit-action@v7` step auto-pushed README
  badge updates on every dev push. Once branch protection landed in
  this sprint, its push was rejected with GH006. Dropped entirely; run
  `make update-readme` before releases instead (the release-manager
  skill already does).

### Security
- Release archives from v0.14.1 onwards can be verified with cosign
  without a key (keyless, via OIDC). See SECURITY.md for the manual
  `cosign verify-blob` invocation.

### Dependency updates
- `cloud.google.com/go/firestore` 1.21.0 → 1.22.0 (#185)
- `@mermaid-js/layout-elk` 0.1.9 → 0.2.1 then **reverted** (#193 breaks
  the Docusaurus `@docusaurus/theme-mermaid` peer-optional; kept at 0.1.9)
- `cloud.google.com/go/kms` 1.26.0 → 1.29.0 (#187)
- `go.opentelemetry.io/contrib/instrumentation/net/http/otelhttp`
  minor bump (#188)
- `golang.org/x/term` 0.41.0 → 0.42.0 (#189)
- `github.com/googleapis/gax-go/v2` 2.21.0 → 2.22.0 (#190)
- `ui/*` all-minor-patch group (#191)

Rejected (peer-dep conflicts with pinned toolchain; tracked for a
coordinated upgrade): #192 (vitest 4), #195 (react 19), #186
(plugin-react 6), #194 (recharts+mermaid 0.2.1 group).

## [v0.14.0] - 2026-04-21

**Design doc**: [M-EVAL-SUITE-PREP](design_docs/implemented/v0_14_0/m-eval-suite-prep.md)
**Sprint plan**: [design_docs/implemented/v0_14_0/m-eval-suite-prep-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_14_0/m-eval-suite-prep-sprint-plan.md)

> **Eval suite reorganisation.** The benchmark corpus is now structured into
> four tiers — `smoke`, `core`, `stretch`, `vision` — with a canonical 12-tag
> taxonomy describing the AILANG feature surface each benchmark exercises.
> The **Core tier** is the new headline metric: `tiers.core.ailang_success_rate`
> in `docs/static/benchmarks/latest.json`, currently **87.3% AILANG vs 81.0%
> Python** on the v0.12.0 baseline.

### Added
- **Tier/tag schema on benchmark YAMLs (M1–M2)** — `tier: smoke|core|stretch|vision`
  and `tags: [...]` are now required fields (tier defaults to `core`, tags
  validated against the canonical 12-tag list at load time in
  `internal/eval_harness/spec.go`). All 51 existing benchmarks tagged; 2 new
  hard benchmarks added (`polymorphic_ord_defaulting`, `typed_refusal`).
- **`ailang eval-suite --tier`** — comma-separated tier filter (e.g.
  `--tier core,stretch`). Applied after benchmark discovery; empty = all.
- **`ailang eval-matrix` report flags (M3)** —
  `--by-tags` emits per-tag AILANG vs Python delta table,
  `--show-saturated` lists benchmarks at 100% across all model × language
  pairs, `--ailang-wins` lists cells where AILANG passes and Python fails.
- **Analysis primitives (M4)** — `internal/eval_analysis` now exports
  `IsSaturated`, `AILANGOnlyWins`, `DetectRefusal`, and per-tier aggregate
  helpers. All exercise >20-iteration determinism tests (`-count=20`).
- **Make targets (M5)** — `make eval-smoke` / `eval-core` / `eval-stretch`
  run a single tier end-to-end against the dev model pool.
- **Dashboard per-tier toggle (M6)** — `BenchmarkDashboard` component renders
  a tier selector; Core is the default and its pass rate is the hero metric.
  `DashboardJSON` carries `Tiers map[string]TierAggregate`, and every
  benchmark entry in `latest.json` now includes `tier` + `tags` fields.
- **Curation guide (M7)** — [`benchmarks/CURATION.md`](benchmarks/CURATION.md)
  documents tier definitions, the tag taxonomy, rotation rules, and
  promotion/demotion criteria. Eval-analyzer skill gains two scripts:
  `category_analysis.sh` (per-tier + per-tag + AILANG-only-wins, one shot)
  and `benchmark_health.sh` (saturation + refusal + tier promotion signal).
- **Evaluation guide** updated with a tier-structure section describing how
  `make eval-smoke|core|stretch` map onto the CI workflow.

### Changed
- **Core tier is now the headline metric.** Release notes and dashboard both
  quote `tiers.core.ailang_success_rate`. Previous aggregate pass-rate numbers
  (overall across all benchmarks) remain available but are no longer
  featured.

### Internal
- `DashboardJSON.Tiers` populated in `internal/eval_analysis/export_json.go`
  alongside the benchmark loop, so tier aggregates and per-benchmark
  tier/tags come from a single source of truth.

## [v0.13.0] - 2026-04-20

**Design doc**: [M-CONCAT-DISAMBIG](design_docs/implemented/v0_13_0/m-concat-disambiguation.md)
**Sprint plan**: [design_docs/implemented/v0_13_0/m-concat-disambiguation-sprint-plan.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_13_0/m-concat-disambiguation-sprint-plan.md)
**Handover notes**: [design_docs/implemented/v0_13_0/m-concat-disambig-handover.md](design_docs/implemented/v0_13_0/m-concat-disambig-handover.md) (M6–M8 mid-sprint inter-session handover)

> **M-CONCAT-DISAMBIG Phase 2 (M4): `++` is now list-only.** The `++` operator
> no longer accepts strings. String-typed operands produce a type error with a
> one-line hint pointing to interpolation, `concat([...])`, or `join(sep, xs)`.
> Phase 1 (string interpolation) shipped in v0.12.1 — any remaining `"a" ++ "b"`
> usages must migrate to `"${a}${b}"` or `concat([a, b])`.

### Changed
- **Restricted `++` to lists only (`M4_CONCAT_STDLIB_LIST_ONLY`)** — The 118-line
  string-vs-list disambiguation heuristic in `typechecker_operators.go` has been
  deleted. `++` now unifies both operands with `[a]` directly; string heads produce:
  ```
  ++ operator at file:L:C: `++` is for lists only.
  For strings use "${expr}" interpolation, concat([parts]), or join(sep, parts).
  ```
  `op_lowering.go` default suffix for `OpConcat` is now `List`, and `op_table.go`
  removes `String` from supported types. Net compiler reduction: ~82 lines.

### Added
- **`std/string.concat`** — New `concat: [string] -> string` builtin backed by
  `_str_join(xs, "")` (O(n) single-allocation via Go `strings.Builder`).
  `std/string.repeat` now uses the same path (via an internal `_repeat_list`
  helper) to avoid relying on string `++`.

### Migrated
- Stdlib (`std/net.ail`, `std/jwt.ail`) — error-message builders use interpolation.
- Internal fixtures (`internal/dashboard_transforms/event_formatter.ail`,
  `heatmap_test.ail`, `pkg/testdata/multi_module/src/core.ail`, the
  `internal/apiserver/templates/web_app/api/handlers.ail` skeleton, and the
