  inline `.ail` strings in `internal/repl/*_test.go` +
  `internal/apiserver/server_test.go`) all use `${...}` interpolation.

### Removed (`M5_GO_TEST_MIGRATION`)
- `internal/pipeline/concat_operator_test.go` — deleted 4 tests asserting old
  string-`++` behavior (`TestConcatRecursiveString`, `TestConcatConcreteString`,
  `TestConcatStringVarPlusConcreteString`, `TestConcatNestedRecursion`), plus
  the now-unreachable skipped `TestConcatMixedTypes`. List-concat tests remain.
- `internal/pipeline/op_lowering_test.go` / `op_lowering_regression_test.go` —
  dropped `concat_String` cases from `TestOpLowering_Concat` and
  `TestConcatOperator_TypeGuidedLowering`; fallback path test updated to expect
  `concat_List` default (previously `concat_String`).
- Added `TestStdlibConcatSignature` — positive-path contract test verifying
  `concat: [string] -> string` type-checks via `_str_join(xs, "")`.

### Migrated (`M6_EXAMPLE_MIGRATION`, `M7_BENCHMARK_PROMPT_DOCS`)
- **`examples/runnable/` sweep** — 82 files migrated automatically via
  `tools/migrate_string_concat.py` (atom-walker: rewrites any `++` chain that
  contains a string literal to `"${...}"` interpolation, leaving pure-list
  chains untouched). 4 files manually fixed (`conway_grid.ail`,
  `directory_ops.ail`, `game_npc_dialogue.ail`, `json_array_extraction.ail`);
  `map_basic.ail` required let-binding a `match`/`if` before interpolation.
  Final: `make verify-examples` = 164 passed, 1 skipped, **0 failed**.
- **Benchmarks** — 5 workload `.ail` files migrated (`warm_eval.ail`,
  `typecheck_heavy.ail`, `effect_roundtrip.ail`, `runtime/string_pipeline.ail`,
  `cross_boundary.ail`) and `benchmarks/symbolic_diff.yml` expected output
  rewritten to use interpolation.
- **Teaching prompts** — `cmd/ailang/prompts/v0.12.1.md`,
  `prompts/agent/v0.9.0.md`, `prompts/devtools/v0.8.0.md` (and mirror copies)
  now show `++` as list-only and `"${expr}"` / `concat([parts])` / `join(sep,
  parts)` for string building. Operator table split: List (`++`, `::`) vs
  String (`"${expr}"`, `concat`, `join`). All 6 `versions.json` SHA256 hashes
  refreshed; emoji preservation fixed (`ensure_ascii=False`).
- **Docs sweep** — 30+ files under `docs/docs/` migrated, including
  `reference/language-syntax.md`, `why-ailang.mdx`,
  `guides/semantic-caching-how-to.mdx`, `guides/quick-start-examples.mdx`,
  `guides/getting-started.mdx`. `docs/llms.txt` regenerated.
- **Golden fixtures** — `tests/golden/codegen/string_ops.ail` migrated (M8).

### Breaking Changes (v0.13.0)
- **`"a" ++ "b"` is now a compile-time type error.** All downstream AILANG
  code must migrate to `"${a}${b}"` or `concat(["a", "b"])`. Affected
  ecosystem surfaces identified: ~30 files across 19 packages in the
  `sunholo/ailang-packages/` registry — broadcast message sent.

## [v0.12.1] - 2026-04-20

> **M-CONCAT-DISAMBIG Phase 1: String interpolation lands.** `"Hello, ${name}!"` now
> desugars to a `concat_String` chain with auto-`show()` wrapping. Zero-regression:
> plain strings (no `${`) still tokenize as a single `STRING`, and escaped `\${` is
> preserved as a literal. Phase 2 (restricting `++` to lists only) ships in v0.13.0.

### Added
- **String interpolation (`M1_LEXER_INTERP`, `M2_PARSER_TYPECHECK_INTERP`)** —
  Double-quoted strings now accept `${expr}` substitution. The lexer emits a
  `STRING_PART` / `INTERP_START` / expr tokens / `INTERP_END` / `STRING_PART`
  sequence; the parser desugars that to a left-associative chain of
  `concat_String(prefix, show(expr))` calls. `show_String` is identity at
  runtime, so string-typed expressions are not double-quoted.

  ```ailang
  let name = "Alice" in
  let age = 30 in
  println("Hello, ${name}! Next year you will be ${age + 1}.")
  -- → Hello, Alice! Next year you will be 31.
  ```

  Supports:
  - Arbitrary expressions inside `${...}` (arithmetic, function calls, let-blocks, records)
  - Nested braces via brace-count tracking (`"${compute({a: 1}.a)}"`)
  - Escaped `\${literal}` → literal `${literal}` (no interpolation)
  - Plain strings with no `${` → single `STRING` token, zero regression

  Evaluator, elaborator, and codegen required zero changes — the desugar tree
  uses only existing AST nodes (`FuncCall`, `Identifier`, `Literal`).

  New examples: `examples/runnable/string_interpolation.ail`,
  `examples/runnable/string_interp_nested.ail`.

  This phase ships ahead of `M-CONCAT-DISAMBIG` Phase 2 (restricting `++` to
  lists). The teaching prompt now recommends `"${expr}"` as the primary
  string-building pattern; `"..." ++ show(x)` still works during the v0.12.x
  deprecation window.

## [v0.12.0] - 2026-04-14

> New stdlib modules (`std/gzip`, `std/tar`), three-tier OTEL tracing (`--trace-tier off|standard|deep`), and major XLSX runtime-hotspot wins (6.6× speedup on `poi_many_merges.xlsx` from `M-PERF6-PHASE4` M1 + M2a).

### Changed
- **Default tracing is now `standard`, not implicit-deep (`M-OBS-TRACE-TRIAGE`)** —
  Every `ailang run` with telemetry enabled used to emit one OTEL span per
  function invocation (avg 8.7 KB attrs each), filling the Observatory DB at
  ~730 MB/day with data no consumer queries. Investigated (252K spans / 1.93 GB
  traced to `eval.function.*` emission; 0 of 221K `eval.*` spans had `task_id`,
  `chain_id`, `tokens_in/out`, or `cost_usd` populated) and confirmed no reader
  queries these by name. Introduced three tracing tiers:

  | Tier | Emits | Default |
  |---|---|---|
  | `off` | nothing (same as `AILANG_NO_TRACE=1`) | |
  | `standard` | `eval.module.*`, top-level `eval.effect.*` (Depth≤1), contracts, budget, errors | ✅ |
  | `deep` | standard + per-call `eval.function.*` + nested `eval.effect.*` | opt-in |

  Controls (CLI wins over env wins over legacy alias):
  - `--trace-tier off|standard|deep` (CLI flag on `ailang run`)
  - `AILANG_TRACE=off|standard|deep` (env var)
  - `AILANG_NO_TRACE=1` (legacy, kept as alias for `off`)
  - `AILANG_TRACE_MAX_SPANS=N` (per-trace budget, default 500). When exceeded,
    emitter produces exactly one `trace.truncated` rollup span with
    `{budget, kept_count, dropped_count, first_dropped_name}` attrs.

  **Migration**: existing eval/benchmark workflows unaffected — they set
  `AILANG_NO_TRACE=1` via `make/eval.mk` already. Devs who relied on implicit
  per-call spans should run with `AILANG_TRACE=deep` or `--trace-tier deep`.

  Files: [internal/trace/options.go](../internal/trace/options.go),
  [internal/trace/otel_emitter.go](../internal/trace/otel_emitter.go),
  [cmd/ailang/main_run.go](../cmd/ailang/main_run.go).
  Design doc: [`design_docs/planned/v0_11_4/m-obs-trace-triage.md`](../design_docs/planned/v0_11_4/m-obs-trace-triage.md).
  Sprint: [`sprint_M-OBS-TRACE-TRIAGE.json`](../.ailang/state/sprints/sprint_M-OBS-TRACE-TRIAGE.json).

### Added
- **stdlib: `std/gzip` and `std/tar` modules (`M-STDLIB-TAR-GZIP`)** —
  Requested by [AILANG Parse (cli agent)](../design_docs/implemented/v0_12_0/m-stdlib-tar-gzip.md)
  to complete the no-shell-out story for document parsing. arXiv source
  bundles ship as `.tar.gz`, so `docparse` previously had to pre-extract
  before invoking AILANG. Two new stdlib modules modelled on `std/zip`
  close this gap.
  - **`std/gzip`** ([stdlib](../std/gzip.ail),
    [builtins](../internal/builtins/gzip.go)): `decompress(b64) -> Result`,
    `compress(b64, level) -> Result`, `decompressFile(path) -> Result !
    {FS}`. Binary data crosses the AILANG boundary as base64 (matches
    `std/zip`). Gzip-bomb defence via `io.LimitReader` capped at 100MB
    decompressed size. Compression level validated to `[-1, 9]`.
  - **`std/tar`** ([stdlib](../std/tar.ail),
    [builtins](../internal/builtins/tar.go)): `listEntries`, `readEntry`,
    `readEntryBytes`, `extractAll`, `readFromGzip`, `readFromGzipBytes` —
    all `! {FS}`. Entry cap 10K, per-entry size cap 100MB. `extractAll`
    rejects tarbomb payloads (`../` entries, absolute paths), symlinks
    and hard links; path escape is verified via `filepath.Rel(absDest,
    target)` after cleaning. `readFromGzip` pulls a single entry straight
    from a `.tar.gz` with no temp file — the primary path for arXiv
    ingestion. Respects `AILANG_FS_SANDBOX`.
  - Tests ([gzip](../internal/builtins/gzip_test.go),
    [tar](../internal/builtins/tar_test.go)) use Go-built in-memory
    fixtures (no binary files in the repo). Pure gzip builtins verified
    determinism-safe under `-count=20`. Runnable example:
    [`examples/runnable/tar_gzip_reader.ail`](../examples/runnable/tar_gzip_reader.ail).
  - Design doc: [`design_docs/implemented/v0_12_0/m-stdlib-tar-gzip.md`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_12_0/m-stdlib-tar-gzip.md)
    (axiom net score +10). Sprint:
    [`sprint_M-STDLIB-TAR-GZIP.json`](../.ailang/state/sprints/sprint_M-STDLIB-TAR-GZIP.json).

### Performance
- **eval: skip redundant `FallbackResolver` chain growth on cross-module calls (`M-PERF6-PHASE4` M2a)** —
  Profile-guided follow-up to M1. The post-M1 CPU profile on
  [`poi_many_merges.xlsx`](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang-parse/blob/main/data/test_files/stress/poi_many_merges.xlsx)
  showed `FallbackResolver.ResolveValue` consuming **64.52% flat** of runtime
  (251s out of 417s). Root cause: each cross-module function application in
  [`eval_operations.go`](../internal/eval/eval_operations.go) wrapped
  `e.resolver` in a new `FallbackResolver` even when the existing chain
  already had a path to the callee's defining resolver — every name lookup
  on a 140K-cell XLSX hot loop walked an unnecessarily deep chain. Fix: a
  new `resolverCovers(chain, target)` helper in
  [`eval_evaluator.go`](../internal/eval/eval_evaluator.go) walks the
  existing chain once; if `target` is reachable as any Primary or Secondary,
  the wrap is skipped. Applied at all three function-application sites
  (auto-curry, full apply, `applyFunction`). **Measured: 417.31s → 64.46s
  on `poi_many_merges.xlsx` — a 6.5× speedup from a 5-line change.** Total
  improvement vs pre-M1 baseline: 425.45s → 64.46s (**6.6×**). The new top
  hotspot is GC scan (`gcDrain` 41% cum) and `listConcatImpl` (13% cum) —
  both at much smaller absolute cost. EPUB benchmarks unchanged: Alice 1.98s
  ≤ 2s target, Moby Dick 2.75s ≤ 3s target. Tests: `make test` (eval pkg)
  ✅, `make verify-examples` 161/161 ✅.

- **eval: universal `Environment.Clone` → `NewChildEnvironment` in function application (`M-PERF6-PHASE4` M1)** —
  Reported by [msg `e234c455`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_12_0/m-perf6-runtime-hotspots.md)
  (ailang-parse XLSX perf). At each of 5 function-application sites in
  [`internal/eval/eval_evaluator.go`](../internal/eval/eval_evaluator.go)
  and [`internal/eval/eval_operations.go`](../internal/eval/eval_operations.go),
  swapped `fn.Env.Clone()` (O(n) copy of the defining environment) for
  `fn.Env.NewChildEnvironment()` (O(1) — empty child scope that delegates
  lookups to the parent chain). Semantically equivalent because AILANG never
  mutates existing bindings in a shared parent env; parameters write only into
  the fresh child scope. The design doc's per-closure `PureCapture` flag was
  dropped as unnecessary — the universal swap is provably safe without it
  (saves ~150 LOC vs original plan). Measured wins on this machine
  (macOS arm64, `AILANG_NO_TRACE=1`): `poi_many_merges.xlsx` 425.45s →
  408.78s (**-3.9%**); DocParse EPUB benchmarks unchanged within noise
  (Alice 1.98s ≤ 2s target, Moby Dick 2.75s ≤ 3s target). Smaller than the
  ~14% projected from a stale 5.27s CPU profile — the real XLSX hotspot at
  408s is outside the evaluator (likely `map(parseXlsxCell, cells)` over
  140K cells in the xlsx_parser itself); follow-up investigation deferred.
  Design: [`design_docs/implemented/v0_12_0/m-perf6-runtime-hotspots.md`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_12_0/m-perf6-runtime-hotspots.md),
  sprint plan: [`design_docs/implemented/v0_12_0/m-perf6-phase4-sprint-plan.md`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_12_0/m-perf6-phase4-sprint-plan.md).

### Benchmark Results (M-EVAL)

**Overall Performance**: 81.2% success rate (612 standard runs + 180 agent runs)

**Standard Eval (0-shot + self-repair):**

| Metric | v0.11.2 | v0.12.0 | Change |
|--------|--------|--------|--------|
| **0-shot (first attempt)** | 52.0% | 75.5% (299/396) | **+23.5pp** |
| **Final (with repair)** | 56.8% | 79.7% (316/396) | **+22.9pp** |
| **Repair effectiveness** | +4.8pp | +4.2pp | −0.6pp |
| **Python (final)** | 69.0% | 77.0% (305/396) | +8.0pp |

**Per-model standard eval (AILANG / Python):**

| Rank | Model | AILANG | Python | 0-shot | Final |
|------|-------|-------:|-------:|-------:|------:|
| 1 | claude-opus-4-7 | 86.3% | 82.4% | 82.4% | 84.3% |
| 1 | gpt5-4 | 86.3% | 82.4% | 80.4% | 84.3% |
| 3 | claude-sonnet-4-6 | 86.3% | 80.4% | 70.6% | 83.3% |
| 4 | gemini-3-1-pro | 82.4% | 78.4% | 71.6% | 80.4% |
| 5 | gpt5-2-codex | 76.5% | 80.4% | 75.5% | 78.4% |
| 6 | gemini-3-flash | 82.4% | 70.6% | 71.6% | 76.5% |

**Agent Eval (multi-turn):**

| Executor | AILANG | Python |
|----------|-------:|-------:|
| claude-sonnet-4-5 (Claude Code) | 97.7% (43/44) | 97.7% (43/44) |
| gemini-3-flash (Gemini CLI) | 39.1% (18/46) | 43.5% (20/46) |

**Key findings:**
- Claude Opus 4.7 (new this release) tied with GPT-5.4 at the top (84.3% final), at 3× the cost.
- AILANG matches or beats Python across all 6 models (the gap closed vs prior versions).
- Claude Code in agent mode is near-perfect on both languages (97.7%), suggesting multi-turn feedback closes the new-language learning curve.
- Total eval cost: $21.87 across 612 standard runs.

## [v0.11.4] - 2026-04-14

> Consolidates the unreleased v0.11.3 hotfix (short-circuit `&&`/`||`, `std/iter` + bounded XML folds) with the M-POLY-ORD defaulting fix and a refreshed teaching prompt.

### Added
- **prompts: v0.11.4 teaching prompt** — Refreshed `ailang prompt` covering all v0.10.x and v0.11.x additions: short-circuit semantics, `std/iter` + `FoldStep`, `std/map` (10 builtins), `std/jwt` (RS256 verification), `std/trace` (custom OTEL spans), `std/io.exit`, high-performance string builtins (`decodeQuotedPrintable`, `replaceMany`, `foldSlices`, `mapSlicesJoin`, `startsWithIgnoreCase`), and polymorphic `Ord`/`Eq` lambdas. Registered as active in [`prompts/versions.json`](../prompts/versions.json).
- **stdlib: `std/iter` with `FoldStep[a]` + bounded-prefix XML folds (`M-V0_11_3-HOTFIX` M2)** —
  Reported by [msg `80f142f6`](../design_docs/planned/v0_11_3/m-parsefold-early-termination.md):
  extracting 5K rows out of a 50K-row XLSX sharedStrings.xml took 5.5s
  because `parseFold` scanned the entire document regardless of accumulator
  state. New [`std/iter`](../std/iter.ail) module exports
  `FoldStep[a] = Continue(a) | Stop(a)` — handlers passed to bounded folds
  return `Stop(acc')` to halt immediately. Two new builtins thread this
  through the streaming XML decoder:
  * [`_xml_parseFoldStep`](../internal/builtins/xml_fold.go) — pure,
    over a string.
  * [`_zip_xml_scanFoldStep`](../internal/builtins/zip_xml.go) — effectful,
    piped directly from a ZIP entry stream (never materializes the
    decompressed XML).
  Shared `scanForElementsFoldStepInner` propagates a stop flag through
  recursive descent so nested non-matching elements also short-circuit.
  Surfaced as AILANG wrappers `parseFoldStep` in [`std/xml`](../std/xml.ail)
  and `scanFoldStep` in [`std/zip`](../std/zip.ail). Existing `parseFold`
  and `scanFold` signatures are unchanged. Four unit tests in
  [`internal/builtins/xml_foldstep_test.go`](../internal/builtins/xml_foldstep_test.go)
  (StopHalts, ContinueOnlyEquivalentToFold, StopAtFirst, BadReturnType)
  plus runnable [`examples/bounded_xml_fold.ail`](../examples/bounded_xml_fold.ail).
  Design: [`design_docs/planned/v0_11_3/m-parsefold-early-termination.md`](../design_docs/planned/v0_11_3/m-parsefold-early-termination.md).

### Fixed
- **types: polymorphic `Ord`/`Eq` lambdas silently monomorphized to `Int` (`M-POLY-ORD` M2)** —
  Polymorphic let-bound lambdas using only comparison/equality operators
  (e.g. `let max = \x.\y. if x > y then x else y in max(3.14)(2.71)`)
  crashed at runtime with `gt_Int: expected IntValue for arg 0, got
  *eval.FloatValue`. Root cause: `pickDefault` in
  [`internal/types/typechecker_defaulting.go`](../internal/types/typechecker_defaulting.go)
  defaulted `Ord`-only / `Eq`-only / `Show`-only constraints to `Int` at
  inner generalization boundaries, monomorphizing the lambda before
  call-site unification could pick a fresh type. The fix returns
  `(nil, nil)` for neutral-only constraint sets so `defaultAmbiguities`
  leaves them unsolved — let-generalization then quantifies the type
  variable, and the call site picks the right dictionary
  (`gt_Int` / `gt_Float` / `gt_String`). `Num` and `Fractional` defaulting
  is unchanged. Regression introduced in 85d47647 (v0.2.0 work, ~6
  months in-tree); examples
  [`polymorphic_comparison_simple.ail`](../examples/runnable/polymorphic_comparison_simple.ail)
  and [`polymorphic_lambdas_phase1.ail`](../examples/runnable/polymorphic_lambdas_phase1.ail)
  now work end-to-end with Float, Int, and String. New regression tests
  in [`internal/types/simple_defaulting_test.go`](../internal/types/simple_defaulting_test.go)
  (`TestPickDefault_OrdOnly_NotDefaulted`,
  `TestPickDefault_NumStillDefaults`). Design:
  [`design_docs/planned/v0_11_4/m-poly-ord-defaulting-regression.md`](../design_docs/planned/v0_11_4/m-poly-ord-defaulting-regression.md).
- **pipeline: module cache did not invalidate on compiler rebuild (`M-POLY-ORD` M1)** —
  [`ModuleCacheKey`](../internal/pipeline/cache_key.go) accepts a
  `compilerVersion` parameter, but
  [`pipeline_module.go`](../internal/pipeline/pipeline_module.go) was
  passing the format constant `"v1"` instead of the build commit. As a
  result, rebuilding `ailang` did not invalidate cached modules — any
  bugfix to elaboration, type-checking, or op-lowering silently did
  nothing for previously-compiled files until someone manually nuked
  `.ailang/cache/compile/`. This was a foot-gun that bit us during
  diagnosis of the polymorphic Ord/Eq regression above (instrumentation
  prints in the type checker simply never ran). The fix introduces a new
  [`internal/version`](../internal/version/version.go) package exposing
  `Version`, `Commit`, and `BuildTime` populated via `-ldflags` (Makefile
  retargeted from `main.*` to `internal/version.*`), with a
  `runtime/debug.ReadBuildInfo()` fallback so `go run` / `go test`
  without ldflags still get a stable commit marker (with `-dirty` suffix
  when the working tree has uncommitted changes). The pipeline now
  threads `version.Commit` into `ModuleCacheKey`, so rebuilds always
  invalidate the cache. Regression test:
  `TestModuleCacheKey_CommitChange` in
  [`internal/pipeline/cache_key_test.go`](../internal/pipeline/cache_key_test.go).

- **evaluator: `&&` and `||` did not short-circuit (`M-V0_11_3-HOTFIX` M1)** —
  Reported by [msg `ce6e078e`](../design_docs/planned/v0_11_3/m-eval-short-circuit-bool.md):
  guarded string indexing like `i > 0 && charAt(s, i - 1) == "\\"` crashed
  with "index -1 out of bounds for string of length N" at `i == 0`, because
  the RHS of `&&` was evaluated regardless of LHS. Before this fix, `&&`
  and `||` were lowered to strict `core.BinOp` nodes and `applyBinOp`
  evaluated both operands before reducing. Now `normalizeShortCircuit` in
  [`internal/elaborate/expr_simple.go`](../internal/elaborate/expr_simple.go)
  desugars them to `core.If` at elaboration time:
  * `a && b` → `if a then b else false`
  * `a || b` → `if a then true else b`
  All downstream phases (evaluator, bytecode VM, SMT encoder) inherit lazy
  branch semantics from the existing `If` lowering — no additional runtime
  changes required. The LHS is ANF-normalized to atomic form; the RHS is
  normalized lazily inside its branch so side-effects stay unevaluated when
  short-circuited. A `DEBUG_STRICT=1` guard in `applyBinOp` panics if the
  strict branch is reached, making future regressions visible. Five unit
  tests in [`internal/elaborate/short_circuit_test.go`](../internal/elaborate/short_circuit_test.go)
  and runnable regressions at [`examples/short_circuit_and.ail`](../examples/short_circuit_and.ail)
  and [`examples/short_circuit_or.ail`](../examples/short_circuit_or.ail).
  Design: [`design_docs/planned/v0_11_3/m-eval-short-circuit-bool.md`](../design_docs/planned/v0_11_3/m-eval-short-circuit-bool.md).

## [v0.11.2] - 2026-04-12

### Fixed
- **pipeline: cache poisoning — edits to .ail files were silently ignored** —
  `runModuleWithContext` was passing `mod.Path` (the *canonical module ID*,
  e.g. `benchmarks/workloads/warm_eval`) into `os.ReadFile` when computing
  the M-PERF6 module cache key. The read silently failed and `sourceContent`
  defaulted to `""`, so every module that shared the same imports collided
  on the same cache key. The bug was latent until M-INCREMENTAL-TYPECHECK M3
  (commit `4f91d27e`, 2026-04-10) wired cache hits to *skip* compilation;
  from that point until this fix, edits to `.ail` source files on the dev
  branch were silently ignored after the first compile (cached output kept
  running). Caught while building latency-budget workloads when `fib(28) →
  fib(24)` edits printed the old result. One-line fix:
  `mod.Path → mod.File.Path` (the actual on-disk path, populated by the
  loader). Added [`cache_invalidation_test.go`](../internal/pipeline/cache_invalidation_test.go),
  an end-to-end regression test that compiles → edits → recompiles and
  asserts the manifest cache key changes; verified to fail before the fix
  and pass after. **Note: no shipped release was affected** — v0.11.1 was
  tagged 2026-04-09, before the cache-skip wiring landed.
  Sprint: `M-LAT-BUDGET` (discovered while building M1).

### Added
- **wasm: `ailangStdlibStatus()` JS diagnostic surface** —
  Browser-side callers can now query which stdlib modules loaded successfully
  vs failed via the new `ailangStdlibStatus()` global. Returns
  `{loaded: string[], failed: [{name, error}]}`. Previously, partial stdlib
  load failures during multi-pass loading were swallowed by the recover() in
  `loadEmbeddedStdlib`, producing mysterious "undefined global variable"
  errors when user code imported a module that had silently failed. The
  per-module last-error map now propagates to JavaScript so devtools can
  surface the underlying load failure. Failed-module logs upgraded from
  `console.warn` to `console.error` with the captured error message.
  Files: `cmd/wasm/main.go`.
- **benchmarks: latency-budget canonical workloads + harness (`M-LAT-BUDGET`)** —
  Design doc: [`design_docs/implemented/v0_11_2/m-latency-budget.md`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_11_2/m-latency-budget.md).
  Six self-contained `.ail` programs at [`benchmarks/workloads/`](../benchmarks/workloads/)
  exercising cold start, warm evaluator, type checking, IO effect dispatch,
  and small/large list pipelines. Each workload is paired with a release-gating
  p95 target in [`benchmarks/budget_ledger.md`](../benchmarks/budget_ledger.md)
  (the authoritative ledger) and an auto-generated baseline at
  [`benchmarks/latency_budgets.json`](../benchmarks/latency_budgets.json). New
  harness `tools/bench_workloads.sh` runs each workload N times with
  `AILANG_NO_TRACE=1`, records p50/p95/min/max, and writes the JSON baseline.
  New Make targets: `make bench-workloads` (5 runs, full capture) and
  `make bench-workloads-quick` (3 runs, dry-run). Initial Apple M2 baseline:
  cold_hello 437ms p95, warm_eval 1124ms p95, list_large 733ms p95.
  The ledger seeds with a +1887 ms dev-pool credit back-filled from the
  M-PERF6B trace-off optimization (live A/B re-measured today shows
  `list_large` runs 4506ms with tracing on vs 733ms with tracing off).
- **benchmarks: three-suite layout documented** —
  [`benchmarks/README.md`](../benchmarks/README.md) now distinguishes the
  three sibling suites: `*.yml` AI evals (model code-gen quality), `runtime/*.ail`
  micro-benchmarks (single hot-loop interpreter changes), and `workloads/*.ail`
  latency-budget probes (release-gating user-visible p95).

## [v0.11.1] - 2026-04-10

### Added
- **trace: WASM trace streaming via `ailangSetTraceHandler`** —
  Real-time trace event streaming from WASM to JavaScript. Register a JS callback
  to receive `function_enter`, `function_exit`, `effect`, `contract_check`,
  `budget_delta`, and `error` events as they occur during AILANG execution.
  Enables browser-based trace waterfall panels and observability dashboards.
  Sprint: `M-WASM-TRACE`.
- **trace: `std/trace` module for custom spans and events** —
  New stdlib module with `spanStart`, `spanEnd`, and `event` functions backed by
  a `Trace` effect. AILANG code can emit custom trace spans that appear in
  `--emit-trace jsonl` output and WASM trace streams. Three new builtins:
  `_trace_span_start`, `_trace_span_end`, `_trace_event`.
- **trace: W3C-compatible OTEL span IDs** —
  All trace events now include `trace_id` (32 hex chars), `span_id` (16 hex chars),
  and `parent_span_id` fields. Function enter/exit pairs share the same span_id.
  Nested calls form a valid parent-child tree. Enables JS-side forwarding to
  Cloud Trace or any OTEL collector.

## [v0.11.0] - 2026-04-10

### Fixed
- **bytecode: fix register allocation bugs (13 EvalOnly → 0)** —
  Two bugs fixed: (1) Image validator incorrectly treated OpGetField's C operand
  (a sorted field index) as a register number, rejecting 9 prototypes whose field
  index exceeded NumRegs. Fix: separate OpGetField from the register-checking group
  in `image.go:validateInstruction`. (2) Switch case bindings and nested let
  bindings permanently consumed registers via `allocPinned` even after their scope
  ended. Fix: scope-aware register recycling — `scopeStack.pop()` now returns
  scoped registers to the allocator's free list, and `allocContig` scans the free
  list for adjacent slots before bumping. EvalOnly reduced from 89 → 76 / 1228
  prototypes (-15%). Parity: 129 MATCH, no regressions.
  Sprint: `M-BYTECODE-REGALLOC-FIX`.
- **bytecode: fix lambda body name resolution in multi-module images** —
  `compileLambda` was not propagating `currentModule` to the inner
  `funcCompiler`, causing all same-module function references inside lambda
  bodies to fail with "unbound variable" / "call to unbound name". One-line
  fix: `inner.currentModule = fc.currentModule`. Reduces docparse EvalOnly
  from 157 → 92 / 1204 prototypes (-41%). Remaining 92 are effectful
  builtins (73), register overflow (11), unknown ADT (2), and std/net
  unbound (2). Parity: 129 MATCH, no regressions.
  Sprint: `M-BYTECODE-LAMBDA-RESOLUTION`.

### Performance
- **runtime: eliminate trace auto-enable overhead + gob cache + stdout buffering** —
  Three optimizations from CPU/allocation profiling of DocParse benchmarks:
  (1) Auto-trace was enabling on `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` alone (a GCP auth
  env var), creating ~2.7M trace objects (21% of allocations) with no exporter.
  Now requires `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT`. Users can still opt-in with
  `--emit-trace`.
  (2) CoreTypeInfo serialization switched from JSON to gob, cutting
  `LoadArtifacts` CPU from 15% to 5.6%.
  (3) CLI stdout buffered with 64KB `bufio.Writer`, reducing syscall overhead
  from many small `println` writes.
  Also added `-memprofile` flag for allocation profiling.

  **Benchmark results (docparse, warm cache, no tracing):**

  | File | Before | After | Speedup |
  |------|--------|-------|---------|
  | Alice EPUB (185KB) | 2.97s | **1.4s** | **2.1x** |
  | Moby Dick EPUB (797KB) | 7.27s | **3.0s** | **2.4x** |
  | 10MB DOCX | 2.18s | **0.4s** | **5.5x** |

  Combined with prior sprints, total improvement from v0.10.14:
  Alice EPUB **12.1s → 1.4s (8.6x)**, Moby Dick **35.1s → 3.0s (11.7x)**.

  Sprint: `M-PERF6B`.

- **pipeline: incremental compilation — skip cached modules entirely** —
  On cache hit, load serialized artifacts (Core AST via gob, CoreTypeInfo +
  Iface + Constructors via JSON) and skip elaboration→typecheck→monomorph→lower.
  Three-milestone sprint: M1 added tagged-union JSON serialization for all 14
  `types.Type` + 4 `Kind` implementations with 20 round-trip tests. M2 added
  `CachedModule` struct with gob registration for all 22 CoreExpr + 7 CorePattern
  types and full Iface reconstruction. M3 wired cache hit → skip in the pipeline
  with `RegisterIface()` for downstream imports.

  **Benchmark results (docparse, warm cache vs cold):**

  | File | Cold | Warm (cached) | Speedup |
  |------|------|---------------|---------|
  | Alice EPUB (185KB) | 3.02s | 2.97s | ~2% |
  | Moby Dick EPUB (797KB) | 10.97s | 7.27s | **34%** |
  | 10MB DOCX | 2.42s | 2.18s | 10% |

  Compilation skip saves ~3.7s on Moby Dick (40+ modules). Alice and DOCX
  show less improvement as runtime I/O dominates. All 40+ docparse modules
  show `SKIP` on warm runs. 159/161 examples pass (2 pre-existing failures).

  Sprint: `M-INCREMENTAL-TYPECHECK`.

- **runtime: eliminate runtime.Stack() bottleneck in builtin dispatch (2.5–3.6x speedup)** —
  Every builtin call was paying ~500ns to extract the goroutine ID via
  `runtime.Stack()` + string parsing, introduced in M-SERVE-API-CONCURRENCY
  (v0.9.4) for per-request evaluator isolation. CPU profiling showed this
  consumed **42% of total CPU** in docparse benchmarks. Fix: replace with
  `github.com/petermattis/goid` (~3ns) and add an atomic fast-path that
  skips the `sync.Map` lookup entirely for single-goroutine programs (CLI,
  REPL). Also consolidated 4 duplicate `goroutineID()` implementations
  across `runtime/`, `apiserver/`, and `embed/` packages. Added `-cpuprofile`
  flag to `ailang run` for Go pprof CPU profiling.

  **Benchmark results (docparse, evaluator mode):**

  | File | Before | After | Speedup |
  |------|--------|-------|---------|
  | Alice EPUB (185KB) | 12.1s | 4.9s | **2.5x** |
  | Moby Dick EPUB (797KB) | 35.1s | 9.8s | **3.6x** |
  | 10MB DOCX | 4.2s | 4.2s | ~1x (I/O bound) |

  Sprint: `M-PERF-GOROUTINE-ID`.

- **pipeline: defer CoreTI substitution + GC tuning (1.6x additional speedup)** —
  Two optimizations that compound with the goroutine-ID fix above:
  (1) Deferred CoreTI substitution: `ApplySubstEverywhere` was applying
  substitution to ALL CoreTI entries at every let-binding generalization
  boundary — O(N×M) where N=AST nodes, M=let-bindings. Now deferred to a
  single O(N) pass via `FinalizeSubstitutions()` before operator lowering.
  Type checking CPU dropped from 35% → 25%.
  (2) GC pressure reduction: set `GOGC=500` for CLI runs (GC triggers at 6x
  heap growth instead of 2x — fine for short-lived CLI processes), and added
  fast-path in `ApplySubstitution` for leaf types (TVar, TCon) that skips
  the pooled visited-map allocation via `followVarChain`. GC CPU dropped
  from 27% → 9%.

  **Benchmark results (docparse, batch mode):**

  | File | Before M-PERF-DOCPARSE | After | Speedup |
  |------|----------------------|-------|---------|
  | Alice EPUB (185KB) | 2,933ms | **1,670ms** | **1.76x** |
  | Moby Dick EPUB (797KB) | 3,727ms | **2,350ms** | **1.59x** |
  | 10MB DOCX | N/A | **1,241ms** | — |

  Combined with M-PERF-GOROUTINE-ID, total improvement from v0.10.14:
  Alice EPUB **12.1s → 1.67s (7.2x)**, Moby Dick **35.1s → 2.35s (14.9x)**.

  Sprint: `M-PERF-DOCPARSE`.

### Added
- **runtime: `AILANG_NO_TRACE=1` env var** — Kill switch to disable all tracing
  regardless of OTEL configuration. Provides ~2x performance gain on workloads
  where tracing overhead dominates. Also added `-memprofile` flag for allocation
  profiling.
- **stdlib: Map[k,v] type and Array gaps** — Added Map type with O(1) lookup
  and filled Array gaps (empty, append) to enable XLSX parsing up to 50 MB
  without OOM. Sprint: `M-STD-MAP-AND-ARRAY-GAPS`.
- **serve-api: fix type coercion in cross-package calls** — Fixed silent type
  mismatches in serve-api when calling functions across packages with different
  record type representations. Sprint: `M-DX-SERVE-API-COERCION`.
- **MCP: quality improvements and route headers** — Proper MCP server filtering,
  portable tool names, doc comment descriptions, named parameter schemas, and
  request header access for @route handlers.
  Sprint: `M-MCP-QUALITY-AND-ROUTE-HEADERS`.
- **bytecode: Statement IR lowering completeness** — Fixed 5 bugs in match
  patterns, recursive functions, and string operators in the Statement IR
  lowering pass. Sprint: `M-LOWER-FIX`.
- **bytecode: 26.3x benchmark speedup** — Bytecode VM achieves 26.3x faster
  execution than tree-walking evaluator on recursive fib(25) with 55x fewer
  heap allocations. 134/141 (95%) test corpus parity.
  Sprint: `M-BYTECODE-2D-BENCH`, `M-BYTECODE-2D-PARITY`.
- **bytecode: wire 17 XML builtins to VM** — All 17 XML builtins now compile
  to bytecode instead of falling back to the evaluator. Includes 3 constructors
  (`xmlElement`, `xmlText`, `xmlComment`), 4 string-returning builtins
  (`getText`, `getTag`, `serialize`, `serializeWithDecl`), 3 parsers (`parse`,
  `parseElements`, `parseWithLimit`), 6 query builtins (`findAll`, `findFirst`,
  `getAttr`, `getChildren`, `findAllTexts`, `findAllAttrs`), and 1 HOF
  (`parseFold`). Strategy: constructors build `bytecode.ADTObj` directly;
  all others bridge through evaluator implementations via `builtins.GetSpec`
  with bidirectional `XmlNode ↔ bytecode.Value` converters. 20 unit tests
  with `-count=20` determinism checks. EvalOnly reduced from 74 → 57 / 1228
  prototypes (-23%). Sprint: `M-BYTECODE-XML-BUILTINS`.
- **bytecode: wire 3 JSON builtins to VM** — `__json_encode`, `__json_decode`,
  and `__json_repair` now dispatch natively in the bytecode VM. JSON encode
  walks the `Json` ADT tree recursively building strings. JSON decode uses Go's
  streaming `encoding/json` parser to construct `Result[Json, string]` ADT
  values. JSON repair fixes truncated JSON by closing unclosed brackets/strings.
  21 unit tests with `-count=20` determinism checks. EvalOnly reduced from
  92 → 89 / 1204 prototypes (-3). Sprint: `M-BYTECODE-PURE-EFFECTS`.
- **bytecode: wire 6 HOF builtins to VM** — `list_map`, `list_filter`,
  `list_foldl`, `str_foldChars`, `str_foldSlices`, and `str_mapSlicesJoin`
  now dispatch via new `OpBuiltinCallHOF` opcode with `VM.CallClosure`
  callback infrastructure. Added `ClosureCaller` interface, `HOFBuiltinFunc`
  type, and `HOFBuiltinTable` in both compiler and VM. Each HOF builtin
  iterates its collection natively and invokes the user callback via
  `CallClosure`, which handles both compiled closures and EvalOnly fallback.
  22 unit tests cover all 6 builtins including edge cases (empty lists,
  single elements, nested closures). EvalOnly reduced from 163 → 157 / 1130
  prototypes. The modest reduction (vs ~90 target) is because most remaining
  EvalOnly ops are from unbound-variable lambda bodies and unknown-ADT
  switches — separate compiler limitations, not HOF-related. Parity: 129
  MATCH, no regressions. Sprint: `M-BYTECODE-HOF-BUILTINS`.
- **bytecode: wire 55 pure stdlib builtins to VM** — String ops (26: split,
  join, trim, find, slice, replace, startsWith, endsWith, upper, lower, len,
  compare, eq, chars, words, charAt, charCode, decodeQP, startsWithIC,
  splitAny, replaceMany, escapeXml, intToStr, floatToStr, stringToInt,
  stringToFloat), math (23: all trig, floor/ceil/round, abs, PI, E, pow, log,
  sqrt, mod, floatToInt, int↔float aliases), and list set ops (6: nth, member,
  dedup, difference, intersect, union) now dispatch via `OpBuiltinCall`.
  Reduces docparse EvalOnly from 220 → 163 / 1130 prototypes (-26%).
  `stringToInt`/`stringToFloat` are the first builtins to create ADT (Option)
  values natively in the VM. HOF builtins (map/filter/foldl) and effectful
  builtins remain EvalOnly. Parity: 129 MATCH, no regressions.
  Sprint: `M-BYTECODE-STDLIB-BUILTINS`.

## [v0.10.15] - 2026-04-09

### Added
- **bytecode: wire Phase 2E pure builtins** — `_not_Bool`, `_intToFloat`,
  `__list_length`, and `_concat_List` now dispatch via `OpBuiltinCall`
  instead of bridging to the evaluator. Reduces docparse EvalOnly from
  247 to 220 / 1129 prototypes. Remaining EvalOnly are effectful builtins
  (IO, FS, XML, string ops) that require capability-aware dispatch.
  Sprint: `M-BYTECODE-PHASE2E`.

### Fixed
- **bytecode: constructor-pattern head in cons pattern** — Match arms of
  the form `TextBlock(t) :: rest => t.style` caused `unbound variable "t"`
  at compile time. Two bugs in `internal/gen/lower/match.go`: (1)
  `lowerPatternCond` for `ListPattern` only checked list length, not the
  constructor tag on head elements — fixed by AND-combining a tag equality
  check for each `ConstructorPattern` element; (2) `lowerPatternBindings`
  for `ListPattern` only handled `VarPattern` elements, silently skipping
  constructor sub-patterns — fixed by binding head to a temp and
  field-accessing each var sub-arg. Regression test:
  `TestLowerMatchStmt_ConsConstructorHead`. Eliminates the last
  sprint-scoped EvalOnly errors in `ailang-parse` docparse.
  Sprint: `M-BYTECODE-MULTIMODULE` M5.

### Added
- **`scripts/bench_ailang_parse.sh`** — Wall-clock benchmark script
  comparing evaluator vs bytecode VM on the runnable example corpus.
  Supports `--iters`, `--only`, `--min-ms` flags. Reports per-file and
  aggregate speedup with a ≥3× gate check.

### Changed
- **serve-api: typed router error envelope** — Router-level 404 responses
  now carry a structured `error_detail` envelope with a stable `code`
  field (`ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`, `MODULE_NOT_LOADED`, `FUNCTION_NOT_FOUND`,
  `METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED`) alongside the existing flat `error` string. When
  a request path doesn't match any registered `@route` on a route-driven
  server, the response now returns `ROUTE_NOT_FOUND` with a "Did you
  mean ...?" suggestion and a bounded `available_routes` list, instead
  of the misleading `module "X" not loaded` error that sent AI agents
  debugging the wrong layer. Legacy `/api/{module}/{func}` dispatch on
  no-`@route` servers still returns `MODULE_NOT_LOADED` with the
  historical message text for backward compatibility. The flat `error`
  field always mirrors `error_detail.message` so existing clients that
  parse the top-level `error` string continue to work unchanged.
  Reported by docparse (inbox `msg_20260409_070210_566f6da6`).
  Implements `M-SERVEAPI-ROUTE-NOT-FOUND`.

## [v0.10.14] - 2026-04-08

### Fixed (SECURITY, P0)
- **`std/rand` no longer deterministic across cold starts** — `init()`
  in `internal/builtins/rand.go` seeded `math/rand` with
  `rand.NewSource(0)`, so every fresh process produced the *same*
  sequence from `rand_int`/`rand_float`/`rand_bool` unless the program
  explicitly called `_rand_seed`. This was a real security bug, not a
  theoretical one: docparse on Cloud Run minted the **identical** 32-char
  API key `dp_fc4af748e9e0a03496c86bdae37091f6` on at least four
  independent cold starts across two GCP projects (`ailang-multivac-dev`
  and `ailang-multivac`), causing Firestore upserts to silently overwrite
  one user's API key with another's. The "first key after cold start"
  was also predictable to anyone able to trigger a cold start. Fix:
  `init()` now seeds from `crypto/rand` via the new `cryptoSeed()`
  helper, which panics on entropy failure rather than falling back to
  a predictable value (per the no-silent-fallbacks rule). Determinism
  for games/tests is preserved — callers must opt in via `SetRandSeed`
  or `std/rand._rand_seed`. Regression test:
  `TestCryptoSeed_NonDeterministic` in `internal/builtins/rand_test.go`.
  Reported by docparse (inbox `msg_20260408_165307_ca9998b6`).

## [v0.10.13] - 2026-04-08

### Fixed
- **serve-api: empty POST body now zero-pads named params instead of
  leaking eval errors** — When a client POSTed to a `@route` handler
  with no body (or no `Content-Type`), `parseArgsWithNames` short-
  circuited to `parseArgs` and returned `nil, nil`, skipping the
  zero-value padding that the `{}` / unmatched-keys path already had.
  Result: single-string handlers received `UnitValue` (crashing
  `_crypto_sha256hex` etc. with `expected String, got *eval.UnitValue`),
  and multi-arg handlers got `function expects N arguments, got 0` —
  both surfaced as raw internal errors to the network. Now an empty
  body with declared params behaves identically to `POST {}`: each
  param gets its type's zero value (`""`, `0`, `false`, `[]`, `{}`),
  so user code runs its normal validation path and the response is the
  handler's own error envelope. Reported by docparse on prod
  (inbox `msg_20260408_104901_78aa2a4f`); fixed in
  `internal/apiserver/handler.go::parseArgsWithNames` with regression
  tests in `named_args_test.go`.

## [v0.10.12] - 2026-04-08

### Changed
- **M-SERVEAPI-UNIFY: serve-api loader refactor — single registration site,
  PhysicalPath identity, no dep-discovery loop** — Replaces the v0.10.7 →
  v0.10.11 cascade's root cause with a structural fix instead of a third
  patch on the dep-discovery loop. The cascade was a *projection drift*
  bug: HTTP routes, OpenAPI spec, MCP tools, and function dispatch each
  derived their own key/filter from the same loader output, and the four
  derivations could disagree. v0.10.11 unified the two derivations inside
  `loadFile`; v0.10.12 retires the entire dep-discovery loop and makes
  drift structurally impossible.

  **New architecture:**
  - `s.modules` is now keyed by `PhysicalPath` (the symlink-resolved
    absolute file path) — the module's *identity*, not a derived string.
  - `registerModule` (`internal/apiserver/module_entry.go`) is the SOLE
    write site for `s.modules`. It computes `PhysicalPath`, `CanonicalID`,
    `DeclaredPath`, and `RelPath` ONCE, and writes them all to the
    `ModuleInfo` struct. Consumers read the projection they need from
    the fields — they never re-derive from source data.
  - `LoadProject` (`internal/apiserver/load_project.go`) is the new
    project-wide loader: one pipeline pass per file, idempotent
    registration via `registerModule`, eager-load by `CanonicalID`. It
    replaces the old per-file `loadFile` + dep-discovery loop. The
    legacy `loadFile` is now a thin wrapper for the single-file form
    (watcher reload, individual file args).
  - The `loadDirectory` walker is deleted entirely (subsumed by
    `LoadProject`).
  - The dep-discovery loop in `loadFile` (~170 lines, source of every
    cascade bug from v0.10.7 onward) is deleted.

  **Consumers migrated** to read `info.Path` (RelPath projection) via
  the new `findModuleByRelPath` helper instead of indexing `s.modules`
  by a derived string: `handler.go` (function dispatch), `meta.go`
  (`/api/_meta/modules/{path}`), `a2a.go` (skill IDs and `tasks/send`),
  `openapi.go` (URL generation), `mcp.go` (tool name generation and
  `engine.Call` dispatch).

  **Tests:**
  - `cold_start_test.go` rewritten for the PhysicalPath-keyed map. The
    four cold-start invariants now assert: each `PhysicalPath` appears
    exactly once, keys ARE absolute file paths (the new identity),
    `Registered:` log line emitted exactly once per module
    (idempotency), all expected RelPaths present.
  - `module_entry_test.go` (new): asserts `registerModule` is the sole
    write site (every entry has populated `PhysicalPath` /
    `CanonicalID` / `Path`, and the map key equals the entry's
    `PhysicalPath`); the under-basePath filter rejects files outside
    the project root; `normalizedBasePath` is correctly Abs +
    `EvalSymlinks` + trailing separator.
  - `TestLoadProject_DocparseShape` (new in `cold_start_test.go`): the
    permanent CI guard against the cascade class. A docparse-shaped
    fixture with three local route-bearing files plus a `pkg/vendor/`
    directory containing a route-bearing file asserts: exact local
    module count, no `pkg/` files leaked into `s.modules`, and
    idempotency on a second `LoadModules` pass.

  **Net change:** `server.go` shrank from 842 → 671 lines (-171).
  Architecturally: 4 projection key derivations → 1; 2 write sites → 1;
  3 specialized filters → 1 under-basePath check; ~600 LOC of cascade
  patches and dep-discovery glue → ~270 LOC of `registerModule` +
  `LoadProject`.

  Design docs:
  [m-serveapi-unify.md](../design_docs/planned/v0_11_0/m-serveapi-unify.md),
  [m-serveapi-unify-sprint-plan.md](../design_docs/planned/v0_11_0/m-serveapi-unify-sprint-plan.md).
  Files: `internal/apiserver/server.go`,
  `internal/apiserver/load_project.go` (new),
  `internal/apiserver/module_entry.go` (new),
  `internal/apiserver/module_entry_test.go` (new),
  `internal/apiserver/cold_start_test.go`,
  `internal/apiserver/handler.go`, `internal/apiserver/meta.go`,
  `internal/apiserver/a2a.go`, `internal/apiserver/openapi.go`,
  `internal/apiserver/mcp.go`.

## [v0.10.11] - 2026-04-08

### Fixed
- **Cold-start regression: dep-discovery double-registration (v0.10.10
  follow-up)** — v0.10.10 populated `ast.File.Path` to fix the MOD011 dedup,
  which inadvertently reanimated dormant code in
  `internal/apiserver/server.go::loadFile`'s dep-discovery loop. That loop
  used a different key derivation (`strings.TrimPrefix` over the pipeline
  canonical ID) than the main `loadFile` path (`filepath.Rel(s.basePath,
  absPath)`), so the same local file was registered under TWO keys in
  `s.modules` whenever a project had multiple `@route` files importing
  each other. The eager-load step then called `engine.Load` twice per
  module — doubling cold-start time and breaking Cloud Run probes for
  docparse (cold start went from ~22s on v0.10.7 to ~90s on v0.10.10).
  Three surgical fixes in the dep-discovery loop:
  1. **Unify key derivation** with the main path via
     `filepath.Rel(s.basePath, absFile)` so both paths produce identical
     `s.modules` keys.
  2. **Existence check moved BEFORE** the four `extract*` calls — repeat
     visits across the multi-file `LoadModules` pass are now O(map lookup)
     instead of O(parse + 4 AST traversals).
  3. **Skip the entry-point file** explicitly so dep-discovery doesn't
     redundantly process the file the main path already handles.

  Also normalizes `s.basePath` (absolute + `EvalSymlinks`) at server
  construction so the prefix check matches loader-resolved file paths
  consistently on macOS, where TMPDIR routes through `/var/folders` →
  `/private/var/folders`. Without this, the prefix check silently dropped
  local files in temp directories.

  Regression tests in `internal/apiserver/cold_start_test.go` cover four
  invariants: no duplicate registration, key consistency (forward-slash
  relative paths only), no redundant dep-discovery work (each module
  logged at most once), and entry-point skipping. Postmortem:
  [m-mcp-cascade-postmortem-and-fixes.md](../design_docs/planned/v0_11_0/m-mcp-cascade-postmortem-and-fixes.md).
  Files: `internal/apiserver/server.go`,
  `internal/apiserver/cold_start_test.go` (new).

## [v0.10.10] - 2026-04-07

### Fixed
- **MOD011: dedupe by resolved file path (regression fix for v0.10.9)** — The
  v0.10.9 MOD011 check false-fired on every package module that uses
  `module_prefix`, because the same physical file was being loaded under two
  canonical IDs (e.g. `pkg/sunholo/ailang_parse/services/csv_parser` AND
  `docparse/services/csv_parser`, both pointing at the same on-disk file).
  The detector now dedupes by `filepath.EvalSymlinks(Abs(mod.File.Path))`
  before comparing declared module paths, so aliasing is no longer flagged —
  only two *genuinely different* files claiming the same module header
  trigger the error. Also populates `ast.File.Path` in the loader so the
  detector (and future consumers) can see the resolved disk path instead of
  the empty string set by the parser. Files:
  `internal/loader/loader.go`, `internal/pipeline/pipeline_module.go`,
  `internal/pipeline/module_collision_test.go`.

## [v0.10.9] - 2026-04-07

### Fixed
- **MOD011: Module path collision detection (silent dispatch footgun)** — When
  two different source files declared the same `module X` header (e.g. a local
  `docparse/services/mcp_tools.ail` and a `module_prefix`-aliased package file
  at `pkg/sunholo/ailang_parse/services/mcp_tools.ail` both declaring
  `module docparse/services/mcp_tools`), runtime dispatch via
  `callFunction("docparse/services/mcp_tools", "mcpParse", ...)` was ambiguous
  — whichever module won the loader cache silently shadowed the other. v0.10.8
  fixed route *registration* in this case, but function *dispatch* still went
  to the wrong module, causing features like `AUTH_REQUIRED` checks to appear
  to "not fire" when they were actually firing on the wrong function. The
  pipeline now detects collisions at load time and returns `Error MOD011`
  listing both source files, consistent with AILANG's "no silent fallbacks"
  principle. Detection happens once per pipeline run, right after
  `modLoader.LoadAll`. Files: `internal/pipeline/pipeline_module.go`,
  `internal/pipeline/module_collision_test.go`.

## [v0.10.8] - 2026-04-07

### Fixed
- **serve-api: package routes leaked through `module_prefix` aliasing** — When a
  project imported a single symbol from a package module that declared its own
  `module_prefix` colliding with the local project namespace (e.g. docparse
  importing `pkg/sunholo/ailang_parse/services/mcp_tools` under the aliased
  path `docparse/services/mcp_tools`), the package's `@route` annotations
  still leaked into the project's route table. The v0.10.6/v0.10.7 filter used
  `strings.HasPrefix(modPath, "pkg/")` + `os.Stat(localFile)`, both of which
  could be fooled by alias collisions. The filter now uses the module's actual
  source file path (`loaded.File.Path`) and only registers routes when the
  file lives under `s.basePath` on disk. Set `DEBUG_APISERVER_ROUTES=1` to log
  which modules are filtered out. Fixes a case where docparse's local
  `mcpParse` wrapper was shadowed by the package's own `mcpParse` at
  `POST /mcp/v1/parse`. Files: `internal/apiserver/server.go`.

## [v0.10.7] - 2026-04-07

### Added
- **MCP-compliant tool names + `@mcp_name` annotation** — All MCP tool names now
  match the strict regex `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,64}$` enforced by Claude Desktop and
  most current MCP clients (no dots, no slashes, max 64 chars). Previously,
  AILANG generated dot-separated names like `docparse.services.mcp_tools.mcpParse`
  that Claude Desktop rejected outright.
  - **New `@mcp_name("toolName")` annotation** lets authors set the exact MCP
    tool name surfaced to clients (e.g. `@mcp_name("parse")` on `mcpParse`).
    Invalid names are logged at startup and the affected tool is skipped.
  - **Auto-generated names** follow a layered fallback: (1) bare function name
    when globally unique among exposed exports (e.g. `mcpParse`), (2)
    `<lastModuleSegment>_<funcName>` for collisions (e.g. `services_parseCsv`),
    (3) deterministic SHA1 hash suffix when truncated to 64 chars.
  - All non-override outputs are guaranteed to satisfy the MCP regex by
    construction. New `validateMCPName()` defensively checks every emitted name.
  - Files: `internal/parser/parser_decl.go`, `internal/apiserver/server.go`,
    `internal/apiserver/routes.go`, `internal/apiserver/mcp_schema.go`,
    `internal/apiserver/mcp.go`, plus tests + `docs/docs/guides/serve-api.md`
    + devtools prompts.

## [v0.10.6] - 2026-04-06

### Fixed
- **MCP --routes-only: exclude external package routes** — When a project imports
  a package that has its own `@route` annotations (via `module_prefix` aliasing),
  those package routes were leaking into the importing project's MCP tools/list and
  REST endpoints. Now only the project's own local `.ail` files contribute routes;
  external package modules are preloaded for execution but their `@route` annotations
  are not registered as server endpoints.

## [v0.10.5] - 2026-04-06

### Fixed
- **MCP --routes-only canonical path dedup** — Modules loaded via `loadDirectory`
  were registered twice: once under the declared path (`docparse/services/mcp_tools`)
  and again under the canonical filesystem path (`Users/.../docparse/services/mcp_tools`).
  On Cloud Run this produced `app.*` prefixed duplicate tools in MCP `tools/list`.
  Fix normalizes canonical paths by stripping basePath before checking the module map.
  Also removed redundant MCP doc-comment exception (`isExposed()` is now the sole filter).

## [v0.10.4] - 2026-04-04

### Added
- **M-MCP-QUALITY: MCP server quality + @route header access** —
  Six fixes making AILANG packages work as proper MCP tool providers for AI agents.
  - MCP `tools/list` now respects `--routes-only` and `@noexpose` (was exposing all 159 exports)
  - Tool names are machine-portable (`docparse.services.parseCsv` not `Users.mark.dev...`)
  - Descriptions use doc comments (`-- comment above func`); type signatures as fallback
  - `inputSchema` uses named parameters with JSON Schema types (was generic `{args:[...]}`)
  - `makeToolHandler` accepts both named params and legacy `{"args":[...]}` format
  - Undocumented non-route helpers auto-excluded from MCP when `--routes-only`
  - `@route` handlers can declare `_headers: Json` parameter to receive HTTP request headers
    (enables multipart file upload + auth header access without switching to `@raw`)
  - New `ExportInfo.DocComment` field extracted from source `--` comment lines
  - Deterministic dedup: modules loaded via both direct and pkg/ paths produce one
    MCP tool (not two), preferring the entry with a doc comment description
  - Example: `examples/runnable/mcp_tools.ail`
  (~450 LOC across implementation + tests)

- **M-PHASE2A-BENCH: Evaluator vs native Go performance benchmarks** —
  Phase 2A gate for bytecode VM decision. 7 workloads (fib30, list map/filter 5K,
  tree pattern match, curried closures, string pipeline, game step, cross-boundary)
  benchmarked with startup excluded. Results: evaluator is 400-441,000x slower than
  native Go across all workloads. Decision: **build bytecode VM** (all workloads exceed
  10x threshold). Benchmarks retained as permanent regression tests.
  Run with: `make bench-phase2a`
  (~550 LOC benchmark harness + 7 AILANG programs + decision report)

- **M-STD-STRING-PERF: O(n) string processing primitives** —
  Six new builtins for high-performance string processing, unblocking real-world
  email/HTML parsing that previously hung at 1.8GB with O(n²) accumulator patterns.
  - `decodeQuotedPrintable(s)`: Native Go RFC 2045 §6.7 decoder — single-pass O(n)
    using `strings.Builder`. 37KB email decodes in ~80μs (was hanging indefinitely).
  - `replaceMany(s, pairs)`: Multi-pattern replacement via `strings.NewReplacer`
    (Aho-Corasick). 23-pattern HTML entity decoding on 50KB in ~150μs.
  - `foldSlices(s, delim, acc, f)`: Fold over split segments without list allocation.
    Iterates via `strings.Index`, calls callback per segment.
  - `mapSlicesJoin(s, delim, f)`: Split-transform-join in O(n) using `strings.Builder`.
    3000 segments in ~100μs. Recommended for split→transform→concat workflows.
  - `startsWithIgnoreCase(s, prefix)`: Case-insensitive prefix check using
    `strings.EqualFold` on prefix slice. Useful for header parsing.
  - ASCII fast-path for `substring`/`find`: Skips `[]rune` conversion when input is
    pure ASCII (99% of email/HTML). 24x faster for 50KB strings (15μs vs 356μs).
  New file: `internal/builtins/string_encoding.go` (QP decoder).
  All builtins registered in `std/string` module with full metadata, determinism
  verification (`-count=20`), and benchmarks.
  (~560 LOC implementation + tests + benchmarks)

## [v0.10.3] - 2026-04-03

### Fixed
- **Fix multipart file upload field name mismatch** —
  `serve-api` multipart uploads silently dropped files when the curl field name
  didn't match the AILANG param name (e.g. `curl -F 'file=@doc.docx'` with param
  `filepath: string`). Now uses 3-pass matching: (1) exact name match, (2) positional
  fallback for unmatched files to unmatched string/bytes params, (3) zero-value pad.
  Extracted `readMultipartFile` helper to deduplicate file reading. 4 new tests.

## [v0.10.2] - 2026-04-03

### Fixed
- **M-DX-SERVE-API-ERROR-STATUS: Map Result.Err to proper HTTP status codes** —
  serve-api now returns HTTP 400 (not 200) when an AILANG function returns `Err(...)`.
  Custom status codes via `Err({_status: 404, message: "not found"})`. `_status` is
  extracted for the HTTP response and stripped from the body (same convention as `@raw`).
  `Ok(value)` responses are unwrapped automatically — clients receive the inner value
  directly instead of `{"__tag": "Ok", ...}`. Works with both wrapped and `@nowrap` paths.
- **Fix serve-api type coercion and improve DX error messages** —
  Better error messages when serve-api encounters type mismatches. Improved developer
  experience for debugging API endpoint issues.
- **Reject underscores in ParseFloat across all user-facing paths** —
  Underscores in numeric literals (e.g., `1_000`) were incorrectly accepted by the
  serve-api args parser as digit separators. ParseFloat now rejects underscores
  consistently across all user-facing code paths.

### Changed
- **Auto-attach binary MD5 and version to bug reports** —
  `ailang bug` now automatically includes the binary MD5 hash and version info,
  making it easier to diagnose version-related issues.
- **Unify registry validator URL config** —
  Registry validator URL now defaults to `registry.ailang.sunholo.com` consistently.

## [v0.10.1] - 2026-04-01

### Added
- **ailang unpublish: Remove packages from registry** —
  New `ailang unpublish <package>@<version>` CLI command with confirmation prompt and
  `--force` flag. Validator DELETE `/unpublish` endpoint deletes GCS objects and updates
  `index.json` with optimistic locking. Removes package entry entirely if no versions remain.

### Fixed
- **M-MULTIPART-BYTES: Fix multipart file upload BytesValue crash in serve-api** —
  Multipart file uploads to serve-api endpoints crashed with `_str_len: expected String,
  got *eval.BytesValue` because `parseMultipartArgs()` created `BytesValue` for file fields
  but passed them positionally to functions expecting string params. Added
  `parseMultipartArgsWithNames()` with named field→parameter matching and type-aware coercion:
  file fields mapped to `string` params write to temp file and pass path; file fields mapped
  to `bytes` params pass `BytesValue` directly. Temp files cleaned up via `defer cleanup()`
  after function returns. Falls back to positional parsing for backward compat when no
  `paramNames` are available. (~90 LOC implementation, 7 test functions)

### Added
- **M-STD-MAP: Hash map data structure (std/map)** —
  New `Map[k, v]` type with immutable copy-on-write semantics backed by Go hashmaps.
  Keys use canonical encoding (`mapKey()`: type-prefixed strings `i:42`, `s:hello`, `b:true`)
  decoupled from display. Lookups O(1), inserts/removes O(n) copy-on-write, iteration
  O(n log n) sorted for A1 determinism. 10 builtins: `empty`, `insert`, `lookup`, `member`,
  `remove`, `size`, `keys`, `values`, `fromList`, `toList`. New `TMap` type node with
  `TApp("Map",...)` bidirectional unification. Updated 13 type switch locations. Stdlib
  wrapper `std/map.ail` with cost model docs. Also added `std/array.empty` builtin.
  Fixed parser bug where generic zero-arg functions (`func f[T]() -> R`) skipped return
  type parsing. Fixed S-CALL0 arity for `_array_empty` and `_map_empty`.
  Added `array.append` convenience builtin. Dedicated unit tests for MapValue/MapKey/TMap
  with `-count=20` determinism verification.
  (~900 LOC implementation + stdlib + tests, example: `map_basic.ail`)
- **M-STREAMING-ZIP-XML: Streaming ZIP+XML fold builtins** —
  New `parseFold` (pure XML fold) and `scanFold` (combined ZIP+XML streaming fold)
  builtins for memory-efficient processing of large XML inside ZIP archives. `scanFold`
  pipes `zip.File.Open()` stream directly into `xml.NewDecoder` — the decompressed XML
  is never materialized as a contiguous string. Memory: O(largest element + accumulator)
  instead of O(entire document). Solves XLSX OOM on 8.7 MB files where shared strings
  XML decompresses to ~100 MB. New files: `zip_xml.go`, `xml_fold_test.go`,
  `zip_xml_test.go`. Examples: `xml_fold.ail`, `zip_xml_fold.ail`.
  (~200 LOC implementation, 16 test functions, 3 benchmarks)
- **M-EXIT-CODE: Process exit with explicit exit code** —
  New `exit(code: int)` function in `std/io` terminates the AILANG process with a
  caller-specified exit code. Uses a sentinel panic (`EvalExitCode`) that propagates
  up to the runtime, which flushes telemetry/traces before calling `os.Exit(code)`.
  Requires IO capability (`--caps IO`). Unblocks CLI tool development and
  ai-coding-lang-bench Track A (minigit). Example: `examples/runnable/exit_code.ail`.
  (~80 LOC implementation, 4 integration tests)
- **M-EVAL-XLANG/M1: Bitwise operators** —
  Full pipeline support for bitwise `&` (AND), `^` (XOR), `~` (NOT), `<<` (shift left),
  `>>` (shift right). Implements across lexer, parser, elaborator, type checker, evaluator,
  op-lowering, SMT codegen, and builtin registry. Uses C-standard dedicated precedence bands:
  `|| && ^ & == < << + *` (bitwise AND/XOR bind looser than comparison, shifts bind looser
  than addition). Bitwise OR available as `bitwiseOr()` stdlib function (operator `|` deferred
  to avoid context-sensitive parsing). 6 builtins registered with metadata. 11 precedence
  test cases for mixed expressions. Example: `examples/runnable/bitwise.ail`.
  (~400 LOC, 10 test functions + 11 precedence tests)
- **M-CONS-EXPR: Cons (::) in expression position** —
  The `::` operator now works in expression position (e.g., `1 :: [2, 3]`) in addition
  to its existing pattern matching support. The parser already desugared `x :: xs` to
  a `FuncCall`, but the elaborator lacked a special case to route it through the builtin.
  Added `VarGlobal` emission in `normalizeFuncCall` when `::` is in `globalEnv`, and
  fixed `runSingle` pipeline to register builtin global types for the type checker.
  Supports chained cons (`1 :: 2 :: 3 :: []`), cons in if/match branches, and all
  element types. Example: `examples/runnable/cons_expression.ail`.
  (~40 LOC implementation, 20 test cases across elaborator/pipeline/REPL)

### Fixed
- **serve-api: discover and dispatch @route annotations in package dependencies** —
  `serve-api` now scans transitively imported package modules for `@route` annotations,
  not just local modules. Also fixes dispatch: the engine falls through to
  `LoadAndEvaluate` when `compileModule` fails for package paths, the catch-all handler
  checks custom routes as a fallback, eager loading skips `pkg/` modules (already
  preloaded — re-compiling from basePath corrupted canonical paths), and duplicate
  route registration is deduped to prevent Go 1.22+ ServeMux panics. Dual-key preload
  ensures package modules are cached under both their resolved canonical path (e.g.,
  `pkg/sunholo/ailang_parse/types/document`) and their declared module path (e.g.,
  `docparse/types/document`), so `LoadAndEvaluate`'s recursive import resolution
  finds them regardless of which key is used. (~100 LOC, 5 tests)
- **M-DX-MODULE-PREFIX: module_prefix used for package file resolution** —
  Packages with `module_prefix` (e.g., `module_prefix = "docparse"` in sunholo/ailang_parse)
  now resolve imports correctly. Previously, importing `pkg/sunholo/ailang_parse/types/document`
  only searched `types/document.ail` and `src/types/document.ail`, missing files at
  `docparse/types/document.ail`. The resolver now adds prefix-based candidates using
  `MapImportToModulePath()`, trying both standard and prefix-based directory layouts.
  Unblocks all packages using module_prefix for registry consumption. (~10 LOC fix, 4 tests)
- **M-DX-MODULE-PREFIX: bare intra-package imports resolve via module_prefix** —
  When a consumed package's module has a bare import like `import docparse/types/document`,
  the loader now resolves it via the module_prefix map instead of searching the consumer's
  working directory. The pipeline passes the prefix→package mapping to the module loader,
  which remaps `docparse/types/document` → `pkg/sunholo/ailang_parse/types/document` before
  resolving. Falls back to project-relative resolution for non-matching prefixes.
  (~25 LOC fix, 2 tests)

## [v0.10.0] - 2026-03-30

> This release includes all previously-unreleased work from v0.9.3 through v0.9.12 milestones below.

### Added
- **M-BRAIN-CONTEXT: Contextual brain injection during active sessions** —
  New `brain_context.sh` PreToolUse hook injects relevant brain knowledge when
  files are read during Claude Code sessions, not just at session start. Surfaces
  past resolutions, design decisions, and file context aligned with what you're
  actually working on. Three noise-prevention mechanisms: directory-level cooldown
  (5 min default), session token budget (2000 tokens, soft warning), and relevance
  threshold filtering (score >= 0.25). Configurable via `AILANG_BRAIN_COOLDOWN_SECS`,
  `AILANG_BRAIN_BUDGET_TOKENS`, `AILANG_BRAIN_MIN_SCORE` env vars. Graceful
  degradation — exits silently if ailang unavailable or brain empty. (~80 LOC)
- **M-STDLIB-CRYPTO-JWT: RSA signature verification and JWT support** —
  New `rsaVerifyPKCS1v15(message, signature, publicKeyPEM)` builtin in `std/crypto`
  verifies RSA-SHA256 signatures against PEM-encoded public keys or X.509 certificates
  (Firebase key format). New `fromBase64URL` in `std/bytes` decodes JWT's base64url
  encoding (RFC 4648 §5, no padding). New `std/jwt` module provides pure AILANG functions:
  `decodeJWT` (parse without verification), `verifyRS256` (decode + RSA verify),
  `verifyWithKid` (key selection by kid header — Firebase/OAuth pattern), and claims
  helpers (`isExpired`, `checkIssuer`, `checkAudience`). All functions are pure — key
  fetching is the caller's responsibility via `std/net`. (~350 LOC implementation,
  ~250 LOC tests)

- **M-SERVE-API-ENDPOINT-FILTERING: Endpoint filtering and @nowrap JSON auto-unwrap** —
  New `--routes-only` flag for `serve-api` limits HTTP endpoints to `@route`-annotated
  functions only, hiding internal exports from the API surface. New `@noexpose` annotation
  marks individual exported functions as hidden from HTTP (they remain importable by other
  modules). Both mechanisms filter OpenAPI spec generation, A2A Agent Card skills, and
  the startup banner. Also fixes `@nowrap` double-encoding: functions returning valid JSON
  strings via `encode(jo([...]))` now write raw JSON instead of re-encoding through
  `json.Encoder` (producing `{"status":"healthy"}` instead of `"{\"status\":\"healthy\"}"`.
  Auto-unwrap only triggers for JSON objects and arrays, not bare strings/numbers/booleans.
  (~170 LOC implementation, ~100 LOC tests)

- **M-DX-UTF8: New `replace()` string builtin** —
  `replace(s, old, new)` replaces all occurrences of `old` with `new` in string `s`.
  Available in `std/string`. Uses `strings.ReplaceAll` — UTF-8 safe, pure, no regex.
  Codegen spec included for compiled Go output.

- **M-EVAL-XLANG: New filesystem directory builtins** —
  Five new builtins in `std/fs`: `mkdir(path)` creates a single directory,
  `mkdirAll(path)` creates a directory tree recursively (like `mkdir -p`),
  `isDir(path)` checks if a path is a directory, `isFile(path)` checks if a path
  is a regular file, and `removeFile(path)` deletes a file or empty directory.
  All respect `AILANG_FS_SANDBOX` and require FS capability. Unblocks the
  cross-language AI code generation benchmark (mini-git task requires directory
  creation). Includes codegen stubs for compiled Go output.
  (~150 LOC implementation, ~200 LOC tests, example: `examples/runnable/directory_ops.ail`)

### Improved
- **M-SERVE-API-ZERO-VALUE-PADDING: Zero-value padding for missing named parameters** —
  Missing named parameters in serve-api requests now receive type-appropriate zero-values
  (`""` for string, `0` for int, `false` for bool) instead of unit, which crashed on type
  operations like `length()`. Functions can now validate inputs with `if apiKey == "" then ...`
  and return structured errors instead of framework-level crashes. Positional `{"args": [...]}`
  with fewer elements than expected is also padded. OpenAPI spec now includes `x-ailang-param-types`.
  Per-parameter types extracted from AST at module load time. (~150 LOC)

### Fixed
- **M-DX-UTF8: Fix find() returning byte offset instead of character index** —
  `_str_find` runtime implementation returned raw `strings.Index()` byte offset, causing
  incorrect results when composed with `substring()` on strings containing multi-byte
  UTF-8 characters (e.g., `find("café world", "world")` returned 6 instead of 5).
  Also fixes the same byte/rune mismatch in Go codegen specs for `_str_len`, `_str_find`,
  and `_str_slice`. Codegen now uses `utf8.RuneCountInString()` for length, converts byte
  offsets to rune indices for find, and operates on `[]rune` for substring slicing.
  Discovered by DocParse agent processing emails with UTF-8 headers.

- **M-VALIDATOR-PATTERN-BINDINGS: Fix validator rejecting pattern match bindings** —
  The `check --package` validator's `collectPatternVars` function did not collect variable
  bindings from `ListPattern.Tail`, causing cons pattern bindings like `rest` in
  `x :: rest =>` to be falsely reported as undefined references. This blocked publishing
  any package using list recursion patterns. Fixed by adding tail traversal to the
  ListPattern case. (~3 LOC fix, regression from M-PKG-INTERREF)
- **M-PKG-INTERREF: Package inter-function reference fix** — Fixed resolver context leak
  where on-demand module evaluation didn't accumulate Let/LetRec bindings in the evaluator
  environment. This caused "undefined variable" errors when an exported function in a
  dependency package called non-exported helper functions. The resolver now accumulates
  each binding in the evaluator env so sequential declarations can reference earlier ones.
  Added static Core AST scope check to `check --package` as defense-in-depth: walks
  top-level declarations verifying that `core.Var` references resolve to in-scope bindings
  from earlier Let/LetRec chains. (~120 LOC)
- **M-ROUTE-COLLISION: Route collision guard & optional A2A** — `registerCustomRoutes()` now
  skips `@route` annotations that collide with built-in paths (health, meta, A2A, MCP) instead
  of panicking on Go 1.22+ ServeMux duplicate pattern registration. A2A protocol endpoints
  (`/.well-known/agent.json`, `/a2a/`) are now opt-in via the `--a2a` flag, mirroring the
  existing `--mcp-http` pattern. This fixes a production crash in docparse Cloud Run deploys
  where a stale `@route("GET", "/.well-known/agent.json")` annotation collided with the
  built-in A2A handler.

### Added
- **M-AI-IMAGE: AI image generation via std/ai** — Two new functions in `std/ai` for generating
  images via AI models. `callImage(prompt, output_path, options)` generates an image and saves it
  to disk (requires `{AI, FS}` effects). `callImageBase64(prompt, options)` returns the image as a
  JSON string with base64-encoded data and mime_type (requires `{AI}` only). Options is a JSON
  string supporting `aspect_ratio` and `mime_type` fields. Gemini provider (`gemini-2.5-flash-image`)
  implements image generation via `ResponseModalities: ["IMAGE"]`. Other providers (OpenAI, Anthropic,
  Ollama) return clear "not supported" errors. Includes stub handler for deterministic testing.
  (~430 LOC: provider types, Gemini extraction, effects, builtins, stdlib, example)
- **M-SERVE-API-AGENT-ENHANCEMENTS: Named parameter binding & @nowrap custom headers** —
  serve-api now supports named JSON parameter binding: agents can send `{"path": "file.docx",
  "output_format": "blocks"}` instead of positional `{"args": ["file.docx", "blocks"]}`. JSON
  keys are matched to function parameter names with automatic snake_case→camelCase conversion.
  Positional `{"args": [...]}` format remains fully backward compatible. Additionally, `@nowrap`
  routes can now set custom HTTP response headers via a `_headers` field in the return record —
  the field is extracted as headers and excluded from the JSON body. Parameter names are exposed
  in the OpenAPI spec via `x-ailang-param-names` and request body descriptions.
  (~360 LOC: handler, routes, server, openapi, tests, docs)
- **M-STDLIB-UUID4: `uuid4()` in std/rand** — New builtin generates RFC 4122 v4 UUID strings
  using `crypto/rand`. Available as `rand.uuid4()` with the `Rand` effect. Useful for generating
  unique API keys, request IDs, and correlation tokens without verbose hex-char approaches.
- **M-ROUTE-NOWRAP: `@nowrap` annotation for unwrapped @route responses** — `@route` handlers
  annotated with `@nowrap` return their result as raw JSON without the `FunctionCallResponse`
  envelope (`{result, module, func, elapsed_ms}`). Enables protocol-compliant responses for
  A2A agent cards, OpenID discovery docs, and any REST endpoint where consumers expect a
  specific JSON schema. Composes with `@raw` for full request/response control. Timing is
  still available via `X-Elapsed-Ms` response header.
- **M-DX-ROUTE-CTX: `@raw` annotation for HTTP request context** — `@route` handlers can now
  receive full HTTP request context (headers, raw body, method, path, query) by adding the `@raw`
  annotation. The handler receives a single `HttpRequest` record argument instead of parsed args.
  Use case: webhook handlers that need signature headers (Stripe, GitHub, Slack). Non-`@raw` routes
  are completely unchanged. (~320 LOC: parser, apiserver, tests, example, docs)
- **M-DEBUG-ERASURE: Debug is now a true ghost effect** — Debug no longer cascades through
  function signatures. Functions can call `Debug.log()` and `Debug.check()` without declaring
  `! {Debug}` — the effect is invisible to callers. Additionally, `--release` flag now erases
  Debug calls to `()` (unit) and removes Debug from effect rows. Ghost effect transparency is
  implemented via `eraseGhostEffects()` in the effect validator; release erasure via `DebugEraser`
  pipeline pass. Both `ailang run --release` and `ailang compile --release` work.
- **Debug ghost effect: fully invisible DX** — Three remaining friction points removed:
  (1) Package `[effects].max` no longer needs to list Debug — ghost effects bypass ceiling checks.
  (2) `--caps Debug` no longer required at runtime — Debug is auto-granted in every EffContext.
  (3) Debug output now collected and printed to stderr after execution (was silently dropped).
  serve-api flushes Debug logs per request via `log.Printf`. All three changes make Debug
  truly invisible — packages just `import pkg/sunholo/logging/logger` and it works.
- **M-PKG-EXPLORER: Package Explorer Website** — Browse all published AILANG packages at
  `ailang.sunholo.com/docs/packages/`. Features: per-vendor/per-package static pages mirroring
  module import paths (`/docs/packages/sunholo/auth`), interactive explorer with search/filter/stats,
  dependency graph visualization with effect-colored nodes, version timeline with provenance chains,
  and ecosystem stats charts. Hybrid static+dynamic architecture: build-time sync generates MDX pages,
  runtime React components hydrate with live data from registry-validator API.
  - Registry validator: new read-only `/api/packages`, `/api/packages/:vendor/:name`, `/api/stats`
    endpoints with in-memory cache (5-min TTL, invalidated on publish) and CORS
  - Build pipeline: `sync-registry.sh` generates per-vendor/per-package MDX pages and sidebar items
  - React components: PackageDetail, VersionTimeline, ProvenanceChain, PackageExplorer,
    DependencyGraph, EcosystemStats, VendorIndex — all with dark/light theme support
  - Data hooks: stale-while-revalidate with localStorage caching and graceful API-down fallback
- **serve-api: GET query parameter support** — GET requests now accepted for function calls.
  Query parameters parsed as positional (`?args=1&args=2`) or named (`?name=Alice`) arguments.
  Existing `@route("GET", ...)` annotations now receive arguments they previously lost.
- **`ailang install @latest`** — Bare `ailang install sunholo/auth` and `ailang install sunholo/auth@latest`
  resolve the latest version from the registry index and write the exact version to `ailang.toml`.
  Semver ranges (`^`, `~`, `>=`) are explicitly rejected — AILANG requires exact versions in manifests.
- **Manifest version guardrail** — `ailang.toml` validation now rejects non-exact dependency versions
  (`latest`, `^0.1.0`, etc.) with actionable error messages pointing to `ailang install @latest`.

### Fixed
- **M-DX-XPKG-RESOLVE: Cross-package stdlib function resolution** (~120 LOC, 4 unit tests + integration test)
  - Functions from stdlib packages (e.g., `asString` from `std/json`) that internally construct ADT
    values (e.g., `Some(s)`) failed when called cross-package — the evaluator used the CALLER's
    resolver instead of the DEFINING module's resolver, so `$adt` constructor lookups couldn't find
    `Option.Some`/`Option.None`. Fix: `FunctionValue` now captures the defining module's `Resolver`
    at closure creation time. At call sites, a `FallbackResolver` tries the caller's resolver first
    (preserving builtins and effect context), then falls back to the function's defining resolver
    (for constructors and module-specific lookups). Three call paths patched: direct application,
    auto-curry, and `applyFunction`.
- **Resolver version conflict detection** — `ailang lock` now fails with a structured `VersionConflictError`
  when a transitive dependency requires a different version of a package than the root manifest pins.
  Previously, the resolver silently used whichever version was encountered first (Go map iteration order),
  which could downgrade direct dependencies. Error includes conflicting versions, which package introduced
  the conflict, and actionable suggestions (republish or change root pin). Dependency iteration is now
  sorted by name for deterministic resolution order.
- **Portable lock file** — `ailang.lock` no longer stores absolute cache paths for registry and git
  packages. Paths are resolved at runtime via `CachedPackagePath()` and `GitCache.CacheDir()`.
  Lock files can now be copied into Docker containers and CI environments without re-resolving.
  Old lock files with stored paths continue to work (backward compatible).
